           TIP maintenance I/O can penetrate read and write inhibits.
           Combinations of inhibits are allowed. However, a write inhibit (W) can be
            established only in combination with a read inhibit (R). If the pack is UP and
            unassigned, inhibits cannot be established unless the assign inhibit (A) is included.

Removing a Pack from the System Pack List
        Use the RD keyin to delete a DN or PRESENT removable pack from the system’s list of
        packs. The RD keyin can be used to make room for new packs when the system pack
        list is full. A pack cannot be removed until all files on the pack have been freed.

Down Pack Cautions and Limitations
        Use the Down Pack feature cautiously in a non-TIP or TIP simplex environment. I/O
        requests to down packs fail with an I/O error 015. If an Exec I/O request receives an
        I/O error 015 (for example, a down pack contains system files), the system can stop. If
        a UDS I/O request receives an I/O error 015 (for example, a down pack contains
        database files), UDS can bring down the entire software application. In this
        environment, you must carefully consider whether using the Down Pack feature, and
        the data recovery procedures it requires, is preferable to a system stop and
        autorecovery.

        A DN,PACK keyin cannot prevent the system stop that can occur when an Exec I/O
        request receives an error status.

        A removable pack is not placed in the list of UP, DN, or PRESENT packs until it has
        been assigned, brought down (using a DN,PACK keyin), or had inhibits placed on it
        (using an MD keyin). Therefore, such a pack does not appear in the output of an
        FS,PACK keyin.

        Since a down pack is disassociated from the disk device that contains it, a device
        containing a down pack does not appear in the output of an FS,RDISKS keyin. An FS
        devnam keyin reports the status of the device, but no information about the down
        pack.




5–28                                                                                 7831 0281–034
                                                                   Tape and Disk Operations




5.2.2. Adding a New Fixed Disk During a Boot
         You can add a new fixed disk to the system at MODIFY CONFIG time, but to be up and
         usable it has to be the current DRS disk. This implies that you are doing a tape boot,
         and the new device is specified as the system disk in SCF. If the new disk is not used
         as the current DRS disk, the boot proceeds, but the following messages are displayed,
         and the disk is internally downed:

           DEVICE nnn, FRESHLY PREPPED
           DN OF nnn INTERNAL REQUEST WAS RECEIVED
           nnn DN

         where nnn is the new disk device.

5.2.3. Unit Duplexing
         Unit Duplexing reduces system vulnerability to mass storage failures. It consists of a
         hardware configuration in which each mass storage device has an associated backup
         device that is transparent to all end-user software and to most system software.

         If a user program issues a request to read or write to a file on a unit-duplexed device,
         the write request is automatically performed on both devices and the read request is
         performed on either device. From a software point of view, the unit-duplexed devices
         are bit-for-bit identical.

         TIP files on unit-duplexed devices must be simplex TIP files.

         For additional information, refer to the Unit Duplexing Planning, Installation, and
         Operations Overview (7830 7592).




7831 0281–034                                                                                  5–29
Tape and Disk Operations




5–30                       7831 0281–034
Section 6
Performing and Processing System
Dumps

         This section describes how to perform system dumps (also referred to as Exec
         dumps, panic dumps, or Exec panic dumps), how to use runstreams from the Dump
         Analysis Library (DMPLIB) to process the dumps, and how to register and process
         dumps through the RSS interface. Information on dump file management is provided in
         the Exec System Software Administration Reference Manual (7831 0323).

         This section also describes how to download the WinDump utility and access the
         WinDump User’s Guide. WinDump provides a GUI interface to DMPLIB and an
         efficient alternative to RSS.


6.1. Dumping System Memory
         Note: The Exec supports only labeled dump tapes. All Exec dump tapes must be
         labeled.

         System dumps are used for diagnosing Exec software problems. The dump process,
         which copies the contents of registers and main memory to either tape or mass
         storage, can be either automatic or manual.

         An automatic dump (part of autorecovery) is generally more efficient than a manual
         dump. A manual dump is an option during a mass storage or tape recovery. The
         system dump file that the Exec writes is SYS$*PANIC$DUMP$$. If this file is copied
         from tape to mass storage, the result is a one-file dump file. See the Exec System
         Software Administration Reference Manual (7831 0323) for information on using the
         COPYDUMP program and other means of managing system dump files, and details on
         system dump file names and formats.

         Mass storage dumps rather than tape dumps are recommended because of the large
         number of tapes required for tape dumps. A consideration for mass storage dumps,
         however, is that you cannot access user files on mass storage while booting the
         system. Thus, you cannot copy the contents of those files into a dump file, and you
         cannot obtain a dump listing of those files.




7831 0281–034                                                                             6–1
Performing and Processing System Dumps




6.1.1.   Automatic Dumps
         These are the steps for performing an automatic dump (to mass storage):

         1.   The system reloads the Exec from disk.
         2.   Answer M to the message
               SAVE PANIC DUMP TO MASS STORAGE M,NO?

         3.   The system automatically assigns mass storage files and writes to them.
         4. When the dump is complete, the message PANIC DUMP TAKEN is displayed on
            the system console.

         If an error occurs so that the dump cannot be written to mass storage, you receive an
         I/O error message, the system frees and deletes the dump file, then asks you again if
         you want to save the dump to mass storage. If jump key 3 is set, autorecovery is
         suppressed.

         To dump to tape, you must do a manual dump (see Section 6.1.2).

6.1.2. Manual Dumps for ClearPath IX Systems
         To perform a manual dump to tape on a ClearPath IX system

         1.   Load the boot tape on the tape drive.
              The tape drive has to correspond to the drive specified in the load path of the
              partition definition.
         2.   Set the boot type to tape by entering SET-TYPE TAPE (or ST TAPE).
         3.   Enter SET-TAPE <device> (or STD <device>) to specify the name of the boot
              device.
              Note: The SET-TAPE <device> command needs to be entered only once on
              your system. This setting stays in effect for future boots. However, if you set
              the type to tape, the setting does not stay in effect for future boots; it defaults
              to disk.
         4. Enter SJ 2 (for jump key 2).
         5.   Enter SP (for start partition).
         6. When you receive the following message, answer with the device name:
                Enter name of tape drive for manual tape dump

         7.   When you receive the following message, answer with the volume serial number
              (transmit to request a labeled blank):
                Enter Volume Serial Number for manual tape dump




6–2                                                                                7831 0281–034
                                                Performing and Processing System Dumps




         8. Mount the tape and answer any messages that appear.
              The message
                Panic dump saved...

              signifies that the dump has been successfully written to tape.

         If an error occurs and the dump cannot be written to the specified tape device, an I/O
         error is issued and you are prompted again for a dump device name.

         To perform a manual dump to disk:

         1.   Set the boot type to disk by entering SET-TYPE DISK (or ST DISK).
         2.   Enter SJ 2 (for jump key 2).
         3.   Enter SP (for start partition).
              You receive the following message:
                Save panic dump to mass storage - M,NO?

         4. Respond with M to save the dump to disk or NO to abort the dump.
              If you respond with M, the message
                Panic dump saved...

              signifies the dump has been successfully written to disk.

         If an error occurs and the dump cannot be written to the specified disk device, an I/O
         error is issued and you are prompted again for a dump device name.

6.1.3. Online Dumps
         If you want to obtain an Exec dump without stopping the system, or if you want to
         look at some data structure at a specific time, take the steps below to perform an
         online dump.

         Note: See the DU MP keyin in Section 11 for information about specifying a run-ID
         to get output on a desired run.

         1.   Enter the online dump keyin as follows:
                DU MP

              The keyin produces the following messages:
                Panic dump taken
                State the reason for the online dump




7831 0281–034                                                                                 6–3
Performing and Processing System Dumps




       2.   Provide a 60-character, free-format response to be placed in the dump file reader
            for future reference.
            The following message appears:
              Save online dump to tape - T,NO?

       3.   Respond with T to write the dump to tape; respond NO to abort the online dump
            and terminate all further DU MP keyin processing.
       4. If you respond with T, the following message is generated in preparation for
          writing the online dump:
              Enter name of tape drive for online tape dump

       5.   Enter the name of an up and available tape drive.

       6. When you receive the following message, answer with the volume serial number
          (transmit to request a labeled blank):
              Enter Volume Serial Number for online tape dump

       7.   Mount a tape and answer any messages that appear.
            The message
              Panic dump saved...

            signifies that the online dump has been successfully written to tape.

       Performing an online dump may produce invalid data, which could lead to
       unacceptable results from DMPLIB processing. Although most DMPLIB functions
       perform general checking, this checking is successful only if the structure being
       processed is valid and correct.


6.2. Processing Dumps
       DMPLIB, which includes Dump Analysis Procedures (DAP) functions, provides
       automated analysis and display of Exec data structures to aid in problem diagnosis.

       The input used in dump analysis is either a dump tape or a one-file mass storage
       dump. DMPLIB output is an edited display or printout of Exec main and mass storage
       structures that vary in format, depending on which type of dump processing you
       select.

       An alternative to the methods described in this subsection is the WinDump utility,
       which lets you use DMPLIB from a PC workstation. See Section 6.3 for more
       information.




6–4                                                                             7831 0281–034
                                             Performing and Processing System Dumps




6.2.1. Dump Processing in Batch Mode
         You can use DMPLIB in batch mode at the system console when a demand terminal is
         unavailable. You can select several different dump types and input formats with the ST
         keyin. The DMPLIB batch runstreams are generated by elements found in file
         EXEC*DUMPLIBS. as follows:

           ST EXEC*DUMPLIBS.type,set,runid,account/user-id

         where:

         type
            specifies the type of analysis to be performed. The following are possible choices:

            ILSDMP            This is an ILES dump. See the special condition word values for
                              ILES dump processing under the description of the set
                              parameter.
            MICRO             This is a MICRO dump. This analysis produces the MICRO dump
                              output as in MICRO/RSS, but does not register the output file
                              with Remote System Support (RSS). This dump can be used if
                              RSS is not installed at your site.
            MICRO/RSS         This is a MICRO dump. This is a summary of data structures that
                              pertain to the problem. The MICRO dump format varies with the
                              problem. Using the RSS program, DMPLIB registers the dump
                              with RSS so it can be transmitted to the Unisys Customer
                              Support Center.


                                              Caution
                You should use MICRO/RSS for tape dumps only. If you use MICRO/RSS to
                process a mass storage dump, the dump file is deleted from the system.
                This prevents further analysis of the problem. To process mass storage
                dumps through RSS, use the RSS-REG runstream described in Section 6.4.



            RSDUMP             This is a SHORT dump. The output is the same as SDUMP, but
                               the DIAG$ file is recovered from the system boot tape that is
                               required as additional input.
                               Note: RSDUMP is supported (and accepts input) only for
                               dumps that have been written to tape. Mass storage dumps
                               cannot be processed with RSDUMP. That is, do not specify 2,
                               4, 5, or 6 as the set value.
            RTSDUMP            This is a Communications Simulator (CS 2200) dump.
            SDUMP              This is a SHORT dump. The edited output contains a detailed
                               analysis of all Exec data structures.




7831 0281–034                                                                                6–5
Performing and Processing System Dumps




       set
             This is a number specifying the input format of the Exec system dump or dumps
             to be processed. The following are possible choices for Exec system dumps:

             0     processes all existing cycles of SYS$*PANIC$DUMP$$ and then asks you
                   for the reel number and equipment type of dump tapes to be processed.
             1     processes several dump tapes. Asks you for the reel number and
                   equipment type of each dump tape to be processed.
             2     processes only the latest cycle of SYS$*PANIC$DUMP$$.
             3     processes only one dump tape. Asks you for the reel number and
                   equipment type of the tape to be processed.
             4     processes all existing cycles of SYS$*PANIC$DUMP$$.
             5     asks you for the mass storage name of the dump file.
             6     processes only the oldest cycle of SYS$*PANIC$DUMP$$.

             For ILES dumps (ILSDMP as type), use one of the following values for the set (or
             condition word) parameter to control the dump processing:

             0     processes the latest cycle of the ILES dump. Do not delete the dump file.
             1     processes the latest cycle of the ILES dump. Delete the dump file.
             2     processes all cycles of the ILES dump. Do not delete the dump files.
             3     processes all cycles of the ILES dump. Delete the dump files.
             4     processes the oldest cycle of the ILES dump. Do not delete the dump file.
             5     processes the oldest cycle of the ILES dump. Delete the dump file.
             6     asks you for the ILES dump file cycle number and delete option.

             Note: To perform an ILES dump, user-ID must be executing with system high
             clearance level, all compartments, and the bypass ownership privilege.

       runid
             is the run ID number for the dump analysis run.

       account
             is the account number for the dump analysis run.




6–6                                                                                7831 0281–034
                                                Performing and Processing System Dumps




Examples of Dump Editing Options
         Following are some examples of the dump editing options and input formats you can
         select with the ST keyin.

         The following console keyin produces an SDUMP, with run-ID SHORT, from a dump
         tape. A query requests the reel number and equipment type of the dump tape to be
         processed. The SDUMP is processed under account 123456 for user-ID ABC.

           ST EXEC*DUMPLIBS.SDUMP,3,SHORT,123456/ABC

         The following keyin produces an SDUMP, with run-ID XER070, from one or three
         user-specified mass storage files. A system console query requests mass storage file
         name or names.

           ST EXEC*DUMPLIBS.SDUMP,5,XER070,123456/ABC

         The following keyin produces an SDUMP recovery dump with the DIAG$ file
         recovered from the system boot tape. A system console query requests the reel
         number and equipment type of both the dump tape and the system boot tape.

           ST EXEC*DUMPLIBS.RSDUMP,3,RECDMP,123456/ABC

         The following keyin produces an SDUMP from the latest cycle of file
         SYS$*PANIC$DUMP$$.

           ST EXEC*DUMPLIBS.SDUMP,2,PNCDMP,123456/ABC

         The following keyin produces a MICRO dump, with run-ID DUMP01, from a dump tape,
         and then registers the output file with RSS. The file can then be transmitted to the
         Unisys Support Center.

           ST EXEC*DUMPLIBS.MICRO/RSS,3,DUMP01,123456/ABC

Additional Operator Queries
         The DUMPLIBS batch runstreams can query you for additional information about the
         dumps to be processed.

         If the set value specified in the DUMPLIBS start statement is 5, the following query
         appears. Enter only one file name and transmit. To terminate the query, enter nothing
         and transmit.

           Enter one dump file name

         For mass storage dumps involving one or more cycles of SYS$*PANIC$DUMP$$ (for
         example, if the set value is 0, 2, 4, or 6), the following query appears. Enter Y (yes) to
         delete the dump cycles or N (no), which is the default, to save them.

           Should cycle(s) of SYS$*PANIC$DUMP$$ be deleted after processing? Y/<N>




7831 0281–034                                                                                    6–7
Performing and Processing System Dumps




       An N or NO response to the following panic dump tape query terminates the query
       and runstream generation.

            Enter panic dump tape REELNO,EQP or NO

       or
            Enter boot (recovery) tape REELNO,EQP

       To specify a reel number for the panic dump tape or boot tape, include the equipment
       type after a comma, as in the following example:

            reel,equip-type

       This assigns the reel on the specified equipment. If you do not specify an equipment
       type, the default is T.

6.2.2. Dump Processing in Demand Mode
       You can process any dump from a demand terminal by entering the following @ADD
       statement:

            @ADD EXEC*DUMPLIBS.IADUMP

       IADUMP queries you for dump information. It then generates a runstream to process
       the dump. IADUMP prints a message stating the name of the file and element that the
       runstream is placed into.

       For example:

            Dump runstream is in file DUMP-hhmmss.DUMPRUN

       Queries encountered in IADUMP are as follows. You can enter EXIT in response to any
       query to terminate IADUMP processing.

Query 1
       This query lets you select the type of dump to be performed. Dump types are
       described in Section 6.2.1:

            Enter the corresponding number to select one of the following dump
            types:
             1: SDUMP - a normal SHORT dump
             2: RSDUMP - a normal SHORT dump with recovered DIAG$ file
             3: MICRO - a MICRO dump
             4: MICRO/RSS - a MICRO dump registered with RSS
             5: RTSDUMP - a CS 1100 dump
             [n]/<EXIT>




6–8                                                                           7831 0281–034
                                              Performing and Processing System Dumps




Query 2
         This query lets you select the type of input media the dump to be processed resides
         on:

            Enter the corresponding number to select the number and type of dump
            files to be processed:

             1: process all existing cycles of SYS$*PANIC$DUMP$$. and then
             query terminal for reel numbers of tape dumps to process.
             2: query for and process several tape dumps.
             3: process only latest cycle of SYS$*PANIC$DUMP$$.
             4: query for and process only one tape dump.
             5: process all existing cycles of SYS$*PANIC$DUMP$$.
             6: query for and process dump in one mass storage file.
             7: process only the oldest cycle of SYS$*PANIC$DUMP$$.

             [n]/<EXIT>

Query 3
         This query lets you indicate whether you want the dump analysis runstream added (A),
         started (S, the default), or held (H):

            Add, Start, or Hold the runstream? A/<S>/H/EXIT

         The possible responses have the following effects:

         A (add)      The generated runstream is dynamically added to the demand session
                      and the dump is processed in demand mode with output directed to
                      the demand terminal.
         S (start)    The generated runstream is submitted as a batch run. This is the
                      default option.
         H (hold)     The generated runstream is placed in an element for future
                      submission. The runstream can be edited in this element prior to
                      submission.


Query 4
         This query appears only if you have chosen to start (S) or hold (H) the generated
         runstream:

            Default Run-Id = rid, Project-Id = pid, Priority = J
            Enter Run-Id, Project-Id, and Run Priority (A-Z) separated by commas:
            rid,pid,pri/EXIT




7831 0281–034                                                                                6–9
Performing and Processing System Dumps




       The run-ID project-ID, and run priority defaults from the current demand session are
       displayed so you can change them. If you do not specify any parameters, the defaults
       are used. Enter each parameter in its corresponding positional field, separated from
       the next field by a comma. Some examples of responses to the query and the effects
       of the responses are as follows.


                Response                Run-ID             Project-ID            Run Priority

          DMP008,6K9100,F          DMP008               6K9100               F
          DMP008                   DMP008               default              default
          ,6K9100,D                default              6K9100               D
          ,,A                      default              default              A
          EXSTOP,,C                EXSTOP               default              C
          TX1040,W13A35            TX1040               W13A35               default


Query 5
       This query asks whether you want to enter additional stream generation statements
       (SGS), which are described in Section 6.2.3, to specify certain processing options for
       runstream generation:

          Additional SGSs? Y/<N>/EXIT

       If you enter Y, you see the following query:

          Enter SGSs. XMIT blank line when done.

       Enter one SGS per input line. Transmit a blank line when you have finished.

       Additional queries appear depending on the type of input media the dump resides on.
       These queries are the same as those seen on the system console when you start a
       batch run and are explained in Section 6.2.1 under "Additional Operator Queries."

Query 6
       If the dump to be processed resides on mass storage, you receive the following
       query:

          Do you wish to save dumpfile on tape? Y/<N>

       Answer Y to instruct IADUMP to generate ECL, which copies the dumpfile to tape
       prior to processing. You are then asked to provide the tape reel-ID and equipment
       type.




6–10                                                                              7831 0281–034
                                               Performing and Processing System Dumps




6.2.3. Optional Runstream SGSs
         Optional runstream SGSs can be entered during demand mode runstream generation
         in response to the query "Additional SGSs?" Enter the SGSs in any order.

Using a DMPLIB Library from Another File
         In certain cases (such as testing a new DMPLIB), it can be desirable to use a DMPLIB
         library that resides in a file other than EXEC*DUMPLIBS. To specify a different DMPLIB,
         use the following SGS:

           USE DUMP LIBRARY FROM FILE file

         where file is the name of a file containing an alternate DMPLIB library. Enter the file
         name without the terminating dot. This SGS generates the *RESTORE directive with
         the specified file name. When processing the dump, the dump analysis run uses the
         functions found in this file.

Calling Functions Individually in Demand Mode
         If you specified that the runstream must be added (see query 3 under Section 6.2.2),
         you can use the following SGS to call individual functions:

           CALL FUNCTIONS INDIVIDUALLY IN DEMAND MODE

         This SGS assigns and loads the dump file (or files), calls the DAP processor, and
         restores the DMPLIB library. The initial DMPLIB function EXECBDRS is then called.
         EXECBDRS sets up the environment for subsequent DMPLIB function calls. The
         demand session is ready for the user to call other DMPLIB functions.

6.2.4. DMPLIBs
         This subsection describes how to maintain alternate levels of DMPLIBs. It also
         identifies a procedure for submitting raw dump files with WinDump or RSS.

         If your site wants to maintain more than one level of DMPLIB on a system (to process
         dumps with different Exec levels), you can do so by storing other DMPLIB levels in
         alternate files. While processing the dump, DMPLIB restores a library file that
         corresponds to the Exec level it finds in the dump. (The runstream must be generated
         by an ST keyin or an IADUMP.)

         The default file used to process dumps is EXEC*DUMPLIBS. If the Exec level that
         DMPLIB detects in the dump matches the installed DMPLIB level, or if the level is not
         found in the table, then the dump is processed using EXEC*DUMPLIBS. If you need to
         process a dump taken from an earlier level of EXEC, you must catalog an alternate
         DMPLIB file and copy the DMPLIB level required for that Exec level into that alternate
         file. See the table below to determine the file names to catalog for alternate DMPLIBs.




7831 0281–034                                                                                 6–11
Performing and Processing System Dumps




       After the dump is processed, you can @START the SDUMP runstream from the
       EXEC*DUMPLIBS file. SDUMP determines the Exec level from the dump and
       processes the dump using the file corresponding file from the table below. The rows
       in the table show the installed DMPLIB level, and the columns show the Dump Exec
       level to process. Use the appropriate row and column to find the corresponding
       DMPLIB file qualifier to use for Alternate Exec level processing. For example, if
       DMPLIB level 9Ry is installed and SDUMP detects the dump originated on a level 48Ry
       Exec, it uses file EXEC48*DUMPLIBS (DMPLIB 8Ry) for the remainder of the
       processing. Note that all DMPLIB files end with the filename DUMPLIBS.


                             Dump Exec Level

                            49Ry                  48Ry
                  9Ry       EXEC*                 EXEC48*
        DMPLIB
        Level     8Ry                             EXEC*


       If you do not know which level of DMPLIB is currently installed, refer to the updated
       source for function/element SELDUMPLIBS in the REL or NEWSO file. It shows the
       DMPLIB level currently installed in file EXEC*DUMPLIBS (the file referenced by ST
       keyins and used by IADUMP) and the file names it uses when switching to different
       levels of DMPLIB.

Submitting Raw Dump Files
       You can submit raw dump files using either WinDump or RSS. WinDump is the
       recommended method.

WinDump
       WinDump is a Windows application that can submit dump files electronically to the
       Support Center Remote Server using FTP. Dumps sent directly to the Support Center
       Remote Server with WinDump can generally be processed more efficiently, because
       of the fewer steps required to prepare a dump for analysis.

       To use WinDump, you must send dumps from an OS 2200 host that allows Telnet
       access and has either TAS or cpFTP installed.

       See Section 6.3 for information about downloading and installing WinDump.

RSS
       The traditional method for submitting dumps that accompany Contacts and User
       Communication Forms (UCFs) is to use the following statement to call an SSG
       skeleton, which submits raw dump files with RSS:

         @ADD EXEC*DUMPLIBS.RSS-DUMP




6–12                                                                           7831 0281–034
                                               Performing and Processing System Dumps




         The SSG skeleton does the following:

             Queries you for information pertaining to the dump.
             Builds and executes a runstream that facilitates the transfer of the dump files.

         Note: The building of the runstream with this skeleton and the subsequent
         execution of this runstream in DEMAND or BATCH modes requires the appropriate
         privileges based on the security level of the system.


6.3. WinDump Interface—Downloading and
     Installing WinDump
         WinDump is a full-service dump-handling and analysis program, a Windows application
         that allows you to work with dumps on a PC workstation through a GUI interface. It is
         an alternative to both RSS and the direct use of DMPLIBs. Using WinDump, you can
         process a dump, manage the dump files, and transmit dumps to the Unisys Support
         Center Remote Server using FTP. Analysts also use WinDump to run DMPLIBs
         functions through the GUI interface.

         Dumps sent directly to Unisys by WinDump generally arrive faster and can be
         processed more quickly than those sent by RSS. Fewer steps are needed to prepare
         the dumps for analysis because WinDump handles the formatting of dumps
         automatically, correctly, and reliably. For this reason resubmission is rarely
         necessary—an additional savings.

         To allow the use of WinDump, the OS 2200 host from which you wish to send dumps
         must allow Telnet access and have either TAS or cpFTP installed.

         WinDump is supplied with both context-sensitive online help and two printable
         documents: an installation guide and a user’s guide. This subsection describes two
         methods for obtaining WinDump and accessing its printable help.

6.3.1. Downloading WinDump from the Support Center
       Remote Server
         You can download WinDump from the Support Center Remote Server by supplying
         the following URL to your browser:

             https://www.support.unisys.com/pub/2200/utilities/windump/

         This location has the latest downloadable version of WinDump in a self-extracting .EXE
         file. The installation instructions are in a file called INSTALL.DOC.

         After you have installed WinDump, you can find the WinDump User’s Guide in the file
         WINDUMP.DOC in the installed directory. Follow the instructions in Section 4.6 of the
         User’s Guide, "Transferring Dump Files," to prepare dumps and transfer them to the
         Unisys Support Center.




7831 0281–034                                                                                    6–13
Performing and Processing System Dumps




6.3.2. Downloading WinDump from DMPLIBs
       WinDump is also available as part of DMPLIBs. After you install DMPLIBs, look for the
       elements Wdlevel/EXE and WDINSTALL/DOC in the file EXEC*DUMPLIBS, where level
       is the current level of WinDump (for example, 36 for 3.6, 37 for 3.7, and so on).
       Element Wdlevel/EXE contains a self-extracting .EXE, which when executed creates
       the installation files. WDINSTALL/DOC is a Microsoft Word document that describes
       how to install WinDump from Wdlevel/EXE.

       To obtain and install WinDump from EXEC*DUMPLIBS, you must download
       Wdlevel/EXE and WDINSTALL/DOC to the workstation on which you wish to install it.
       Open an MS-DOS window on the workstation as follows:

       1.    Click Start.
       2.    Click Run.
       3.    Type command in the Run dialog text box.
        4. Click OK.

       Enter the following commands:

            >ftp.exe

            >open [IP-address or host-name]

              (where IP-address or host-name is the IP address or symbolic Internet host
              name for FTP of the OS 2200 system from which you are downloading)

            >[userid]

            >[password]

            >[project]

            >type binary

            >quote site ftype omn

              (Not needed if using TAS; required if using cpFTP)

            >get exec*dumplibs.wd[level]/exe c:\temp\wd[level].exe

            >get exec*dumplibs.wdinstall/doc c:\temp\wdinstall.doc

            >quit

            >exit

       After downloading the two files, open the file C:\TEMP\WDINSTALL.DOC in Microsoft
       Word and follow the instructions for installing WinDump.




6–14                                                                           7831 0281–034
                                             Performing and Processing System Dumps




         When you have finished the installation, you can find the WinDump User’s Guide in
         the file WINDUMP.DOC in the installed directory. Follow the instructions in the
         WinDump User’s Guide to prepare dumps and transfer them to the Unisys Support
         Center.


6.4. RSS Interface
         This subsection explains how to register, process, and submit dumps through the
         Remote System Support (RSS) interface.

6.4.1. Registering Dumps
         Mass storage dump files can be registered with RSS for future analysis by using the
         RSS-REG runstream. RSS-REG takes all existing mass storage dump files and copies
         their contents to RSS files. After the dump data is given to RSS, the mass storage
         dump files (all SYS$*PANIC$DUMP$$ F-cycles) are deleted.

         The ST keyin that starts RSS-REG from the system console is

           ST EXEC*DUMPLIBS.RSS-REG,,,acct-id/user-id

6.4.2. Processing Dumps with RSS
         Every mass storage dump registered by RSS-REG can be analyzed by using the RSS
         PROCESS task. System dump files are registered under the name EXEC. The following
         three process names are associated with each dump file:

         MICRO         This process name creates a brief synopsis of the problem, containing
                       only pertinent information. This summary file is registered with RSS
                       and can be transmitted to the Unisys Support Center.
         SHORT         This process name creates and prints a complete listing of all Exec
                       internal structures. The SHORT process name can be used when the
                       support center requests information that you cannot transmit
                       electronically in a MICRO dump.
         TAPE          This process name creates a tape containing the unprocessed mass
                       storage dump data. The tape file is in @COPY,G format. The TAPE
                       process name can be used on the rare occasions when a SHORT
                       dump listing is insufficient to diagnose the problem and all dump data
                       must be examined at the support center. Note that the tape created
                       here is different from a tape dump the Exec creates directly.
                       Therefore, it cannot be used as input to the batch dump analysis
                       runstream described in Section 6.2.1.




7831 0281–034                                                                              6–15
Performing and Processing System Dumps




6.4.3. Submitting Raw Dump Files with RSS
       Before submitting dumps that accompany contacts and UCFs, enter the following:

         @ADD EXEC*DUMPLIBS.RSS-DUMP

       This statement calls an SSG skeleton which queries you for information pertaining to
       the dump and builds and executes a runstream that facilitates the transfer of the
       dump files.


6.5. Dumping XPC Storage
       Selected nonvolatile storage (NVS) segments of the XPC can be dumped to tape or
       mass storage for analysis of specific XPC internal tables. Refer to the XPCDUMP keyin
       description in Section 11 for keyin format and other information. The dump keyin can
       be initiated anytime after the MODIFY CONFIG message.

       Processing and analysis of XPC dumps cannot be performed with the same utilities
       available for system dumps. XPC dumps are typically used by Unisys engineering
       personnel as troubleshooting aids.




6–16                                                                          7831 0281–034
Section 7
Integrated Recovery and System
Operation

         This section provides operations information on Audit Control and Step Control, which
         are used in Integrated Recovery.


7.1. Audit Control and the Audit Trail
         The audit trail is a log of recoverable database and message processing information
         for an application group. The number of the audit trail is also the number of the
         application group. You control an audit trail with the Audit Trail (AT) keyin.

         There are five audit trail states:

            Open (OP)
             An open audit trail has audit data files and an Audit Control Interface (ACI) file
             assigned. Audit records are received and written to the data files while the trail is
             open.
            Closed (CL)
             A closed audit trail does not have audit data files assigned. When an audit request
             is received, a trail can be automatically opened, or you can be prompted to open a
             trail. The initial state of an audit trail is closed.
            Down (DN)
             An audit trail in the down state does not have main storage, audit data files, or an
             ACI file assigned. Audit requests are not allowed to a trail in a down state.
            Deferred (DF)
             An audit trail is in the deferred state when the audit recovery file (ARF) is not
             assigned to the Exec. This can occur if ARF recovery of the trail resulted in an
             error or has not occurred (recovery has been deferred). The system displays a
             message notifying you of the deferred state. Use the AT n IN, AT n UP, and AT n
             OP to recover.




7831 0281–034                                                                                   7–1
Integrated Recovery and System Operation




            In transition
             Normal auditing is temporarily suspended or is in transition when one of the
             following events occurs:
                You use certain operator keyins (for instance, IN, OP, or LC).
                There is an I/O error (for example, autoswap processing).
                The audit trail is being initialized and recovered during system recovery.

        The initial state of an audit trail is closed (CL). During a mass storage recovery boot,
        each audit trail recovers its state from the previous session and completes any activity
        in process during the previous session.

        The Master Configuration Table (MCT) copy of an audit trail database can be used to
        reinitialize the database for an audit trail. This can occur when:

            You perform a tape boot and there is no database to recover from.
            You perform a tape boot and the database is created, but a comparison of the
             database with MCT data indicates that the configuration parameters in the MCT
             have been modified since the database was created.

        You can defer recovery of one or more audit trails by using the AT n NR keyin. This
        keyin must be entered before the MODIFY CONFIG (select jump key 1 boot) message
        is answered.

        Table 7-1 defines and describes the AT keyin functions. Refer to Section 11 for
        complete information on the AT keyin.


                                        Table 7–1. AT Keyin Functions


            Function          Definition                              Description

            CL               Close            Closes the audit trail for the specified application group
                                              and frees its audit data files. This keyin can be
                                              restricted by system generation.
            DF               Deferred         Puts an audit trail in the deferred (DF) state
            DN               Down             Disables an inactive (closed) audit trail if not restricted
                                              by system generation
            FS               Facility         Displays the status of audit trails
                             status
            IN               Undefer          Initiates recovery of a trail from a deferred (DF) state
            LC               Leg change       Lets you change the mass storage file (leg) assignment
                                              parameters for the specified leg. The change takes
                                              effect when the leg is swapped to the next F-cycle, if
                                              the trail is already open, or if the trail is closed, when it
                                              is opened.
            LD               Leg display      Displays media summary characteristics of each leg of
                                              audit trails



7–2                                                                                         7831 0281–034
                                              Integrated Recovery and System Operation




                                   Table 7–1. AT Keyin Functions


            Function       Definition                              Description

           MV             Move              Allows a spare audit trail to be moved from its current
                                            tape drive to another drive
           NR             No recovery       Permitted only during system recovery before the
                                            MODIFY CONFIG message is answered. The specified
                                            audit trail is placed in a deferred (DF) state. This keyin is
                                            not permitted for the system log audit trail.
           OP             Open              Opens the specified audit trail and makes it available
                                            for auditing
           RM             Remove            Lets you remove a premount unit within system
                          premount          generation constraints
           SP             Spare             Lets you add a premount unit (spare) within system
                          premount          generation constraints
           SW             Swap              Closes the current tape reel or mass storage leg and
                                            then opens the next reel or F-cycle
           UP             Up                Changes the state of an audit trail from down (DN) to
                                            closed (CL), thus permitting auditing to resume


7.1.1.   Audit Trails Configured as Tape
         All audit tapes have 8-bit ASCII labels, and you can assign one or more spares to the
         audit trail. (A spare is a premounted reel, prepared for a future need to change reels.
         When a swap is made to a premount, the system prompts you to mount the next
         premount.)

         If you premount a reel with a specified expiration date and then fail to use it, you must
         initialize the expiration date before you can use the reel again.

         There are special procedures to handle audit tapes, depending on the tape labeling
         system configuration options that follow:

         TLAUTO          set for automatic tape labeling
         TLSIMP          set to control degree of tape labeling
         TLSUB           set to allow reel number substitution during label check
         TPOWN           set to enable account number checking during label check


         Audit Control assigns blank tapes and controls tape labeling with an Executive request.
         Whether you mount a labeled tape or an unlabeled tape depends on how your system
         is configured. For systems with automatic tape labeling set false, you must mount a
         labeled tape. For systems with automatic tape labeling set true, unlabeled (as well as
         labeled) tapes are acceptable. After you mount the tape, each reel of an audit trail has
         the file name appropriate for the audit complex (Step Control, TPM, COD, log).




7831 0281–034                                                                                          7–3
Integrated Recovery and System Operation




        See the Exec System Software Administration Reference Manual (7831 0323) for
        details on the file names. Audit Control tries to self-swap when the self-swap option is
        on and no premount (spare) is available.

        You have a range of options for controlling the audit trail. You can choose to specify
        audit devices by name, by type, by control unit, or with a default type. Audit Control
        first tries to use the type you specified (the preferred assignment). For example, if a
        choice of tapes to premount exists at swap, the premount is chosen on either the
        same control unit (if both swap-from and swap-to are specified by control unit) or on
        the same logical channel.

        When you choose a subsystem, Audit Control maintains this choice over successive
        swaps (if premounts are also available on this subsystem).

        You can use another approach if you want to maximize the use of tape units for other
        (nonaudit) uses. In this case, you control the swapping by anticipating end-of-reel and
        using the swap-specific keyin (such as AT n SW). When you swap to a new device, the
        closing device is freed for other uses, unless the swap is to a spare. In this case, the
        system prompts you to mount a blank labeled tape, and the closing device is used as a
        spare.

        On tape, if the end-of-reel is found or if there is an unrecoverable I/O error, writing
        automatically continues on a new reel (probably on a different tape unit). If no spare is
        available and self-swap is not enabled, a new unit is assigned. This new unit has a
        preferred assignment according to the original assignment, if the terminating reel was
        assigned by control unit name or tape type. Otherwise, the audit trail’s default tape
        type is used.

7.1.2. Audit Trails Configured as Mass Storage
        On disk, if there is an end-of-file error or an unrecoverable I/O error, an autoswap is
        triggered. This allows auditing to continue on the next (+1) F-cycle of the mass storage
        audit leg. The current F-cycle of the file is freed, and the (+1) F-cycle is assigned and
        cataloged.

        To catalog the (+1) F-cycle and continue auditing, you must allow the oldest F-cycle to
        be deleted after the maximum number of allowable F-cycles for a mass storage audit
        file has been reached. This might occur under the following circumstances:

            The audit file (leg) is opened.
            You use the AT n SW Li keyin.
            An I/O error occurs, causing an autoswap.
            An end-of-file occurs, causing an autoswap.

        For the system log audit trail, F-cycle wraparound automatically occurs. The system
        notifies you by displaying the following message:

            AT n Li *F-CYCLE WRAP-AROUND* MAX: xx




7–4                                                                               7831 0281–034
                                                Integrated Recovery and System Operation




         For all other audit trails, the system displays this message:

              a- AT n Li *F-CYCLE WRAP-AROUND* MAX: xx CONTINUE YN?

         where:

         a
               is the message number.

         n
               is the trail-ID.

         Li
               is the mass storage leg-ID (L1 or L2).

         xx
               is the maximum number of relative F-cycles allowed for the file.

         Respond Y to allow F-cycle wraparound. This effectively destroys the oldest relative
         F-cycle data for the specified leg of the trail. Answer N to prevent an F-cycle
         wraparound.

         These situations can arise:

              If the trail is being opened automatically when the wraparound occurs, the trail is
               automatically brought down.
              If the trail is being opened by an operator keyin when the wraparound occurs and
               the trail down (DN) keyin is disallowed, the system prompts you to try again,
               disallowing a down response.
              If the trail is being opened by operator keyin when the wraparound occurs and the
               trail down keyin is allowed, the trail is brought down.
              If you did a swap keyin to cause the wraparound, the swap does not occur; the
               trail remains open, using the current F-cycle.
              If an I/O error caused the wraparound, the trail is automatically placed in a down
               state. Any pending audit I/O, for all legs of the trail, and any queued audit requests
               are terminated. A trail unavailable status is returned to all requestors.
              If there is a wraparound when an end-of-file is being processed, the result is the
               same as for an I/O error.
         The current time is recorded in each block written to an audit file.




7831 0281–034                                                                                    7–5
Integrated Recovery and System Operation




7.2. Step Control Recovery and Initialization
     Options
        This subsection describes the Exec Step Control recovery and initialization options. For
        information on other Integrated Recovery concepts, see the Integrated Recovery
        Conceptual Overview (7830 8186). For more information on the Integrated Recovery
        Utility, see the Integrated Recovery Utility Operations Guide (7830 8194). For Exec
        recovery status and error messages, see the System Console Messages Reference
        Manual (7833 3275).

        Step Control tracks the progress of each message and transaction through an
        Integrated Recovery application group. Step Control uses queues and data structures,
        referred to as queued items, to identify each message and its associated database
        changes. When a failure occurs, Integrated Recovery uses the contents of the queue
        items to maintain the integrity of the database by ensuring that only changes
        requested by successfully completed transactions are applied.

        Note: In the message definitions throughout this subsection, the field n refers to
        the application group number.

7.2.1. Introduction to the Options
        The following are the Exec Step Control recovery and initialization options:

        PANIC         This option uses information from the system dump (also referred to as
                      panic dump) and the audit trail to recover the application group’s queue
                      items. The system dump is created during a disk recovery (or reboot).
                      PANIC recovery ensures that the queue item state changes existing at
                      system failure time match the queue item state changes that were
                      successfully written to the audit trail.
        REBUILD       This option is the same as PANIC, except that it uses information from
                      the periodic savefile and the audit trail rather than the system dump to
                      recover the application group’s queue items.
        SUTIL         This option is the same as REBUILD, except that the IRU (rather than
                      the Exec) reads queue items from the audit trail. SUTIL recovery closes
                      the audit trail if the audit trail was open. To recover the application
                      group’s queue items, SUTIL uses information from the periodic savefile
                      plus information passed in from the IRU short recovery command. The
                      IRU short recovery command uses information from the periodic save
                      audit records and queue item audits to provide information to SUTIL.
        INIT          This option closes the audit trail if the audit trail was open. It initializes
                      the application group’s queue item storage in memory and various files,
                      including the periodic savefile. During INIT, the system does not
                      attempt message and database recovery because both types of
                      recovery use the recovered queue items, which are not available during
                      INIT processing.




7–6                                                                                 7831 0281–034
                                                  Integrated Recovery and System Operation




         DOWN               This option releases queue item storage in memory, closes the audit
                            trail if the audit trail was open, and leaves the application group in a
                            down state. You can perform application group recovery at a later time
                            (SUTIL and INIT options are available later).
         DEFER              This option is the same as DOWN except that it frees the audit
                            recovery file (ARF) and leaves the application group in a deferred state.


Options Available After a System Failure
         After a system failure, the system attempts to recover the application groups and can
         prompt you with console messages to select the initialization or recovery option you
         want. Two different recovery messages can appear. Which one you encounter
         depends on the highest application group number configured on your system and the
         setting of jump key 7.

         Recovery Message 1 is displayed if the highest application group number configured
         on your system is greater than nine and jump key 7 is set. Recovery Message 2 is
         displayed in all other cases. Refer to the appropriate section below.

Recovery Message 1
         When the highest application group number configured on your system is greater than
         nine and jump key 7 is set, a multiline read-and-reply message is displayed to let you
         select the recovery option for each application group. The options for an application
         group and the corresponding console message depend on the type of boot, as
         follows:

            Initial boot

                Specify Recovery Action for the following application groups:
                Non-recoverable APPs specify (INIT/DOWN)
                Non-recoverable APPs are: application group numbers
                Recoverable APPs specify (INIT/DOWN/DEFER)
                Recoverable APPs are: application group numbers
                0-Specify Recovery Action with "AP x RA" keyin, then answer DONE.

            Tape recovery boot

                Specify Recovery Action for the following application groups:
                Non-recoverable APPs specify (INIT/DOWN)
                Non-recoverable APPs are: application group numbers
                Recoverable APPs specify (REBUILD/SUTIL/INIT/DOWN/DEFER)
                Recoverable APPs are: application group numbers
                0-Specify Recovery Action with "AP x RA" keyin, then answer DONE.




7831 0281–034                                                                                     7–7
Integrated Recovery and System Operation




           Disk recovery boot

              Specify Recovery Action for the following application groups:
              Non-recoverable APPs specify (INIT/DOWN)
              Non-recoverable APPs are: application group numbers
              Recoverable APPs specify (PANIC/REBUILD/SUTIL/INIT/DOWN/DEFER)
              Recoverable APPs are: application group numbers
              0-Specify Recovery Action with "AP x RA" keyin, then answer DONE.

        Use the AP x RA keyin to respond to the message (refer to the AP keyin information in
        Section 11 for a complete description of the AP x RA keyin). You can respond using
        one or more of the application group numbers or a range of application group
        numbers. Recovery of the specified application group or groups begins as soon as the
        keyin is entered.

        After responding to the outstanding read-and-reply message and specifying the action
        for each application group, a final message is displayed to which you must respond
        with "done." If you answer with anything other than "done," the message is repeated.

        If you answer "done" and there are application groups for which you have not yet
        specified a recovery action, the multiline read-and-reply message is repeated and
        displays the remaining application groups. You must specify a recovery action for each
        application group.

        Note: The AP x RA keyin is valid only during system initialization (I-boot or
        recovery boot) while the read-and-reply message is outstanding. If the AP x RA
        keyin is entered at any other time, the system responds with an error message.

Recovery Message 2
        After a system failure, the system attempts to recover the application groups
        automatically. If automatic recovery fails, or if jump key 7 is set, the system prompts
        you to enter one of the initialization or recovery options. The options for a recoverable
        application group and the corresponding console message depend on the type of boot
        as follows:

           Initial boot

              APP n INITIALIZE, DOWN, OR DEFER? ENTER: (INIT/DOWN/DEFER)

            Specify INIT, DOWN, or DEFER.

            If jump key 7 is not set, the system assumes INIT. If valid data exists on the
            periodic savefile, the system changes INIT to SUTIL.




7–8                                                                               7831 0281–034
                                                Integrated Recovery and System Operation




             Tape recovery boot

                APP n RECOVERY TYPE? ENTER: (REBUILD/SUTIL/INIT/DOWN/DEFER)

              Specify REBUILD, SUTIL, INIT, DOWN, or DEFER. REBUILD and SUTIL recover the
              application group as it was previously configured. If you configured a new
              application group, enter INIT for the tape recovery boot. If an application group is
              not used immediately after the tape reboot, enter DOWN or DEFER.

              If jump key 7 is not set, the system automatically tries REBUILD; if REBUILD fails,
              the system automatically performs the Exec portion of SUTIL recovery (SUTIL
              setup) and prompts you to start IRU short recovery.

             Disk recovery boot

                APP n RECOVERY TYPE? ENTER: (PANIC/REBUILD/SUTIL/INIT/DOWN/DEFER)

              Specify any of the six options.

              If jump key 7 is not set, the system automatically tries PANIC recovery; if PANIC
              does not succeed, the system automatically tries REBUILD; if REBUILD does not
              succeed, the system automatically performs SUTIL setup.

         For a nonrecoverable application group, the following message appears:

             APP n NON-RECOVERABLE APPLICATION - ANSWER INIT OR DOWN

         Answer INIT or DOWN. A nonrecoverable application group is an application group
         that does not have recoverable messages and database files. Specifically, the
         application group configuration does not specify a periodic savefile, Universal Data
         System (UDS) retention file or files, or rollback page file; only one message retention
         file (MRF) for nonrecoverable messages is configured.

Options Available After an Application Group Failure
         If a single application group fails, bring the application group up with an AP n UP keyin.
         Select a recovery or initialization option by answering the prompt that is produced, or
         include the recovery option on the AP n UP keyin (AP n UP SUTIL or AP n UP INIT, for
         example).

         The following options are available:

             Recoverable application group - SUTIL, INIT, DOWN, or DEFER
             Nonrecoverable application group - INIT or DOWN

         If the Message Control Bank (MCB) or UDS Control component of an application group
         aborts, no console message appears prompting you for a recovery or initialization
         option. Instead, just start IRU short recovery to perform recovery and to bring up MCB
         and UDS Control. The Step Control queues and audit trail remain intact after an MCB or
         UDS Control abort.




7831 0281–034                                                                                   7–9
Integrated Recovery and System Operation




7.2.2. Short Recovery Options
        PANIC, REBUILD, and SUTIL are Exec short recovery options. The following parts of
        recovery occur with these options:

            Queue item and audit trail recovery (method varies according to the recovery
             option)
            Message and database recovery
            Application group restart

        The Exec short recovery procedures work together with the Integrated Recovery
        Utility short recovery procedure to recover queue items, messages, and databases,
        and to bring up an application group. After an Exec short recovery procedure is
        completed, Integrated Recovery Utility short recovery must generally be started to
        complete recovery processing. Integrated Recovery Utility short recovery cannot be
        necessary if an application group is configured without MCB or UDS Control and the
        Step Control application group is configured with the "Bypass IRU Short Recovery"
        SGS.

        Notes:
            If the "Bypass IRU Short Recovery" SGS is not configured, even application
             groups that are not MCB or UDS require Integrated Recovery Utility short
             recovery.
            If the “Bypass IRU Short Recovery” SGS is configured, you cannot have UDS or
             MCB configured and audit-only recovery processing is turned off.

PANIC Option
        Figure 7-1 illustrates PANIC and Integrated Recovery Utility short recovery for a
        recoverable application group that uses TIP, Universal Database Control, and MCB. The
        following steps describe the combined PANIC and Integrated Recovery Utility short
        recovery:

        1.   Exec Audit Control uses the ARF to recover the audit trail. Step Control recovers
             queue items from the system dump and the audit trail, updates the information in
             the Step Control queues, and initiates TIP database recovery. Messages appear at
             the console, indicating progress of the recovery and the status of the audit trail;
             for example:
               APP 3 SAVEFILE RECOVERY IS FROM LEG 1
               AT 3 L1 TAPJ2 REEL PR5312 1202:34 CST
               AT 3 L1 BLK 29 TBSN 27

             When PANIC successfully recovers the Step Control queue items, the following
             message appears:
               APP n PANIC COMPLETE - QUEUE ITEMS RECOVERED FROM PANIC DUMP FILE




7–10                                                                              7831 0281–034
                                               Integrated Recovery and System Operation




         2.   TIP File Control recovers the TIP database files using the rollback page file and the
              Step Control queues. The following messages appear:
                APP n {Host host-id} Rollback recovery started
                APP n {Host host-id} Rollback recovery completed

         3.   If any audit-only recoverable steps exist, Step Control uses the audit trail to build
              the audit-only recovery portion of the Audit Control interface (ACI) file. In doing so,
              Step Control locates the audit records that are required for audit-only recovery and
              copies them to the ACI file.
         4. The following messages appear at the console:
                APP n PANIC AUDIT-ONLY RECOVERY SETUP COMPLETE
                APP n PANIC - START IRU

              Start IRU short recovery at this time.
              Messages appear at the console indicating application group recovery was
              successful.
              Note: If the “Bypass IRU Short Recovery” SGS is configured, the START IRU
              message does not appear, Integrated Recovery Utility short recovery is
              bypassed (steps 5 and 6 are skipped), and the Exec, rather than the Integrated
              Recovery Utility, performs step 7.




7831 0281–034                                                                                   7–11
Integrated Recovery and System Operation




               Figure 7–1. Short Recovery with the PANIC Option




7–12                                                              7831 0281–034
                                               Integrated Recovery and System Operation




         5.   Integrated Recovery Utility short recovery queries Step Control about the
              application group state. For short recovery, IRU calls MCB to recover the MCB
              messages and Universal Database Control to recover the UDS database. This is
              accomplished by using the MRFs, Step Control queues, and UDS retention files.
         6. If any audit-only recoverable steps existed in step 3, Step Control indicates to
            Integrated Recovery Utility that the audit-only recovery portion of IRU short
            recovery is required. Integrated Recovery Utility uses the ACI file to recover the
            database updates for the audit-only recoverable steps.
         7.   Integrated Recovery Utility requests Step Control to restart the application group.
              The following messages appear at the console:
                APP n RESTARTED
                run-id: App n short recovery complete

              where run-id is the run-ID of the IRU run.

REBUILD Option
         Figure 7-2 illustrates REBUILD recovery for a recoverable application group that uses
         TIP, Universal Database Control, and MCB. The following steps describe REBUILD
         recovery:

         1.   Exec Audit Control uses the ARF to recover the audit trail. Step Control recovers
              queue items from the periodic savefile and the audit trail, updates the information
              in the Step Control queues, and initiates TIP database recovery. Messages appear
              at the console indicating progress of the recovery and the status of the audit trail;
              for example:
                APP 3 SAVEFILE RECOVERY IS FROM LEG 1
                APP 3 REBLD PERIODIC SAVE FILE VERIFIED
                AT 3 L1 TAPJ2 REEL 536E 1030:31 CST
                AT 3 L1 BLK 53 TBSN 50
                APP 3 REBLD QUEUE ITEMS REBUILT FROM SAVEFILE AND AUDIT

         2.   TIP File Control recovers the TIP database files using the rollback page file and the
              Step Control queues. The following messages appear at the console:
                APP n {Host host-id} Rollback recovery started
                APP n {Host host-id} Rollback recovery completed

         3.   If any audit-only recoverable steps exist, Step Control uses the audit trail to build
              the audit-only recovery portion of the Audit Control interface (ACI) file. In doing so,
              Step Control locates the audit records that are required for audit-only recovery and
              copies them to the ACI file.




7831 0281–034                                                                                   7–13
Integrated Recovery and System Operation




              Figure 7–2. Short Recovery with the REBUILD Option




7–14                                                               7831 0281–034
                                               Integrated Recovery and System Operation




         4. The following messages appear at the console:
                APP n REBLD AUDIT-ONLY RECOVERY SETUP COMPLETE
                APP n REBLD - START IRU

              Start IRU short recovery at this time.
              Note: If the "Bypass IRU Short Recovery" SGS is configured, the START IRU
              message does not appear, IRU short recovery is bypassed, and the Exec, rather
              than the IRU, performs step 7.
         5.   IRU short recovery queries Step Control about the application group state. For
              short recovery, IRU calls MCB to recover the MCB messages and UDS Control to
              recover the UDS database. This is accomplished by using the MRFs, Step Control
              queues, and UDS retention files.
              Messages appear at the console indicating application group recovery was
              successful.
         6. If any audit-only recoverable steps existed in step 3, Step Control indicates to IRU
            that the audit-only recovery portion of IRU short recovery is required. IRU uses the
            ACI file to recover the database updates for the audit-only recoverable steps.
         7.   IRU requests Step Control to restart the application group. The following message
              appears at the console:
                APP n RESTARTED
                run-id: App n short recovery complete

              where run-id is the run-ID of the IRU run.

SUTIL Option
         Figure 7-3 illustrates SUTIL recovery for a recoverable application group that uses TIP,
         UDS Control, and MCB. The following procedure describes SUTIL combined with short
         IRU recovery:

         1.   The system uses the ARF to close the audit trail if it is not already closed. Step
              Control obtains application group queue item information from the periodic
              savefile and uses the information to set up the Step Control queues in memory
              (that is, to start queue item recovery). Messages appear at the console, indicating
              progress of the recovery and the status of the audit trail; for example:
                APP 3 SAVEFILE RECOVERY IS FROM LEG1
                AT 3 ABNORMALLY CLOSED
                APP 3 SUTIL PERIODIC SAVE FILE VERIFIED

         2.   The following messages appear at the console:
                APP n SUTIL SETUP COMPLETE
                APP n SUTIL START IRU

              These indicate that SUTIL setup is complete and that the site must start IRU short
              recovery.




7831 0281–034                                                                                7–15
Integrated Recovery and System Operation




               Figure 7–3. Short Recovery with the SUTIL Option




7–16                                                              7831 0281–034
                                               Integrated Recovery and System Operation




         3.   IRU queries Step Control about the application group state. IRU starts at the
              beginning of the audit trail reel/file obtained from the query (usually the last
              reel/file) and reads forward until it finds the periodic savefile audit record that
              matches the state of the application group’s queue items from the periodic
              savefile. IRU reads the audit trail from the periodic savefile audit record to the end
              of the audit trail and passes each queue item audit record found in that portion of
              the audit trail to Step Control. Step Control then updates the encountered queue
              item state change in queue item storage. When IRU reaches the end of the audit
              trail, it informs Step Control that there are no more queue items. Step Control
              initiates TIP database recovery and completes queue item recovery.
              The following messages appear at the console:
                APP n {Host host-id} Rollback recovery started
                APP n {Host host-id} Rollback recovery completed



                run-id: Recovery of appl n queue items successful

         4. When queue item and TIP database recovery are complete, IRU performs short
            recovery by calling MCB to recover the MCB messages and UDS Control to
            recover the UDS database. This is accomplished by using the MRFs, Step Control
            queues, and UDS retention files.
              Messages appear at the console indicating application group recovery was
              successful.
         5.   If any audit-only recoverable steps were active when the system or application
              group failed, IRU performs the audit-only recovery portion of short recovery by
              reading the after-looks on the audit trail for commit-in-progress steps and applying
              them to the database.
         6. IRU requests Step Control to restart the application group. The following
            messages appear at the console:
                APP n RESTARTED
                run-id: App n short recovery complete

              where run-id is the run-ID of the IRU run.

7.2.3. INIT Option
         Use the INIT option followed by IRU short recovery when creating an application
         group.

         Use the INIT option followed by IRU medium or long recovery in the following
         situations:

             The periodic savefile does not contain recovery information (for example, it was
              lost).
             Before performing long queue item recovery (See the Integrated Recovery Utility
              Operations Guide (7830 8194) for more information.)




7831 0281–034                                                                                   7–17
Integrated Recovery and System Operation




        INIT does not perform message and database recovery. To recover messages,
        databases, queue items, or a combination, perform some form of recovery, such as
        IRU medium recovery or long database, message or queue item recovery.

        Figure 7-4 illustrates the INIT procedure for a recoverable application group that uses
        TIP, UDS Control, and MCB.

        In this example, the site initializes the application group by performing IRU short
        recovery after INIT. The following steps describe the INIT procedure followed by IRU
        short recovery.




         Figure 7–4. Creating an Application Group with the INIT Option

        1.   The following messages appear when you select INIT following a recovery boot:
               APP n INIT MODE - APPLICATION QUEUE ITEMS TO BE INITIALIZED
               APP n INITIALIZE STEP CONTROL QUEUES? ANSWER NO OR GO

             Answer GO to proceed with initialization.

        2.   The system uses the ARF to close the audit trail if it is not already closed. Exec
             Step Control initializes the queue items and the periodic savefile.
        3.   The following messages appear at the console:
               APP n INITIALIZED FOR SHORT UTILITY
               APP n INIT - START IRU

             Start the IRU short recovery procedure.


7–18                                                                               7831 0281–034
                                                  Integrated Recovery and System Operation




              Note: If the "Bypass IRU Short Recovery" SGS is configured, the START IRU
              message does not appear, IRU short recovery is bypassed, and the Exec, rather
              than the IRU, performs step 6.
         4. IRU queries Step Control about the application group state and calls MCB and UDS
            Control. MCB initializes the message retention files, and UDS Control discards the
            information in the UDS retention files. IRU requests Step Control to restart the
            application group.
              Messages appear on the console indicating application group initialization was
              successful.
         5.   TIP File Control initializes the TIP rollback page file. The following messages
              appear:
                APP n {Host host-id} Rollback initialization started
                APP n {Host host-id} Rollback initialization completed

         6. The following messages appear at the console:
