Section 11
Keyin Reference

         This section describes the unsolicited keyins that are available on OS 2200 systems.
         The following information is included as applicable for the different operator keyins:

             Purpose
             Keyin formats
             System response
             Restrictions and other considerations
             Examples of correct use

         See Section 2 for general information about entering unsolicited keyins.


11.1. $!—Stop the System
         The $! keyin stops the system for diagnostic purposes and is also used to recover
         from a hung system (assuming there is response to console input or user terminals).

11.1.1. Format
             $!

11.1.2. System Response
         The system is rebooted with an XER 0150.


11.2. ABORT—Halt Processing of DC, UD, and
      XPCDUMP Keyins
         The ABORT keyin halts the processing of DC, UD, and XPCDUMP keyins. You can
         enter the keyin at any host, not just the host from which the original keyin was
         entered.

11.2.1. Format
             ABORT keyin

         where keyin is the entire string of the keyin to be halted.




7831 0281–034                                                                                11–1
Keyin Reference




11.2.2. Examples
            ABORT DC DISKA,DISKB

             Halts DC DISKA,DISKB.

            ABORT UD DISK1,DISK2

             Halts UD DISK1,DISK2.

            ABORT XPCDUMP XPC0

             Halts XPCDUMP XPC0.

11.2.3. System Response
        Response messages include "ABORT" followed by the first 12 characters of the
        ABORT keyin (the 12 characters include the keyword ABORT). The ABORT keyin can
        be rejected because the user

            Specified a device name that is not a disk
            Specified a disk pack not involved in a disk copy operation (when trying to halt a
             DC)
            Is trying to halt a DC or UD just as a copy is completing
            Issued the ABORT against a keyin other than DC, UD, or XPCDUMP

        A UD keyin that receives the following message is a special case:

            Disk Copy will destroy files on destination-device Continue Copy Y, <N>

        If you answer Y to this message, you must prep the destination device again before it
        can be used.


11.3. AC—Console Control
        The AC keyin sets and clears the display of message groups on console devices, lists
        the associations between control units, console devices, and message groups, and
        controls the use of the backup console.

11.3.1. Formats
        AC C and AC S
            AC C/ALL/devname1, devname2, ...,devnameN
            AC C/groupname/devname1, devname2, ...,devnameN

            AC S/ALL/devname1, devname2, ...,devnameN
            AC S/groupname/devname1, devname2, ...,devnameN




11–2                                                                              7831 0281–034
                                                                             Keyin Reference




            AC C clears the message groups you specify from being displayed on the console
            devices you specify. AC S sets the message groups you specify to be displayed
            on the console device you specify.

         AC I
           AC I or AC I/ALL
           AC I/groupname

            The AC I keyin displays the associations between console devices and message
            groups.

            AC I (or AC I/ALL) lists all the console devices for all message groups and the
            response console for each message group.

            AC I/groupname lists all the console devices that display the specified message
            group and gives the response console for that message group.

         AC R
           AC R/ALL/devname
           AC R/groupname/devname

            These AC R keyins change the response console for read-and-reply messages (for
            systems with multiple consoles only).

            where:

            ALL
                  indicates all message groups.

            devname
                  is the console name.

            groupname
                  is the message group name.

11.3.2. System Response
         When you enter an AC keyin, a system message verifies completion.

         If you perform an AC C keyin to clear a console for read-and-reply messages and
         receive either of the following messages, perform an AC R keyin directing the
         message group to another device and retry the AC C keyin:

           CLEAR OF group-name RESPONSE CONSOLE NOT ALLOWED

           AC C/ALL/COMPLETED BUT RESPONSE CONSOLE NOT CLEARED




7831 0281–034                                                                                 11–3
Keyin Reference




        The system response to an AC I/groupname keyin is

          group-name RESPONSE CONSOLE IS devname

        If the response console is a remote console, the messages are

          group-name RESPONSE CONSOLE IS CURRENTLY SITEID site-id

          DEFAULT group-name RESPONSE CONSOLE IS devname

11.3.3. Restrictions
        Messages entered through the main storage SIZE message are displayed only on the
        bootstrap console. Messages following the MASS STORAGE message are routed to
        the console devices as specified during system generation and modified by AC keyins.

        If you use the AC keyin to route system messages to a remote demand console in
        addition to a PC console, note the following: If you enter any of the jump key keyins
        (DJ, CJ, and SJ) at the demand terminal, the keyin response is displayed only on the
        demand terminal, not on the PC console.

        The AC keyin cannot be entered from a remote console (@@CONS), nor do AC R
        keyins affect remote-response consoles. Read-and-reply messages issued at a
        remote-response console are not rerouted. Any message group specified on an AC R
        keyin remains assigned to its @@CONS remote-response console.

        Changes to the console message group that result from an AC keyin are saved over a
        disk or autorecovery bootstrap.

11.3.4. Examples
          AC S/SYSMSG/CNSOL3,CNSOL4

            Allows message group SYSMSG to be displayed on devices CNSOL3 and CNSOL4,
            as well as the system console.

          AC I/IOMSG/CNSOL1

            Causes the system to list the devices on console CNSOL1 that display message
            group IOMSG.

          AC R/ALL/CNSOL2

            Makes the CRT on console CNSOL2 the response device for all read-and-reply
            messages.




11–4                                                                             7831 0281–034
                                                                              Keyin Reference




11.4. AP—Application Group Status and Control
         The AP keyin displays the status of application groups and brings up or shuts down an
         application group.

11.4.1. Formats
         AP FS
           AP ALL FS

            Display the status of all application groups.

           AP n FS

            Display the status of application group n.

         AP DN
           AP n DN

            Bring down application group n.

           AP n DN RECOVER, option

            Bring down application group n, and initialize or recover the application group.
            option is REBUILD, SUTIL, INIT, or DOWN.

         AP DF
           AP n DF

            Defer the application group. The application must be in the DN state. All system
            files assigned by Step Control for the application group are freed.

         AP UP
           AP n UP

            Start up application group n.

           AP n UP option

            Bring up application group n, and initialize or recover the application group. The
            option can be REBUILD, SUTIL, or INIT.

            Note: The INIT option of this keyin, followed by IRU short recovery, discards all
            queue items, database updates, and XPC-L locks.




7831 0281–034                                                                                  11–5
Keyin Reference




        AP RA
          AP x RA option

           Recover application groups across the boot. It is valid only when the highest
           application group number configured in the system is 10 or higher and jump key 7
           is set, and while the read-and-reply application group recovery message is
           outstanding (see Section 7).

           where:

           x
                is any of the following:

                   A single number
                   A series of numbers separated by commas
                   A range of numbers connected with a hyphen
                   A combination of any of the preceding
                   ALL (for all application groups)
                   An asterisk (*) to represent the remaining application groups numbers

           option
                can be any valid recovery option: PANIC, REBUILD, SUTIL, INIT, DOWN, or
                DEFER.

           Example
               AP 1,3-5,7,9-11 RA PANIC

        AP RS
          AP n RS

           Resets recovery coordination flag on all hosts (for this application) to Database
           Recovery Complete With Unlock (DCRWU). Application on executing host must be
           in a DN state. On all other hosts, the application (and audit trail) must be DEFER'ed
           or the host itself must be down. Applies to concurrent application groups only.




11–6                                                                             7831 0281–034
                                                                              Keyin Reference




11.4.2. System Response
         The response to the AP ALL FS keyin displays status information starting with the
         smallest numbered application group and ending with the highest configured
         application group. Information is displayed in the following format:

           APP 3 state qitem-usage PRG:steps REC:type MCB:MCB-status
           APP 3 TYPE: ag-type DR: DR-flag READY STEPS: num-ready
           APP 7 state qitem-usage PRG:steps REC:type MCB:MCB-status
           APP 7 TYPE: ag-type DR: DR-flag READY STEPS: num-ready

         where:

         state
            is the state of the application group: UP, DN, recovery-in-progress (RCV), DF, or
            not configured. (If no application groups are configured, no other information is
            displayed.)

         qitem-usage
            is the percentage of queue items in use.

         PRG:steps
            is the total number of queue items on the active, ready, and commit-in-progress
            lists if the application group is up, where steps is a 1- to 6-digit decimal number
            that corresponds to the number of application group program activities executing
            within a step. If the application state is still RCV, PRG:steps is replaced with the
            IRU short recovery status—Either IRU:PND for IRU short recovery pending or
            IRU:INP for IRU short recovery in progress.
         REC:type
            is the type of application group recovery executed, where type can be one of the
            following:

            INIT           Initialized
            SUTIL          Recovered by short utility processing
            REBLD          Recovered by rebuilding the queue items
            PANIC          Recovered from the system (panic) dump
            (blank)        Down or has recovery-in-progress

         MCB:MCB-status
            is the status of MCB: up, down or abort-in-progress (AIP). The field is omitted if
            the application group is not using MCB.




7831 0281–034                                                                                11–7
Keyin Reference




        ag-type
           is the application group type configured at system generation. This field can be set
           to one of the following:

           LOCAL
           CONCURRENT

        DR: DR-flag
           is the status of medium database recovery. This consists of one of the following
           values:

           COMPLETELK               Medium database recovery is complete, but the locks for this AG
                                    or AG/host still exist at this point.
           COMPLETENOLK             Medium database recovery is complete and the locks have been
                                    unlocked for this AG or AG/host.
           IN-PROGRESS              Medium database recovery is in progress.
           NONE                     If the Ag or AG/host is up (UP), it signifies that short recovery is
                                    complete. If the Ag or AG/host is down (DN) or recovery is in
                                    progress, it signifies that no short or medium recoveries have
                                    occurred since the last AG/host failure.
           READYSREMAIN             Medium database recovery is complete, but some ready steps
                                    remain in the Ag or AG/host, awaiting completion of the
                                    distributed transactions.
           REMOTERDYINP             Medium database recovery is complete, but some ready steps
                                    remained. Interhost resolution of at least one of the ready steps
                                    was started for this AG/host. (This status applies only to
                                    concurrent application groups.)
           REMOTERDYCMP             Medium database recovery is complete. Interhost resolution of
                                    the ready steps was also completed for the AG/host. (This status
                                    applies only to concurrent application groups.)
           UNKNOWN                  The status of the database is unknown. Operator intervention is
                                    required to continue with recovery.

        READY STEPS:num-ready
           is the number of ready steps present in the AG or AG/host.

        The system responds to an AP n UP keyin with the following message:

          APP n RECOVERY TYPE - ENTER: (REBUILD/SUTIL/INIT/DOWN/DEFER)

        See Section 7 for information on the recovery options.

        The system responds to an AP x RA keyin with a message indicating whether the
        keyin was successful or unsuccessful.




11–8                                                                                   7831 0281–034
                                                                                    Keyin Reference




         If you try to select an invalid option, enter unconfigured application group numbers, or
         make other syntax errors when entering an AP x RA keyin, system messages appear
         to inform you of the error. Refer to the System Console Messages Reference Manual
         (7833 3275) for explanations.

         The system responds to an AP x RS keyin with the following type and read message:

              AP RS keyin will disable coordination of application recovery. All
              updates in progress will be lost. Once a response of RESET is entered,
              application n must be INITIALIZED on ALL hosts, even if the keyin does
              not complete successfully. Failure to do so could result in database
              corruption on subsequent recoveries. Answer RESET to continue.

         The user must enter RESET in order for the keyin to proceed. If the user transmits or
         enters any other data, the AP RS keyin is ignored. If you try to select an invalid option,
         enter an unconfigured application group number, or make other syntax errors when
         entering an AP x RS keyin, system messages appear to inform you of the error. Refer
         to the System Console Messages Reference Manual (7833 3275) for explanations.

11.4.3. Examples
         Example 1
         The system displays the following read-and-reply message during recovery:
               Specify Recovery Action for the following application groups:
               Non-recoverable APPs specify (INIT/DOWN)
               Non-recoverable APPs are: 1-3,6,8
               Recoverable APPs specify (PANIC/REBUILD/SUTIL/INIT/DOWN/DEFER)
               Recoverable APPs are: 4-5,12,15
               0-Specify Recovery Action with "AP x RA" keyin, then answer DONE.

         Operator response is as follows:
         1.    The operator first wants to perform an SUTIL recovery of application groups 4, 12,
               and 15, so the following keyin is entered:
                 AP 4,12,15 RA SUTIL

               This results in application group 4 beginning SUTIL recovery followed by
               applications 12 and 15.
         2.    The operator then wants to initialize application groups 1, 2, 3, and 8, so the
               following keyin is entered:
                 AP 1-3,8 RA INIT

               This results in application groups 1, 2, 3, and 8 beginning initialization.
         3.    Next, the operator wants to bring down the remaining application groups 5 and 6,
               so the following keyin is entered:
                 AP * RA DOWN




7831 0281–034                                                                                    11–9
Keyin Reference




              This results in application group 5 and 6 being marked DOWN.
        4. Finally, the operator must tell the system that the recovery is complete, so the
           following keyin is entered:
                0 DOWN

              This terminates the outstanding operator message.

        Example 2
        The system displays the following read-and-reply message during recovery:
              Specify Recovery Action for the following application groups:
              Non-recoverable APPs specify (INIT/DOWN)
              Non-recoverable APPs are: 1-3,6,8
              Recoverable APPs specify (PANIC/REBUILD/SUTIL/INIT/DOWN/DEFER)
              Recoverable APPs are: 4-5,12,15
             0-Specify Recovery Action with "AP x RA" keyin, then answer DONE.

        Operator response is as follows:
        1.    The operator wants to initialize all application groups, so the following keyin is
              entered:
                AP ALL RA INIT

              This results in the following messages:
                 APP 1 INIT - START IRU
                 APP 2 INIT - START IRU
                 APP 3 INIT - START IRU
                 APP 6 INIT - START IRU
                 APP 8 INIT - START IRU
                1-APP 4 INITIALIZE STEP CONTROL QUEUES- ANSWER NO OR GO
                2-APP 5 INITIALIZE STEP CONTROL QUEUES- ANSWER NO OR GO
                3-APP 12 INITIALIZE STEP CONTROL QUEUES- ANSWER NO OR GO
                4-APP 15 INITIALIZE STEP CONTROL QUEUES- ANSWER NO OR GO

              All nonrecoverable application groups are initialized. The operator has to confirm
              initialization of each of the recoverable application groups, because recoverable
              information exists from the previous session. The operator responds GO to
              proceed with initialization, NO to stop initialization.
        2.    After recovery options are selected for all application groups, the following keyin
              is entered:
                0 DOWN

              This terminates the outstanding operator message.




11–10                                                                                7831 0281–034
                                                                            Keyin Reference




11.5. ARCMR—Monitor Run Control
         The ARCMR keyin displays information about the monitor run and terminates the
         monitor run with or without a dump.

11.5.1. Formats
         ARCMR TERM
           ARCMR TERM

            Terminate the monitor run without producing any dumps. The termination process
            involves deregistering components that were registered with monitor run.

            If any components are registered, the following message is displayed:

                ARCMR: n COMPONENTS REGISTERED WITH MONITOR RUN...CONTINUE TERM Y/N?

            If you answer Y, the components are deregistered, and both the monitor run and
            the backup monitor run completes (FIN) normally. If you answer N, the run is not
            terminated.

         ARCMR ABORT
           ARCMR ABORT

            Terminate the monitor run with an ER EABT$. A postmortem dump and a dump of
            the common data bank are produced for both the primary and backup monitor
            runs. Aborting the monitor run does not deregister components. If a system
            recovery occurs, these components are still registered and is restarted. To avoid
            unwanted components from restarting, initialize monitor run. Only those
            components reregister with monitor run will restart.

         ARCMR STATUS
           ARCMR STATUS

            Display information about the monitor run in the following format:

                ARCMR: PRIMARY RUNID: prunid BACKUP RUNID: brunid
                ARCMR: RECOVERY MODE: mode yymmdd/hhmm:ss
                {ARCMR: NUMBER OF COMPONENTS REGISTERED: n} or
                {ARCMR: NO COMPONENTS REGISTERED}
                ARCMR: RUNIDS OF REGISTERED COMPONENTS: runid1 runid2 ...
                ARCMR: runid3 runid4 ...
                .
                .

            where:

            prunid
                 is the run-ID for the primary monitor run.



7831 0281–034                                                                            11–11
Keyin Reference




           brunid
                 is the run-ID for the backup monitor run. If the backup run’s run-ID is not
                 available, the brunid field displays the following:
                    NOT AVAILABLE

           mode
                 is the recovery mode: either SHARED or LOCAL.

           yymmdd/hhmm:ss
                 is the time of the keyin.

           n
                 is the total number of components registered with the monitor run. If n is
                 zero, the message
                    ARCMR: NO COMPONENTS REGISTERED

                 is displayed.

           runid1 runid2 ...
                 The run-IDs of the components registered with the monitor run. If no
                 components are registered, NONE is displayed.

        ARCMR RUNSTATUS
          ARCMR RUNSTATUS runid

           Displays status information for a monitor run component in the following format:

                ARCMR: RUNID: runid yymmdd/hhmm:ss
                ARCMR: HEARTBEAT ID: id HEARTBEAT INTERVAL: n MS
                ARCMR: TERMINATE BEFORE RESTART: yes/no
                ARCMR: RECOVERY RUNSTREAMS:
                ARCMR: COMPONENT: cfile
                ARCMR: LOCAL: lfile
                ARCMR: SHARED: shfile

           where:
           runid
                 is the run-ID of the component on the ARCMR keyin.

           yymmdd/hhmm:ss
                 is the time of the keyin

           id
                 is the heartbeat-ID of the component (in decimal).




11–12                                                                              7831 0281–034
                                                                              Keyin Reference




            n
                is the heartbeat interval (5,000 to 30,000 milliseconds).

            yes/no
                indicates whether the run should be terminated before starting a recovery
                runstream for the component. If set to yes, terminate the current run and start
                the recovery runstream. If set to no, start the recovery runstream without
                terminating the run.

            cfile
                is the file or file.element that contains the component recovery runstream. The
                component recovery runstream is started (@START) to recover a component
                when the component fails, but the system is still active. If a runstream is not
                registered, NONE appears in the field.

            lfile
                is the file or file.element that contains the local recovery runstream. The local
                recovery runstream is used to recover components after a host failure and
                reboot. If a runstream is not registered, NONE appears in the field.

            shfile
                is the file or file.element that contains the shared recovery runstream. The
                shared recovery runstream is used to recover a component on the backup
                host when the production host fails. If a runstream is not registered, NONE
                appears in the field.

11.5.2. Restrictions
         The ARCMR keyins are available only when monitor run is active on the system.




7831 0281–034                                                                                11–13
Keyin Reference




11.6. AT—Controlling the Audit Trail
        The AT keyins are used to perform various manual functions to audit trails of all types.
        There are currently four supported types of audits trails: Step Control, System Log
        (LOG), Transaction Processing Monitor (TPM) and Capacity On Demand (COD).
        Although each type of trail collects a unique set of data, all audit trails are constructed,
        configured, and manipulated exactly the same.

        The parameters for each function on the AT keyin vary according to trail configuration.
        Li refers to the audit trail leg, leg 1 or 2. The variable n refers to the audit trail name.
        For Step Control type trails, n is the application number associated with this trail. For
        the system log trail, the default name of the trail is LOG. For TPM type trails, the
        default trail name is TPM. The system COD default trail name is COD.

11.6.1. Formats
        AT CL
          AT n CL

            Closes the audit trail for the application group specified by n and frees its audit
            data files. This keyin can be restricted by system generation.

        AT DF
          AT n DF

            Puts the audit trail of the application group specified by n in a deferred state. The
            trail must be in the down (DN) state before using this keyin.

        AT DN
          AT n DN

            Places the inactive (closed) audit trail specified by n in a down state, if not
            restricted by system generation.

        AT FS
          AT n FS

            Displays the status of the audit trail specified by n. When n = ALL, it displays the
            status of all configured audit trails. When n = LOG, it displays the status of the
            audit trail for the system log. When n = COD, it displays the status of the audit trail
            for COD. When n = TPM, it displays the status of the audit trail for TPM.

        AT IN
          AT n IN

            Starts recovery of the audit trail specified by n. The audit trail must be in the
            deferred (DF) state before this keyin is used.




11–14                                                                               7831 0281–034
                                                                               Keyin Reference




         AT LC
           AT n LC Li

            Changes the audit trail leg specified by i. If the trail is open, the change takes
            effect when the leg is swapped to the next F-cycle. If the trail is closed, the
            change takes effect when the trail is opened. See Section 11.6.2 for details on
            what can be changed and how it is done.

            Note: You cannot use the AT n LC Li keyin to switch the audit trail from a fixed
            disk to a removable disk or conversely. A system generation is required to
            switch an audit trail from a fixed to a removable disk or from a removable to a
            fixed disk.

         AT LD
           AT n LD

            Displays the media summary for audit trail n. When n = ALL, it displays the media
            summary for all audit trail legs.

         AT MV
           AT n MV unit1[,unit2]

            Allows an audit trail to be moved from its current tape drive to another, spare,
            drive.

            Parameter unit-1 specifies a tape drive mnemonic for a "spare" currently assigned
            to Exec audit trail n. Parameter unit-2 specifies a tape unit to which to move unit1
            and can be any one of the following:
                Assign mnemonic (HIC or T, for example)
                Tape control unit mnemonic (T34, for example)
                unit1 mnemonic, for instance (T34A0, for example)
                Void, which allows Audit Control to select the most compatible assign device
                 to unit1

         AT NR
           AT { n | ALL } NR

            Bypasses the recovery of the specific audit trail n, or ALL configured audit trails
            except for the system LOG audit trail. This action leaves each target audit trail in a
            deferred (DF) state.

            The NR keyin is only permitted during system recovery prior to the start of audit
            trail recoveries. You can assure this by setting jump key 1 and entering the NR
            keyin before you respond to the MODIFY CONFIG read/reply message.

            The NR keyin is not permitted for the system LOG audit trail.




7831 0281–034                                                                                    11–15
Keyin Reference




        AT OP
          AT n OP [name-1/name-2,spare-unit-1/spare-unit]

           Opens the audit trail specified by n so that it is available for auditing.

           Parameters name-1 and name-2 specify which legs of the trails to open, and
           whether the trail is open for simplex or duplex auditing. They also specify a tape
           unit for tape audit trails.

           Parameters spare-unit-1 and spare-unit-2 specify spare or premount tape file
           assignments.

        AT RM
          AT n RM unit

           Allows you to remove the premount unit specified by unit from the audit trail
           specified by n.

        AT SP
          AT n SP unit

           Adds a spare premount unit to the audit trail specified by n.

        AT SW
          AT n SW unit1[[,unit2] [reel-id]]

           For manual control of tape swapping, this keyin closes the current tape reel (reel-
           id) for the audit trail specified by n, then opens the next reel.

           where:

           unit1
                is the name of a tape unit device currently assigned and ready to receive audit
                data.

           unit2
                is a tape assign mnemonic, such as
                    Tape device name (for example, T40A0)
                    Tape assign type (for example, HICL)
                    Tape control unit name (for example, CU42A)

           reel-id
                is the identifier of the unit2 tape that is assigned to complete the tape swap.
                The reel-ID is written in the close record of the tape assigned to the unit1
                device before the unit is freed. This provides a forward link to the swap-to
                device.



11–16                                                                                   7831 0281–034
                                                                                      Keyin Reference




            Notes:
                    Minimum spares are ensured by using unit1 as a spare when appropriate.
                    Spare unit selection, if called for, is based on the same logical channel
                     assign, the same control unit assign, the same assign type, or the default
                     assign.
                    If assigned, the unselected spare is placed so that it is selected as the next
                     choice of a swap-to spare (to rotate spares).

           AT n SW Li

            Closes the current mass storage leg, Li, for the audit trail specified by n; then
            opens the next generation (file cycle or F-cycle).

            The Li (leg) parameter can be L1 or L2, and is order-independent.

         AT TI
           AT { n | ALL } TI

            Reinitializes the specified audit trail, or all audit trails, to their configured values.
            Each target audit trail is placed in a closed or down state, depending on the
            RECOVERY system generation option.

            You can use the TI keyin to recover from a software problem, or to ensure that
            parameters that were changed during system generation are applied or reapplied.
            The TI keyin copies the configuration source of the Audit Control structure to the
            audit recovery file (ARF). The target trail must be in a not-recovered state (during
            system boot), or in a deferred (DF) state (after trail recovery completes).

            The results of the TI keyin differ, depending on whether the keyin is issued at boot
            time or after system recovery:

                   If you issue the keyin at system boot time, the keyin must take effect prior to
                    the start of audit trail recoveries. To assure this, set jump key 1 and enter the
                    keyin before responding to the MODIFY CONFIG read/reply message.
                    Even if TI action is set for a trail, the trail is not initialized until the trail is
                    actually recovered. Recovery of trails configured with the RECOVERY,DEFER
                    attribute bypass recovery at boot time; they are not initialized until they are
                    acted upon by an operator AT n IN keyin. Likewise, trails targeted by an NR
                    keyin bypass recovery, and initialization of those trails does not occur until
                    after IN processing.
                    TI action bypasses recovery of open audit trails. Close records are not written,
                    and duplex legs are not resynchronized. PANIC and REBUILD fail if a trail is
                    related to Step Control. The state of a trail upon completion of TI action is
                    closed or down.
                   If the TI keyin is issued after audit trail recoveries begin, a trail must be in a
                    deferred state for TI action to be applied. TI action is skipped for trails that are
                    not in DF state. The keyword ALL is not allowed at this time.
                    Audit close records are written normally and duplex files are synchronized.


7831 0281–034                                                                                         11–17
Keyin Reference




              The system LOG and COD trails are never in a deferred state, so TI keyins are only
              allowed for these trails at system boot time (AT LOG TI, AT COD TI, or AT ALL TI
              keyins).

              To initialize an open audit trail after audit trail recoveries are complete, the
              operator keyin sequence is as follows:

                AT n CL
                AT n DN
                AT n DF
                AT n TI

        AT UP
            AT n UP

              Changes the state of the audit trail, specified by n, from down (DN) to closed (CL).

11.6.2. System Response
        The system response to the AT n LC Li keyin is a message that lets you change
        parameters:

            a - AT n Li FIX/REM[,cache] parameters

        To change one parameter, you must reenter all of them. If you do not reenter a
        parameter, the system uses a default value. The parameters are

            [,cache] type/res/gran/max/rfp[/place][,pack-id/...]

        where:

        a
              is the message number.

        cache
              Currently, cache’ing is not supported. Therefore, only a cache value of DNC is
              allowed.

        type
              is the media type of mass storage, either FIX or REM. This cannot be changed and
              must be resupplied as is.

        res
              is the initial memory reserve. The value of res must be greater than 0 and less
              than 262143. It must also be equal to the value for max.




11–18                                                                                  7831 0281–034
                                                                                      Keyin Reference




         gran
               is the physical granularity used for input/output, which can be TRK (track) or POS
               (position).

         max
               is the maximum memory reserve. The value of max must be greater than 0 and
               less than 262143. It must also be equal to the value for res.

         rfp
               is the removable mass storage audit F-cycle pack-ID placement mnemonic, which
               can have the following values:

               RFPON          F-cycle to pack-ID placement is enabled for this removable file pack set.
               RFPOFF         F-cycle to pack-ID placement is disabled for this removable file pack set.

         place
               is the placement on a controller or on a device.

         pack-id
               is the name of the disk pack when the file is maintained on removable mass
               storage.

         Notes:
              The AT n LC keyin provides flexibility in manipulating files, but use it cautiously.
               Only someone who understands the effects of modifying the audit trail
               parameters should use this keyin. For example, the pack-ID can be changed only
               if a file has no previous F-cycles. When you are changing pack-IDs, the new
               pack-ID must have the same directory type (standard or shared) as that of the
               old pack-ID. Also, a small value for max can cause frequent swaps to occur.
              The parameters for the audit trail changed with the AT n LC keyin are saved in
               the audit recovery file (ARF) for the target audit trail. They stay in effect until a
               system tape boot occurs, which results in the ARF being rewritten due to a
               change in the static configuration of the audit trail. When this occurs, the
               parameters established by system generation replace those previously set by
               the keyin, except for the cache parameters as described above.

11.6.3. Restrictions
         When changing the parameters of the system log audit trail, always set the initial
         reserve and maximum granules to the same value.

         The AT n NR and AT ALL NR keyins are permitted only during system recovery before
         all audit trail recoveries begin (for example, before the MODIFY CONFIG message is
         answered). The NR keyins are not permitted for the system LOG audit trail.




7831 0281–034                                                                                        11–19
Keyin Reference




11.6.4. Examples
        Example 1
          AT 3 LC L1

           The system responds with the following statement about audit trail 3, leg 1, where
           0 is the message number:
                0-AT 3 L1 FIX F/128/TRK/128

           To change the maximum number of tracks (max parameter) for audit trail 3, leg 1
           from 128 to 256, respond with the message number and the required changes:
           (You must also change the initial memory reserve, res, to 256):
                0 FIX F/256/TRK/256

           The system then displays the final results to verify your change:
                AT 3 L1 FIX F/256/TRK/256

        Example 2
          AT 2 LC L2

           The system responds with the following statement about audit trail 2, leg 2:
                0-AT 2 L2 REM F50/128/TRK/128,10P

           To change the pack-id parameter from 10P to 50P for audit trail 2, leg 2, respond
           with the message number and the required changes:
                0-REM F50/128/TRK/128,50P

           The system displays the final results:
                AT 2 L2 REM F50/128/TRK/128,50P

        Example 3
          AT 3 LC L2

           The system responds with the following statement about audit trail 3, leg 2:
                0-AT 3 L2 FIX F/200/TRK/200

           Changes to system defaults for audit trail 3, leg 2, respond with the message
           number and the required changes:
                0 FIX

           or
                0 FIX///

           The system displays the final results:
                AT 3 L2 FIX



11–20                                                                          7831 0281–034
                                                                             Keyin Reference




         Example 4
           AT 7 LC L1

            The system responds with the following statement about audit trail 7, leg 1:
                0-AT 7 L1 REM F50/256/TRK/256,20P

            To change to system defaults for audit trail 7, leg 1. Respond with the message
            number and the required changes:
                0 REM F50/256//256,20P

            The system displays the final results:
                AT 7 L1 REM F50///,20P

         Example 5
           AT 2 LC L2

            There is an XPC on this system, so the system responds with the following
            statement about audit trail 2, leg 2:
                0-AT 2 L2,DOC REM F50/256/TRK/256,20P

            To change to system defaults for audit trail 2, leg 2, respond with the message
            number and the required changes:
                0 REM,DEF F50/256//256,20P

            The system displays the final results:
                AT 2 L2 REM,DEF F50/256//256,20P

         Example 6
           AT LOG MV T34A0

            Moves the log audit trail from T34A0 to another drive.
            The Exec selects a new unit that is most compatible with unit T34A0. The Exec
            releases device T34A0. If the selected unit cannot be acquired, the original unit
            remains in use, allowing you to issue the AT n MV keyin again.

         Example 7
           AT 1 MV T34A1,T34A2

            Moves audit trail 1 from T34A1 to T34A2.
            The Exec switches to tape device T34A2 and releases device T34A1. If the new
            unit cannot be acquired, the original unit remains in use, allowing you to issue the
            AT n MV keyin again.




7831 0281–034                                                                               11–21
Keyin Reference




        Example 8
          AT 1 MV T34A2,T34A

           Moves audit trail 1 from T34A2 to another drive.
           The Exec switches to a tape drive (that is configured to control unit T34A) as the
           new spare tape unit and releases tape unit T34A2. If the new unit cannot be
           acquired, the original unit remains in use, allowing you to issue the AT n MV keyin
           again.

        Example 9
          AT 2 MV T34A1,HIC

           Moves audit trail 2 from T34A1 to another drive.
           The Exec selects a tape drive that can satisfy the assign mnemonic HIC, and
           releases the premounted tape unit T34A1. If the new unit cannot be acquired, the
           original unit remains in use, allowing you to issue the AT n MV keyin again.

        Example 10
          AT 3 MV T34A0,T34A0

           Moves file assigned to T34A0. The Exec issues a tape swap for the file assigned
           to T34A0. A labeled blank reel must be mounted on device T34A0. If the new unit
           cannot be acquired, a new labeled blank tape might or might not be solicited.
           Another tape will always be solicited to replace the source unit to preserve the
           premount minimum value established for audit trail n by system generation. If the
           premount minimum value is still preserved in the event that the destination tape
           assignment fails, no further replacement is solicited. The actual number of spares
           for audit trail n is reduced by 1.

        Example 11
          AT 3 OP

           Audit trail 1 is opened and tape or mass storage file assignments are made as
           determined by system generation default specifications.

        Example 12
          AT 3 OP L1

           Opens mass storage audit trail 3, leg 1.

        Example 13
          AT 1 OP L2

           Opens mass storage audit trail 1, leg 2.




11–22                                                                           7831 0281–034
                                                                             Keyin Reference




         Example 14
           AT 1 OP L1/L2

            Opens mass storage audit trail 1, legs 1 and 2.

         Example 15
           AT 1 OP T20A1

            Opens audit trail 1 as simplex on device T20A1 with no spare.

         Example 16
           AT 1 OP T20A,T20A

            Opens audit trail 1 as simplex on control unit T20A with a spare on the same
            control unit.

         Example 17
           AT 1 OP T20A/T36A

            Opens audit trail 1 as duplex with one unit on T20A/T36A control unit T20A, one
            unit on control unit T36A, and two spares, one on control unit T20A and one on
            control unit T36A.

         Example 18
           AT 1 OP T20A/T36A, T20A

            Opens audit trail 1 as duplex with one unit on control unit T20A, one unit on control
            unit T36A, and one spare on control unit T20A. The spare is used by the first active
            unit to swap.

         Example 19
           AT n SW unit1

            Swap from unit1 without specifying a reel-ID.
            If a spare is available, the system selects the swap-to unit. If the audit trail is
            configured for self-swap and there is no spare assigned, the operator is solicited
            for a reel-ID. If the trail is not configured for self-swap and there is no spare
            assigned, the system assigns the swap-to device before releasing the unit1 tape.

         Example 20
           AT n SW unit1 reel-id

            Swap from unit1 to the same unit (self-swap) with the reel-ID specified. Self-swap
            must be configured, otherwise reel-ID is ignored and a swap to another unit is
            issued.




7831 0281–034                                                                              11–23
Keyin Reference




        Example 21
          AT n SW unit1,unit1

           Swap from unit1 to the same unit. The operator is solicited for the reel-ID.

        Example 22
          AT n SW unit1,unit2

           Swap from unit1 to unit2.
           If a spare is assigned, unit2 must be a tape assign mnemonic (tape device name,
           tape assign type, or tape control unit name). If a spare is not assigned, the system
           assigns an appropriate unit before releasing unit1.

        Example 23
          AT n SW unit1,unit1 reel-id

           Self-swap on unit1 with the specified reel-id.

        Example 24
          AT logtrailname FS

           Displays the status of the audit trail for the system log file, as follows:
                AT LOG OP
                AT LOG L1 FIX(4,28) 23% 00:00:43 CDT
                AT LOG L1 BLK 24 TBSN 39

           where:

           OP                  indicates that the trail is open.
           L1                  is the leg-ID.
           FIX                 indicates fixed mass storage.
           4,28                4 is the number of currently cataloged file F-cycles for leg 1 for the
                               LOG trail. The currently active file F-cycle assigned is absolute file
                               F-cycle 28.
           23%                 indicates the percentage of the file that is full.
           00:00:43 CDT        is the time that the file was assigned.
           BLK 24              indicates the file block number.
           TBSN 39             indicates the trail block sequence number.

        Example 25
          AT LOG LD

           Displays the mass storage file assignment parameters for the system log file audit
           trail, as follows:
                AT LOG L1 FIX F/5000/TRK/5000




11–24                                                                                   7831 0281–034
                                                                                     Keyin Reference




            where:

            L1                 is the leg-ID.
            FIX                indicates fixed mass storage.
            F                  indicates the addressing mode of the file (in this case, sector
                               formatted).
            5000               is the number of granules (tracks) initially required by the field.
            TRK                is the granule size (TRK = 1 software track).
            5000               is the maximum number of granules (tracks) of the file.

         Example 26
           AT LOG SW L1

            Closes the current generation of the system log file and opens a new one.
            The system displays the following three lines:
                 AT LOG L1 FIX(4,26) *CLOSED* 10:50:30 CST
                 AT LOG L1 BLK 1791 TBSN 3496
                 AT LOG L1 FIX(4,26) *SWAP* FIX(5,27) 19:50:21

            where:

            L1                 is the leg-ID.
            FIX                indicates fixed mass storage.
            4,26               indicates the number of currently cataloged generations for leg L1 and
                               the absolute generation for the file.
            *CLOSED*           indicates that the trail is closed.
            BLK 1791           indicates the file block number.
            TBSN               indicates the trail block sequence number.
            10:50:30 CST       is the time that the file was assigned.
            4,26               is the file cycle that was closed.
            5,27               is the file cycle that you opened.

         Example 27
           AT logtrailname SW L1

            Closes the current F-cycle of the system log file and opens a new one.

         Example 28
           AT 1 FS

            This keyin displays the following:
                 AT 1 L1 REM(2,1) PL:ATL1P1 16:13:01
                 AT 1 L1 BLK 17 TBSN 14




7831 0281–034                                                                                        11–25
Keyin Reference




             AT 1 S1 REM(2,2) PL:ATL1P2 16:13:03
             AT 1 L2 FIX(2,1) PL:*CU20H2 16:13:01
             AT 1 L2 BLK 17 TBSN 14
             AT 1 S2 FIX(2,2) *SPARE* 16:13:03

           which shows that L1 is configured as removable and L2 is configured as fixed.
           Placement (PL:) is defined for fixed and removable files. F-cycle 1 is contained on
           pack-ID P1 and F-cycle 2 is contained on P2.

          AT 1 SW L1

           This keyin swaps L1 removable F-cycle 1 to spare F-cycle 2 and displays the
           following:
             AT 1 L1 REM(2,1) *CLOSED* 16:1302
             AT 1 L1 BLK 17 TBSN 14
             AT 1 L1 REM(2,1) *SWAP FROM* 16:13:02
             AT 1 L1 *SWAP TO* 16:14:40

          AT 1 FS

           This keyin displays the following:
             AT 1 L1 REM(3,2) PL:ATL1P2 16:13:06
             AT 1 L1 BLK 1 TBSN 14
             AT 1 S1 REM(3,3) PL:ATL1P3 16:13:06
             AT 1 L2 FIX(2,1) PL:*MS20H2* 16:13:01
             AT 1 L2 BLK 17 TBSN 14
             AT 1 S2 FIX(2,2) *SPARE* 16:13:03

           which shows removable F-cycle 2 is contained on P2. The spare message is not

          AT 1 LC L1

           This keyin disables removable file placement for AT 1 L1 and displays message 1,

             1. 0-AT 1 L1 REM,DOC F70M/10000/TRK/10000/RFPON
             2. REM,DOC F70M/10000/TRK/10000/RFPOFF

           The operator responds to read message 1 by changing RFPON to RFPOFF in
           message 2.

             AT 1 L1 REM,DOC F70M/10000/TRK/10000/RFPOFF,ATL1P1/ATL1P2/ATL1P3
             AT 1 L2 FIX,DOC F70/10000/TRK/10000/*CU20H2

           which verifies that the RFP status of L1 has been changed to disabled.
