11.7. B—Setting the Maximum Number of Batch
      Runs
         The B keyin sets the maximum number of open batch runs allowed in the system
         (maximum 99,999).

11.7.1. Format
             B nnnnn

         where nnnnn is 0 through 99999.


11.8. BL—Display Runs in Backlog
         The BL keyin displays information about runs in the backlog queue.

11.8.1. Formats
         BL[,NU] [Dnnn] [ID1,ID2,...IDn]

         where:

         U

              indicates that the ID arguments are user IDs.

         N

              indicates to negate any mask, that is display all runs except those specified in the
              ID fields.

         (Dnnn)

              indicates the maximum number of runs displayed.

         One or more IDs can be specified, separated by commas and with or without masking.
         If “U” is not specified, the IDs refer to run IDs; if “U” is specified, the IDs refer to user
         IDs.

         Examples

              BL D               Detailed output of all runs held in backlog.
              BL D20             Detailed information of the first 20 runs held in the backlog.
              BL BA3*            All runs in the backlog that start with BA3.
              BL D319?           Details for runs starting with D319 with a maximum of five characters
                                 in the run ID.
              BL,U U1            All runs that execute under user ID U1.
              BL,NU U2           All runs that will not execute under user ID U2.



7831 0281–034                                                                                     11–27
Keyin Reference




          BL [D | Dnnn]

        When BL is specified alone, the keyin displays all runs in backlog; those in initial facility
        hold are preceded by an asterisk. When D is specified, the system displays detailed
        information about each run; runs are ordered by priority (excluding the held runs).
        When nnn is specified with D, up to a maximum of nnn runs are displayed.

11.8.2. System Response to BL Keyin
        The response to a BL keyin is

          BACKLOG: run-id run-id run-id run-id

        All backlog runs are displayed, in no particular order. Four run-IDs are displayed per
        line. Held runs are preceded by asterisks.

11.8.3. System Response to BL D Keyin
        The response to a BL D keyin is as follows:

          BACKLOG: CAND./HOLD=num-candidate-runs/num-held-runs yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm:ss zone
          run-id/site-id*hold run-id/site-id*hold run-id/site-id*hold

        All backlog runs except the held runs are displayed in order of priority. Three run-IDs
        are displayed per line. On BL Dnnn keyin, the first num-candidate-runs runs of the
        expected output by BL D are displayed. The possible values for each entry are as
        follows:

        hold
            is the hold state, which can be any of the following:

               AD       The run is in an initial facility hold waiting for access to an arbitrary device.
               CT       The run is in an initial facility hold waiting for access to a cataloged file.
               F        This indicates a FIN statement hold (the @FIN statement has not yet been
                        encountered as the Exec reads the runstream).
               MS       The run is in an initial facility hold waiting for an available disk or mass storage.
               O        A CS H keyin is in effect for this particular run.
               OH       The run is in an initial facility hold waiting for access to specific facilities, other
                        than AD, CT, MS, and TP.
               S        This indicates an S option hold.
               ST       The run start time is pending.
               TP       The run is in an initial facility hold waiting for an available tape.




11–28                                                                                           7831 0281–034
                                                                                  Keyin Reference




         num-candidate-runs

              is the number of candidate runs for opening in backlog at the time of keyin. The
              value of num-candidate-runs can be up to 3 digits; a larger value results in a BL
              KEYIN ERROR message.

         num-held-runs

              is the number of held runs in backlog at the time of keyin.

         yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm:ss zone

              is the date and time of the BL D keyin. The zone field contains the configured time
              zone mnemonic.

         run-id
              is the run identification of the run in backlog.

         site-id
              is the symbiont name from which the run was submitted.

         *
              is the separator between the site-ID and the hold state.

11.8.4. Examples
             BL

              Displays all run-IDs in backlog, as follows:
                   BACKLOG: JHND2 X0427X WELLT XXPAUF
                   BACKLOG: SKT4 KILROY DIUUT LBHTLX
                   BACKLOG: WELLSB JP

             BL D

              Displays detailed information of all runs in backlog, as follows:
                   BACKLOG: CAND./HOLD=64/16 2013-04-26 17:18:26 CDT
                   SKT4/ WELLL/ WELC/
                   FREEDQ/*ST BCK022/*ST

             BL D5

              Displays detailed information on the first five runs held in backlog, as follows:
                   BACKLOG: CAND./HOLD=68/16 2013-04-26 16:55:21 CDT
                   FM513/ QQ38R6/ TAPMPP/
                   LRBTIP/ RLBQ1/




7831 0281–034                                                                                     11–29
Keyin Reference




11.9. BP—Controlling the Breakpoint Setting
        The BP keyin sets, clears, and displays the breakpoint register setting and should be
        used only at the direction of your site administrator. The breakpoint register is set to
        trap a suspected problem at a specific main storage location.

11.9.1. Formats
        Display

          BP

              Displays the current breakpoint register setting.

        Clear

          BP 0

              Clears the breakpoint register setting.

        Set

          BP address operation

              Sets the breakpoint register setting.

              where:

              address
                  is where the breakpoint is to occur. It has the form

                      L,BDI,offset

                  where:

                  L
                        is a symbolic name specifying the address tree level. SYS is the only name
                        supported on all systems and is the default if a level is not supplied.
                  BDI
                        is either a 1- to 6-digit octal number or the symbolic bank name on an Exec
                        bank. This subfield, along with the L specifies the bank to be changed or
                        displayed.
                  offset
                        is a 1- to 6-digit octal number specifying the offset into the bank. This
                        offset differs from the relative address used when the segment field is
                        supplied. This is relative to the bank base.




11–30                                                                                7831 0281–034
                                                                                  Keyin Reference




            operation
                 is the operation you want to perform with this keyin. The following operations
                 can be entered in any combination, and are interpreted as follows:

                P       indicates that a breakpoint interrupt is desired when the specified main
                        storage address is referenced as an instruction.
                R       indicates that a breakpoint interrupt is desired when the specified main
                        storage address is referenced as the operand on a read (load) operation.
                W       indicates a breakpoint interrupt is desired when the specified main storage
                        address is referenced as the operand on a write (store) operation.

11.9.2. System Response
         A KEY ERROR message is displayed if the address field is missing, contains other than
         an octal integer, or exceeds 24 bit. A KEY ERROR message is also displayed if the
         operation field contains a letter other than P, R, or W, or it contains any of these
         letters more than once.

         The occurrence of the breakpoint interrupt for the Exec results in an XER 0160.

         The system response to a BP 0 keyin is as follows:
           BP CLEARED

         In response to any other BP keyin, the input is echoed.


11.10. C—Display or Modify Main Storage
         The C keyin displays main storage locations or modifies them to any value.

         Refer to the Exec System Software Administration Reference Manual (7831 0323) for
         information about this keyin.


11.11. CJ—Clear Jump Keys
         The CJ keyin clears jump keys.

11.11.1. Formats
           CJ number1,number2,...numberN

            Clears the jump keys specified by number (1 through 36). See Section 3 for a list
            and description of the jump keys.

           CJ A[LL]

            Clears all jump keys (1 through 36).




7831 0281–034                                                                                      11–31
Keyin Reference




11.11.2. System Response
        The system displays one of the following messages:

            JUMP KEYS SET: number1, number2,...numberN

            JUMP KEYS SET: NONE

        where number is the number of a jump key that is set.

        A jump key is not cleared until the system displays the above response at the system
        console indicating the jump key is no longer set.


11.12. CK—Checkpoint
        The CK keyin saves the state of a batch run. If you need to take the system down for
        service or if the system fails, the save gives you a point from which to restart the run.
        The RS keyin is used to restart the run after a CK keyin. See also

            The System Console Messages Reference Manual (7833 3275) for the messages
             that you see on the console during a checkpoint/restart procedure
            The ClearPath OS 2200 Software Products Installation Guide (7831 0612) for
             additional information on the CKRS product.

11.12.1. Format
            CK,[options] [filename,]run-id,[equipment,reel/reel],,,,[CTL-pool]

        where:

        options
             can be any of the following, and you can specify more than one option:

             A       Do not terminate for certain nonfatal errors.
             B       Do not save the absolute element being executed. Checkpoint always saves the
                     current state of the program, however. Read access to the program file is required
                     to save the absolute element.
             C       Checksum the checkpoint data.
             J       Create an unlabeled tape.
             M       Print full diagnostic messages without the diagnostic snapshot while processing
                     the full checkpoint data.
             N       Do not print error messages or diagnostics in the run’s print file. This must be
                     used on production runs that are printing reports.
             P       Display diagnostics on the console.
             Q       Cause the full checkpoint common bank to put diagnostic snapshots in the run’s
                     print file. This must be used only for debugging purposes.
             R       Save the symbiont files for the run.



11–32                                                                                   7831 0281–034
                                                                                  Keyin Reference




             T        Terminate the run when the checkpoint is complete. If any errors occur during the
                      checkpoint (for example, the checkpoint is not valid), the run is not terminated.
             Z        Cause the partial checkpoint common bank to put diagnostic snapshots in the
                      run’s print file. This must be used only for debug purposes.

         filename
             is the file name to which the checkpoint is written. If the file name is omitted,
             CKRS uses the file name C\KRS$TAPE. You must type a period at the end of the
             file name.

         run-id
             is the run-ID of the run to be checkpointed.

         equipment
             is the equipment type on which the checkpoint tape is mounted. The default is T.

         reel/reel
             is the reel number of the tape on which the checkpoint is written.

         CTL-pool
             is the CTL pool name used to allocate a BLANK tape on which the checkpoint is
             written. Enter NONCTL in this field to use freestanding cartridge tape drives.


11.13. CMR—Console Message Redisplay
         The CMR keyin redisplays console messages, sets the parameters for how messages
         are redisplayed, or terminates or interrupts console message redisplay.

         The format of the CMR keyin depends on the operation to be performed. There are
         CMR keyins for requesting message redisplay, controlling CMR use (at console and
         printer), terminating CMR and interrupting CMR.

11.13.1. Format for Requesting Message Redisplay
           CMR[,option] time1: time2

         where:

         option
             can be either of the following characters:

             B       Breakpoints the requested messages. If this CMR call selects any messages from
                     the CMR database, they are printed on the selected printer.
             R       Displays console messages in reverse order. This option is valid only when making
                     a new redisplay request.




7831 0281–034                                                                                    11–33
Keyin Reference




        time1
           specifies the desired starting time (or, if you chose the R option, ending time). This
           time is either the actual time of day (hhmm based on a 24-hour clock) or is relative
           to the current time in minutes (mm).

        time2
           specifies the desired ending time (or, with the R option, starting time). This time
           cannot be relative, and you can use it only when time1 is the actual time of day.

           When specifying the starting and ending times, give the times in chronological
           order and specify times less than 24 hours old. The current time must be after the
           starting and ending time or equal to only one of them.

           Note: Include all four digits when specifying time of day. Relative times must
           not exceed 60 minutes.

          CMR [+mm|-mm]

        Skips forward or backward mm minutes (mm must be between 1 and 60). Skipping
        past the ending time causes completion of the request. You can adjust the beginning
        time.

        CMR provides special commands to interrupt previous requests from the same
        console. You cannot, however, cancel messages that are generated but not yet
        physically displayed on the console. The DELAY parameter slows message redisplay
        so that you can interrupt a request. To eliminate unwanted remote console output,
        use the @@X O statement. Refer to the Executive Control Language (ECL) and
        FURPUR Reference Manual (7830 7949) for more information on the @@X statement.

        CMR CAN[CEL]
           Cancels the previous request. An error message results if there is no active
           request.

        CMR SUS[PEND]
           Suspends the current request. An error message results if this console is not
           active.

        CMR RES[UME]
           Resumes the previous request. An error message results if this console is not
           suspended.

        Note: When the request is breakpointed (using the B option on the CMR keyin), only
        the CANCEL command is allowed. The CANCEL command stops the redisplay and
        deletes the alternate print file.




11–34                                                                            7831 0281–034
                                                                               Keyin Reference




11.13.2. Format for Controlling CMR Use
         The format of a CMR control keyin is as follows:

           CMR function

         where function is one of the following:

         TRU[NCATION] ON|OFF
            lets you truncate long messages or display them on two lines. Long messages
            might occur because each redisplayed message has its original time displayed
            with it. The parameter is initially set to ON for all consoles. You can change the
            default value at execution time.

         DEL[AY] mls
            specifies the length of time mls between successive lines of redisplayed output (0
            to 2,000 msec). This keeps redisplayed messages from locking out normal console
            traffic. The default delay time for all consoles is 150 msec. You can change the
            default at execution time.

         PRI[NTER] devnam
            Specifies the printer name (devnam)for breakpointed requests. The default printer
            for all consoles is PR; you can change the default printer name at the execution
            time.
         TYP[E] type
            where type can be one or more of the following parameters, separated by
            commas:

            ALL                       Redisplays all four types of console messages
            Or, where type can be one or more of the following parameters, separated by commas:
            1 or MES[SAGE]              Console read-only messages
            2 or OUT[STANDING]          Console read-and-reply messages
            3 or REP[LY]                Replies to console read-and-reply messages
            4 or KEY[IN]                Unsolicited keyins




7831 0281–034                                                                                11–35
Keyin Reference




11.13.3. Format for Displaying CMR Configuration
        To display the console redisplay configuration, enter the following:

             CMR STA[TUS] status

        where status can have one of the following values:

        ALL or blank              Displays all the information.
        ID                        Displays the site-ID for the remote console from which this request
                                  is entered, or the console number (0 to 3) if entered from a system
                                  console.
        TRU[NCATION]              Displays ON or OFF based on the parameter setting.
        DEL[AY]                   Displays the delay time in milliseconds.
        TYP[E]                    Displays the selected console message type.
        PRI[NTER]                 Displays the selected printer for breakpointed requests.
        CON[SOLE]                 Displays the CMR status for the local console or remote console
                                  that entered this request. The status is ACTIVE, INACTIVE, or
                                  SUSPENDED.
                                  CMR provides special commands to interrupt previous requests
                                  from the same console. You cannot, however, cancel messages that
                                  are generated but not yet physically displayed on the console. The
                                  DELAY parameter slows message redisplay so that you can
                                  interrupt a request. To eliminate unwanted remote console output,
                                  use the @@X O statement. Refer to the Executive Control
                                  Language (ECL) and FURPUR Reference Manual (7830 7949) for
                                  more information on the @@X statement.

11.13.4. Format for Terminating CMR
        To terminate CMR, enter

             CMR TERMINATE

        The service run is normally active at all times.

11.13.5. System Response
        If you receive the following message when you enter a CMR keyin, the CMR service
        run is not open:

             CMR KEYIN NOT REGISTERED * [,option function]

        The CMR service run must be open for you to do the CMR keyin.

        A CMR keyin to redisplay messages from a console that is currently redisplaying
        messages immediately cancels the first request and begins processing the new
        request.




11–36                                                                                  7831 0281–034
                                                                              Keyin Reference




11.13.6. Examples
         Example 1
           CMR 2330:0115

            Displays messages beginning at 11:30 p.m. yesterday and ending at 1:15 a.m. today.

         Example 2
           CMR,R 2330:0015

            Displays messages in reverse order for the period just defined.

         Example 3
           CMR,B 1300

            Displays messages from 1:00 p.m. to the time the request was made. Breakpoints
            the messages to a print file and prints them.

         Example 4
           CMR,R 15

            Redisplays any messages displayed in the past 15 minutes, in reverse order.

         Example 5
           CMR 1500:1200

            Displays 21 hours of messages, from 1500 (3:00 p.m.) yesterday until 1200 (noon)
            today.

         Example 6
           CMR 1000:1015
           CMR -30
           CMR +45

            This combination of CMR keyins shows how you can change parameters using
            relative time. The first CMR keyin requests messages starting at 10:00 a.m. and
            ending at 10:15 a.m. The second entry modifies that request to messages starting
            at 9:30 a.m. (10:00 - 30 minutes = 9:30). And the final CMR keyin modifies and
            completes the request by selecting a time that equals the original end time (9:30 +
            45 minutes = 10:15).




7831 0281–034                                                                             11–37
Keyin Reference




        Example 7
          CMR 1000:1015
          CMR SUSPEND
          CMR -15

           This combination of CMR keyins show suspension of the request and changing the
           start time. The first CMR keyin requests messages starting at 10:00 a.m. and
           ending at 10:15 a.m. The second CMR keyin temporarily halts display. Also the final
           CMR keyin backs up the start time to 9:45 a.m. (10:00 - 15 minutes = 9:45) and
           restarts display.

        Example 8
          CMR TYPE MES,REP
          CMR TYPE OUTSTANDING
          CMR TYPE REPLY, KEYIN
          CMR TYPE ALL

           These are examples of the CMR TYPE keyin.


11.14. CS—Set or Release Hold Conditions
        The CS keyin sets or releases various system-specific and run-specific hold conditions
        that control and direct run execution.

11.14.1. Formats
        CS A

          CS A

           Removes the master hold condition set by CS H. Each run in the backlog can be
           rescheduled except those individually held by CS H run-ID.

          CS[,N] A runid[,runid,runid,...]

           When the N option is not specifed, removes the hold condition established by a CS
           H or CS H runid keyin on the specified run or runs. If the N option is specified,
           removes the hold condition established by the CS H or CS H runid keyin on all runs
           except those specified on the keyin. The list of runids may contain masking and/or
           wildcard characters.

          CS,U[N] A userid[,userid,userid,...]

           When the N option is not specifed, removes the hold condition established by a CS
           H or CS H runid keyin on all runs that have the userid or userids specified. If the N
           option is specified, removes the hold condition established by the CS H or CS H
           runid keyin on all runs that do not have one of the userids specified on the keyin.
           The list of userids may contain masking and/or wildcard characters.




11–38                                                                           7831 0281–034
                                                                               Keyin Reference




         CS AD

           CS AD

            Removes the hold on demand run scheduling set by CS HD.

         CS ALL

           CS ALL

            Removes the master hold condition and all individual console hold conditions on
            backlogged runs. It does not remove the hold set by CS HT or CS HD.

         CS AT

           CS AT

            Removes the hold on inactive demand terminals set by CS HT.

         CS H

           CS H

            Holds all runs in backlog. The hold set by this keyin is called the master hold. This
            hold does not include installation runs.

           CS[,N] H runid[,runid,runid,...]

            When the N option is not specified, places a hold on the backlogged run or runs
            specified on the keyin. If the N option is specified, places a hold on all backlogged
            runs except those specified on the keyin. The list of runids may contain masking
            or wildcard characters or both.
           CS,U[,N] H userid[,userid,userid,...]

            When the N option is not specified, places a hold on the backlogged run or runs
            that have the userid or userids specified. If the N option is specified, places a hold
            on all backlogged runs that do not have the userid specified on the keyin. The list
            of userids may contain masking or wildcard characters or both.

         CS HD

           CS HD

            Places a hold on demand run scheduling. Only demand symbiont control
            statements are accepted. The remote batch input mode can be entered to place
            runs in the backlog.




7831 0281–034                                                                                11–39
Keyin Reference




          CS HT

             CS HT

               Places a hold on demand terminals. It prohibits inactive demand terminals from
               completing the activation process. Active terminals remain active.

          CS run-id

             CS run-id*[PxDhhmmShhmmLyF]

               Changes the priority, deadline time, and start time for run-id. The P, D, and S
               options can be used individually or in any combination (such as CS run-id*D1200).

               where:

               Px
                    changes the priority scheduling to the letter specified by x (A through Z),
                    except for a critical deadline run.
               Dhhmm
                    sets the deadline time to the wall clock specified by the 24-hour clock value
                    hhmm.
               Shhmm
                    sets the start time of hhmm specified in 24-hour clock time; this is ignored for
                    an open run.
               Ly
                    indicates a change in the processor dispatching priority (A through Z).
               F
                    forces the run-id out of backlog. This option works only on runs that are
                    candidates for opening; runs held in backlog that are on facility hold, S option
                    sequencing, or operator hold cannot be forced out. The F option is used alone.

          Table 11-1 shows the hold types, the affected run types, and how the hold is imposed
          and removed.
