11.15. CTL—Control CTL System
          The CTL keyin changes the state of a cartridge tape library (CTL), displays information
          about a CTL system, and clears the CTL-controlled status from all tape drives
          registered with a particular CTL-ID.

11.15.1. Formats
          General Format
          The general format of CTL keyin is:

             CTL [ctl-id] [FS | HOLD | REJECT | CLEAR ]

               If a ctl-id is not specified all registered CTLs are affected, but there are some
               restrictions. Refer to section 11.15.3 for more information on restrictions.




11–42                                                                                 7831 0281–034
                                                                              Keyin Reference




         Display State of All CTLS
           CTL

            Displays the state of all registered CTLs.

         Display State of a Specific CTL
           CTL ctl-id FS

            Displays the state of the CTL identified by ctl-ID.

         Place a CTL in Hold State
           CTL ctl-id HOLD

            Places the CTL in a hold state. All cartridge tape requests are held until the CTL is
            reinitialized. Tape assignments can be held by the hold state.

         Place a CTL in Reject State
           CTL ctl-id REJECT

            Places the CTL in a reject state. The CTL rejects tape requests that require the
            CTL.

         Clear CTL Status
           CTL ctl-id CLEAR

            Clears the CTL-controlled status from all tape drives registered with this ctl-id that
            is not registered with another CTL. Subsequent CTL FS keyins for this CTL display
            NOT REGISTERED.

11.15.2. System Response
         The system responds to the CTL FS keyin with one of the following messages:

           CTL: ctl-id NOT REGISTERED

           CTL: ctl-id ACTIVE

           CTL: ctl-id HOLD (AND DEREGISTERED)

           CTL: ctl-id REJECT (AND DEREGISTERED)




7831 0281–034                                                                                11–43
Keyin Reference




11.15.3. Restrictions
        The following are the restrictions:

            The CTL HOLD keyin can be performed only when the CTL is in an active state.
            The CTL REJECT keyin can be performed only when the CTL is in an active or hold
             state.
            The CTL HOLD and CTL REJECT keyins must specify a ctl-id if more than one CTL
             is registered.
            If a ctl-id is specified on the CTL CLEAR keyin, the CTL must be in a reject state.

11.15.4. Examples
            CTL

             In a system with two registered CTLs, the system responds with this information:
                  CTL: CTL-1A ACTIVECTL:
                  CTL-1B REJECT (AND DEREGISTERED)

            CTL CTL-1A HOLD

             The system responds with the following:
                  CTL: CTL-1A HOLD (AND DEREGISTERED)

            CTL CTL-1B CLEAR

             The system responds with the following:
                  CTL: CTL-1B AND CTL-1B TAPE DRIVES CLEARED

            CTL CLEAR

             The system responds with the following:
                  CTL: ALL TAPE DRIVES CLEARED

            CTL BAD

             The system responds with the following:

                  CTL: INVALID KEYIN. FORMAT IS CTL [ID] [FS HOLD REJECT CLEAR]




11–44                                                                               7831 0281–034
                                                                               Keyin Reference




11.16. D—Display or Set System Date and Time
         The D keyin is used to display or set the system date and time.

         For additional information on D keyins, their operation, and their responses, see the
         Exec System Software Administration Reference Manual (7831 0323).

         OS 2200 systems support the internal use of Coordinated Universal Time (UTC). Some
         D keyins support the use of a UTC time value.

11.16.1. Formats
         D

             D[,UTC]

              Displays the system date and time. If the UTC option is specified, it displays the
              current local time, its corresponding UTC value, and all of the time offsets
              between the two.

         D ENTER

             D[,UTC] ENTER

              Bypasses the clock calendar as the time source and lets the operator set the date
              and time for a specific partition. This keyin is valid only at MODIFY CONFIG when
              this read-and-reply message is waiting for its response. (The MODIFY CONFIG
              message occurs on all tape boots and jump key 1 disk boots.)

              After a D ENTER keyin, system clock synchronization is disabled for the system
              session, because the clock calendar was not used to set the dayclock.

              If the UTC option is specified, a UTC time value will be solicited from the operator.

              The time entered in response to the D ENTER keyin solicitation is only valid for the
              current boot session. It cannot be recovered across reboots and must be
              re-entered.

              Not supported on systems without a clock calendar.

         D RESYNC

             D RESYNC

              Forces Exec to reset the system dayclock from the clock calendar. This keyin is
              valid only after the system has reached SYS FIN.

              D RESYNC is useful for allowing a site to change to or from daylight saving time
              without rebooting the system. It can also be used for dayclock and clock calendar
              resynchronization if clock synchronization was previously disabled.




7831 0281–034                                                                                11–45
Keyin Reference




                 Not supported on systems without a clock calendar.

        D SHIFT

             D SHIFT

                 Controls a seasonal time shift (for example, Daylight Saving Time).

11.16.2. System Response
        In response to a D keyin, the system displays the date and time in this format:

             The current date and time is day dd mmm yyyy hh:mm:ss [zone]

        In response to a D ENTER keyin, the following message acknowledges that the keyin
        has been accepted:

             The date and time will be solicited during Exec initialization.

        In response to a D RESYNC keyin, the following message indicates that the dayclock
        and clock calendar are within the Exec’s tolerances:

             The current date and time is day dd mmm yyyy hh:mm:ss [zone]
             Exec Dayclock/Clock-Calendar synchronization is now enabled.

        In response to a D SHIFT keyin, the system displays the current indicator setting.

             Seasonal time shift is ON.

        or

             Seasonal time shift is OFF.


11.16.3. Examples
        Example 1
             D

                 Displays the date and time as follows:
                   The current date and time is TUE 02 MAY 2000 15:36:08 CDT

        Example 2
             D RESYNC

                 The following message is displayed when the D RESYNC request has been
                 honored:
                   The current date and time is THU 24 AUG 2000 23:45:01 CDT
                   Exec Dayclock/Clock-Calendar synchronization is now enabled.




11–46                                                                                  7831 0281–034
                                                                            Keyin Reference




         Example 3
           D,UTC

            The following message is displayed when the D,UTC request has been honored:
                The current date and time is WED 11 SEP 2002 03:06:40 UTC
                Time zone displacement from UTC is: -06:00:00
                Additional seasonal displacement is: +01:00:00
                The current date and time is TUE 10 SEP 2002 22:06:40 CDT

            If a D,UTC keyin is entered on a system that does not support Exec’s use of UTC,
            the keyin is rejected with
                The current system configuration does not allow D,UTC keyins.

         Example 4
           D SHIFT ON

            Turns seasonal time shift on.

         Example 5
           D SHIFT OFF

            Turns seasonal time shift off.


11.17. DC—Copy a Disk Pack
         The DC keyin copies the contents of one disk pack to another of the same type. It can
         be used to copy local and shared disks.

11.17.1. Format
           DC devnam1,devnam2

         where:

         devnam1
            identifies the source disk.

         devnam2
            identifies the destination disk.

         Note: Processing of the DC keyin can be aborted with the ABORT keyin.




7831 0281–034                                                                            11–47
Keyin Reference




11.17.2. System Response
        After a DC keyin, disk capacity is compared. If the destination disk capacity is smaller
        than the source disk capacity, the copy aborts. If the destination disk capacity is larger
        than the source disk capacity, the following read-and-reply message is displayed:

          DC WILL SHRINK devnam2 BY xxx SW TRKS CONTINUE COPY? Y,<N>

        If you respond with a Y, the extra capacity of the destination disk will be lost. (Small
        differences in size are usually a result of marking the bad tracks on the source disk.)
        After the size differences are resolved, the following read-and-reply message is
        displayed to remind you that any data on the destination disk will be overwritten by
        the new data:

          DISK COPY WILL DESTROY FILES ON devnam2 CONTINUE COPY? Y,<N>

        Respond with an N to abort the copy process. If you continue (by entering a Y), the
        system displays the following message:

          DC devnam1,devnam2 - USE DEFAULT PARAMETERS? <Y>/N

        Normally, DC parameters are supplied by configuration tags DC_MAX_READ_ERRORS
        (DCMAXRERR) and DC_SPLIT_DADS_ON_ERROR (DCSPLTDAD).
        DC_MAX_READ_ERRORS specifies the maximum number of unrecoverable read
        errors allowed before the copy is aborted. DC_SPLIT_DADS_ON_ERROR specifies
        whether device area descriptors (DAD) can be split during read error recovery. To
        override these default parameters, enter an N and respond to the following two
        messages. The first message is as follows:

          DC devnam1,devnam2 - MAXIMUM NUMBER OF UNRECOVERED READ ERRORS? 0-20<xx>

        where xx is the default number. Enter a number between 0 and 20.

        The system then displays the second message:

          DC devnam1,devnam2 - DAD SPLITTING ON UNRECOVERED READ ERRORS? Y/<N>

        Respond Y if DADs can be split during read error recovery, or respond N if they can
        not be split.

        Once you have responded to these two messages, the copy begins and the following
        message is periodically displayed as the copy proceeds:

          DISK COPY IN PROGRESS FROM devnam1 TO devnam2

        When the copy is complete, the system displays the following messages:

          devnam1 COPIED TO devnam2
          devnam1 DN, devnam2 [UP|SU|RV] [R|F] pack-id

        where R indicates a removable disk and F indicates fixed disk.



11–48                                                                              7831 0281–034
                                                                                Keyin Reference




         As part of DC keyin processing, the status of the source unit is changed to DN, and
         the status of the destination unit is changed to the original status of the source unit.
         The source unit requires reprepping before the system can use it again.

11.17.3. Restrictions
         These restrictions apply to the DC keyin:

            A shared disk can be copied only to another shared disk, and a local disk can only
             be copied to another local disk.
            If you are copying a shared disk, file sharing must be up on the copying host.
            Your Exec must have either the Network-MHFS or the XPC version of Multi-Host
             File Sharing configured and installed to copy shared disks. All hosts active in the
             file-sharing environment must have the shared copy functionality.
            All active shared copies are aborted if a host enters or is verified leaving the file
             sharing environment. This can happen with an MH,DN keyin or by answering the
             "Problems Detected" messages to indicate that the host is actually down.
            If a disk is configured as shared and then moved into the local directory (with an
             MH,AD keyin) it cannot be copied to another local or shared disk, nor can another
             local or shared disk be copied to it.
            Physically removable disk types cannot be copied.
            The destination disk must be reserved (RV), unassigned, and have an equal or
             greater capacity than the source disk.
            The source unit must be up (UP), suspended (SU), or reserved (RV). It must not be
             caching, and it cannot have any outstanding messages.
            Both disks must have the same equipment types, must have equal prep factors,
             and must be controlled by equal or equivalent handlers.
            Neither disk can be actively unit duplexing. The unit duplexing association must be
             broken by using the DN keyin before the DC keyin can be successfully used.
            Neither disk can be under the control of the arbitrary device handler (in use by
             DPREP).

11.17.4. User Access During Copy
         While the copy is underway, users can read and write to the source disk. All user
         writes are duplexed to both the source and destination disks, and all reads access the
         source disk.

         If an error occurs while writing to the destination disk (either copy I/O or duplexed
         user I/O), the DC keyin is aborted.




7831 0281–034                                                                                 11–49
Keyin Reference




11.17.5. Read Error Recovery
        If an unrecoverable read error occurs on the source disk and configuration tag
        DC_MAX_READ_ERRORS is zero, the DC keyin is aborted.

        Optionally, the copy handler can attempt to complete the copy in spite of
        unrecoverable read errors. If configuration tag DC_MAX_READ_ERRORS is greater
        than zero, or you modified the default parameter to allow unrecoverable read errors,
        the errors in cataloged files are recorded in the system log file. Each entry in the log
        contains the error’s device relative address, file relative address and the name of the
        file affected, its qualifier, absolute cycle number, and directory ID. A warning message
        is also sent to the system console.

        Note: The information entered into the system log for a cataloged file cannot be
        determined for temporary files. If the error occurs on a temporary file, only the
        device relative address and the I/O status are recorded. The DC keyin then aborts.

        The device area descriptor (DAD) that describes the uncopyable area is marked as
        corrupted. Subsequent attempts to read this area receive an I/O status of 011. If the
        DAD describes a TIP duplex file, DNR and DNW are also set.

        Since the area described by an existing DAD is often larger than the uncopyable area,
        the damage can be limited by creating a new DAD that describes only the unreadable
        spot. For sites with recovery procedures that depend on a particular DAD layout, DAD
        splitting can be defeated by setting CONFIG$ tag DC_SPLIT_DADS_ON_ERROR to zero.

        If an error occurs while reading the disk label, the MFDF, or the DRS area, the copy is
        aborted. If the maximum number of unrecoverable read errors is exceeded, the copy
        is also aborted.

        Notes:
           It is not unusual for read errors to occur during a successful DC keyin. For
            efficiency, the DC keyin handler tries to read the source disk in large pieces
            without regard for unallocated tracks. If these large I/Os fail, the area is reread
            in smaller track and record-sized pieces, skipping unallocated areas. An error
            must occur while reading at the record level before an unrecoverable read error
            has occurred. If the operator answers G to an I/O error message and the copy
            completes with no error message, it means all data was successfully copied by
            avoiding bad spots or areas that were unallocated.
           The DC keyin handler uses special device-relative I/O functions RDRA and
            WDRA to read and write the target disks. I/O error type-and-read messages that
            result from these functions display a filename of SYS$*MFDF$$, despite the
            actual file-relative location of the error.




11–50                                                                            7831 0281–034
                                                                                    Keyin Reference




11.18. DISPLVL—Change Switching Priority
         The DISPLVL keyin changes the switching priority of demand or batch jobs. The keyin
         has an activation format, a termination format, and two display (report) formats.

11.18.1. Formats
         Activation Format
         The activation format changes the switching priority of a specific job or a category of
         jobs:

           DISPLVL,option operand,level

         where:
         option operand
             is the job category affected by the keyin:

             A acct#         All jobs with the specified account number acct#
             P projid        All jobs with the specified project-ID number projid
             R runid         The job with the run-ID runid
             T type          All jobs of the specified type (type can be BATCH, DEDLIN, or DEMAND)
             U userid        All jobs with the specified user-ID userid

         level
             is a symbolic name. It can be a default or a site-specific name that indicates one of
             the named switching levels. The default switching-level names assigned by the
             Exec are TXN, BATCH, DEMAND, and USER. BATCH and DEMAND exist only if the
             user switching levels were split into batch and demand groups during system
             configuration. In this case USER does not exist. USER is a default name only if the
             user levels are not split into separate batch and demand. Refer to the description
             of the SYSTEMLEVEL SGS in the Exec System Software Installation and
             Configuration Guide (7830 7915).

         The operand may include wildcard or masking characters unless the T type is
         specified. Then the operand must include one of the following: BATCH, DEDLIN, or
         DEMAND.




7831 0281–034                                                                                 11–51
Keyin Reference




        Termination Format
        The termination format resets activated jobs to their site’s normal priority:

          DISPLVL,option operand

        where:
        option operand
            is the job category affected by the keyin:

        A acct#        All jobs with the specified account number acct#
        P projid       All jobs with the specified project-ID number projid
        R runid        The job with the run-ID runid
        T type         All jobs of the specified type (type can be BATCH, DEDLIN, or DEMAND)
        U userid       All jobs with the specified user-ID userid

        The operand may include wildcard or masking characters unless the T type is
        specified. Then the operand must include one of the following: BATCH, DEDLIN, or
        DEMAND.

        Partial Display Format
        The partial display (report) format displays a list of active DISPLVL keyins:

          DISPLVL,option

        where:
        option
            is the job category to be displayed:

            A         Account number
            P         Project-ID
            R         Run-ID
            T         Run type
            U         User-ID

        Full Display (Report) Format
        The full display (report) format displays a list of all active DISPLVL keyins:

          DISPLVL




11–52                                                                                7831 0281–034
                                                                                Keyin Reference




11.18.2. System Response
         After a DISPLVL keyin, the system displays the following message:

              DISPLVL: Keyin complete


11.19. DJ—Display Jump Key Settings
         The DJ keyin displays the current jump key settings.

11.19.1. Format
              DJ


11.19.2. System Response
         The system displays the following message:

              JUMP KEYS SET: number1, number2,...numberN

         or
              JUMP KEYS SET: NONE

         where number is the number of a jump key that is set.


11.20. DN—Bring Down Components and Interfaces
         The DN keyin brings down components and interfaces that are not operating properly
         or that are required for use in another partition. You can use the DN keyin to bring
         down the following items:

              An instruction processor (IP)
              An input/output processor (IOP)
              An Extended I/O Interface Processor (XIIP)
              An Extended Processor Complex (XPC)
              A memory module
              A channel module
              A control unit
              A device such as a disk drive, system console, printer, or tape drive
              An interface between a specified channel module and control unit




7831 0281–034                                                                             11–53
Keyin Reference




11.20.1. Formats
        Bring Down a Component
        To bring down a component, use the following keyin format:

          DN component[,com2,com3,...]

        Bring Down an Interface
        To bring down an interface, use the following keyin format:

          DN cmod/[intf#]/cu[,cmod//cu2,...]

        This keyin makes the interface between the specified channel module (cmod) and the
        control unit (cu) unavailable. If you do not specify an interface number (intf#), the Exec
        supplies a value. You can also use this keyin for bringing down an interface to the
        record lock processor (RLP).

        Bring Down Devices
        To bring down all devices attached to a control unit, use the following keyin format:

          DN,ALL cu[,cu2,cu3,...]

        Disable XPC Caching
        To disable XPC caching, use one of the following keyin formats:

          DN,XCACHE xpcname

          DN,XCACHE xpcname/LOCAL

          DN,XCACHE xpcname/SHARED

        where xpcname is the logical name of the XPC.

        Use the DN,XCACHE xpcname keyin to disable local and shared caching for each host
        using the XPC.

        Use the DN,XCACHE xpcname/LOCAL keyin to disable a host’s local caching using the
        XPC. If local caching is disabled using this keyin, local caching can only be enabled
        using an UP,XCACHE xpcname/LOCAL keyin.

        Use the DN,XCACHE xpcname/SHARED keyin to disable shared caching using the XPC.




11–54                                                                              7831 0281–034
                                                                               Keyin Reference




         Bring Down Disk Packs
         To bring down one or more removable disk packs, use the following keyin format:

           DN,PACK pack-id[/[dir-id]//[eqp-mnemonic],[pack-id]...]

         where:

         dir-id
             is the directory identifier (SHARED or STD).

         eqp-mnemonic
             is the mnemonic name of the unit the pack is on.

             This keyin places the specified packs in the DN state. Refer to Section 5. This
             keyin should only be used by those with a very specific environment.

         Note: For use of the DN,PACK keyin in a local environment, the NEWCON
         configuration parameter REQNBRPST0 must not be set to 0. In a shared
         environment, the parameter REQNBRPST1 must not be set to 0. See the Exec
         System Software Installation and Configuration Guide (7830 7915) for more
         information on these configuration parameters.

         Bring Down a Time Source
         To make a time source associated with iopname unavailable to the Exec, use the
         following format:

           DN,TIME iopname

         This keyin is useful for forcing the Exec to choose another external time source by
         removing the current one from the partition.

         This keyin is applicable only on systems that support an external time source.

11.20.2. System Response
         Before the system brings down a control unit, it tries to disable all interfaces to the
         control unit. If the system cannot do this, it brings down the control unit and displays
         the following message:

           DISABLE OF cmod/intf#/cu INTERFACE FAILED

         If the system stops because it cannot disable an interface, you must recover the
         system from tape.




7831 0281–034                                                                                  11–55
Keyin Reference




11.20.3. Restrictions
        When you bring down a fixed disk with a status of UP or SU, or when you bring down
        a disk that is both reserved and assigned, you lose access to all files on that disk. A
        message is displayed that requires you to respond Y or N. Reply Y to continue
        processing the keyin; this results in an XER 055 system stop. Reply N to tell the
        system to ignore the keyin.

        When you bring down a removable disk that is assigned, a XER 055 system stop
        occurs and the status change occurs after the next disk boot. When you bring down a
        removable disk that is not assigned, the status changes immediately and no XER 055
        system stop occurs.

        When you bring down an active printer, the device stops immediately. If you want to
        allow the current activity to finish, lock out the device (see the description of the SM
        device L keyin) before bringing it down.

        You cannot bring down the last processor.

        Bringing down a tape drive that is assigned to a run causes the run to abort.

        You cannot use the DN,PACK keyin on unit-duplexed devices, but you can use the DN
        keyin on one of the devices to break the Unit Duplexing association.

        When you bring down the last XIIP on an XPC, the XPC also becomes unavailable (NA).

        If a DN IOP results in no path available to the XPC FO, a new type and reply message is
        displayed as follows:

          IOP name is connected to <VIchannelname>.
          DN OF <IOPname> will disable this hostÆs last path to XPC name.
          Continue to DN, enter DISABLE, else CANCEL.

        If you answer DISABLE to the message, EXEC continues the DN of IOP. Otherwise, the
        EXEC aborts processing of the DN IOP.

11.20.4. Examples
        Example 1
          DN DSKT0

            Brings down disk DSKT0 and displays this message:
              DSKT0 DN

        Example 2
          DN PRA,PRB

            Brings down printers PRA and PRB and displays this message:
              PRA DN,PRB DN




11–56                                                                             7831 0281–034
                                                                            Keyin Reference




         Example 3
           DN,ALL CUTAPD

            Brings down all of the devices (TAPED0 and TAPED1) attached to the control unit
            CUTAPD and displays these messages:
                CUTAPD UPTAPED0 DN,TAPED1 DN

         Example 4
           DN XPCA

            Brings down the XPC on host A and displays the XPC status:
                XPCA DN

         Example 5
           DN,TIME IOP18

            Makes all time sources associated with iopname unavailable to the Exec
                IOP18 (TIME)     PRIMARY        DN
                                 SECODARY       DN

            When no time sources are installed on the indicated IOP, the following message is
            displayed:
                DN,TIME IOP18
                 IOP18 has no external time sources installed.

            If the request makes the last time source unavailable to Exec, the following
            message is displayed:
                DN,TIME IOP18
                Exec was unable to locate a viable external time source.
                The SCF Clock-Calendar is being used instead. Answer GO
                IOP18 (TIME)    PRIMARY    UP
                                SECONDARY DN    NP

            If entering console keyins related to CSB maintenance on a system that does not
            support a clock calendar or an external time source, the following message is
            displayed:
                DN,TIME keyins are not supported on this system




7831 0281–034                                                                              11–57
Keyin Reference




11.21. DU MP—Generate an Online Dump
        The DU MP keyin generates an online dump of system memory. An online dump lets
        you gather data without stopping the system, but because parts of the system are still
        running during the dump process, the data produced can lack integrity.

        If the system is hung, an online dump is not a substitute for a normal system dump.
        (See Section 6 for more information on system dumps.)

        Online dumps can be saved to tape only. They cannot be saved to mass storage,
        because the dump operation breaks linkages between the dump and any working set
        of files that exist when the dump is taken.

        Online dumps produce only selective dumps of main storage. This minimizes the
        possibility of an online dump causing either a system stop or a system hang by
        consuming too much memory. If main storage is too tight when the online dump is
        started, the system responds with the following message:

            Main storage is tight, no online dump taken

        In this case, further DU MP processing is terminated.

11.21.1. Format
            DU MP [run-id]

        where run-id is the unique run-ID of a run or TIP transaction.

        The DU MP keyin is normally entered without a run-ID. A run-ID is useful only if a
        particular run or TIP transaction is of interest.

        If a run-ID is included in the keyin, the following apply:

            The resident pages associated with the domain of the run or transaction are
             included in the dump, along with the resident pages associated with the domains
             of any application-level banks that the run or transaction currently has based.
            The dump process hangs if the named run is waiting for console or terminal input
             when you enter the DU MP keyin. In this case a second DU MP keyin is rejected.
            The keyin is also rejected in either of these cases:
                The specified run-ID or transaction does not exist on the system when the
                 keyin is entered.
                The specified run-ID or transaction is suspended (SP keyin).
             In either case, an error message is routed to the console from which the keyin
             originated, and no dump is produced. See the System Console Messages
             Reference Manual (7833 3275) for the messages.




11–58                                                                            7831 0281–034
                                                                                Keyin Reference




11.21.2. System Response
         While the dump is executing, all processing activity stops. No runs or files are lost, but
         real-time programs can lose communications data, and response to users can be
         delayed.

11.21.3. Processing an Online Dump
         When you attempt to process the online dump output, keep in mind that the dump
         output is a snapshot of a moving (dynamic) system. Structures that are linked or
         chained together (such as EXPOOL buffers or program control tables), or structures
         that are in the process of initialization (such as the Exec bank descriptor table), are
         being dumped at a moment when they are either in the process of
         expanding/contracting, linking/unlinking, or initializing. Because these structures are
         being captured at a moment when they are not completely updated or initialized, they
         most likely are not valid when the online dump is taken.

         When you use DMPLIB functions on dynamic or improperly initialized structures, the
         results cannot be useful.


11.22. E—Terminate a Run in Error
         The E keyin terminates an active run in error. If the keyin specifies a batch run, the run
         terminates with an error and allows a postmortem dump (PMD) or the invocation of
         the Programmers Advanced Debugging System (PADS). If the keyin specifies a
         demand run, the current task is terminated but the run continues with the next control
         statement.

         Multiple E keyins can be required to terminate a run if the dynamic configuration
         parameter run_termination_task is not blank. When the run_termination_task
         parameter is set, a termination task can start. If a termination task starts, another E
         keyin is required to terminate that task.




7831 0281–034                                                                                 11–59
Keyin Reference




11.22.1. Format
          E[,options] run-id

        where:
        options
           are one or both of the following letter options:

           N        Prevents diagnostics from being written and a PMD from being invoked or
                    prevents PADS from being invoked (unless diagnostics were generated by a
                    previous activity of the object module). Absolute (or legacy) programs use PMD
                    to format diagnostic dumps while object modules invoke PADS. If a previous
                    activity of the object module terminated in error and generated diagnostics, then
                    the N option prevents additional diagnostics from being generated—PADS can
                    still be invoked against the diagnostics already generated. The results of using
                    this option vary slightly depending on the type of program terminated.
                       In a batch run, this option always prevents a PMD/PADS for the run.
                       In a demand run, this option prevents a PMD/PADS for the currently
                        executing program.
                       For a transaction, this option prevents diagnostics from being written
                        (unless the transaction is extended mode and diagnostics were
                        generated by a previous activity of the object module). No entry is
                        written to the error history file.
           S        Terminates a suspended run in error. You can only specify this option for a run
                    that has been suspended. If the S option is specified for a run that has not been
                    suspended, an error message is displayed. The effect of the S option, compared
                    to the run state, is as follows:
                    Run State              S Option Specified             S Option Not Specified

                    Suspended              Error terminate run            Keyin rejected

                    Not suspended          Keyin rejected                 Error terminate run


        run-id
           is the name of the active run to terminate.




11–60                                                                                 7831 0281–034
                                                                              Keyin Reference




11.22.2. Restrictions
         If a run is suspended and you do not include the S option, the system responds as
         follows:

             run-id IS SUSPENDED - RESUME RUN OR SPECIFY S OPTION ON KEYIN

         If a run is not suspended and you include the S option, the error message is as
         follows:

             run-id IS NOT SUSPENDED - S OPTION NOT ALLOWED, KEYIN IGNORED

         You can terminate a user run at the user’s request; however, do not terminate system
         runs without your site administrator’s approval.

11.22.3. Example
             E BOB

              Terminates the run named BOB. The system response is as follows:
                BOB ABORT
                BOB ABORT FIN


11.23. ERUNS keyin
         The ERUNS keyin lets you terminate multiple runs with a single keyin. It works like the
         E keyin and some of the keyin options are the same.

11.23.1. Format
             ERUNS[,{B|D}NSUX] ID1[,ID2,...,IDn]

         where:

         B

              specifies to terminate batch runs only.

         D

              specifies to terminate the current task for demand runs only. Both B and D can not
              be specified.

         N

              indicates to negate any mask, that is terminate all runs except those specified.




7831 0281–034                                                                                11–61
Keyin Reference




         S

              has the same meaning as the S option on the E keyin, it specifies that suspended
              runs are to be terminated.

        U

              indicates that the ID arguments are user IDs.

        X

              has the same meaning as the N option on the E keyin, which prevents diagnostics
              from being written for the terminated runs.

         One or more IDs can be specified, separated by commas and with or without
         masking. If the U option is not specified, the IDs refer to run IDs; if the U option is
         specified, the IDs refer to user IDs.

        Examples

              ERUNS A1,A2,A3               error terminate runs A1, A2 and A3.
              ERUNS MYRUN                  identical to 'E MYRUN'
              ERUNS,SXBU USR1*             terminate all batch runs executing under user IDs that
                                           start with USR1. The S option indicates to terminate
                                           suspended runs. The X option indicates that no
                                           diagnostics will be generated for runs that are
                                           terminated.


11.24. EU—Display EXPOOL Usage
        The EU keyin displays the EXPOOL usage of your system (in decimal words).

11.24.1. Format
             EU


11.24.2. System Response
        The system response to the EU keyin is

             SIZE(I/C/M) - initsz/currsz/maxisz USE(C/M) - curuse/maxuse E11(C)- cure11

        where:

        initsz
              is the initial size of EXPOOL.

        currsz
              is the current size of EXPOOL.



11–62                                                                                   7831 0281–034
                                                                            Keyin Reference




         maxisz
            is the maximum total size of EXPOOL.
            Note: Because the maximum basic EXPOOL size is always taken, the initsz,
            currsz, and maxisz are all equal.

         curuse
            is the current amount of EXPOOL in use.

         maxuse
            is the maximum amount of EXPOOL used since the last boot or midnight.

         cure11
            is the current amount of EXPOOL index 011 buffers available.

11.24.3. Example
           EU

            Displays EXPOOL usage as follows:
                  SIZE(I/C/M) - 176848/176848/176848 USE(C/M) - 25776/27832 E11(C) û 283


11.25.EXPAND—Expand to Larger Disk Capacity
         The EXPAND keyin expands the internal OS data structures of a pack to make use of
         the larger disk capacities. It can be used to expand local and shared disks.

11.25.1. Format
           EXPAND pack-id

         where:

         pack-id

            identifies the pack to be expanded.




7831 0281–034                                                                              11–63
Keyin Reference




11.25.2. System Response
        Upon completion of the EXPAND pack keyin, the system displays one of the following
        informational messages:

             Disk capacity for directory-id pack pack-id has been increased by
             nnnnnn tracks.

        Or

             Disk capacity is restricted by the pack’s scaling factor.

             Directory-id pack pack-id has been increased by nnnnnn tracks.

        Or

             Disk capacity is restricted by the capacity of duplexed device device-
             id.
             Directory-id pack pack-id has been increased by nnnnnn tracks.

        Or

             Disk capacity is restricted by the maximum supported pack size.
             Directory-id pack pack-id has been increased by nnnnnn tracks.

        Or

             EXPAND pack for directory-id pack pack-id completed during recovery.
             Pack pack-id new capacity is nnnnnn tracks.

        The pack is identified by pack-id and has been increased by nnnnnn tracks. The
        directory-id is either local or shared.

11.25.3. Restrictions
        The maximum track capacity value that will be supported is the smaller value of either

          the defined capacity for the device (For example, configured in the “bin” file), or
          the largest size allowed for the device’s (MBT) Scaling Factor.

        If the pack resides on unit-duplexed devices, the track capacity can only be expanded
        to the maximum capacity of the smaller device.

        For shared devices, all hosts must be at a level that supports the expand pack feature.




11–64                                                                             7831 0281–034
                                                                              Keyin Reference




11.26. FA—Simulate an Attention Interrupt
         The FA keyin simulates an attention (device ready) interrupt and allows disk pack label
         inspections.

11.26.1. Format
           FA devnam

         where devnam is the device name of a disk drive.


11.27. FB—Display or Change Flagbox Settings
         The FB keyin displays or changes the flagbox settings. The flagbox is a set of software
         switches that control TIP system operation by turning TIP features on and off.

         Refer to the Transaction Processing Administration and Operations Reference
         Manual (7830 7881) for a description of the flagbox settings.

11.27.1. Formats
         FB

           FB

              Displays the current flagbox bit settings.

         FB bit

           FB bit

              Displays the status of the specified flagbox bit (on or off).

         FB LST

           FB LST

              Lists all flagbox bits.

         FB OFF

           FB OFF bit

              Turns off the flagbox bit specified by bit.

         FB ON

           FB ON bit

              Turns on the flagbox bit specified by bit.



7831 0281–034                                                                              11–65
Keyin Reference




        FB E

          FB,E OFF SCHEDULE

           Turns TIP scheduling off and purges the transaction system. All transaction
           programs are aborted, RTPS and stuck programs are unloaded, and the VINDEX
           and VALTAB are reloaded from the VALTAB file into memory.

           If TIP scheduling is already turned off, the E (error) option still causes the
           transaction system to be purged. The VINDEX and VALTAB are always reloaded.

           The E option is meaningful only with the SCHEDULE flagbox bit.

          FB,E ON SCHEDULE

           Terminates a transaction-system purge or drain and allows TIP scheduling to
           resume. This keyin, the counterpart to the FB,E OFF SCHEDULE keyin, is provided
           for emergencies when scheduling must be resumed before a purge or drain can
           complete. The VINDEX and VALTAB are not reloaded from the VALTAB file, so it is
           possible for the online VALTAB and VINDEX to be out of date and cause
           transaction errors.

           The E option is meaningful only with the SCHEDULE flagbox bit.

11.27.2. Examples
          FB

           The system lists all flagbox bits that are currently set, as follows:
                TIPTRACE ON
                SCHEDULE ON
                MESSAGES ON
                DEBUGXX ON
                OP$MAIN ON

          FB LST

           The system displays all flagbox bits, as follows:
               VTBLOG         TPURLOG          SUPURLOG         D$FAST
               D$1782         D$0432           OP$TEST          OP$ALT
               OP$MAIN        CKCKONSCH        DEBUGXX          CSUPPRESS
               INHIBSTF       MAXTIMENQ        MAXTIMEDP        SDEADLOCK
               NOCMPINIT      STICKING         MESSAGES         TRAINFB
               TIPLOG         BTLOADING        RECOVERY         SCHEDULE
               TIPTRACE

           The system displays the status of flagbox TIPTRACE, as follows:
                TIPTRACE ON




11–66                                                                              7831 0281–034
                                                                                Keyin Reference




11.28. FC—Function Control
         The FC keyin enables and disables internal Exec functionality.

11.28.1. Formats
         FC ACTON

           FC ACTON

             Turns on tracing of activity activations and deactivations.

         FC ACTOFF

           FC ACTOFF

             Turns off tracing of activity activations and deactivations.

         FC ACTCLR
           FC ACTCLR

             Clears and turns off tracing of activity activations and deactivations.

         FC ARMON

           FC ARMON

             Turns on FCNARM tracing during mass storage allocations and releases. For each
             request, the FCNARM work buffer, the request packet, qualifier, file name, run-ID,
             and file item is traced. Also, device area descriptor (DAD) entries that are added,
             changed, or released are traced.

         FC ARMOFF

           FC ARMOFF

             Turns off FCNARM tracing during mass storage allocations and releases.

         FC BPTRACE

           FC BPTRACE ON

             Acquires a trace container and permit activation of tracing using the breakpoint
             register.

           FC BPTRACE OFF

             Releases the trace container and tracing. Using the breakpoint register is no longer
             permitted.




7831 0281–034                                                                              11–67
Keyin Reference




        FC DADON

          FC DADON

           Turns on DAD table verification for the following:

              Previous and next table links.
              Starting and ending file-relative addresses.
              The number of allocated tracks equal to the sum of the file item Quota counts.
              The number of allocated tracks equal to the number of granules assigned to
               the file.

           If the verification fails, an XER 156 is taken. This keyin impacts mass storage
           allocation, release performance, and I/O performance.

        FC DADOFF

          FC DADOFF

           Turns off DAD table verification checking.

        FC MBTON

          FC MBTON

           Initiates disk master bit table (MBT) summation checking.

        FC MBTOFF

          FC MBTOFF

           Terminates disk master bit table summation checking.

        FC PCTON

          FC PCTON

           Turns on tracing of program control table (PCT) buffer usage.

        FC PCTOFF

          FC PCTOFF

           Turns off tracing of PCT buffer usage.




11–68                                                                            7831 0281–034
                                                                            Keyin Reference




         FC RECON

           FC RECON

            Engages the bad track/position or MFD sector release error recovery code so that
            no XER 057 or 157 stops occur.

         FC RECOFF

           FC RECOFF

            Disengages the bad track/position or MFD sector release error recovery code so
            that XER 057 or 157 stops occur on bad track/position or MFD sector release.

         FC UPDON

           FC UPDON

            Turns on tracing of IP usage by activities.

         FC UPDOFF

           FC UPDOFF

            Turns off tracing of IP usage by activities.

         FC EVTON

           FC EVTON

            Turns on tracing of SCIOP event reporting.

         FC EVTOFF

           FC EVTOFF

            Turns off tracing of SCIOP event reporting.

         FC INTON

           FC INTON

            Activates the processor interrupt tracing. When active, the trace retains copies of
            the ASP from the ICS frame for the last 8 interrupts on per IP basis. These entries
            are available in any subsequent Exec dump as a potential analysis aid.

         FC INTOFF

           FC INTOFF

            Deactivates the processor interrupt tracing.



7831 0281–034                                                                            11–69
Keyin Reference




        FC STATUS

          FC STATUS

            Checks the status of FC keyin functions and displays whether they are on, off, or
            in some other state applicable to that keyin.

        FC RSI$ON

          FC RSI$ON

            Activates tracing of ER RSI$ requests. The trace records the first five words of
            the RSI$ packet on both the call and return.

        FC RSI$OFF

          FC RSI$OFF

            Deactivates tracing of ER RSI$ requests.

11.28.2. System Response
        A confirming message is displayed on the console in response to any FC keyin.


11.29. FF—Display File Information
        The FF keyin displays all run-IDs or common name sections that have a specific
        cataloged file assigned. The FF keyin is used to determine which user has a file
        assigned when a hold condition occurs, such as when the file is assigned exclusively
        to another run. Users with the LIMITED console keyin capability can use this keyin.
        Note: When using the FF keyin to display information for a file that was assigned
        prior to a name change, be certain you enter the new file name. If you enter the FF
        keyin using the old file name, the keyin returns a not found status, because the old
        file name no longer exists in the file directory. Entering the FF keyin using the new
        file name returns all users who have the file assigned by either the old or new
        name.

11.29.1. Format
          FF [dir-id#]qualifier*filename[(F-cycle)] [.]

        where:
        dir-id
            is the directory of the file (STD or SHARED). If you do not specify the directory-ID
            on the keyin, all directories are assumed. The STD directory is displayed, followed
            by the SHARED directory. If MHFS is down on the host, then the SHARED
            directory is ignored.




11–70                                                                             7831 0281–034
                                                                                  Keyin Reference




         qualifier
             is the file qualifier, from 1 to 12 characters in length. This field can include the
             characters A to Z, 0 to 9, -, and $.

         filename
             is the file name, from 1 to 12 characters in length. This field can include the
             characters A to Z, 0 to 9, -, and $.

         F-cycle
             is the absolute F-cycle number, expressed as an octal integer (indicated by a
             leading zero) with a range of 0 to 01747, or as a decimal integer with a range of 0
             to 999. An F-cycle of 0 is equivalent to no cycle. Relative F-cycle numbers are
             neither recognized nor accepted.
             If you do not specify an F-cycle, all cycles of the requested file are displayed (up to
             32). The keyin displays the information ordered by F-cycle. All name sections with
             the first file of the F-cycle set assigned are displayed first; then all name sections
             with the second set, and so on. (In most cases, however, only one or two cycles
             exist.)

         You must specify at least the qualifier and file name when using the FF keyin.

11.29.2. System Response
         If the file is found, the system displays one of these messages, which explains the file
         assignment:

           CNS: c-n-s HAS dir-id#qualifier*filename(f-cycle)

           RUN: run-id HAS dir-id#qualifier*filename(f-cycle)

         For shared files, the system displays this message:

           HOST: host-id HAS SHARED#qualifier*filename(f-cycle)

         If a SHARED file is assigned on other hosts, then host-IDs of the other hosts are
         displayed (the current host is not included). The message displayed is

           HOST: host-id HAS dir-id#qualifier*filename(f-cycle)

         where:

         c-n-s
             is the name of the common name section that has this file assigned.

         run-id
             is the 6-character run-ID of the run or TIP run that has this file assigned. If the
             operating system has the file assigned, EXEC8 appears in place of the run-ID.




7831 0281–034                                                                                       11–71
Keyin Reference




        host-id
           is the 1-character host-ID of the system (other than the one on which the FF keyin
           was initiated) that has the shared file assigned.

11.29.3. Restrictions
        No security checking is performed, and no read/write access to the assigned file is
        verified before the keyin response.

        User runs can temporarily stall while opening or assigning files, because locking is
        involved in FF keyin processing. These periods are normally extremely short and are
        not necessarily visible to you.

        The FF keyin is not allowed until the SYS run has started execution (SYS START).

11.29.4. Examples
        Example 1
          FF STD#Q*F.

           Displays the directory-ID of file Q*F in directory STD. Even if MHFS is not
           configured, the directory-ID, STD, is displayed.

        Example 2
          FF SHARED#Q*F.

           Displays the directory-ID of file F in directory SHARED with qualifier Q, as follows:
              RUN: BJP HAS SHARED#Q*F(1)
              RUN: BJP HAS SHARED#Q*F(2)

        Example 3
          FF Q*F(2)

           If both STD#Q*F(2) and SHARED#Q*F(2) are assigned on the same host, the
           system displays these messages:
              RUN: BJP HAS STD#Q*F(2)
              RUN: BJP HAS SHARED#Q*F(2)
              CNS: COMMONNAME01 HAS SHARED#Q*F(2)




11–72                                                                            7831 0281–034
                                                                              Keyin Reference




         Example 4
           FF Q*F

            If cycles 1, 2, and 5 of file STD#Q*F are assigned, and cycles 74 and 80 of
            SHARED#Q*F are assigned, the system displays these messages:
                RUN: RUN1 HAS STD#Q*F(1)
                RUN: RUN3 HAS STD#Q*F(1)
                RUN: RUN1 HAS STD#Q*F(2)
                RUN: RUN2 HAS STD#Q*F(2)
                RUN: RUN1 HAS STD#Q*F(5)
                RUN: RUN4 HAS STD#Q*F(5)
                CNS: COMMONNAME01 HAS SHARED#Q*F(74)
                CNS: COMMONNAME02 HAS SHARED#Q*F(80)
                RUN: RUN1 HAS SHARED#Q*F(80)

         Example 5
           FF SHARED#Q*F(2)

            If the current host is host A, and hosts B and C have cycles of the file
            SHARED#Q*F assigned, the system displays these messages:
                RUN: RUN1 HAS SHARED#Q*F(2)
