Keyin Reference




11.30. FS—Display Status of Components and
       Interfaces
        The FS keyin displays the status of a component or interface.

11.30.1. Formats
        There are many formats for the FS keyin. You can specify an option to display the
        status of all the components of a certain type, specify a particular component, or
        specify an interface. You can also specify components from that component down to
        the device or the control unit level by using the ALL option.

        Display Status for All Components of a Specific Type
        To display the status for all the components of a specific type, use the format:

             FS,component

        where component is one of the following:

        ARB                 All arbitrary devices configured in the system.
        CM                  All channel modules (CM) configured in the system.
        CU                  All control units (CU) configured in the system.
        IOP                 All input/output processors (IOP) configured in the system.
        IP                  All instruction processors (IP) configured in the system.
        MEM                 All main storage modules configured in the system.
        TIME                All the time sources installed on the system.

        Display Status of the Disk Device Options
        Note: Options with [S] designated indicates that plural option is allowed.

        ADISK[S]            Display all UP, RV, or SU disks configured in the system.
        DISK[S]             Display all disks configured in the system.
        DDISK[S]            Display all downed (DN) disk drives.
        DRS                 Display the active local and active shared DRS disks.
        DRS,ALL             Display all UP or SU disks with packs prepped DRS.
        FDISK[S]            Display all FIXED disks configured in the system.
        MS                  Display all mass storage devices configured in the system.
        PACK [/ dir-id]
                            Display all UP, DN, or PRESENT removable packs in the file directory, dir-
                            id, [STD (local) or SHARED] (Default is STD).




11–74                                                                                     7831 0281–034
                                                                                            Keyin Reference




         PACK pack-id [/[dir-id],[pack-id] /[dir-id]...]
                                Display the status, inhibit status, and assign count of the specified
                                removable disk pack[s] in the UP, DN, or PRESENT state.
         PACK pack-id/SHARED
                                The status, inhibit status, assign count, and hosts that have files
                                assigned to the specified SHARED pack-id.
         RDISK[S]               Display all REMOVABLE disks configured in the system.
         SHRD                   Display all disks active in the file-sharing environment.
         STD                    Display status of all standard (local) directory disks.
         UD                     Display all unit-duplexed disks in the system.



         Display Status of the Tape Device Options
         Note: Options with [S] designated indicates that plural option is allowed.

         ATAPE[S]               Display all available tape drives in a reserved (RV) or up (UP) state, and
                                whether they are ACS-controlled, CTL-controlled, or premounted (PM)
                                drives.
         AVTH                   Display all OS2200 VTH tape devices in a UP or RV state.
         DTAPE[S]               Display all downed (DN) tape drives.
         DVTH                   Display all OS2200 VTH tape devices in a DN or NA state.

         MTAPE[S]               Display all tape drives with mounted tapes.
         P or PM                Display premounted tape devices in the system.
         TAPE[S]                Display all the tape drives configured in the system and whether they
                                are ACS-controlled, CTL-controlled, or premounted (PM) drives.
         UTAPE[S]               Display only the tape devices that are in use including assigned,
                                mounted, premounted, and service message waiting.
         VTH                    Display only OS2200 Virtual Tape Handler (VTH) devices.



         Display Status of the Communication Component Options

         ACOM                   Display only UP and RV communication control units (CUs) and
                                devices.
         COM                    Display all communication CUs and devices.
         DCOM                   Display only DN and NA communication CUs and devices.




7831 0281–034                                                                                                11–75
Keyin Reference




        Display Status of a Specific Component
        To display the status for a specific component, use the following format:

             FS component

        where component is one of the following variables:

        cmod                Display the specific channel module.
        cu                  Display the specific control unit.
        devnam              Display the specific device.
        iop                 Display the specific input/output processor.

        ip                  Display the specific instruction processor.
        mmod                Display the specific memory module.
        xiipname            Display the specific Extended I/O Interface Processor.
        xpcname             Display the specific Extended Processing Complex.



        Display Status of an Interface
        To display the status of an interface, use the following formats:

             FS cmod/[intf#]/cu

              Displays the status of the specified channel-module-to-control-unit interface. If
              you do not specify an interface number, the Exec supplies a value. Also, use this
              keyin for displaying the status of the interface to the record lock processor (RLP).

             FS cu/[intf#]/dev

              Displays the status of the specified control-unit-to-device interface. If you do not
              specify an interface number, the Exec supplies a value. The status of the control-
              unit-to-device interface cannot be modified by the operator.

        Display Status of Components to Control Unit or Device Level
        To display the status for all components from a specified component name to the
        control unit or device level, use the following format:

             FS,ALL component

        where component is one of the following variables:

        Cmod                Display all CUs and devices on the specified channel module.
        Cu                  Display all devices attached to the specified control unit.
        Iop                 Display the IOP components down to the control unit level.




11–76                                                                                     7831 0281–034
                                                                                       Keyin Reference




         ip                   Display all IP components down to the control unit level.
         Xpcname              Display the specified XPC and the status of all attached XIIPs.



         Display Availability of Disk Units
         To display the availability of fixed and removable disk units, use the following format:

              FS,AVAIL [cu1,cu2,...cuN] [device1, device2,...deviceN]

         where:

         cu
                 displays availability of all mass storage devices attached to the control units
                 specified.

         device
                 displays availability for the disk units specified.

         The FS,AVAIL keyin is not configurable.

         Display Status of External Time Sources
         To display the current state of the external time sources in the partition, use the
         following format:

              FS,TIME [iopname]

         This keyin is applicable only on systems that support an external time source.

11.30.2. System Response
         There are many display formats to accommodate the various system components, as
         described in the following.

         Instruction Processors
         For instruction processors, the display format is

              processor state [NA] [ACTIVE/JP]

         where:

         processor
                 is the name of the processor whose status is being displayed.

         state
                 is the state of the processor (UP, DN, or RV).




7831 0281–034                                                                                      11–77
Keyin Reference




        NA
                indicates that the processor is not available.

        ACTIVE
                indicates that the processor is running.

        Devices Other Than Tape or Disk
        For an IOP, channel module, control unit, or any device other than a tape drive or disk
        drive, the display format is

          unit state [NA]

        where:

        unit
                is the name of the IOP, channel module, control unit, or device whose status is
                being displayed.

        state
                is the state of the component, as follows:

                 UP or DN for an IOP, channel module, or control unit
                 UP, DN, SU, or RV for a device
                 UP, DN, or RV for a partition

        NA
                indicates that the component is not available.

        XPCs
        For an XPC in local mode, the FS,XPC keyin displays the mode of the XPC and the state
        of caching. The current status of the master file directory (MFD) file caching and
        shared system device SHDRUM file caching is also displayed. The following
        information for each host participating in the multihost environment is displayed:

          HOST host-id active-state [xpc-interface-id/xiipname]...

        where:

        host-id
                is the name of the host.

        active-state
                is active or inactive.

        xpc-interface-id
                is the interface ID of the XPC for the particular XIIP.


11–78                                                                              7831 0281–034
                                                                                    Keyin Reference




         xiipname
                is the name of the XIIP (site specific). It also refers to VI interfaces.

         For example:

           XPCA UP MEM MHM - XPC LOCAL CACHING ENABLED FOR THIS HOST
            XPC SHARED CACHING ENABLED
            LOCAL MFD CACHING IS ENABLED
           HOSTA ACTIVE 00/XII1 02/XII2 03/XII4 15/XII5
           HOSTB ACTIVE 04/XII1 06/XII4 O8/XII5 16/XII5 11/XII7
           HOSTC INACTIVE
           HOSTD INACTIVE 21/XII1 25/XII2 31/XII5

         For a file-sharing system, the following example is displayed:

           XPCA UP MEM MHM - XPC LOCAL CACHING ENABLED FOR THIS HOST
            XPC SHARED CACHING ENABLED
            LOCAL MFD CACHING IS ENABLED
            SHARED MFD AND SHDRUM CACHING IS ENABLED
           HOSTA ACTIVE 00/XII1 02/XII2 03/XII4 15/XII5
           HOSTB ACTIVE 04/XII1 06/XII4 O8/XII5 16/XII5 11/XII7
           HOSTC INACTIVE
           HOSTD INACTIVE 21/XII1 25/XII2 31/XII5

         For a specified XPC, FS,ALL xpcname displays the status in the following format:

           xpcname status [NA] mode
           xiipname(1) status [NA]
            . . .
            . . .
            . . .
           xiipname(n) status [NA]

         where:

         xpcname
                is the name of the XPC (site specified).

         xiipname
                is the name of the XIIP (site specified).

         status
                is the current status of the component, up or down.

         NA
                indicates that the component is not available.

         mode
                is the current mode of the XPC, either local or MHM.


7831 0281–034                                                                                 11–79
Keyin Reference




        Tape Drives
        For a tape drive, the display format is

            tape state[,ACS][,CTL][,PM] [NA] [* RUNID run-id REEL [reel-#] ring]

            [POS [*]ffff{+|-}[*]bbbbbb|POS LOST]

        where:

        tape
                is the name of the tape drive.

        state
                is the tape drive’s status (UP, DN, or RV).

        ,ACS
                indicates the drive is controlled by an ACS (Automated Cartridge System).

        ,CTL
                indicates that the drive is registered to the CTL system (Cartridge Tape Library).

        ,PM
                indicates the tape drive is in a premount state. (See FS,PM keyin)

        NA
                indicates that the drive is not available.

        *
                indicates that the drive is assigned by a run.

        run-id
                is the name of the run with the drive assigned.

        reel-#
                is the number of the reel mounted on the drive. If a BLANK reel request is made,
                U-BLNK indicates an unlabelled BLANK tape and L-BLNK indicates a labelled
                BLANK tape is to be loaded.

                The following applies only to DVD: If ****** is displayed as reel-#, a medium that
                is not accessible by OS 2200 is premounted on the drive. If the drive is
                subsequently assigned by OS 2200, the medium is unloaded.

        ring
                is either RING or NORING, if specified on assignment.




11–80                                                                                7831 0281–034
                                                                                        Keyin Reference




         POS
                 indicates that a tape is currently mounted on the drive. The tape position is given
                 as follows:

                Fffff           is the current number of files that are extended. For an unlabeled tape,
                                this is the actual number of files. For a labeled tape, the formula for the
                                actual number is
                                   (fffff + 1) / 3

                +bbbbbb         is the current number of blocks that are extended forward from the last
                                tape mark.
                -bbbbbb         is the current number of blocks that are extended backward from the last
                                tape mark.
                *               in front of either fffff or bbbbbb means that the actual file or block count
                                is different from the number displayed. Either a Locate Block command
                                was issued, or the actual count is greater than 99,999 files or 999,999
                                blocks.


         POS LOST
                 indicates that a tape is currently mounted on the drive and that tape loss of
                 position has occurred.

         Disks
         For a disk, the display format is

             disk [S]      state [CQ] [NA] [ [*] R/F PACKID pack-id] [EXEC DRS]

         where:

         disk

                 is the name of the disk device.

         S
                 indicates the disk is shared with other hosts. (File Sharing only)

         state

                 is the state of the disk device (UP, DN, RV, SU).

         CQ

                 indicates the disk is in Command Queuing mode.

         NA
                 indicates that the drive is not available.




7831 0281–034                                                                                           11–81
Keyin Reference




        *
                indicates that the drive is assigned. For disk drives that have removable packs, a
                pack can be assigned even if the drive is not assigned.

        R
                indicates that the pack on the drive is prepped as removable.

        F
                indicates that the pack on the drive is prepped as fixed.

        pack-id
                is the identification of the pack on the drive.

        EXEC DRS
                indicates the disk where the Exec is stored.

        For disks in a Unit Duplexing association, the display format is

            disk1 -UD- disk2 [S] state [CQ] [NA] [[*] R/F PACKID pack-id] [EXEC DRS]

        where:

        disk1

                is the source disk.

        disk2

                is the destination disk.

        All other fields are the same as previously described.

        Channel-Module-to-Control-Unit Interface
        For the channel-module-to-control-unit interface, the display format is as follows in
        response to an "FS chan//cu" keyin:

            chan/intf#/cu state [NA]

        where:

        chan

                is the name of the channel module in the interface.

        intf#

                is the number of the interface supplied by the Exec.




11–82                                                                               7831 0281–034
                                                                              Keyin Reference




         cu

                 is the name of the control unit in the interface.

         state

                 is the state of the interface (UP or DN).

         NA

                 indicates that the interface is not available.

         Control-Unit-to-Device Interface
         For control-unit-to-device interface, the display format is as follows in response to an
         "FS cu//devname" keyin:

              cu/intf#/devname state [NA]

         where:

         cu

                 is the name of the control unit in the interface.

         intf#

                 is the number of the interface supplied by the Exec.

         devname

                 is the specified device name.

         state

                 is the state of the interface (UP or DN).

         NA

                 indicates that the interface is not available.

         Removable Packs
         For removable packs, the display format is

              pack-id state - INHIBITS = inhibits ASSIGN COUNT = count dir-id HOSTS = hosts

         where:

         pack-id
                 is the name of the pack from the FS,PACK devnam keyin or the pack-ID for each
                 device on the FS,PACK keyin.



7831 0281–034                                                                               11–83
Keyin Reference




        state
                is UP, DN, or PRESENT.

        inhibits
                can be R (read), W (write), A (assign) or NONE (no inhibits assigned).

        count
                is the number of files assigned on the pack.

        dir-id
                is the directory-ID, either STD or SHARED.

        hosts
                is a list of hosts that have files assigned on a shared pack in the DN state.

        AVAIL
        For the FS,AVAIL keyin, the display format is

            disk [S] state [CQ] [*] [R/F] PACKID packid

            disk [availability] [LOCAL/SHARED] TRACKS AVAILABLE

        where:

        disk

                is the name of the disk device.

        S

                indicates the disk is shared with other hosts. (File Sharing only)

        state

                is the state of the disk device (UP, DN, RV, SU).

        CQ

                indicates the disk is in Command Queuing mode.

        *

                indicates that the drive is assigned. For removable disk drives, a pack can be
                assigned even if the drive is not assigned.

        R

                indicates that the pack on the drive is prepped as removable.



11–84                                                                                7831 0281–034
                                                                                Keyin Reference




         F

                indicates that the pack on the drive is prepped as fixed.

         pack-id

                is the identification of the pack on the drive.

         availability

                is the number of tracks available on the disk device.

         LOCAL/SHARED

                is the file directory where the disk device resides. LOCAL is the standard (STD)
                directory. SHARED is the shared (SHRD) directory.

11.30.3. Examples
         Example 1
             FS,PACK

              Displays the status, inhibit status, and assign count of all removable disk packs in
              the list of packs in the UP, DN, or PRESENT status, as follows:
                 91Y5 UP - INHIBITS = NONE ASSIGN COUNT = 8
                 91K5 UP - INHIBITS = NONE ASSIGN COUNT = 168

         Example 2
             FS,PACK 91Y5

              Displays the status, inhibit status, and assign count of removable disk pack 91Y5 in
              the list of packs in the UP, DN, or PRESENT status, as follows:
                 91Y5 UP - INHIBITS = NONE ASSIGN COUNT = 8

              If removable disk pack 91Y5 is not found in the system list of packs that are UP,
              DN, or PRESENT, its status is reported as NOT PRESENT:
                 91Y5 NOT PRESENT - INHIBITS = NONE ASSIGN COUNT = 0

         Example 3
             FS,PACK 91Y6/SHARED

              Displays the status, inhibits, assign count, and host-ID of hosts that have files
              assigned on shared pack 91Y6, as follows:
                 91Y6 DN - INHIBITS = NONE ASSIGN COUNT = 8 SHARED HOSTS = AC




7831 0281–034                                                                                     11–85
Keyin Reference




        Example 4
          FS IP0

           Displays information about instruction processor 0, as follows:
             IP0 UP ACTIVE

        Example 5
          FS TAPED0, PRC

           Displays information about tape unit TAPED0 and device PRC, as follows:
             TAPED0 UP ,PRC UP

        Example 6
          FS T40P10

           Displays information about tape device T40P10, on which a tape is currently
           mounted and extended, as follows:
             T40P10 UP * RUNID MAPPER REEL 6MRP11 RING POS 5+480

           If the tape is unlabeled, the actual number of files extended is 5. If the tape is
           labeled, the actual number is 2. (See the formula given with the POS description,
           above.)

        Example 7
          FS,ATAPES

           Displays information about tapes’ drive status and indicates whether they reside in
           an ACS, as follows:
             TP1 UP
             TP2 RV
             TP3,ACS UP
             TP4,ACS DN

        Example 8
          FS,ALL CUTAPD

           Displays information about all devices on control unit CUTAPD, as follows:
             CUTAPD UP
             TAPED3 UP
             TAPED4 UP

                70R1 UP

            Displays the status of UP and SU devices containing packs prepped DRS after
            shared and local mass storage is initialized or recovered, as follows:
                20S0 S UP F PACKID 20S0 SHARED DRS
                20S1 S UP F PACKID 20S1 DRS
                94L0 UP * F PACKID 94L0 EXEC DRS
                94L1 UP * F PACKID 94L1 DRS

            Displays the status of UP and SU devices containing packs prepped DRS after
            shared and local packs are registered, as follows:
                94L0 UP * R PACKID 94L0 EXEC DRS
                94L1 UP * R PACKID 94L1 DRS
                94L2 UP * R PACKID 94L2 DRS

            Displays the status of the active DRS devices (shared and local) when unit

        Example 21
          FS,TIME

           Displays the current state of all external time sources in the partition.
                FS,TIME
                IOP08 (TIME)       PRIMARY           DN
                                   SECONDARY         DN
                IOP10 (TIME)       PRIMARY           UP EXEC
                                   SECONDARY         UP DEGR
                IOP38 (TIME)       PRIMARY           UP
                                   SECONDARY         DN    NP
                IOP41   (TIME)     PRIMARY           DN
                                   SECONDARY         DN    NP

           where:

           EXEC          Denotes that Exec has currently selected this time source for its use.
           DEGR          Indicates that this time source is operating in a degraded mode and might be
                         unsuitable for use in Exec.
           NP            Indicates that this time source is not present.

           If a specific IOP is indicated, the following message is displayed:
                FS,TIME IOP18
                IOP18 (TIME)       PRIMARY           UP       EXEC
                                   SECONDARY         UP

           If no time sources are installed on the indicated IOP, the following message is
           displayed:
                FS,TIME IOP18
                IOP18 has no external time sources installed

           If no time sources exist in the partition, the following message is displayed:
                FS,TIME
                No external time sources are available.

           If entering console keyins related to CSB maintenance on a system that does not
           support a clock calendar or an external time source, the following message is
           displayed:
                FS,TIME keyins are not supported on this system
