11.31. HK—Execute Unsolicited Keyins on Other
       Hosts
         The HK keyin executes an unsolicited keyin on other hosts in an MHFS environment
         from a single host. The keyin responses appear on the console of the sending host.

         See the Multi-Host File Sharing (MHFS) Planning, Installation, and Operations Guide
         (7830 7469) for more information on the HK keyin.

11.31.1. Formats
           HK keyin

            Executes a keyin on all hosts except the sending host.

           HK,host-id keyin

            Executes a keyin on the specified host (host-id).

           HK,ALL keyin

            Executes a keyin on all hosts, including the sending host.




7831 0281–034                                                                           11–93
Keyin Reference




11.32. HM—Send Messages to Other Hosts
        The HM keyin lets you send messages to other hosts in an MHFS environment.

        See the Multi-Host File Sharing (MHFS) Planning, Installation, and Operations Guide
        (7830 7469) for more information on the HM keyin.

11.32.1. Formats
          HM message

            Sends the message to all hosts except the sending host.

          HM,host-id message

            Sends the message to the specified host (host-id).

          HM,ALL message

            Sends the message to all hosts, including the sending host.

        If you send a message to the host from which you entered the HM keyin, the
        message is displayed on the system console but not on remote demand user
        terminals in console mode. On hosts other than the sending host, the message is
        displayed on the remote terminals in console mode on those hosts.


11.33. HU—Display Host Resource Usage
        The HU keyin displays a list of up to 10 run-IDs and the percentage of total available IP
        SUPs used by each run-ID. The information is displayed on the console 1 second after
        entering the keyin. The operating system captures the first 10 run-IDs that accumulate
        IP SUPs after the keyin and displays the run-IDs in descending order of IP SUP usage.
        This display is preceded and followed by informational header and trailer lines to show
        that the operating system is responding to the keyin and to show that all appropriate
        information has been displayed.

        If the Software Controlled Performance (SCP) percentage is nonzero (that is,
        performance is less than 100 percent), the SCP percentage is factored into the total
        available IP SUPs used by each run. This allows consistent comparisons of IP use
        across systems with different SCP percentages.

        The HU keyin also displays the statistics for application level test and set queued
        activities. This information can be used to determine which subsystem or common
        bank is causing the system hang. If a subsystem is causing the system hang, a
        subsystem deactivate can be executed to clear the bottleneck. If a common bank is
        causing the system hang, a common bank reload can be done to the bank to clear the
        bottleneck.




11–94                                                                             7831 0281–034
                                                                             Keyin Reference




11.33.1. Format
           HU


11.33.2. System Response
           RUN-ID: nn%

         where nn is the percentage of total available IP SUPs used by the run during the 1
         second after entry of the HU keyin.

         If no run-IDs accumulate IP SUPs during the 1 second after the HU keyin is entered,
         the following message is displayed between the header and trailer lines:

           The operating system has used all of the available IP SUPs.

           TOTAL TEST AND SET QUEUED ACTIVITIES: nnn

         where nnn is the total number of activities queued in application level subsystems and
         common banks.

           pp% SUBSYSTEM       Subsystem definition element name

         where pp is the percentage of nnn activities queued in this subsystem.

           pp% COMMON BANK     Common bank name FILE common bank file

         where pp is the percentage of nnn activities queued in this common bank.

         If you enter the HU keyin while one is already in progress, the system displays the
         following message:

           HU keyin ignored because one is already in progress.


11.33.3. Example
           HU

         Displays host resource usage as follows:
                PERCENTAGE OF AVAILABLE IP SUPS USED BY RUN-ID:
                  WEBDMD: 1% SILAS : 1% WEBS22: 1% MCB1 : 1%
                  MCB16 : 1% MCB5 : 1% CSC : 1% DDPFJT: 1%
                  *SVC7L: 1% *SVC7K: 1%
                  127 Total Test and Set Queued Activities
                  38% Subsystem STD#SYS$LIB$*CPFTP$LIB(22).SSDEF
                  29% Subsystem STD#SYS$LIB$*ASIS-OMS(9).ASIS$SSDEF
                  13% Common Bank MCBBNK FILE STD#MCB1*MCB$(1).
                  12% Subsystem STD#SYS$LIB$*COMAPI$B(56).SSDEF$API$B
                  END HU KEYIN




7831 0281–034                                                                             11–95
Keyin Reference




11.34. II—Initiate an Interrupt in the Current Run
       Task
        The II keyin causes an interrupt in the current task of a run that has registered a
        contingency for the II keyin.

        Use this keyin only under the direction of your site administrator.

11.34.1. Format
          II run-id [instruction]

        where:

        run-id
            is the id of the run.

        instruction
            is the data you want to send to the user program (maximum six characters). If you
            do not specify an instruction, the run can prompt you for it.

11.34.2. Example
          II MAPPER

            Causes an interrupt in the MAPPER run. The system responds with
                 0-MAPPER*ENTER DIRECTIVE
                 0 RLON
                 MAPPER*RUN LOG ON


11.35. IN—Initialize Fixed Mass Storage
        The IN keyin destroys all files on the specified mass storage device and makes all of
        its space available for new files.

11.35.1. Format
          IN devnam

        where devnam is the name of the fixed mass storage device to initialize.

11.35.2. Restrictions
        The status of devnam must be UP or SUSPENDED. It cannot be shared mass storage.
        Use this keyin at MODIFY CONFIG time. If the keyin is used after MODIFY CONFIG
        time, it is fatal and produces an XER 055 error. Initialization then occurs during the
        subsequent reboot.
        You are notified when the keyin is fatal, and you can decide whether to proceed.


11–96                                                                              7831 0281–034
                                                                                      Keyin Reference




11.36. IODELAY—Set an Artificial Time Delay for
      Unit Duplexing
         The IODELAY keyin provides the ability to set an artificial I/O hardware service time
         delay for secondary units of duplexed pairs. The delay values are not saved when the
         host operating system is rebooted. In multi-host systems, the keyin must be entered
         on each host operating system that requires the delay I/O values.

11.36.1. Format
           IODELAY[,OPTION]            OPERATOR[,OPERAND][,OPERAND][,OPERAND]

         where:

         OPTION
            is one of the following values:

                STATUS         Displays the values of the delay times for read, write, and RBW
                               operations. This is the default, if no OPTION and no OPERANDs
                               are provided.
                CLEAR          Zeros out all delay times. OPERANDs are not allowed.



         OPERATOR
            UD - I/O delay for secondary legs of unit duplexed devices.

         OPERAND
            func=vvvvvvtu

            where

                        func         Type of function. It can have one of the following values:
                                     R - Read
                                     W - Write
                                     RBW - Read before write
                        vvvvvv       A decimal integer between 0 and 999999 which an
                                     operator selects to set the desired time delay.
                        tu           Time unit of vvvvvv. It can have one of the following
                                     values:
                                     US - Microseconds
                                     MS - Milliseconds
                                     S - Seconds
                                     M - Minutes




7831 0281–034                                                                                     11–97
Keyin Reference




11.36.2. Examples
        Example 1
             IODELAY   UD,R=300US,W=600US,RBW=1200US

              Setting the read delay to 300 microseconds, the write delay to 600 microseconds,
              and the RBW delay to 1200 microseconds.

        Example 2
             IODELAY   UD,RBW=1200MS,R=400MS

              Setting the read delay value to 400 milliseconds and the RBW delay value to 1200
              milliseconds. This does not change the write delay value.

        Example 3
             IODELAY,CLEAR     UD

        or

             IODELAY     UD, R=0US,W=0US,RBW=0US

              Turning the delay logic off.

        Example 4
             IODELAY,STATUS     UD

        or

             IODELAY   UD

              Displaying the current delay values.


11.37.IOPDUMP—Dump IOP
        The IOPDUMP keyin saves the storage of specified IOPs to a tape or mass storage
        file. If the output medium is tape, the IOPDUMPs for each of the selected IOPs
        entered in a single keyin are saved onto the same tape medium. If the mass storage
        (MS) option is specified in the keyin, the dump for each selected IOP is saved in
        separate mass storage files. The information in an IOPDUMP is used to analyze both
        software and hardware problems.

        For Dorado systems, an automatic IOP dump capability causes an IOP dump when an
        IOP (SIOP, XIOP, or NIOP) is failing, either through a timeout or because an IOP was
        internally downed because of a problem. By default, the automatic IOP dump
        capability is enabled. This feature is available when the IODBUG configuration tag is
        set to a value of 1; you can also use an IOPDUMP keyin to turn the automatic capability
        off or on.

        You can also use the IOPDUMP keyin to perform a manual dump of an IOP.



11–98                                                                           7831 0281–034
                                                                                Keyin Reference




11.37.1. Manually Dumping an IOP
         Format
             IOPDUMP[,MS[X]] iop-name1,iop-name2, ... iop-nameX

         where:

         iop-name1, iop-name2, iop-nameX
              are the names of the IOPs to be dumped.

         MS
              specifies a mass storage file.

         X
              aborts the previously initiated IOPDUMP.

         If the MS option is specified, the dump is to a mass storage file. The mass storage file
         is cataloged as a private file with a cycle number one greater than that of the most
         recently cataloged cycle using the file name SYS$*IOP$DUMP$. A mass storage file
         cannot be specified at "Modify Config" time. A mass storage file cannot be specified
         unless the system is beyond the point where a mass storage file can be cataloged.

         If the MS option is not specified, the dump is to a tape file. The file name used for a
         tape file is SYS$*IOP$DUMP$. Tape dump files are assigned as labeled tapes without
         the f option. This means that the filename is written into the label blocks. This same
         filename must be used when reading gthe tape file at a later time.

         If the X option is specified, the previously initiated IOPDUMP is aborted. The tape or
         mass storage assignment is freed which causes the original activity to receive an error
         and terminate. If a IOPDUMP is already in progress and the X option is not specified,
         an error message is displayed and the keyin is ignored.

         If the dump file is a tape, the following read and reply message is displayed:

             IOP dump: Enter tape device name <exit$>

         The operator should answer with the name of the tape drive. If no characters are
         entered for this entry, the IOP dump routine is exited. If an invalid character is entered,
         an error message is displayed and the operator is solicited again for the tape device
         name.

         After the tape device name has been entered, the following read and reply message is
         displayed:

             IOP dump: Enter tape reel ID <exit$>

         Answer with the reel-ID of the tape to be used. This entry can be from one to six
         alphanumeric characters. If no characters are entered for this entry, the IOP dump
         routine is exited.



7831 0281–034                                                                                 11–99
Keyin Reference




         If an invalid character is entered, an error message is displayed and the operator is
         solicited again for the tape reel-ID. The program uses the entered tape device name
         and reel-ID to do an absolute @ASG of the tape device.

         After dumping each selected IOP, the following completion message is displayed:

             IOP dump: Completed dumping iop-name into SYS$*IOP$DUMP$(n).
             IOP dump: Block count = xxxxxx, Block size = 1792 words

         where n indicates the file cycle number for mass storage files.

11.37.2. Enabling Automatic IOP Dumping
         An automatic IOP dump is attempted when a standard IOP is failing. The automatic IOP
         dump is written to the mass storage file SYS$*IOP$DUMP$(n).

         Format
             IOPDUMP,AUTO { ON | OFF }

         This keyin allows the operator to turn automatic IOP dumping off or on. The automatic
         dump feature is on by default.

         Note: The IODBUG configuration tag must be set to a value of 1 in order for this
         keyin to have an effect. If IODBUG is not set to 1, automatic IOP dumping is
         unavailable.

         One of the following messages is displayed:

             IOPDUMP: Automatic IOP dump turned ON
             IOPDUMP: Automatic IOP dump turned OFF
             IOPDUMP: Automatic IOP dump NOT available, need CONFIG tag IODBUG = 1


11.38. IT—Control I/O Traces and Timeout Values
         The IT keyin has four main functions:

             Control Host I/O traces
             Control SCIOP/XIIP based I/O traces or display SCIOP/XIIP Microcode Level and
              Hardware Version
             Set the timeout value for mass storage I/O
             Set the timeout value for realtime tape I/O

         The IT keyin has an effect only on the Host on which it is entered. The IT keyin action
         is not restored to the new session when the Host system is rebooted.




11–100                                                                            7831 0281–034
                                                                                       Keyin Reference




11.38.1. Control Host I/O Traces
         When used to control I/O traces, the IT keyin can be used to turn I/O initiation and
         completion traces on and off for debugging and check the status of the traces for all
         subsystem types configured on the system (except for console subsystems). The
         keyin can also be used to copy I/O traces to an external user file for subsequent
         analysis. If you do not utilize the IT-keyin, the trace routines will default to trace I/O
         initiations and completions for all configured subsystem types. The IT-keyin must be
         re-entered if the Host system is rebooted.

         Format for control of I/O traces:

             IT[,operation] [operand] [,t]

         where:

         operation
              is one of the following functions:

              ON              Turns trace on.
              OFF             Turns trace off.
              STATUS          Displays the status of the trace. This is the default.

         operand
              is the trace (or traces) to be affected by the operation. The operand can be any
              site-defined mnemonic name for a control unit or any device configured as a
              control unit, in which case the trace for that handler is affected. It can also be one
              of the following:

              cuname          Specifies control unit name and its handler.
              devname         Specifies device name and its handler
              MS              Specifies all mass storage traces.
              DISKS           Specifies all disk traces.
              TAPES           Specifies all tape traces.
              ALL             Specifies all configured traces. This is the default.

         t
              Indicates whether the initiation or completion traces are affected by the operation
              field, and can be one of the following values:

         C            Affects only the completion traces.
         I            Affects only the initiation traces.
         A            Affects both the initiation and completion traces. This is the default value. It is
                      meaningless on a status request because it returns a status of both I and C traces.




7831 0281–034                                                                                       11–101
Keyin Reference




         M           Controls the copying of the trace data to mass storage. If the operation field is ON,
                     then a new F-cycle of file SYS$*IOTRACE$ is created on fixed mass storage. As
                     trace data identified by the operand is generated, it is copied to this file. The
                     operand field must specify a site-defined mnemonic name for a control unit or any
                     device configured on a control unit. It is not allowed to specify MS, DISKS, TAPES, or
                     ALL. If the operation field is OFF, the copying of the trace data stops and file
                     SYS$*IOTRACE$ is closed and released.
         T           Controls the copying of the trace data to tape. If the operation field is ON, then a
                     tape drive is assigned using the system default tape mnemonic with a reel-ID of
                     blank. As trace data identified by the operand is generated, it is copied to this tape.
                     The operand field must specify a site-defined mnemonic name for a control unit or
                     any device configured on a control unit. It is not allowed to specify MS, DISKS,
                     TAPES, or ALL. If the operation field is OFF, the copying of the trace data stops and
                     the tape is closed and released.

         Example 1
         Displays the current type of I/O tracing for each subsystem.

             IT

                  STANDARD CHANNEL TAPE TRACING INITIATIONS/COMPLETIONS
                  STANDARD CHANNEL DISK TRACING INITIATIONS/COMPLETIONS
                  ARBITRARY DEVICE HANDLER TRACING INITIATIONS/COMPLETIONS

         Example 2

         Turns on only completion traces for mass storage devices.

             IT,ON MS,C

                  STANDARD CHANNEL DISK TRACING COMPLETIONS

         Example 3

         Turns on only initiation traces for all handlers.

             IT,ON ALL,I

                  STANDARD CHANNEL TAPETRACING INITIATIONS
                  STANDARD CHANNEL DISKTRACING INITIATIONS
                  ARBITRARY DEVICE HANDLER TRACING INITIATIONS

         Example 4

         Turns off only completion traces for all handlers.

             IT,OFF ,C

                  STANDARD CHANNEL TAPETRACING INITIATIONS
                  STANDARD CHANNEL DISKTRACING INITIATIONS
                  ARBITRARY DEVICE HANDLER TRACING INITIATIONS




11–102                                                                                      7831 0281–034
                                                                            Keyin Reference




         Example 5

         Displays the status of tracing for all disk handlers.

           IT DISKS

                STANDARD CHANNEL DISK TRACING INITIATIONS

         Example 6

         Displays the status of tracing for all tape handlers.

           IT TAPES

                STANDARD CHANNEL TAPE TRACING INITIATIONS

         Example 7

         Creates the file SYS$*IOTRACE$ and begins to copy trace data for DISKA0 and all
         devices using the same handler to this file.

           IT,ON DISKA0,M
              USE $IOT$.,SYS$*IOTRACE$(+1).
              CAT $IOT$.,F/10000//10000,,,,ACRDIA
              ASG,A $IOT$.
              STANDARD CHANNEL DISK *TRACING INITIATIONS/COMPLETIONS

         Example 8

         Turn off DISKA0 trace. The operand field must specify the same unit mnemonic that
         was used on a previous IT,ON UNIT,M keyin.

           IT,OFF DISKA0,M
              STANDARD CHANNEL DISK TRACING INITIATIONS/COMPLETIONS
              FREE $IOT$.
              EXTENDED DEVICE TRACE FILE HAS BEEN CLOSED

         Example 9

         Assigns a blank tape and begins to copy trace data for TAPEY1 and all devices using
         the same handler to this tape.

           IT,ON TAPEY1,T
              ASG,TZJ $IOT$,T,BLANK
              LOAD UNLABELED BLANK T98N0 $IOT$ - 1 EXEC 8

         After blank tape is loaded

                STANDARD CHANNEL TAPE *TRACING INITIATIONS/COMPLETIONS




7831 0281–034                                                                           11–103
Keyin Reference




         Example 10

         Turn TAPEY1 trace off. The operand field must specify the same unit mnemonic that
         was used on a previous IT,ON UNIT,T keyin.

           IT,OFF TAPEY1,T

               STANDARD CHANNEL TAPE TRACING OFF
               FREE $IOT$.
               EXTENDED DEVICE TRACE FILE HAS BEEN CLOSED

         Example 11

         Assigns a blank tape and begins to copy trace data for DISKA0 and all devices using
         the same handler to this tape.

           IT,ON DISKA0,T
              ASG,TZJ $IOT$,T,BLANK
              LOAD UNLABELED BLANK T98X3 $IOT$ - 1 EXEC 8

         After the tape is mounted

               STANDARD CHANNEL DISK *TRACING ON

         Example 12

         Turns off the copying of the trace data to tape and releases the tape drive. The
         operand field must specify the same unit mnemonic that was used on a previous
         IT,ON UNIT,T keyin.

           IT,OFF DISKA0,T

               STANDARD CHANNEL DISK TRACING OFF
               FREE $IOT$.
               EXTENDED DEVICE TRACE FILE HAS BEEN CLOSED


11.38.2. Control SCIOP/XIIP-Based I/O Traces or Display
SCIOP/XIIP Microcode Level and Hardware Version
         The IT keyin also provides an interface to control SCIOP/XIIP-based I/O traces. After
         these traces are enabled and the trace info has been gathered, an IOP dump is taken
         to retrieve the trace information. Since the trace is enabled in the SCIOP, the trace
         remains set when the Exec operating system is rebooted. During the reboot, the
         SCIOP trace settings are retrieved from the SCIOP and saved in Exec memory, so the
         IT keyin will display the current SCIOP trace settings after the Exec is rebooted. This IT
         keyin will also display the microcode level and hardware version of the SCIOP.

         Format
           IT,operation [operand][,iopname]

         Where:


11–104                                                                             7831 0281–034
                                                                               Keyin Reference




         Operation must be specified and is one of the following functions::

         ON         Turns trace on.

         OFF        Turns trace off.

         STATUS     Displays the status of the IOP traces.


         operand is one of the following options. To enable multiple options, repeated IT keyins
         must be done.

          IOPTR       IOP Traces
          IOPPER      Performance Trace
          IOPTIM      Timer Activity Trace
          IOPFMT      Formatter Trace
          IOPDEB      General Debug Trace
          ALL         Sets, clears, or displays all traces
          IOPINQ      Displays SCIOP/XIIP microcode level and hardware version
          IOPNAME     Specifies the SCIOP/XIIP name which is the target of this IT keyin


         Example 1
           IT,ON IOPTR,IOP01
              IOP01 TRACES ENABLED: IOPTR

         Example 2
           IT,ON IOPPER,IOP01
              IOP01 TRACES ENABLED: IOPTR,IOPPER

         Example 3
           IT,OFF ALL,IOP01
              IOP01 TRACES ENABLED: NONE

         Example 4
           IT,ON ALL,IOP01
              IOP01 TRACES ENABLED: IOPTR,IOPPER,IOPTIM,IOPFMT,IOPDEB

         Example 5
           IT,STATUS ALL,IOP01
              IOP01 TRACES ENABLED: IOPTR,IOPPER,IOPTIM,IOPFMT,IOPDEB

         Example 6
           IT,STATUS ,IOP01
              IOP01 TRACES ENABLED: IOPTR,IOPPER,IOPTIM,IOPFMT,IOPDEB




7831 0281–034                                                                              11–105
Keyin Reference




         Example 7
         If the operand is not one of the options listed above, the following message is
         displayed:

           IT,ON IOPPPP,IOP01
              IT KEYIN INVALID - TRACE DOES NOT EXIST FOR DEVICE TYPE ENTERED

         Example 8
         If the IOP does not support trace or inquiry functionality, the following error message
         is displayed:

           IT IOPINQ,IOP13
              THE IOP DOES NOT SUPPORT THE REQUESTED FUNCTIONALITY
           IT IOPINQ,IOP01
              COMPONENT IOP01 MICROCODE: 09.30.30 HARDWARE: 79SCIOP
           IT IOPINQ,XIP10
              COMPONENT XIP10 MICROCODE: 15.8.0 HARDWARE: 80-RLP

         Example 9
         If the IOP name is not valid, the following error message is displayed:
           IT IOPINQ,XPP10
              THE REQUESTED IOP IS NOT CONFIGURED


11.38.3. Set the Timeout Value For Mass Storage I/O
         The IT,TIMER keyin provides the ability to alter the default disk I/O timer values. The
         time value specified is used for timing ALL disk I/O. The keyin is provided for
         maintenance activity when the site needs to override the default disk I/O timing. The
         I/O timers consist of a hardware timer used by the SCIOP to time disk I/O and a
         software timer used by the Host to time the SCIOP I/O request. The SCIOP timer
         default is currently 4 seconds. It can be modified to any number of whole seconds
         between 4 and 12282 seconds. The Exec software timing uses the periodic timer
         which is approximately a 6.5 second interval to time events. The time interval
         calculated by the keyin always covers the hardware timer value requested by the
         operator. The timer values are not saved if the Host operating system is rebooted; the
         IT,TIMER keyin must be entered again after the system is rebooted. In multi-host
         systems, the keyin must be entered on each host operating system requiring the
         change to the I/O disk timer values.




11–106                                                                             7831 0281–034
                                                                              Keyin Reference




         Format of the disk timer keyin:

           IT,TIMER operand

         where,

         operand is the value used to alter the default disk timer values. Valid operand values
         are:

         CLEAR         When "CLEAR" is entered as a value, the disk timer values are set back
                       to the default timing for all disk I/O requests.

         1 – 12282     These values are the range of seconds which the operator may select
                       to alter the default disk I/O timer. The values are integer representations
                       of the number of seconds to wait for the I/O request to complete
                       before beginning error recovery. Non-zero values which are less than
                       the default disk I/O timer value will cause the disk I/O timer to be set to
                       the default (4 seconds).

         (No value)    When the operand is not specified, the request is to display the current
                       I/O disk timer values.


         Example 1

         Set the IOP hardware timer to 7 seconds.

           IT,TIMER 7
              DISK IOP TIMEOUT VALUE IS 7 SECONDS.
              DISK HOST TIMEOUT VALUE IS 13 TO 19 SECONDS

         Example 2

         Display current values used for disk I/O timers.

           IT,TIMER
              DISK IOP TIMEOUT VALUE IS 7 SECONDS.
              DISK HOST TIMEOUT VALUE IS 13 TO 19 SECONDS

         Example 3

         Specify time less than 4 seconds.

           IT,TIMER 2
              DISK IOP TIMEOUT VALUE IS 4 SECONDS.
              DISK HOST TIMEOUT VALUE IS 6 TO 13 SECONDS




7831 0281–034                                                                              11–107
Keyin Reference




         Example 4

         If an operand value of zero is entered.

           IT,TIMER 0
              IT ERROR: "0" NOT VALID, USE "CLEAR" TO RESET TIMERS

         Example 5

         Clear the current disk I/O timers to use system default timer values.

           IT,TIMER CLEAR
              DISK TIMER VALUES RESET TO DEFAULTS
              DISK IOP TIMEOUT VALUE IS 4 SECONDS.
              DISK HOST TIMEOUT VALUE IS 6 TO 13 SECONDS.


11.38.4. Set the Timeout Value For Realtime Tape I/O
         The IT,TAPTIM keyin provides the ability to change the tape timer value for tape
         requests made by runs executing at realtime priority. The timer value is not saved
         when the Host operating system is rebooted. In multi-host systems, the keyin must
         be entered on each Host operating system requiring the change to tape timer values.

         Format of the keyin:

           IT,TAPTIM operand

          Where,

          operand is the number of integer seconds to wait for realtime tape I/O requests to
          complete. The value must be greater than 0 and less than 301. If a non-zero value
          less than 12 is entered, the default of 12 is used. If the value is greater than 300, then
          300 is used. If an operand of "CLEAR" is specified, then the realtime timer value is
          cleared and the non-realtime tape value for the internal tape handler is used. If the
          operand is not specified, then the current realtime timeout value is displayed.

          CLEAR                     Reset the realtime tape timer value to the tape handler's
                                    non-realtime timer value.

          1 - 999999                These values are the range of seconds which the operator
                                    may select to alter the realtime tape I/O timer value. The
                                    values are integer representations of the number of
                                    seconds to wait for the I/O request to complete before
                                    beginning error recovery. Non-zero values which are less
                                    than the default tape I/O timer will cause the tape I/O timer
                                    to be set to the default of 12. Non-zero values which are
                                    greater than 300 seconds will be set to the maximum timer
                                    value of 300 seconds.




11–108                                                                               7831 0281–034
                                                                             Keyin Reference




          (No value)                When the tape I/O timer value is not specified, the request
                                    is to display the current tape I/O realtime timer value.


         Example 1

         Set the realtime tape timer value to a Host software timing interval which covers 26
         seconds.

           IT,TAPTIM 26
              TAPE REALTIME TIMEOUT VALUE IS 26 SECONDS

         Example 2

         Displays the current tape realtime timer setting.

           IT,TAPTIM
              TAPE REALTIME TIMEOUT VALUE IS 26 SECONDS

         Example 3

         Clear realtime timer value setting.

           IT,TAPTIM CLEAR
              CURRENTLY USING NON-REALTIME VALUES FOR TAPE I/O REALTIME TIMER

         Example 4

         Using 0 as an operand is not allowed and the maximum timer value is 300 seconds.

           IT,TAPTIM 0
              IT ERROR: "0" NOT VALID, USE "CLEAR" TO RESET TIMERS
           IT,TAPTIM 999999
              TAPE REALTIME TIMEOUT VALUE IS 300 SECONDS.


11.39. JH—Control Jump History
         The JH keyin enables or disables jump history (P trace) and displays whether the jump
         history is enabled or disabled. The jump history is maintained in a circular buffer in
         which a limited number of jumps are kept. The jump history is used in dump analysis.

         Using the EXTENDED option allows the operating system to collect more jump history
         entries in the buffer and therefore provides a greater debug capability. Note that
         turning on JH will have a negative effect on system performance.




7831 0281–034                                                                             11–109
Keyin Reference




11.39.1. Format
           JH [ON|OFF] [EXTENDED]

         where ON enables the jump history, OFF disables the jump history, and EXTENDED
         increases the number of jump history entries collected.

         When you enter JH without an option, a message is displayed indicating whether jump
         history is enabled or disabled and if the extended jump stack is in use.


11.40. KEYINS – Display Registered User Keyins
         Programs can be registered to receive unsolicited console keyins where each keyin
         name must be unique. A program can register for up to four keyins.

         The KEYINS keyin displays all keyins that have been registered by such programs.

11.40.1. Format
         KEYINS      [run-id]

         where:

           run-id

         is the name of a run.

11.40.2. System Response
         If no run-ID is specified, all registered keyins for all runs are displayed. For example:

           RUN: IK002 REGISTERED KEYIN(S): ABCDEF7,ABCDEFG*

           RUN: IK003 REGISTERED KEYIN(S): SMA,SMB,SMC,SMD

         If a run-ID is specified on the keyin, only the keyins registered by the specified run are
         displayed. The display has the same format as shown above.


11.41. LB—Control or Display Logbox Settings
         The LB keyin turns TIP Performance Monitor (TPM) selection bits on or off. Two
         system masks, the mandatory mask and the optional mask, specify the TPM records
         that are to be collected. Each selection bit can be set in either of the two masks, but
         not in both.

         See the Transaction Processing Administration and Operations Reference Manual
         (7830 7881) for more information on the LB keyin and the TIP Performance Monitor.




11–110                                                                               7831 0281–034
                                                                                     Keyin Reference




         These are the selection bits for the TPM system mask:

         L$BD           Allows TPM to collect records for batch and demand programs. Batch and
                        demand information is not collected unless this bit is set.
         L$BDH          Allows TPM to record batch and demand header areas for certain TPM
                        records collected on behalf of batch and demand programs. This area is
                        available in all the TPM type 015 records and in all type 012 and 013 records
                        except the P$SPER record. For this area to be available, the L$BD bit must
                        also be set.
         L$CALL         Permits logging of the types and counts of ERs and CALLs performed by a
                        transaction. This option generates an L$CALL area within a TPM reord type
                        015, subtype 03.
         L$DLCK         Permits logging of FCSS deadlock conditions, which occur on the system. For
                        FCSS deadlocks involving shared (XTC) TIP files to be logged, the TIP
                        FLAGBOX bit SDEADLOCK must also be enabled on the system.
         L$FCSS         Permits logging of each TIP File Control function performed by a program. A
                        record is generated each time the program performs an I/O; therefore, this
                        option should be used with caution.
         L$FSRL         Allows TPM to collect information about Freespace deferred release
                        processing. This option causes a TPM record type 015, subtype 010 to be
                        generated when an application's Freespace deferred release activity
                        terminates.
         L$HVSU         Permits logging of user-passed data. The user passes an ACW on EXIT$, and
                        the indicated information is written to the TPM audit trail. This option
                        generates an L$HVSU area within a TPM record type 015, subtype 03.
         L$MISC         Permits logging of miscellaneous information regarding a transaction's
                        execution. This option generates an L$MISC area within a TPM record type
                        015, subtype 03.
         L$QI           Allows TPM to record queue-item data areas for TPM records. For message
                        and state-change records, this option is ignored, and QI data are always
                        logged. This area is available for various TPM 012 and 013 audit record types.
         L$SUP          Allows TPM to record double-word SUP data areas for TPM records. This
                        area is available for various TPM 012 and 013 audit record types.
         L$TDAT         Permits logging of information regarding ER TDATE$ requests made on the
                        system.
         L$TERM         Permits logging of termination information for all transactions. This option
                        generates a termination record type 015, subtype 03.
         L$TPIO         Permits logging of the types and counts of TIP I/O performed by a
                        transaction. This option generates less overhead than the L$FCSS option. This
                        option generates an L$TPIO area within a TPM record type 015, subtype 03.
         L$UDIO         Allows TPM to collect counts of the reads and writes done by IOW$ and
                        DM$IOW ERs, as well as the extended-mode call interface UDS$IOW, to both
                        Exec and UDS/TIP files. This option generates an L$UDIO area within a TPM
                        record type 015, subtype 03.
         L$USER         Permits the user to direct information to the audit trail.




7831 0281–034                                                                                     11–111
Keyin Reference




11.41.1. Format
                    LB[,option] [keyword] [recordname][,trailname]

               where:

               option
                     is M, valid only with the ON and TRL keywords. With the ON keyword, this option
                     causes the specified recordname bit to be set in the mandatory mask. With the
                     TRL keyword, it causes the system trail specification to be set.

               keyword
                     is one of the following:

         ON             Turns on collection of the specified recordname.
         OFF            Turns off collection of the specified recordname. This option clears the recordname bit
                        in either the mandatory or the optional mask, wherever it is set.
         LST            Displays the possible record types in the system mask.
         STA            Displays the status of individual record subtypes.
         TRL            Specifies the system trail. When used with the STFON parameter, this keyword sets the
                        system trail flag on the specified record and audit trail. Without the STFON parameter,
                        no change is made to the system trail flag.
                        The console display of the LB TRL keyin displays the current mask setting for the
                        specified record selection bit: OPT[tional], MAN[datory], or OFF.

               recordname
                     is the name of the selection bit, such as L$TERM, to be added to a mask or
                     otherwise manipulated, or the group record name L$ALL, P$ALL, or U$ALL.

               trailname
                     is the name of the TPM audit trail to which the keyin applies.

11.41.2. System Response
               These are the allowed format combinations and their effects:

               LB                                               Displays the status of all bits set in the system
                                                                masks. The bits that are OFF are not displayed.
               LB recordname                                    Displays the status of the specified record
                                                                selection bit.
               LB LST                                           Displays all possible record selection bits in the
                                                                system masks.
               LB ON recordname                                 Sets the specified record selection bit in the
                                                                optional mask.
               LB,M ON recordname                               Sets the specified record selection bit in the
                                                                mandatory mask.




11–112                                                                                             7831 0281–034
                                                                                  Keyin Reference




         LB OFF recordname                           Clears the specified record selection bit in the
                                                     appropriate mask.
         LB,M TRL recordname,trailname               Specifies trailname as the system trail for the
                                                     specified record and sets the system trail flag.
         LB TRL recordname,trailname                 Specifies trailname as the system trail for the
                                                     specified record and clears the system trail flag.
         LB,M TRL recordname                         Specifies the default TPM trail "TPM" for
                                                     recordname and sets the system trail flag.
         LB TRL recordname                           Specifies the default TPM trail "TPM" for
                                                     recordname and clears the system trail flag.

         An LB keyin attempted with any other format displays the following console message:

           TIP LB keyin syntax error - LB keyin rejected

         If a record collection bit is already in the state requested by an LB keyin, the keyin
         displays the current setting, and no change occurs.

         Setting a record collection bit in one of the system masks causes the bit to be cleared
         in the other mask.

11.41.3. Restrictions
         In an XTC system, TPM record selection bits are not communicated between hosts.


11.42. LC—Control TIP Performance Monitor (TPM)
         The LC (Logging Control) keyin lets you control the TIP Performance Monitor (TPM).
         When TPM is on, transactions that have the appropriate VALTAB options set have
         TPM records written to the specified TPM audit trail.

         See the Transaction Processing Administration and Operations Reference Manual
         (7830 7881) for more information on the LC keyin and the TIP Performance Monitor.

11.42.1. Format
           LC [option] [trailname]

         where:

         option
             is ON or OFF.

         trailname
             is the name of a TPM audit trail.




7831 0281–034                                                                                     11–113
Keyin Reference




         These are the allowed format combinations and their effects:

         LC ON trailname             Starts collection on the TPM audit trail named trailname, displays
                                     the collection state, and shows the current date and time. If
                                     trailname is not open, an attempt is made to auto-open it.
         LC OFF trailname            Turns off collection on the TPM audit trail named trailname, displays
                                     the collection state, and shows the current date and time. If
                                     trailname is already turned off, no action is taken.
         LC ON                       Starts collection on the default TPM audit trail, displays the
                                     collection state, and shows the current date and time. If the default
                                     audit trail is not open, an attempt is made to auto-open it.
         LC OFF                      Turns off collection on the default TPM audit trail, displays the
                                     collection state, and shows the current date and time. If the default
                                     trail is already turned off, no action is taken.
         LC TPM                      Displays the collection state of the default TPM audit trail.

         LC                          Displays the collection state of each configured TPM audit trail.


11.42.2. System Response
         The system displays the following output of the LC keyin for TPM ON or OFF:

              tpm-trail-id ON|OFF [date/time] [error-text] [runid]

         where:

         tpm-trail-id

               is the ID of the TPM trail (that is TPM).

         date/time

               are the date and times that the last TPM ON or OFF occurred, if any, in the format
               yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm:ss zone. The zone field contains the configured time zone
               mnemonic. Both the local and the UTC times are displayed.

         error-text

               is either “Trail in Recovery” or “State Change in Progress”.

         runid

         is the runid that requested the change (not used for console keyin requests).




11–114                                                                                      7831 0281–034
                                                                             Keyin Reference




11.42.3. Examples
         Example 1
           LC TPM

           TPM   ON 2014-01-07 12:20:25 CST         2014-01-07 18:20:25 UTC

         Example 2
           LC ON TPMVOD

           TPMVOD    ON 2014-01-07 14:35:45 CST        2014-01-07 20:35:45 UTC

         Example 3
           LC OFF

           TPM   OFF 2014-01-07 14:40:07 CST 2014-01-07 20:40:07 UTC




11.43. LG—Make an Entry in the System Log File
         The LG keyin lets you make entries in the system log file, which contains information
         about run starting and completion times, console messages, checkpoints, device
         assignments and releases, and hardware fault information.

         If your site has the optional TeamQuest Log Analyzer (TeamQuest LA) software, you
         can subsequently request the message report, which extracts the console printer
         listing from the system log file. The message report includes the text that you entered
         by using the LG keyin.

11.43.1. Formats
           LG text

            Enters the specified text into the system log file. The text usually concerns the
            general operation of the system. (The system records the message in the console
            printer listing and in the system log file.)

           LG *run-id text

            Enters the specified text about a particular run into the system log file. You can
            use this keyin format only when the run is active. If the run-id cannot be found
            among the active runs, the keyin is rejected. The text also appears with the run’s
            summary information when the run is finished.




7831 0281–034                                                                             11–115
Keyin Reference




11.43.2. Additional Information
         For more information about the system log file, refer to the System Log Operations
         and Support Reference Manual Volume 1 (7831 0315).

11.43.3. Examples
           LG SHIFT CHANGE - NEW OPERATOR

           LG *BOB TAPE 123TST NOT IN LIBRARY


11.44. MAINT—Change XIIP State
         The MAINT keyin moves an XIIP from the DN state to an UP maintenance partition
         state so that the XIIP microcode can be dumped or loaded.

11.44.1. Format
           MAINT xiip_name

         where xiip-name is the PCIOP that requires microcode loading or dumping.

         The xiip_name is removed from the MAINT partitioning state by entering

           DN xiip_name

         When the MAINT xiip_name keyin completes, the status reported on the system
         console is:
           UP MT

         An FS keyin shows the XIIP in the UP MT state.

11.44.2. Examples
         Example 1
           FS XIP44

           XIP44 UP MT

         Example 2
           FS,XIIP

           XIP44 UP MT, XIP45 UP




11–116                                                                         7831 0281–034
                                                                              Keyin Reference




11.44.3. System Response
         If the MAINT xiip_name keyin is entered when the XIIP is not in the DN partition state,
         the following message is displayed:
           xiip_name must be DN when the MAINT keyin is entered.

         If a MAINT request is entered for a non-XIIP component, the following message is
         displayed:
           MAINT keyin invalid for logical name component_name, input ignored.

         If a MAINT keyin is entered for a PCIOP, which is already in the MAINT partition state,
         the following message is displayed:
           MAINT keyin already performed for xiip_name.


11.44.4. Additional Considerations
         An FS keyin of the XIIP performed after IOP load has started provides an indicator on
         the FS display that a microcode load is currently in progress.

         UP or DN keyins affecting an XPC-L component while the IOP load is in progress are
         not allowed and a message indicates this.

         When the microcode load or dump operation is completed, the XIIP must be downed
         to remove the XIIP from the MAINT partition state. An UP of the XIIP moves the XIIP
         back into the partition for system use.

         If the system stops when the XIIP is in MAINT mode, the XIIP remains in MAINT mode
         when the partition is rebooted. If the XIIP state is moved from UP to DN by the
         operator at the System Control Facility while the system is stopped, the MAINT mode
         is cleared when the partition is rebooted.


11.45. MD—Modify a Disk Pack
         The MD keyin lets you establish, modify, or remove the inhibits on a removable disk
         pack.

         If the pack is unassigned or not currently mounted on any system device, it is given a
         status of PRESENT. The main benefit of the PRESENT state is that it allows you to
         place inhibits on a pack not currently on the system, so that when the pack is put on
         the system, its access is restricted as needed.

11.45.1. Format
           MD pack-id[/[dir-id]/[inhibits],[pack-id]...]

         where:

         pack-id
             is the name of the pack.


7831 0281–034                                                                              11–117
Keyin Reference




         dir-id
             is the directory-ID supplied on the keyin, either STD or SHARED. STD is for a local
             pack, and SHARED is for a shared pack. The default is STD.

         inhibits
             is any valid combination of the assign (A), read (R), or write (W) inhibits. Whenever
             you use the write inhibit, you must also use the read inhibit. If you add inhibits to a
             pack in the UP or PRESENT state when the assign count is zero, you must include
             the assign inhibit. To remove existing inhibits from a pack, leave this field blank.

11.45.2. System Response
         The response to the MD keyin is

           pack-id state - INHIBITS = inhibits ASSIGN COUNT = count dir-id

         where:

         pack-id
             is the name of the pack from the keyin.

         state
             is UP, DN, PRESENT, or NOT PRESENT. When you add inhibits to a pack that is not
             currently on the system, its state is PRESENT. When you remove inhibits from a
             pack with a status of PRESENT, its state reverts to NOT PRESENT.

         inhibits
             can be R (read), W (write), or A (assign).

         count
             is the number of files assigned on the pack.

         dir-id
             is the directory-ID, either STD or SHARED.

11.45.3. Restrictions
         You cannot use the MD keyin on a unit-duplexed device.

         To use the MD keyin in a local environment, the NEWCON configuration parameter
         REQNBRPST0 must be greater than 0. To use the MD keyin in a shared environment,
         the NEWCON configuration parameter REQNBRPST1 must be greater than 0. See the
         Exec System Software Installation and Configuration Guide (7830 7915) for more
         information on these configuration parameters.




11–118                                                                               7831 0281–034
                                                                              Keyin Reference




11.45.4. Examples
         Example 1
           MD DKSF1/STD/RWA

            Places the read (R), write (W), and assign (A) inhibits on DSKF1 and places it in the
            PRESENT state. The response is
                DSKF1 PRESENT - INHIBITS = RWA ASSIGN COUNT = 0 STD

         Example 2
           MD DSKF1

            Removes inhibits on DSKF1.

         Example 3
           MD PACKA/STD/RW

            Places the read (R) and (W) inhibits on PACKA (assuming PACKA has been brought
            up and assigned). The system response is
                PACKA UP - INHIBITS = RW ASSIGN COUNT = 1 STD

         Example 4
           MD PACK1/STD

            Removes the inhibits on PACK1, where PACK1 is up and has an assign count equal
            to 1. The system response is
                PACK1 UP - INHIBITS = NONE ASSIGN COUNT = 1 STD

            If the pack status is PRESENT and the inhibits are removed, the pack is removed
            from the system list of UP, DN, or PRESENT packs. The system response is
                PACK1 NOT PRESENT - INHIBITS = NONE ASSIGN COUNT = 0

         Example 5
           MD PACK2/STD/AWR

            Places assign (A), write (W), and read (R) inhibits on PACK2, assuming that the
            DN,PACK keyin was used to unassign PACK2 and place it in a down state as
            follows:
                DN,PACK PACK2///D8433

            The system response is
                PACK2 DN - INHIBITS = AWR ASSIGN COUNT = 0 STD




7831 0281–034                                                                               11–119
Keyin Reference




         Example 6
           MD PACK3/STD/WA

            Place the write (W) and assign (A) inhibits on PACK3, assuming that PACK3 was
            assigned and placed in a DN state.
            The system response is
               PACK3 DN - INHIBITS = WA ASSIGN COUNT = 1


11.46. MH—Control MHFS Activities
         The MH keyin controls Multi-Host File Sharing (MHFS) activities and displays
         information about the facility.

         For more information on the Multi-Host File Sharing environment, see the Multi-Host
         File Sharing (MHFS) Planning, Installation, and Operations Guide (7830 7469).

11.46.1. Formats
         MH FILES

           MH FILES

            Displays how many runs on a single host have shared files assigned, and the
            number of runs assigned to the Exec once the system is up and running.

         MH RUNS

           MH RUNS

            Displays all runs with shared files assigned.

         MH AD

           MH,AD

            Used in a MHFS system at Modify Config time to allow data to be recovered
            completely from devices that were configured as shared devices but were
            changed to local devices using the MH,AD STD,devnam keyin. The MH,AD keyin
            causes the boot process to be preempted following successful entrance into the
            file-sharing environment so that MH,AD STD,devnam keyins can be successfully
            entered. Refer to the Multi-Host File Sharing (MHFS) Planning, Installation, and
            Operations Guide (7830 7469) for complete information.

           MH,AD dir-id,devnam1,[devnam2,...devnamN]

            Changes the directory-ID of the specified device to the specified directory. The
            specified device must be marked down (DN) to perform this keyin.




11–120                                                                           7831 0281–034
                                                                              Keyin Reference




           MH,AD SHARED|STD,devnam1

            Changes the directory-ID of the disk drive from STD (standard) to SHARED or from
            SHARED to STD. The disk drive must be marked down (DN) to perform this keyin.

         MH DN

           MH,DN

            Removes the host (of the console from which you entered the keyin) from the
            file-sharing environment. Future shared file assignments are rejected as long as
            the removed host stays down.

           MH,DN HOST,host-id

            Removes the specified host from the file-sharing environment. Future shared file
            assignments are rejected as long as the host is down. Removing the host results
            in the termination of all runs with shared files assigned and prevents future file
            assignments. Use with extreme caution.

         MH FS

           MH,FS HOST

            Displays the host-ID and site-ID for your host and all other hosts active in the
            file-sharing environment.

           MH,FS HOST,ALL

            Displays the site-ID and status of all hosts in the file-sharing environment.

           MH,FS HOST,host-id

            Displays the status of the specified host-ID in the file-sharing environment.

         MH IN

           MH,IN DIR,SHARED

            Marks the shared directory for initialization before the system runs. Enter this
            keyin after the MODIFY CONFIG message is displayed.




7831 0281–034                                                                               11–121
Keyin Reference




         MH RC

           MH,RC DIR,SHARED

             Builds the shared master configuration table (MCT). Unlike the MH,IN keyin, the
             MH,RC keyin causes the shared devices to be recovered, not initialized. Enter this
             keyin after the MODIFY CONFIG message is displayed.

         MH RV

           MH,RV

             Removes your host from the file-sharing environment. Runs that have shared files
             assigned can finish as long as no new shared files are cataloged. This keyin is not
             allowed until after SYS FIN.

         MH UP

           MH,UP

             Enters your host into the file-sharing environment while the system is running and
             notifies the other active hosts of the action.


11.47. MIPS—Set Desired MIPS for a Partition
         This keyin is used to set the desired MIPS for the partition within the limits set by the
         maximum MIPS and minimum MIPS specified in the PDB.

11.47.1. Format
           MIPS [xxxxx]

         where xxxx is optional and specifies the new MIPS setting for this partition.

         If the system is not running with a MIPS-based or MIPS-metered Exec Image Enabler
         key, the following message is displayed:

           MIPS keyin has no effect since the Image Enabler key registered is not
           MIPS-based or MIPS-metered.

         See the Exec System Software Administration Reference Manual (7831 0323) for
         more information about setting MIPS.




11–122                                                                              7831 0281–034
                                                                          Keyin Reference

         Consumption Keyin Response
           Key Registered: Consumption
           SCP Key      MCN: mmmmmmmmmmmmmmmm                PDB MCN: pppppppppppppppp
             NORMAL KEY
               Model: nnnnnnnnnnnn                         SCN: ssssssssssss
               Key ID: kkkkkkkkkkkk                        Partitionable: [YES|NO]
               Expiration Date: yyyy-mm-dd                 IP Capacity: jjj%
               Hard Expiration Date: yyyy-mm-dd            Number of IPs: ii

         Unknown Keyin Response
           Key Registered: Unknown
           SCP Key MCN: UNKNOWN                             PDB MCN:
              NORMAL KEY
                Model: UNKNOWN                                 SCN: UNKNOWN
                Key ID: UNKNOWN                                Partitionable: NO
                Expiration Date: NONE                          IP Capacity: 100%
                Hard Expiration Date: NONE                     Number of IPs: 32

         Note: This message occurs when the system is running without an Image
         Enablerkey. This message can be displayed multiple times until a key is registered
         or performance of the system is degraded.

         Explanation of Output
         mmmmmmmmmmmmmmmm
           is the SCP Exec key Manufacturing Control Number (MCN); otherwise, UNKNOWN
           until a key is registered.

         pppppppppppppppp
            is the PDB Manufacturing Control Number.

         nnnnnnnnnnnn
            is the normal or optional model style when the SCP Exec key is SOLAR registered;
            otherwise, for the normal key, UNKNOWN is shown until a key is registered.

         kkkkkkkkkkkk
            is the unique key identifier, consisting of the MCN and partition number.

         rrrrrr
              is the normal MIPS specified on the MIPS-based Image Enabler key in effect.

         oooooo
            is the optional MIPS specified on the MIPS-based Image Enabler key in effect.

         bbbbbb
            is the prepaid baseline MIPS for MIPS-metering Image Enabler keys.

         aaaaaa
            is the maximum (ceiling) MIPS for MIPS-metering Image Enabler keys.




7831 0281–034                                                                               11–125
Keyin Reference




         jjj%
                is the normal or optional contracted SCP performance level percentage when the
                SCP key is registered; otherwise, the default is used. The default performance
                varies by model family. The registered percentage reflects the contracted
                performance for this partition. When an optional key is registered, the percentage
                is shown only in the optional key portion of the output.

                If this value is less than 100 percent, the difference represents the performance
                reserve available for system growth with a new contracted SCP performance
                level.

         ii
                is the normal or optional contracted IPs when the SCP is registered; otherwise, the
                default is used.

         ll
                is the number of IPs in use by other partitions.

         yyyy-mm-dd
                specifies the normal or optional expiration year, month, and day.

         cccccccccccc
                is the metering contract identifier.

         ssssssssssss
                is the System Control Number (SCN). The SCN is used for entitlement software
                use.

         xxxxxx
                is the desired MIPS at which you want this partition to be running. If the desired
                MIPS is 0 when the system is booted, the partition uses all the available MIPS left
                on the key registered (minimum of MIPS on the key and hardware MIPS available
                in the partition). Changing the desired_mips parameter changes this value.

         yyyyyy
                is the minimum MIPS needed by this partition. If not 0, the MIPS keyin can be used
                to modify the desired MIPS within the range provided by minimum MIPS and
                maximum MIPS.

         zzzzzz
                is the maximum MIPS possible for this partition. If not 0 and not equal to the
                minimum MIPS, the MIPS keyin can change the desired MIPS within the range
                provided by minimum MIPS and maximum MIPS.

         dddddddddddd
                is the name of the partition displaying the message.




11–126                                                                               7831 0281–034
                                                                              Keyin Reference




         qqqqq
            is the MIPS being used by the partition displaying the message.

         eeeeeeeeeeee, ffffffffffff, gggggggggggg, hhhhhhhhhhhh
            are the names of other partitions using the MIPS from the same Image Enabler
            key identifier.


11.49. MR—Recall Outstanding Messages
         The MR keyin lets you recall outstanding read-and-reply messages that have scrolled
         off the screen at remote terminal sites. Only local consoles keep read-and-reply
         messages from scrolling off the screen. When using the remote console capability of
         the Exec, which permits a demand terminal to be used as a console, messages can
         scroll off the screen before they can be answered.

         Use this keyin in conjunction with the CMR keyins to recall previously displayed
         console messages.

11.49.1. General Format
         The general format of the MR keyin is

           MR function [,subfunction] [identifier]

         where function is TR or RC, and subfunction and identifier are optional parameters.

11.49.2. MR TR Keyin
         The MR TR keyin displays information about messages queued for remote consoles.
         Formats for the MR TR keyin are as follows:

           MR TR

            Displays information on all site-IDs in RESPONSE mode if the keyin is entered from
            an onsite console or a remote console in RESPONSE mode.

           MR TR site-id

            Displays outstanding read-and-reply messages for the console specified by
            site-id.

           MR TR,N

            Displays the site-IDs of consoles that are in RESPONSE mode, and the number of
            outstanding read-and-reply messages for each of them.

           MR TR,N site-id

            Displays the number of outstanding read-and-reply messages for the console
            specified by site-id.



7831 0281–034                                                                               11–127
Keyin Reference




           MR TR,n

            Displays the site-IDs of consoles that have at least n outstanding read-and-reply
            messages, where n is 1–9 or + (+ means 10).

11.49.3. MR RC Keyin
         The MR RC keyin displays information about active remote consoles that have one or
         more message groups under their control. Formats for the MR RC keyin are as
         follows:

           MR RC

            Displays the site-IDs of all active remote consoles that have one or more message
            groups under control, and indicates which message groups are under control.

           MR RC,S site-id

            Displays information on the message groups that are under control of the console
            specified by site-id.

           MR RC,M message-group

            Displays the site-IDs of the active remote consoles that have the specified
            message group under control.

           MR RC,M ALL

            Displays the site-IDs of the active remote consoles that have each message group
            under control.

11.49.4. MR OS Keyin
         The MR OS keyin displays information about messages queued for onsite consoles.
         Following are the formats for the MR OS keyin:

           MR OS

            Displays information on all read-and-reply messages queued to onsite consoles.
            The display includes the reply number, the name of the console to which the
            message is queued, and the text of the message.

           MR OS console-name

            Displays information on all read-and-reply messages queued to the onsite console
            specified by console-name.




11–128                                                                           7831 0281–034
                                                                              Keyin Reference




11.50. MS—Display Mass Storage Information
         The MS keyin displays the following information on the availability of fixed and
         removable mass storage devices:

             A summary of the total accessible (up and suspended) fixed mass storage tracks
             The total available (up and unallocated) fixed mass storage tracks
             The fixed mass storage threshold level at which the system will begin unloading
              fixed mass storage
             The fixed mass storage availability goal when the unload process completes

11.50.1. Formats
         All Mass Storage

             MS

              Displays the availability of both local and shared mass storage devices.

         Local Mass Storage

             MS,STD

              Displays information on local mass storage devices only.

         Shared Mass Storage

             MS,SHRD

              Displays information on shared mass storage devices only.

11.50.2. System Response
         Local Mass Storage
         The information displayed for local mass storage is

             SUMMARY: STD FIXED TRACKS ACCESSIBLE = std-ft-access
              STD FIXED TRACKS AVAILABLE = std-ft-avail
              STD ROLOUT START THRESHOLD = std-rolout-start
              STD ROLOUT AVAILABILITY GOAL = std-rolout-goal
              STD FIXED DIRECTORY TRACKS ACCESSIBLE = std-dir-access
              STD FIXED DIRECTORY TRACKS AVAILABLE = std-dir-avail

         where:

         std-ft-access
              is the decimal number of total accessible fixed mass storage tracks (up and
              suspended) in the directory for the system.



7831 0281–034                                                                               11–129
Keyin Reference




         std-ft-avail
             is the decimal number of total available fixed mass storage tracks (up and
             unallocated) in the directory for the system.

         std-rolout-start;
             is the decimal number of remaining fixed mass storage tracks, derived from the
             dynamic configuration parameter STD_ROLOUT_START_THRESHOLD, at which the
             system run ROLOUT gets started to unload fixed mass storage. It specifies the
             minimum amount of tracks to unload when stdstarttrks is greater than the
             difference between stdgoaltrks and stdstarttrks.

         std-rolout-goal
             is the decimal number of available fixed mass storage tracks, derived from the
             dynamic configuration parameter STD_ROLOUT_START_THRESHOLD, which the
             system run ROLOUT targets as tracks available when ROLOUT completes.

         std-dir-access
             is the decimal number of accessible fixed directory tracks. This is computed as
             4096 times the number of fixed disks that are up.

         std-dir-avail
             is the decimal number of fixed directory tracks currently available for expansion.

         Shared Mass Storage
         The information displayed for shared mass storage is

           SUMMARY: SHRD FIXED TRACKS ACCESSIBLE = shared-ft-access
            SHRD FIXED TRACKS AVAILABLE = shared-ft-avail
            SHRD SROLUT START THRESHOLD = shared-srolut-start
            SHRD SROLUT AVAILABILITY GOAL = shared-srolut-goal
            SHRD FIXED DIRECTORY TRACKS ACCESSIBLE = shared-dir-access
            SHRD FIXED DIRECTORY TRACKS AVAILABLE = shared-dir-avail

         where:

         shared-ft-access
             is the decimal number of total accessible fixed mass storage tracks (up and
             suspended) in the shared directory for the system.

         shared-ft-avail
             is the decimal number of total available fixed mass storage tracks (up and
             unallocated) in the shared directory for the system.




11–130                                                                            7831 0281–034
                                                                              Keyin Reference




         shared-srolut-start
            is the decimal number of remaining fixed mass storage tracks, derived from the
            dynamic configuration parameter SHRD_SROLUT_START_THRESHOLD, at which
            the system run SROLUT gets started to unload fixed mass storage. It specifies the
            minimum amount of tracks to unload when shrdstarttrks is greater than the
            difference between shrdgoaltrks and shrdstarttrks.

         shared-srolut-goal
            is the decimal number of available fixed mass storage tracks, derived from the
            dynamic configuration parameter SHRD_SROLUT_START_THRESHOLD, which the
            system run SROLUT targets as tracks available when SROLUT completes.

         shared-dir-access
            is the decimal number of accessible fixed directory tracks. This is computed as
            4096 times the number of fixed disks that are up.

         shared-dir-avail
            is the decimal number of fixed directory tracks currently available for expansion.

11.50.3. Examples
           MS,STD

            Displays the availability of local mass storage devices, as follows:
            SUMMARY: STD FIXED TRACKS ACCESSIBLE = 70893754
            STD FIXED TRACKS AVAILABLE = 6611732
            STD ROLOUT START THRESHOLD = 1430241
            STD ROLOUT AVAILABILITY GOAL = 4443225
            STD FIXED DIRECTORY TRACKS ACCESSIBLE = 262144
            STD FIXED DIRECTORY TRACKS AVAILABLE = 251596

           MS,SHRD

            Displays the availability of shared mass storage devices, as follows:
            SUMMARY:SHRD FIXED TRACKS ACCESSIBLE = 15463406
            SHRD FIXED TRACKS AVAILABLE = 600776
            SHRD SROLUT START THRESHOLD = 234294
            SHRD SROLUT AVAILABILITY GOAL = 618536
            SHRD FIXED DIRECTORY TRACKS ACCESSIBLE = 57344
            SHRD FIXED DIRECTORY TRACKS AVAILABLE = 55526
