11.52.MV—Move a Disk Pack
         The MV keyin applies to physically removable disk subsystems only, and moves a disk
         pack from a failing drive to an unassigned working drive that is either suspended or
         up. The keyin must be entered before the pack is moved. If the disk packs are moved
         first, I/O errors can occur on the unit from which the pack was moved. The pack can
         be moved to any unit of the same equipment type and logical channel type.

         As part of MV keyin processing, the status of the unit the disk pack is moved from is
         changed to down (DN). You can change the status to reserved (RV) to run diagnostic
         tests on the unit.

11.52.1. Format
           MV devnam1,devnam2

         where:
         devnam1
               is the device from which the pack is to be moved.
         devnam2
               is the device to which the pack is to be moved.

11.52.2. System Response
         The system response to the keyin is

           devnam1 MOVED TO devnam2
           devnam1 DN, devnam2 [UP/SU] [R/F] pack-id

         where:
         pack-id
               is the pack-ID.
         UP/SU
               specifies whether devnam2 is up (UP) or suspended (SU).
         R/F
               specifies whether devnam2 is removable (R) or fixed (F).




11–136                                                                           7831 0281–034
                                                                             Keyin Reference




         A LOAD message and possibly SERVICE messages (if the pack change has not
         occurred at the expiration of 2-minute intervals) are displayed on the system console.

11.52.3. Restrictions
         The MV keyin is not allowed on units in a Multi-Host File Sharing environment or on
         unit-duplexed devices.


11.53. OM—Display or Change the Operating Mode
         The OM keyin displays or changes the operating mode of a system running SMART, or
         lets you send a message to the console from SMART. SMART is optional software
         that automates many functions normally performed by an operator. The operating
         mode can be attended, casual, or unattended.

         For more information about SMART, see the SMART Administration and Operations
         Guide (7830 7337).

11.53.1. Formats
              OM[,{A|C|U|X}] [{ON|OFF}/option[/option]...][,{ON|OFF}/option[/option]...] ...

         or

              OM,M message-text

         where:

         A
               specifies attended operation mode.

         C
               specifies casual operation mode.

         U
               specifies unattended operation mode.

         X
               terminates the OM keyin handler.

         M
               sends the specified message text to the console.

         ON or OFF
               turns the following options on or off.




7831 0281–034                                                                            11–137
Keyin Reference




         option
             represents the following options, in any order:

            AAMS          Turns the autoaction message system (AMS) on or off. This is meaningful
                          only in attended mode, since AMS is always on in casual and unattended
                          mode.
            DBUG          Turns the debug mode version of the AMS on or off. This is a mode of AMS
                          where the actual message response does not take place, but the preferred
                          response is indicated. This mode is used for AMS database debugging or
                          for operator training. Note that turning DBUG on turns autoresponse off. To
                          return to autoresponse, DBUG must be turned off.
            TRME          Terminates a nonprivileged (no SSCONSOLE privilege) user’s run with error
                          type termination. This occurs when it issues a message requiring a
                          response and that response is not in the AMS database.
            UALM          Rings an alarm when any user run terminates abnormally.

         message-text
            is the read-only message sent to the console. The message can be 1 to 60
            alphanumeric characters in length. You do not need to enclose the message in
            quotation marks.

11.53.2. System Response
         The following information is displayed after an OM keyin:

           OPERATION MODE: {ATTENDED|CASUAL|UNATTENDED}

           OM OPTIONS (NEXT UNATTENDED): option [, option]...

           OM OPTIONS (CURRENT): option [, option]...

         In casual mode and unattended mode, all read-and-reply and read-alert messages that
         do not have a response in the AMS database ring an alarm.

         If you enter an incorrect OM keyin, the following message is displayed:

           OM KEYIN FORMAT ERROR AFTER xx

         where xx are the 2 characters immediately preceding the error.

         If you enter an option on the keyin that also has a mode change, and that option is
         illegal for that mode, the following message is displayed:

           OM OPTION option NOT CONSISTENT WITH SPECIFIED MODE




11–138                                                                               7831 0281–034
                                                                              Keyin Reference




11.53.3. Examples
           OM

            Displays the current operation mode, as follows:
                  OPERATION MODE: ATTENDED
                  OM OPTIONS (NEXT UNATTENDED):
                  OM OPTIONS (CURRENT):

           OM,A ON/AAMS/DBUG

            Sets attended mode with AAMS and DBUG options on. The system displays
                  OPERATION MODE: ATTENDED
                  OM OPTIONS (NEXT UNATTENDED):
                  OM OPTIONS (CURRENT): AAMS, DBUG

           OM ON/UALM

            Turns on UALM option for the next change to unattended mode. The system
            displays
                  OM OPTIONS (NEXT UNATTENDED): UALM
                  OM OPTIONS (CURRENT):


11.54. PATHS—Display Paths to a Device
11.54.1. Format
           PATHS [device1,device2,...deviceN]

         where:

         device
            displays all paths and their states for the device name specified. If the device is
            not specified, all paths in the system are displayed using one device for each
            subsystem.

11.54.2. System Response
         The PATHS display is in the following format:

           channel-type path-number TO device FOR iop IS cmod/intf#/cu state

         where:

         channel-type
            is the type of channel (for example, SBCON, FICON, FCSCSI, SCSI, NTWCA).




7831 0281–034                                                                               11–139
Keyin Reference




         path-number
             is the sequential number starting at 1 for each path-displayed device.

         cmod
             is the module name of the channel.

         intf#
             is the interface number displayed in hexadecimal. Each byte (two digits) is
             separated by a colon.

             The interface number is defined by SCMS II. This number is an aggregation of the
             following fields:

             FCSCSI

             •   SAN address (Port ID or PID), which comprises

                     8-bit domain (0-FF values allowed)

                     8-bit area (also known as port id, 0-FF values allowed)

                     8-bit ALPA (also known as address (SCMS II), 0-FF values allowed)

             FICON

             •   Logical CU – Logical control unit (also known as CU image number (SCMS II),
                 or subsystem, 0-0xF values allowed)
             •   SAN address (port ID or PID), which comprises
                     8-bit domain (0-FF values allowed)

                     8-bit area (also known as port id, 0-FF values allowed)

                     8-bit ALPA (also known as address (SCMS II), 0–FF values allowed)

             Example:

             FICON 1 TO DLEE15 FOR IOP10 IS I10C70/0E:13:19:00/DLEEA UP

             The interface number 0E:13:19:00 breaks down into

             •   LCU = 0x0E
             •   PID = 0x131900
             The SCMS II-defined interface number in a report or FS cmod/[intf#]/cu keyin
             would be 236,132,608.




11–140                                                                            7831 0281–034
                                                                                Keyin Reference




               SBCON

               •   Logical CU – Logical control unit (also known as virtual control unit number
                   (SCMS II), or subsystem, 0-0xF values allowed)
               •   Control unit address (also known as address (SCMS II), 0–FF values allowed)
               Example:

               SBCON 1 TO MA3A8 FOR IOP11 IS I11C50/ 00:03:C2/MA3B UP

               The interface number 00:03:C2 breaks down into

               •   Unused for SBCON = 0x00
               •   LCU = 0x03
               •   Control unit address = 0xC2
               The SCMS II -defined interface number in a report or FS cmod/[intf#]/cu keyin
               would be 962.

         cu
               is the name of control unit.

         state
               is the state of the path. It may be UP, DN, or SU (suspended). A suspended path is
               an UP path that is currently not available for general use.

11.54.3. Examples
         Example 1

         PATHS

         Displays all paths, along with their state, in the system using one device for each
         subsystem.

              FICON    1 TO DLM300 FOR IOP35       IS I35C50/03:11:1D:00/DLMB3E UP
              FICON    2 TO DLM300 FOR IOP21       IS I21C50/03:11:1D:00/DLMB3B DN
              FICON    3 TO DLM300 FOR IOP31       IS I31C50/03:0F:18:00/DLMB3B UP
              FICON    4 TO DLM300 FOR IOP32       IS I32C00/03:0F:18:00/DLMB3C UP
              FICON    5 TO DLM300 FOR IOP21       IS I21C00/03:0F:18:00/DLMB3A DN
              FICON    6 TO DLM300 FOR IOP30       IS I30C50/03:0F:18:00/DLMB3A DN
              FICON    7 TO DLM300 FOR IOP22       IS I22C10/03:11:1D:00/DLMB3D DN
              FICON    8 TO DLM300 FOR IOP32       IS I32C10/03:11:1D:00/DLMB3D SU

              FCSCSI   1 TO D4A000 FOR IOP01       IS I01C10/     03:D2:00/D4B        UP
              FCSCSI   2 TO D4A000 FOR IOP02       IS I02C10/     0A:1D:00/D4C        UP
              FCSCSI   3 TO D4A000 FOR IOP03       IS I03C10/     05:18:00/D4D        UP
              FCSCSI   4 TO D4A000 FOR IOP00       IS I00C00/     0B:F1:00/D4A        UP




7831 0281–034                                                                                11–141
Keyin Reference




         Example 2

           PATHS D4A001

         Displays all paths (along with their state) to D4A001.

           FCSCSI    1 TO D4A001 FOR IOP01       IS I01C10/       03:D2:00/D4B    UP
           FCSCSI    2 TO D4A001 FOR IOP02       IS I02C10/       0A:1D:00/D4C    UP
           FCSCSI    3 TO D4A001 FOR IOP03       IS I03C10/       05:18:00/D4D    UP
           FCSCSI    4 TO D4A001 FOR IOP00       IS I00C00/       0B:F1:00/D4A    UP


11.55. PAUSEIO—Interrupt I/O to Mass Storage
         The PAUSEIO keyin temporarily halts all I/O to mass storage. The interruption provides
         a brief quiet period in which to split TimeFinder Business Continuance Volumes (BCV)
         from their associated standard volumes. Once the split process is complete, I/O is
         allowed to resume. The interruption normally lasts less than a minute.

         More information about TimeFinder, BCVs, and PAUSEIO is available in the
         Exec System Software Administration Reference Manual (7831 0323).

11.55.1. Format
           PAUSEIO

             Halts all I/O to mass storage until a DONE or ABORT response is entered.

11.55.2. System Response
         The response to the PAUSEIO keyin is

            All disk I/O is paused at yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm:ss zone
           0-To resume all disk I/O when done, enter DONE, else ABORT

         where:

         yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm:ss zone

             is the time at which the PAUSEIO keyin was entered. The zone field contains the
             configured time zone mnemonic.

             Note: If Data Integrity Verification (DIV) is active when a PAUSEIO keyin is
             entered, the system can automatically adjust the data_verification_interval to a
             large value after the operator answers DONE to resume all disk I/O. The amount
             of the adjustment depends on the length of time the PAUSEIO keyin was in
             effect. You can need to reset the DIV interval to its original value, using dynamic
             Exec COMUS configuration of that parameter. See Section 16 of the Exec
             System Software Administration Reference Manual (7831 0323) for more
             information.




11–142                                                                           7831 0281–034
                                                                               Keyin Reference




         This keyin is performed under the control of either an Operations Sentinel script or the
         system administrator, normally a script. Once it has issued the PAUSEIO, the
         Operations Sentinel script is designed to wait for messages from EMC Symmetrix
         TimeFinder and respond to them by issuing a DONE or ABORT keyin. The system then
         responds with the following console message:

           All disk I/O was resumed at yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm:ss zone, response = <DONE
           | ABORT>

         where:

         yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm:ss zone
             is the time at which the script issued the DONE or ABORT response. The zone
             field contains the configured time zone mnemonic.

         <DONE | ABORT>
             specifies which of the two keyins was issued. DONE indicates that the split
             completed successfully; ABORT indicates that the configured time allowed for the
             I/O interruption elapsed before the split completed.


11.56. PM—Designate a Tape Unit as Premount Only
         The PM keyin designates a tape unit as a premount-only unit, on systems with the
         Tape Automatic Volume Recognition (TAVR) feature installed.

11.56.1. Formats
         Specified Device

           PM devnam

             Places the specified device in premount-only mode.

         All Devices on a Control Unit

           PM,ALL cu

                Places all devices attached to the specified control unit in premount-only mode.




7831 0281–034                                                                               11–143
Keyin Reference




11.56.2. Restrictions
         The TAVR feature must be installed before you can use the PM keyin. If you try to use
         it when the feature is not installed, you receive this message:

           PM KEYIN (TAVR) FEATURE NOT INSTALLED

         Only tape units can be designated as premount-only. A unit designated for
         premount-only can be used only when the unit is premounted or aftermounted with
         the requested reel, or when it is a pool unit of that user. One exception to this is an
         absolute device or control unit assignment, which overrides the premount-only
         designation.


11.57.PR—Remove Premount-Only Designation
         The PR keyin removes the premount-only designation of tape units on systems with
         the Tape Automatic Volume Recognition (TAVR) feature installed. The premount-only
         condition is maintained across disk recovery boots. The keyin causes runs held for this
         type of unit to be removed from the hold queue.

11.57.1. Formats
         Specified Device

           PR devnam

             Removes the premount-only designation on the specified device .

         All Devices on a Control Unit

           PR,ALL cu

             Removes the premount-only designation on all devices attached to the specified
             control unit.

11.57.2. System Response
         The response to the PR keyin is
           tape state [NA] [* RUNID run-id REEL [reel-#] ring [POS]]

         where:
         tape
             is the name of the tape drive.

         state
             is the tape drive’s status (UP, DN, or RV).




11–144                                                                             7831 0281–034
                                                                             Keyin Reference




         NA
              indicates that the drive is not available.

         *
              indicates that the drive is assigned by a run.

         run-id
              is the name of the run with the drive assigned.

         reel-#
              is the number of the reel mounted on the drive.

         ring
              is either RING or NORING, if specified on assignment.

         POS
              indicates that a tape is currently mounted on the drive.


11.58. PURGE—Remove Residue Data from
       the XPC
         The PURGE keyin removes data that has been left in the XPC (typically after testing or
         after a host is removed from the XTC environment). The keyin makes it unnecessary
         to reboot the host and destage the data.

11.58.1. Format
             PURGE xpcname/hostid

         where:
         xpcname
              is the logical name of the XPC.

         hostid
              is the host-ID: A, B, C, and so on.

         Restrictions
         This keyin cannot be used on a host with a status of UP in the XTC environment. The
         host must be DN before the keyin can be used. An XPC-L device does not support this
         keyin.




7831 0281–034                                                                            11–145
Keyin Reference




11.59. RC—Display Information on a Specific Run
         The RC keyin displays specific information on backlog runs, active runs, and runs
         terminated with output files remaining.

11.59.1. Format
             RC[,option] run-id

              where:
          option
              L              If run-id is a batch run, displays the run origin information.
                             There is no effect for other types of runs.

11.59.2. System Response
         Backlogged or Active
         This response indicates that the specified run is backlogged or active:
             run-id,RP account [site-id] state
             mode TIME=hhhh:mm:ss/hhhh:mm:ss PAGES=nnn
             CARDS=nnn SIZE=xxx T(DL)=hh:mm
             PROJECT=project-id BL TIME=hh:mm:ss or PROGRAM=name
             USERID = [user-id ] [AG=appl-num]
             ORIGINAL RUN-ID = run-id OPENED yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm:ss zone COND = status
             ORIG: dir-id#qualifier*element(F-cycle).[element(S-cycle)]

         where:

         run-id
              is the run-ID from the keyin.

         R
              is the run priority of the run (A to Z or 1 to 5). For demand runs, priority equals 0.
              For batch runs, if the run was removed by the RM run-id keyin, priority equals 0.

         P
              is the processor dispatching priority (A through Z).

         account
              is the account number charged for the run.

         site-id
              is the user’s site-ID for demand runs; blank for batch runs.




11–146                                                                                 7831 0281–034
                                                                                    Keyin Reference




         state
            is the state of the run, as follows:

            [BL/OPEN]/FACIL/MASS             The run is waiting for a mass storage I/O facility.
            [BL/OPEN]/FACIL/OTHER            The run is waiting for an I/O facility other than tape or
                                             mass storage.
            [BL/OPEN]/FACIL/TAPE             The run is waiting for a tape I/O facility.
            BL/FINCRD                        The @FIN statement has not yet been read by the input
                                             device.
            BL/OPER                          The run is held because of the CS H run-ID keyin.
            BL/SOPT/name                     The @RUN control statement has an S option. This run is
                                             held until the previous run (whose run-ID is name) from
                                             the same input device has finished.
            BL/T(ST)/hh:mm                   The run is held until the start time specified by hh:mm.
            BL/UNOPND                        The run is backlogged and is not being held.
            OPEN/FACIL/CTL                   The run has an outstanding CTL request.
            OPEN/FACIL/TSWAP                 The run is held because a CTL is in use and a tape swap
                                             has occurred. If you need to resolve this TSWAP hold
                                             condition quickly instead of letting the run wait for a
                                             compatible drive to become available, either bring up
                                             another compatible drive or move the tape to the drive
                                             that is currently allocated for the run.
            OPEN/INCOR                       The run is in main storage.
            OPEN/SUSPENDED                   The run is suspended.

         mode
            is the mode used: R/T (real time), DEMAND, DEDLIN (deadline batch), or BATCH.

         TIME
            is the estimated run time for a backlogged run only. For an open run, it includes
            both the estimated and used time. For a privileged run (a run that will not abort
            due to a maximum time condition), the estimated time field is left empty.

         PAGES
            indicates both the estimated and used print output page count for all OPEN states;
            indicates only the estimated count for all other states.

         CARDS
            indicates both the estimated and used punch output card count for all OPEN
            states; indicates only the estimated count for all other states.




7831 0281–034                                                                                      11–147
Keyin Reference




         SIZE
            is the current size of the program being executed in thousands of words decimal;
            this field is present for all OPEN states. The size is the total size of all banks
            contained in the program and activity level BDTs.

         T(DL)
            indicates the time the run must start in order to be complete by its deadline time.

         PROJECT
            is the project-ID that was specified on the @RUN control statement.

         BL TIME
            is the time that the run went into backlog.

         PROGRAM
            is the last program loaded if the run is in a facilities hold or if the control card of a
            run is still being processed; otherwise, signifies the currently executing program.
            If the program name cannot be found, displays:
                 NO PROGRAM YET LOADED

            The following message is displayed if no element was specified on the last
            statement to start a program load:
                 FILE NAME ONLY AVAILABLE

         USER-ID
            is the user-ID the run is running under. (If the run was started by an @START,U, the
            user-ID is not necessarily the same as that of the user who opened the run.)

         AG=appl-num

            The application number displayed by this keyin identifies the Integrated Recovery
            application group in which the program is executing.

         ORIGINAL RUN-ID
            is the original run-ID of the run specified on the RC keyin. If the run is still in
            backlog, this is the only field on the sixth line of RC keyin display.

         OPENED
            gives the date and time at which the run specified on the RC keyin opened. The
            zone field contains the configured time zone mnemonic. This field is not displayed
            if the run is still in backlog.




11–148                                                                                 7831 0281–034
                                                                              Keyin Reference




         COND
             is the status or condition of the run at this point, that is, whether the run has
             encountered an error so far. The possible conditions are NORMAL, ERROR,
             ABORT, and FAC ERROR. These have the same meanings as the identical terms on
             the run-id FIN message when the run ends.
             This field is not displayed if the run is still in backlog.

         ORIG
             is the file or element from which batch run originated

         Finished or Never in the System
         This response indicates the specified run was never in the system or has finished
         printing:

           run-id NOT FOUND

         Finished
         This response indicates that the specified run has finished, and the queued print and
         punch files are displayed as described for the SQ run-ID * R keyin:

           run-id HAS FINNED
           run-id: file xx EPGS priority rank/sname
           run-id: file xx CARDS priority rank/sname
           run-id: file TAPE priority rank/sname

         Held
         This response indicates that the specified run has been selected for processing, but is
         being held because the run’s initial facility requests cannot be satisfied at this time:
           run-id - INITIAL FACILITY HOLD

         This response indicates that the specified run is being held briefly for an automated
         cartridge system (ACS) query:
           RUN HELD FOR:
            TAPE ASSIGN, AUTOMATED CARTRIDGE SYSTEM (ASC) QUERY FOR REEL-ID

         This response indicates why the run is being held:
           Network device assign, unit not available device = devnam




7831 0281–034                                                                              11–149
Keyin Reference




11.59.3. Examples
         Example 1
           RC JJPA

            Displays the following information for the run JJPA:
              JJPA,0 5CH091 [RA1402] OPEN/INCOR
              DEMAND TIME = 16:39:00/00:00:00 PAGES = 50000/3
              CARDS = 10000/0 SIZE = 51
              PROJECT = YUR$KP PROGRAM = FURPUR
              USERID = [JPARKS ]
              ORIGINAL RUN-ID = JJP OPENED 2000-07-14 16:06:53 CDT COND = NORMAL

         Example 2
           RC MAPPER

            Displays the following information for the MAPPER run, which is a real-time run:
              MAPPER,A CSFMAP OPEN/INCOR
              R/T TIME = 72:47:04/26:47:18 PAGES = 50000/979
              CARDS = 262000/0 SIZE = 776
              PROJECT = CSFMDB PROGRAM = MAPPER
              USERID = [CSF1 ]
              ORIGINAL RUN-ID = MAPPER OPENED 2000-01-12 03:06:53 CDT COND = NORMAL

         Example 3
           RC DCR

            Displays the following information about the DCR run, which is a batch run:
              DCR ,A 005452 OPEN/INCOR
              BATCH TIME = 36:24:31/13:35:11 PAGES = 50000/16
              CARDS = 263000/0 SIZE = 91
              PROJECT = CED PROGRAM = DCR
              USERID = [SMITH ]
              ORIGINAL RUN-ID = DCR OPENED 2000-08-01 08:01:40 CDT COND = NORMAL

         Example 4
           RC GNN

            Displays the following message when GNN is not found:
              GNN NOT FOUND




11–150                                                                          7831 0281–034
                                                                              Keyin Reference




         Example 5
           RC GNAB

             Displays the following message if GNAB has finished:
                  GNAB HAS FINNED
                  GNAB: PR 2EPGS J 1ST/PR

         Example 6
           RC,L YE004

             Displays the following about the YE004 run, which is a batch run:
                  YE004 ,MM 164140     OPEN/INCOR
                  BATCH TIME = 16:40:00/ 00:00:08 PAGES = 50000/16
                  CARDS = 9999/0 SIZE = 36
                  PROJECT = ETP    PROGRAM = PMD
                  USERID = [RGW ]
                  ORIGINAL RUN-ID = YE004 OPENED 2007-12-31 13:10:28 CDT COND = NORMAL
                  ORIG: STD#ETP*SOURCE$(1).YE004(0)


11.60. RD—Remove Disk Packs from Pack List
         The RD keyin removes unassigned packs in the DN or PRESENT state from the list of
         packs. A typical use for this keyin is to remove packs to make room for other packs
         when the pack status table is full. The maximum number allowed in the table is
         defined by configuration parameters REQNBRPST0 and REQNBRPST1.

11.60.1. Format
           RD pack-id[/[dir-id],[pack-id]...]

         where:

         pack-id
             is the name of the pack.

         dir-id
             is the directory-ID supplied on the keyin, either STD or SHARED. STD is for a local
             pack, and SHARED is for a shared pack. The default is STD.




7831 0281–034                                                                              11–151
Keyin Reference




11.60.2. System Response
         When the RD keyin is successful, the pack is removed from the pack status table, and
         the following message is displayed:

           pack-id REMOVED

         where pack-id is the name of the pack.

         An RD keyin is not allowed on a pack in the UP state. The RD keyin is rejected, and the
         following message displayed:

           RD OF PACK pack-id FAILED - pack-id IS UP

         An RD keyin is not allowed on a pack that does not exist in the system list of UP, DN,
         or PRESENT packs (that is, its status is NOT PRESENT). The RD keyin is rejected, and
         the following message displayed:

           RD OF PACK pack-id FAILED - pack-id IS NOT PRESENT

         The following error message is displayed if you attempt any of the preceding keyins
         for packs mounted on unit-duplexed devices:

           keyin KEYIN NOT ALLOWED - PACK pack-id MOUNTED ON UNIT DUPLEXED DEVICE.

         where:

         keyin
            is the keyin entered.

         pack-id
            is the pack-ID specified on the keyin.

11.60.3. Restrictions
         You cannot use the RD keyin on a unit-duplexed device.

         To use the RD keyin in a local environment, the NEWCON configuration parameter
         REQNBRPST0 must be greater than 0. To use the RD keyin in a shared environment,
         the NEWCON configuration parameter REQNBRPST1 must be greater than 0. See the
         Exec System Software Installation and Configuration Guide (7830 7915) for more
         information on these configuration parameters.

11.60.4. Example
           RD DKSF1

            Removes pack DKSF1 from the list of packs in the DN or PRESENT state. After the
            pack is removed, the following message is displayed:
                 DKSF1 REMOVED



11–152                                                                           7831 0281–034
                                                                                 Keyin Reference




11.61. RE—Resume a Run
         The RE keyin resumes a run that is suspended with the SP keyin.

11.61.1. Format
             RE[{B|D}U] ID1[,ID2,...,IDn]

         where:

         B

              specifies resume batch runs only.

         D

              specifies resume demand runs only. Both B and D cannot be specified.

         U

              indicates that the ID arguments are user IDs.

         One or more IDs can be specified, separated by commas and with or without masking.
         If the U option is not specified, the IDs refer to run IDs; if the U option is specified, the
         IDs refer to user IDs.

         For Examples, See SP keyin.

11.61.2. System Response
         If the keyin is accepted, the system responds as follows:
             run-id RESUMED yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm:ss zone

         If the keyin is not accepted, the system displays a message indicating why it was not
         accepted.

11.61.3. Restrictions
         Remote demand console users must have the FULL, DISPLAY, or RESPONSE console
         capability to use the RE keyin.




7831 0281–034                                                                                  11–153
Keyin Reference




11.62. RL—Common Bank Reload
         The RL keyin reloads a common bank from a library file. Use it only at the direction of
         your site administrator.
         For more information on common banks, refer to the Exec System Software
         Common Banks Programming Guide (7830 7386).

11.62.1. Format
              RL bankname

         where bankname is the name of the bank. The name is defined when the common
         bank is created.

11.62.2. System Response
         When you enter an RL keyin, the system responds with an RLOAD message as
         follows (if the message is repeated after several minutes, the system has exceeded
         the time limit for reloading the common bank):
              0-RLOAD bankname IEST

         or
              0-RLOAD bankname FROM FILE filename IEST

         Respond by entering an I, E, S, or T to designate the type of reload, as follows:

         I        Immediate request. Current users are not notified.
         E        Error request. Users are given contingency.
         S        Stall request. A stall request waits for all users of all common banks currently in use
                  to finish using the common banks. During the waiting period, all new attempts to
                  access the common bank to be reloaded are queued. In a busy system, the stall (wait)
                  period is likely to expire before reaching the point where the common bank can be
                  reloaded. If the wait period expires, you are notified. You can then decide whether to
                  repeat or terminate the load request.
         T        Terminate the reload request.

         During processing of the keyin, the system displays an information message that
         gives the status of the reload.
         If the reload is successful, the system displays this confirming message:
              RLOAD bankname COMPLETED

         The System Console Messages Reference Manual (7833 3275) lists the common bank
         error messages and the corrective action to take. All common bank error messages
         contain the symbol -#-, where # is an integer between 0 and 9 that helps you to
         determine which lines of a message belong together when other console messages
         appear between the lines of common bank messages.




11–154                                                                                   7831 0281–034
                                                                               Keyin Reference




         For example
             COMMON BANK bank-name
             -2- RELOAD ERROR.
             -2- ALTERNATE FILE COMMON BANK STALL RELOAD NOT ALLOWED.

         If there is more than one common bank on the system with the name specified on the
         RL keyin, the system responds with a message that solicits the name of the file from
         which the common bank is to be loaded. When you respond, provide both the qualifier
         and file name. If more than one file with the same qualifier and file name exists, you
         must specify the file cycle (F-cycle). If the file name is unknown, enter B. This
         response allows the system to continue processing reload requests.

11.62.3. Restrictions
         You cannot use the RL keyin to reload an alternate file common bank (AFCB) unless
         the bank has already been reloaded or based by a program. If you attempt to reload an
         AFCB with this keyin, using the I response for an immediate reload, and the old and
         new copies of the AFCB are of different execution modes, the keyin is rejected, the
         AFCB is not reloaded, and this message is displayed on the console:
             RLOAD bankname MODE ERROR

         For an uninstalled NCCB, the response would be
             bankname NOT FOUND


11.63. RM—Remove a Run from the Backlog Queue
         The RM keyin is used to remove runs from the backlog queue.

11.63.1. Format
             RM[,ALL|NU] ID1[,ID2,...IDn]

         where:

         ALL

               specifies to remove all runs held in the backlog. Other options and IDs cannot be
               specified with this option.

         U

               indicates that the ID arguments are user IDs.

         N

               indicates to negate any mask that is remove all runs except those specified in the
               ID fields.




7831 0281–034                                                                              11–155
Keyin Reference




         One or more IDs can be specified, separated by commas and with or without masking.
         If the U option is not specified, the IDs refer to run IDs; if the U option is specified, the
         IDs refer to user IDs.

         Examples:

         RM,ALL                    removes all runs held in the backlog
         RM,ALL R3                 invalid keyin
         RM R1,RX?A,RV*            removes run R1, all four-character runs that start with RX and end
                                   with A, and all runs that begin with RV.

11.63.2. System Response
         When the run is removed, it is opened and then terminates in error. The following
         message is printed in the run’s output listing:
           OPERATOR REMOVED RUN

         If the run is in a sequencing hold (S option on the @RUN statement), the run is not
         removed from backlog until the preceding run from the same device has ended.

         If the run is removed, the system displays messages in this format:
           run-id START
           run-id ERROR FIN

         Already Executing
         If the run is already executing, the system displays a message in this format:
           run-id ACTIVE - NOT REMOVED

             In this case, use an E or X keyin to terminate the run.

         Finished
         If the run has finished and output is queued, the system displays a message in this
         format:
           run-id FINNED - NOT REMOVED

         Finished or Never in the System
         If a run has finished or was never in the system, the system displays a message in this
         format:
           run-id NOT FOUND

         Scheduling Error
         If a scheduling error has occurred and the run cannot be removed, the system displays
         a message in this format:
           run-id INTERNAL ERROR - KEYIN IGNORED




11–156                                                                                 7831 0281–034
                                                                                Keyin Reference




11.63.3. Example
              RM CJM321

               Removes the CJM321 run. The system displays the messages
                CJM321 START
                CJM321 ERROR FIN


11.64. RP—Remove Pack
         The RP keyin removes an unassigned removable pack’s file information from the
         master file directory. After using this keyin, the pack must be reregistered (see the FA
         keyin) before files on the pack can be accessed.

11.64.1. Format
              RP pack-id[/dir-id]

         or
              RP pack-id

         where:
         pack-id
               is the name of the pack.

         dir-id
               is the directory-ID supplied on the keyin, either STD or SHARED. STD is for a local
               pack, and SHARED is for a shared pack. The default is STD.

11.64.2. System Response
         After the keyin, the system displays one of the following messages:

              PACK pack-id number FILES REMOVED

              dir-id PACK pack-id number FILES REMOVED

               Shows the number of removed files. The variable number indicates the number of
               files.

              PACK pack-id NO FILES CATALOGED

              dir-id PACK pack-id NO FILES CATALOGED

               Informs you that the pack is registered but contains no cataloged files or contains
               only multipack files.




7831 0281–034                                                                                11–157
Keyin Reference




             PACK pack-id NOT CURRENTLY REGISTERED

             dir-id PACK pack-id NOT CURRENTLY REGISTERED

              Informs you that the pack has not been previously registered.


11.65. RS—Restart a Run
         The RS keyin restarts a run after a CK keyin. See also

             The System Console Messages Reference Manual (7833 3275) for information
              about the messages displayed on the console during a checkpoint/restart
              procedure
             The ClearPath OS 2200 Software Products Installation Guide (7831 0612) for
              additional information on the CKRS product

11.65.1. Format
             RS [,priority/options/pdp] run-id, [account-nbr][/ user-id], [project-id],
             filename, [checkpoint number, [equipment,] reel/[reel]]

         where:

         priority
              is the scheduling priority of the run.

         options
              can include any of the following. You can use more than one option.

              J        Specifies the checkpoint tape is unlabeled.
              N        Specifies do not print diagnostics in the run’s print file.
              P        Prints diagnostics on the console. This should always be used with the RS keyin.
              Q        Causes the full restart common bank to put diagnostic snapshots in the run’s
                       print file. This is for debugging purposes.
              R        Causes the saved symbiont files to be restored. This is useful when a system
                       crash causes a loss of these files.
              U        Specifies that CKRS files should not be verified after a restart. Use this option to
                       bypass file verification if the CKRS_FILE_VERIFY parameter is set to TRUE. If both
                       the U and V options are used on the restart request, the U option overrides the V
                       option.
              V        Specifies that CKRS files should be verified after a restart. Use this option to
                       force file verification if the CKRS_FILE_VERIFY parameter is set to FALSE. If both
                       the U and V options are used on the restart request, the U option overrides the V
                       option.
              Y        Causes the restart initiation processor to put diagnostic snapshots in the run’s
                       print file. This is for debugging purposes.




11–158                                                                                     7831 0281–034
                                                                                    Keyin Reference




               Z        Causes the partial restart common bank to put diagnostic snapshots in the run’s
                        print file. This is for debugging purposes.

         pdp
               is the processor dispatching priority

         run-id
               specifies the run-ID to be used for the restart run. This run- ID must be the same
               as the generated run- ID of the checkpointed run.

         account-nbr
               specifies the account number of the user- ID for the restarted run.

         user-id
               is required when user- ID s are configured or the terminal security system is
               configured.

         project-id
               is the project- ID for the restarted run. The default is Q$Q$Q$.

         filename
               is the file name that contains the checkpoint.

         checkpoint number
               is the checkpoint number to be restarted. This must be specified if the checkpoint
               is on tape.

         equipment
               is the equipment type on which the checkpoint tape is mounted. The default is T.

         reel/reel
               is the reel number of the tape on which the checkpoint is written.

11.65.2. System Response
         The user-ID and clearance level are required for runs restarted with the RS keyin. After
         the system has accepted the keyin, the following message is displayed at the system
         console:

           ENTER CLEARANCE LEVEL FOR : run-id

         where run-id is the one specified on the RS keyin.




7831 0281–034                                                                                    11–159
Keyin Reference




         The clearance level is optional. If you do not enter a clearance level, the minimum
         clearance level for your user-ID is assumed. If the supplied user- ID or clearance level
         is not valid, the system displays the following message:

           run-id:INVALID CLEARANCE LEVEL


11.66. RV—Reserve a Component
         The RV keyin reserves a component. Reserved components are available for use only
         by users that request them by name, such as online maintenance.

11.66.1. Formats
         The following keyins specify a particular component:

           RV ip

             Reserves the specified instruction processor for dedicated activities.

           RV mmod

             Reserves the specified memory module for dedicated activities.

           RV devnam

             Reserves the specified device for a particular assignment.

         The following keyin specifies all devices for a control unit:

           RV,ALL cu

             Reserves all devices attached to the specified control unit for specific
             assignments.

11.66.2. Restrictions
         XIIPs and XPCs cannot be reserved.

11.66.3. Example
           RV,ALL CUTAPD

             Reserves all devices attached to CUTAPD. These lines are displayed:
              CUTAPD UP
              TAPED3 RV,TAPED4 RV




11–160                                                                             7831 0281–034
                                                                                 Keyin Reference




11.67. SB—Control SIP Performance Data
       Collection
         The SB keyin controls the collection of performance data with the Software
         Instrumentation Package (SIP) and I/O trace, including

             Initiating and terminating SIP and I/O trace data collection, either together or
              separately
             Controlling the specific functional areas of SIP that are to be collected
             Controlling the composition of the SIP output file
             Inhibiting the freeing of SIP and I/O trace tape files when data collection
              terminates
             Displaying the current status of SIP and I/O trace on the system console
             Specifying device names for SIP and I/O trace tape files

         Use this keyin only under the direction of your site administrator.


                                                 Caution
                  The duration of a SIP data collection session should not exceed 24 hours.
                  The validity of the data cannot be guaranteed after 24 hours because
                  certain counters can overflow. The counters are cleared when you turn SIP
                  off (SB OFF); you can then immediately turn SIP on again (SB ON).



11.67.1. Format 1: Initiating SIP ON Mode
             SB ON

         This format initiates SIP ON mode, which starts SIP data collection unconditionally. To
         prevent data overflow, enter the SB OFF keyin within 24 hours after entering SB ON.

         A SIP data collection can be one of two modes—ON or AUTO. These modes are
         initiated by the SB ON and SB AUTON keyins. For these keyins, if I/O trace is
         configured (SIPIO=TRUE), I/O trace data collection in the ON mode unconditionally
         initiates unless the SB NOIOT keyin is in effect. The OFF keyin terminates the SIP data
         collection.

         The following rules apply to ON processing:

             If the SIP output file is configured to mass storage (SBFILE=0), the mass storage
              file is created except in the following instances:
                   Timed output of SIP data is not configured (CONTIM=0 and SBTIMEON=0).
                   Timed output is configured (SBTIMEON>0), but the output time interval is
                    configured as zero (CONTIM=0) and has not been altered by the SB Tx keyin.




7831 0281–034                                                                                    11–161
Keyin Reference




                Timed output is configured, and the output time interval has been set to zero
                 by the SB T0 keyin.
            If the SIP data file is not created during SIP initialization, it is created prior to the
             first write of a data block to the file. This data block can be one of the following
             types:
                Time interval block
                 This type of data block is written to the file if the output time interval is
                 changed to a nonzero value.
                Run termination block
                 This type of data block is written to the file if it is configured (SBRUN>0) and
                 not inhibited by the SB RUNOFF keyin.
                Summary block
                 This type of data block is written to the file at SIP termination. The following
                 statements create the SIP data file on mass storage:
                  @CAT,PZ SYSBAL$LOG$(+1),F2/1/POS/n
                  @ASG,AZ SYSBAL$LOG$

                 where n is the value of the configuration parameter SIP_FILE_MAX_SIZE
                 (default value is 10).
                 Note: When SENTRY_CONTROL=TRUE, the SYSBAL$LOG$ file is not
                 cataloged as public; instead, the ACR ACRDIA is attached to the file.

            If the SIP output file is configured to tape (SBFILE=1), the tape file is assigned
             during initialization except in the following instances:
                Timed output of SIP data is not configured.
                Timed output is configured, but the output time interval was configured as
                 zero and has not been altered.
                Timed output is configured, but the output time interval has been set to zero.
                The SIP output file is currently assigned.
                 If the file is not assigned at the time of the first data block write, the assign
                 takes place at that time. The following statement creates the SIP data file on
                 tape:
                    @ASG,TZ SYSBAL$LOG$,xxx

                 where xxx is the value of the system standard equipment type for tape
                 assignments.
            Data collection is initiated for the SIP functional areas not inhibited by the SB
             FLGOF keyin (except IO+). It continues until terminated by the SB OFF keyin.
            If the SIP file was assigned, it remains assigned throughout the collection unless
             both run termination and time interval output are disabled. When this occurs, a
             summary block is written and the SIP file is freed. Whenever the file is not
             assigned and a data block write is initiated, the assignment is made.




11–162                                                                                   7831 0281–034
                                                                                 Keyin Reference




             If the I/O trace feature is configured, I/O trace data collection in the ON mode is
              initiated, unless the SB NOIOT keyin is currently in effect or an I/O trace data
              collection is currently in progress.

11.67.2. Format 2: Initiating SIP AUTO Mode
             SB AUTON [x] or SB AUTON T[y]

         This format initiates SIP AUTO mode, where x can contain up to six decimal digits, and
         y can contain up to five decimal digits.

         In this mode, SIP data collection takes place only when the number of open runs in the
         system is equal to or greater than a specified threshold. When the first form (x) is
         used, the threshold value is set to the total number of runs open at the time of the SB
         AUTON keyin, plus x. If x is blank, it is assigned a value of 0. When the second form
         (T[y]) is used, the threshold value is set to y. If y is blank, it is assigned a value of 1.

         The first data collection begins when a run opens and the number of open runs in the
         system is equal to or greater than the threshold value. The following message is
         displayed:

             SYSTEM BALANCE: ON yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm:ss zone

         When an AUTO mode data collection is in progress and the number of runs drops
         below the threshold, SIP data collection terminates and the following message is
         displayed:

             SYSTEM BALANCE: OFF yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm:ss zone

         When the number of open runs again meets the threshold value, a data collection is
         initiated and the SYSTEM BALANCE: ON yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm:ss zone message is
         displayed.

         The SIP data space is never reinitialized while AUTO mode remains in effect. That is,
         the SIP data always contains the cumulative values from all collection intervals during
         the AUTO session.

         Each AUTO mode data collection creates an individual SIP data file F-cycle when SIP is
         configured to mass storage. When configured to tape, a summary block is written at
         the end of each AUTO session, and the same reel remains mounted until filled or SIP is
         turned off.

         I/O trace data collection in AUTO mode is continuous and is initiated in the same
         manner as in ON mode.

         AUTO mode remains in effect until terminated by the SB OFF keyin.




7831 0281–034                                                                                 11–163
Keyin Reference




11.67.3. Format 3: Terminating ON and AUTO Modes
             SB OFF

         This format terminates ON and AUTO modes.


                                                 Caution
               The duration of a SIP data collection session should not exceed 24 hours.
               The validity of the data cannot be guaranteed after 24 hours because
               certain counters can overflow. The counters are cleared when you turn SIP
               off (SB OFF); you can then immediately turn SIP on again (SB ON).



         When an SB OFF keyin is entered with SIP in the ON or AUTO mode, that mode is
         terminated and the following events occur:
             If SIP data collection is in progress, it terminates and a summary block is written to
              the SIP data file.
             If the SIP file is configured to mass storage, it is freed and the following message
              displayed:
                SIPFILE FREE

             If the SIP file is configured to tape, the tape is freed unless an SB NOFREE keyin is
              in effect. The following message is displayed:
                SIPFILE FREE

             If I/O trace is in the ON mode, I/O trace data collection is terminated (see SB IOFF
              keyin). The following message is displayed:
                IOTRACE FILE SYS$*IODATFILE FREE


11.67.4. Format 4: Controlling I/O Trace Data Collection
             SB { IOMAN | IOFF }

         This format initiates and terminates I/O trace data collection independent of SIP data
         collection. These keyins are invalid if I/O trace is not configured.

         If I/O trace is configured to mass storage (IOTRACE_FILE_MAX_SIZE > 0) and the file is
         not assigned at the time the keyin is entered, the following statements are performed:

             @CAT,PSZ IODATFILE(+1),F2/n/POS/n
             @ASG,AZ IODATFILE.

         where n is the value of the configuration parameter IOTRACE_FILE_MAX_SIZE.

         I/O trace data collection is always initiated unless an I/O trace is in progress. MANUAL
         mode continues until terminated by the SB IOFF keyin.




11–164                                                                              7831 0281–034
                                                                               Keyin Reference




         If I/O trace is in ON mode when the IOMAN SB keyin is entered, the SB IOMAN keyin
         places I/O trace in MANUAL mode. When I/O trace is in MANUAL mode, the SB IOFF
         keyin is honored, but the SB OFF keyin has no effect upon I/O trace.

         SB IOFF terminates the MANUAL mode of I/O trace data collection. When this keyin is
         entered and I/O trace is in MANUAL mode, the data collection terminates. If writing to
         tape, the tape file is freed unless the SB NOFREE keyin is in effect. If writing to mass
         storage, the file is freed regardless of the SB NOFREE keyin. If I/O trace is not in
         MANUAL mode, no action is taken.

         The following keyins are used to exercise control over a current or future data
         collection or to display information about the current status of SIP and I/O trace.

11.67.5. Format 5: Controlling I/O Trace Data Collection in ON
         or AUTO Mode
           SB { NOIOT | IOT }

         This format inhibits and enables I/O trace data collection in ON or AUTO mode.
         Normally, both SIP and I/O trace data collection are initiated when an SB ON or SB
         AUTO keyin is entered.

         SB NOIOT inhibits I/O trace data collection in ON or AUTO mode; SB IOT reverses the
         effect of the NOIOT keyin and enables I/O trace data collection in ON or AUTO mode.

         These keyins can be entered whether or not SIP data collection is in progress, but they
         affect only subsequent data collections.

11.67.6. Format 6: Controlling the Freeing of SIP and I/O Trace
         Tape Files
           SB { NOFREE | FREE }

         This format inhibits and enables the freeing of the SIP and I/O trace tape files. These
         keyins have no effect upon the SIP data file or the I/O trace data file when they are not
         configured as tape files. These keyins are honored whenever the I/O trace or SIP
         output files are configured to tape (IOTFILMAXSIZ>0 or SBFILE=1).

         Normally, the SIP tape file is freed when ON or AUTO mode is terminated, and the I/O
         trace tape is freed when I/O trace data collection is terminated. When the SB NOFREE
         keyin is entered, however, these files are not freed as long as this keyin remains in
         effect, unless an I/O error or end-of-tape condition occurs. The use of the SB NOFREE
         keyin enables stacking of multiple files on one tape file.

         The SB FREE keyin negates the effect of the SB NOFREE keyin. In addition, if a SIP tape
         is assigned and SIP is not in ON or AUTO mode, the SIP file is freed. Likewise, if an I/O
         trace tape is assigned and an I/O trace data collection is not in progress, the I/O trace
         file is freed.




7831 0281–034                                                                                  11–165
Keyin Reference




11.67.7. Format 7: Controlling SIP Data Collection for
         Functional Areas
           SB { FLGOF | FLGON } flag

         This format inhibits and enables the collection of SIP data for a specific functional area
         or for all functional areas. These keyins are allowed only if a SIP data collection is not
         in progress when they are entered.

         Most of the SIP statistics are assigned to one of the 10 functional areas of SIP. Data
         collection for each of these areas is controlled by a specific SIP flag, which can be
         turned on and off by the SB FLGOF and SB FLGON keyins. To turn all flags on or off,
         enter ALL for flag. At system initialization, all flags default to on, except IO+ which is
         off. The flags are as follows:


                Flag                                           Area

          CAU             Processor activity
          EXP             EXPOOL
          FNC             Not used
          HM              Not used
          IO              I/O. Turning on IO only turns on IO. Turning off IO also turns off IO+.
          IO+             Controls the same statistics as the IO flag plus statistics for block
                          multiplexer devices. Turning on IO+ also turns on IO. Turning off IO+ only
                          turns off IO+ (IO remains on).
          MEM             Main storage use, program and bank loading activity
          MIS             System activity, interrupts, Executive requests and calls
          QTM             Switching activity
          RSP             Demand response times


         The Exec System Software Administration Reference Manual (7831 0323) shows the
         statistics summarized by SIP level and lists each SIP data item and the flag associated
         with it. Some low-overhead SIP statistics, such as the idle statistics, are not
         associated with any flag and are therefore collected whenever SIP is on, regardless of
         the state of the SIP flags.

11.67.8. Format 8: Altering the Interval of Writing Data Blocks
           SB T[x]

         When SIP time interval output is configured (SBTIMEON>0), this format alters the
         interval at which time interval data blocks are written to the SIP output file, where x is
         a one-digit to five-digit integer specifying the new interval size in seconds. If x is
         missing, an invalid keyin message is displayed. If x is 0, time interval output is turned
         off. This keyin is honored whether or not SIP is turned on; it remains in effect until
         altered by a subsequent keyin. The new interval size must be greater than or equal to
         0, and must be less than or equal to 34359.


11–166                                                                                     7831 0281–034
                                                                              Keyin Reference




11.67.9. Format 9: Controlling the Writing of Run Termination
         Data Blocks
           SB { RUNOFF | RUNON }

         SB RUNOFF and SB RUNON inhibit and enable the writing of run termination data
         blocks to the SIP file. These keyins are not valid when run termination blocks are not
         configured (SBRUN=0).

         When run termination blocks are configured, the writing of a SIP data block occurs and
         is initially enabled each time a run terminates. The writing of run termination data
         blocks can be inhibited by the SB RUNOFF keyin.

         The SB RUNON keyin reverses the effect of the SB RUNOFF keyin and enables the
         writing of run termination data blocks.

         These keyins are honored whether or not SIP data is being collected when they are
         entered.

11.67.10. Format 10: Generating a SIP and I/O Trace Status
          Display
           SB STATUS

         This format generates a display of the current status of SIP and I/O trace on the
         system console.

         The STATUS display has the following format (all flags shown in their default status):

           FLAG: MIS* IO * MEM* CAU* RSP* IO+
           FLAG: FNC* EXP* QTM* HM *
           SIP-status : [XPMON|XPMOFF]
            TIME : x
           IO TRACE IS trace-status

         where:
         FLAG:
             displays the status of each of the SIP flags listed in the description of SB FLGOF
             and SB FLGON. An asterisk (*) following a flag name indicates that the flag is
             enabled.
             Note: All flags except IO+ are enabled by default.

         SIP-status
             indicates the current status of SIP. SIP-status can be one of the following:

             AUTO T[y]       SIP is in AUTO mode
             ON              SIP is in ON mode
             OFF             SIP is off



7831 0281–034                                                                                11–167
Keyin Reference




         XPMON | XPMOFF
              indicates whether this host is allowed to collect XPC Performance Monitor data
              (XPMON) or is prevented from collecting it (XPMOFF). If neither XPMON nor
              XPMOFF is displayed, the SB XPMON|XPMOFF keyin was not issued on this host,
              and the host can collect.

         x
              displays the current time interval in seconds for the output of SIP time interval
              data blocks. This line is displayed when time interval data blocks are configured.

         trace-status
              displays the current status of I/O trace. This line is present on the STATUS display
              only when I/O trace is configured. One of the following values appears for
              trace-status:

              ON             I/O trace is on and was initiated by the SB ON or SB AUTON keyin.
              MANUAL         I/O trace is on and was initiated or set to MANUAL mode by the SB
                             IOMAN keyin.
              OFF            I/O trace is not on.

11.67.11. Format 11: Specifying a Device Name for a SIP Tape
          File
             SB SBDEV [ device ]

         This format specifies a logical device name for the SIP tape file, SYSBAL$LOG$. If
         device is not specified, then the system default type is used. The file (SYSBAL$LOG$)
         must be configured as a tape file.

         If an SB NOFREE keyin is in effect, then the tape device cannot be changed until an SB
         FREE keyin is entered. The device specified is saved until the SB FREE is entered, and
         the next time SYSBAL$LOG$ is assigned the specified device is used on the
         assignment statement.

11.67.12. Format 12: Specifying a Device Name for the I/O
          Trace Tape File
             SB IODEV [ device ]

         This format specifies a logical device name for the I/O trace tape file, IODATFILE. If
         device is not specified, then the system default type is used. The file (IODATFILE)
         must be configured as a tape file.

         If an SB NOFREE keyin is in effect, then the tape device cannot be changed until an SB
         FREE keyin is entered. The device specified is saved until the SB FREE is entered, and
         the next time IODATFILE is assigned the specified device is used on the assignment
         statement.




11–168                                                                                 7831 0281–034
                                                                               Keyin Reference




11.67.13. Format 13: Specifying Hosts on Which XPM Data May
          Be Collected
             SB { XPMON | XPMOFF }

         Controls collection of XPC Performance Monitor (XPM) data. The SB XPMON keyin
         enables XPM data to be collected on the host issuing the keyin. SB XPMOFF prevents
         the issuing host from collecting XPM data.

         In a multihost environment, you should issue SB XPMOFF on all hosts except the one
         designated to collect XPM data. On that host, issue SB XPMON. Both keyins should be
         completed before SIP data collection is turned on.

11.67.14. System Response
         When a valid SB keyin is entered, the Exec echoes the keyin by displaying the
         message
             SYSTEM BALANCE: text        yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm:ss zone

         where text contains the text of the keyin following the SB prefix, up to a maximum of
         13 characters.
         When you enter an invalid SB keyin, the system responds
             SYSTEM BALANCE: text IS INVALID        yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm:ss zone

         The keyin can be invalid because you did not enter it correctly or because the
         particular keyin pertains to a SIP feature that is not configured in the operating system.
         When SIP is not configured in the operating system, the response to any SB keyin is
             SB KEY ERROR

         SB keyins are processed sequentially. When you enter an SB keyin and the system
         does not echo the input, that keyin has been queued. When queuing occurs, it is
         generally because SIP is waiting for an operator action such as a tape mount or a reply
         to a console message.

11.67.15. Examples
         Following are examples of sequences of SB keyins that generate SIP or I/O trace data
         collections. These examples illustrate techniques for generating a variety of different
         types of data collections. Unless otherwise noted, the following assumptions are
         made about the configuration of SIP:

             Run termination and time interval blocks are configured.
             I/O trace is configured.

         Some of the effects of other configurations are noted in the examples.




7831 0281–034                                                                               11–169
Keyin Reference




         Example 1: SIP and I/O Trace Data Collection
              SB ON [1]
              .
              .
              .
              SB OFF [2]

         1.    Initiate SIP and I/O trace data collection. Both files are assigned. A SIP data block is
               written to the SIP file every x seconds, where x is the value of the configuration
               parameter CONTIM.
         2.    Terminate SIP and I/O trace data collection. A summary block is written to the SIP
               file. The SIP and I/O trace files are freed.

         Example 2: SIP Data Collection Based on the Number of Runs Open
              SB NOIOT [1]
              SB FLGOF MEM [2]
              SB AUTON T15 [3]
              .
              .
              .
              SB OFF [2]

         1.    Inhibit I/O trace data collection.
         2.    Disable the collection of main storage statistics.
         3.    Assign the SIP file. SIP data collection is initiated when the number of open runs
               becomes equal to or greater than 15; data collection terminates when the number
               of open runs becomes less than 15. SIP continues to go off and on, depending
               upon the number of runs open. If the SIP file is configured to mass storage, the file
               is freed when a data collection terminates, and a new cycle is assigned each time
               a data collection is initiated. If SIP is configured to tape, the tape file remains
               assigned throughout the AUTO session.
         4. If a SIP data collection is currently in progress, terminate it and write a summary
            block to the SIP file. The SIP file is freed.




11–170                                                                                 7831 0281–034
                                                                                   Keyin Reference




         Example 3: Two SIP Data Collections in One SIP File
         The following example shows two SIP data collections in one SIP file. The SIP file is
         configured to tape.

              SB NOIOT [1]
              SB RUNOFF [2]
              SB T60 [3]
              SB ON [4]
              SB NOFREE [5]
              SB OFF [6]
              .
              .
              .
              SB ON [7]

              .
              .
              .
              SB FREE [8]_
              SB OFF [9]

         1.    Inhibit I/O trace data collection.
         2.    Inhibit the writing of SIP run termination blocks.
         3.    Change the output time interval for the writing of SIP time interval blocks to 60
               seconds.
         4. Assign the SIP tape and initiate SIP data collection.
         5.    Inhibit the freeing of the SIP tape.
         6. Terminate SIP data collection and write a summary block to the SIP file.
         7.    Initiate SIP data collection.
         8. Enable the freeing of the SIP file at SIP termination.
         9. Terminate the SIP data collection, write a summary block to the SIP file, and free
            the file.

         Example 4: I/O Trace Data Collection
              SB IOMAN [1]
              .
              .
              .
              SB IOFF [2]

         1.    Assign the I/O trace file and initiate I/O trace data collection.
         2.    Terminate I/O trace data collection and free the file.




7831 0281–034                                                                                11–171
Keyin Reference




         Example 5: SIP and I/O Trace Data Collection with Independent
         Terminations
              SB ON [1]
              .
              .
              .
              SB IOMAN [2]
              SB IOFF [3]
              .
              .
              .
              SB OFF [4]

         1.    Assign the SIP and I/O trace files and initiate SIP and I/O trace data collection.
         2.    Enable MANUAL mode for I/O trace.
         3.    Terminate I/O trace data collection and free the I/O trace file.
         4. Terminate the SIP data collection, write a summary block to the SIP file, and free
            the file.

         Example 6: SIP Data Collection with a File Assigned for Summary Block
         Write Only
         The file created in this example is configured to tape and has a summary block as its
         only data block. This type of collection can be useful when the availability of tape units
         is a concern.

              SB NOIOT [1]
              SB RUNOFF [2]
              SB T0 [3]
              SB ON [4]
              .
              .
              .
              SB OFF [5]

         1.    Inhibit I/O trace data collection.
         2.    Inhibit the writing of run termination blocks.
         3.    Inhibit the writing of time interval blocks.
         4. Initiate SIP data collection. The SIP file is not assigned until a data block write is
            initiated.
         5.    Terminate SIP data collection. The SIP file is assigned and the summary block is
               written. The file is then freed.




11–172                                                                                 7831 0281–034
                                                                                Keyin Reference




         Example 7: SIP Data Collection with an Altered Collection Control Flag
              SB NOIOT [1]
              SB FLGOF ALL [2]
              SB FLGON CAU [3]
              SB FLGON IO+ [4]
              SB ON [5]
              SB STATUS [6]
              .
              .
              .

         1.    Inhibit I/O trace data collection.

         2.    Disable all collection control flags.

         3.    Enable processor activity statistics collection.

         4. Enable I/O and IO+ statistics collection.

         5.    Assign the SIP file and initiate SIP data collection.

         6. Display SIP status.

         7.    Terminate the SIP data collection, write a summary block to the SIP file, and free
               the file.

         8. Enable all collection control flags.
