11.68. SEC—Save or Restore Security Files on Tape
         The SEC keyin provides a single keyin procedure for saving and restoring all the
         security files. The files are saved and restored automatically in the following order:

         1.    SEC@USERID$

         2.    SMDTF$

         3.    SEC@ACR$

         4. ACCOUNT$R1

         5. SEC@ACCTINFO

         Note: The SC and SF keyins are no longer supported, and the SV and SR keyins can
         no longer be used with the security files.

         The following considerations apply:

              SEC,RESTORE is allowed after SYS FIN.

              SEC,SAVE writes a version 2 tape.

              SEC,RESTORE accepts only a version 2 tape when it is entered after SYS FIN.

11.68.1. Formats
         SEC SAVE
              SEC,SAVE [device,reel/reel,exp-days/mmspec,CTL-pool name]

               Saves all security files to tape.

         where:
         device
               can be an assign mnemonic, a tape device name preceded by an asterisk (*), or
               the name of an automated cartridge system (ACS).

         reel/reel
               specifies up to four reels. The field is optional.

         exp-days/mmspec
               specifies the default tape expiration and Media Manager specification.

         CTL-pool name
               specifies the cartridge tape library (CTL) pool to be used.




11–174                                                                              7831 0281–034
                                                                              Keyin Reference




         SEC RESTORE
             SEC,RESTORE [device,reel/reel]

         where:
         device
              can be an assign mnemonic, a tape device name preceded by an asterisk (*), or
              the name of an ACS.

         reel/reel
              specifies the SEC,SAVE tape or tapes. The field is optional on systems configured
              to allow reading of tapes assigned as BLANK.

         Note: All files saved using the SEC,SAVE keyin can be restored at reboot. See
         Section 8.

11.68.2. System Response
         If the Authentication and Session Initiation Subsystem (ASIS) is not active, the system
         responds to a SEC,RESTORE keyin by prompting you with the following message:
             SEC,RESTORE Do you really want to restore security file <file>? Y/N

         If N is the response, that file is skipped and the system prompts you about the next
         file on the tape.

         If the ASIS is active, the system responds to a SEC,RESTORE keyin by displaying the
         following message:
             The keyin to restore the SEC@USERID$ may be delayed while awaiting
             coordination with the Authentication Subsystem (ASIS)

         Then the system waits approximately 2 minutes until the ASIS responds. If the ASIS
         responds appropriately, the system performs the restore and the ASIS performs any
         necessary cleanup.

         If the ASIS does not respond appropriately, the system prompts you with the
         following message:
             The Authentication Subsystem (ASIS) has not yet authorized the SEC@USERID$
             update. Do you want to wait again, or terminate the ASIS? W or T

         If W is the response, the timeout period starts over.

         If T is the response, the system aborts the ASIS as follows:

             All pending authentications are returned to VALA as rejected.
             The system erases its knowledge of the ASIS’ domain, causing subsequent
              SERVER$GET and SERVER$PUT requests to be rejected, and resulting in an ASIS
              shutdown.




7831 0281–034                                                                             11–175
Keyin Reference




             The system reactivates any waiting SERVER$GET activity and returns "ASIS not
              registered" status.
             The system changes the ASIS state to "Temporarily Not Available".

         Contact the site security officer if you see the following message after using
         SEC,RESTORE to load all security files from a tape created with SEC,SAVE:
             SEC,RESTORE *ERROR* Tape block count error. reel-id.
             Restore terminated. Answer - Go.

         Contact the site security officer if you see the following message after using
         SEC,RESTORE to load all security files from a tape created with SF:
             BLOCK COUNT ERROR IN SECURITY SAVE TAPE - GO

         Note: If you restore ACCOUNT$R1 while runs are open, cataloged mass-storage
         quota values can be incorrect afterward.

11.68.3. Examples
             SEC,SAVE

              Saves all security files to a BLANK cartridge tape.

             SEC,SAVE HICL,S2998

              Saves all security files to tape S2998.

             SEC,SAVE HICL,,,POOL-A

              Saves all security files to a BLANK cartridge tape from CTL POOL-A.

             SEC,SAVE ,,7

              Saves all security files to a BLANK, labeled cartridge tape in which the label
              expires in 7 days.

             SEC,RESTORE HICL,S2998

              Restores all five security files from tape S2998.




11–176                                                                              7831 0281–034
                                                                               Keyin Reference




11.69. SJ—Set Jump Key
         The SJ keyin lets you set jump keys.

11.69.1. Formats
           SJ number1,number2,...numberN

            Sets the specified jump keys (1 to 36). See Section 3 for a list and description of
            the jump keys.

           SJ A[LL]

            Sets all jump keys (1 to 36).

           SJ O octal-number

            Sets the jump keys by their octal representation. (The bit pattern is right-justified
            and zero-filled if you use fewer than 12 digits.)

11.69.2. System Response
         After the system sets the jump keys requested, it displays the following message:

           JUMP KEYS SET: number1, number2,...numbern

         where number is the number of a jump key that is set.

         A jump key is not set until the system displays the above response at the system
         console.


11.70. SM—Control Symbiont Devices
         The SM keyin displays the status of symbiont devices and control symbiont operation.
         Symbiont devices include card readers, card punches, printers, and remote terminals.

11.70.1. Formats
           SM sname

            Displays the status of symbiont device sname.

           SM sname operation

            Controls the symbiont devices.
         where:
         sname
            is the symbiont device name.




7831 0281–034                                                                               11–177
Keyin Reference




         operation
             is the operation to be performed. If you do not specify an operation, the system
             displays the device status: suspended, locked, active, or inactive. You can use one
             of the following values:

             B            Displays the current print band cartridge-ID or allows dynamic changes of the
                          default cartridge-ID for the specified device.
             C            Displays the current page format for a printer or changes the current page
                          format for a printer.
             E            Creates an end-of-file (EOF), terminating the active symbiont file.
             I            Initiates an inactive onsite device; resumes onsite device operation;
                          simulates an attention interrupt.
             L            Locks out an onsite device.
             Q            Requeues the current file and locks out the device.
             R            Creates an EOF, removing the runstream that the input device is currently
                          reading.
             Raaa         Reprints or repunches aaa pages or cards, where aaa is a decimal number.
             R+aaa        Advances aaa pages or cards and then begins printing or punching, where
                          aaa is a decimal number.
             RALL         Reprints or repunches the entire file.
             S            Suspends device operation.
             T            Terminates the device with an EOF, losing the remainder of the file. The
                          device is locked out.

11.70.2. Operations
         The operations are described in detail in the following.

         B[AND]—Display Cartridge-ID
           SM sname B[AND]

         This operation displays the cartridge-ID of the print band or font currently on a printer.
         The printers in your system can use a variety of print bands, which you can change at
         a user’s request. Each print band has a cartridge-IDthat identifies it to the system.

         The system displays this message:
           sname CARTRIDGE-ID, id

         where id is the current 2-character cartridge-ID.




11–178                                                                                   7831 0281–034
                                                                               Keyin Reference




         B[AND]—Enter New Cartridge-ID
         Your system maintains a default print band for each printer. Whenever it processes a
         file, the system reads the current cartridge-ID and checks it against the default. If the
         two do not match, the system assumes that you installed the wrong cartridge and
         issues an error message.

         If you did install the wrong cartridge, replace it with the correct one. If you want to
         use the cartridge, however, use this SM keyin to enter the current cartridge-ID as the
         new system default:
             SM sname B[AND],id

         where id is a 2-character cartridge-ID (hexadecimal).

         The new cartridge-ID takes effect the next time the printer is inactive, and it is
         assumed for the printer specified until one of the following events occurs:

              You enter another SM B[AND] keyin for the device, specifying a new cartridge.
              You perform a system boot from tape.

         When you issue a valid SM B[AND] keyin, the new values overwrite the values issued
         by a previous SM B[AND] keyin. You cannot restore those previous values (other than
         the original configuration values) except by specifying them with a new keyin.

         C[HANGE]—Display Page Format
             SM sname C[HANGE]

         This operation displays the current page format of a printer.

         The response displayed depends on the type of printer represented by sname. For the
         0770 and 0776 impact printers, the system displays this message:
             sname PSIZ=psiz, TOP=top, BOT=bot, LPI=lpi

         where:

         sname
               is the printer name.

         psiz
               is the number of lines per page.

         top
               is the number of lines for the top margin.

         bot
               is the number of lines for the bottom margin.




7831 0281–034                                                                                 11–179
Keyin Reference




         lpi
                is the number of lines per inch.

         C[HANGE]—Change Page Format
         The C operation also changes the current page format for your printer. To change a
         parameter, include its new value in the appropriate position.

               SM sname C[HANGE],[siz,top,bot,lpi]

         where:

         sname
                is the name of the printer.

         siz
                is the number of lines per page. The maximum is 999.

         top
                is the number of lines for the top margin. The maximum is 999.

         bot
                is the number of lines for the bottom margin. The maximum is 999.

         lpi
                is the number of lines per inch (6, 8, or 12). The standard number of lines per inch
                is 8.

                Use an asterisk (*) to specify the system default value for any of the parameters.
                Your site coordinator must know the defaults. To skip a parameter (leave it as it
                is), leave the parameter field blank.

         C[HANGE]—Restore Defaults
               SM sname C[HANGE],DEF[AULT]

         or
               SM sname C[HANGE],*,*,*,* (9246, 0770, and 0776 impact printer)

         This operation restores all printer values to system standards.

         After the SM C[HANGE],siz,top,bot,lpi keyin is accepted, the new values specified
         become the controlling values for the device. The new values remain in effect until
         one of the following events occurs:

               An SM sname C[HANGE],siz,top,bot,lpi keyin specifying new values is issued for
                the device.
               An SM sname C[HANGE], DEF[AULT] keyin is issued for the device.



11–180                                                                                7831 0281–034
                                                                               Keyin Reference




             You perform a system boot from tape.

         E—Terminate an Active File
             SM sname E

         This operation terminates an active file. Use the E operation for on-site devices only.
         Use the E operation when the remaining part of the input or output file is not desired.
         A card reader bypasses the remainder of the card deck and processes the next card
         deck. If no more cards remain, the card reader becomes inactive. A printer or card
         punch bypasses the rest of the current file and then processes the next available file.

         I—Initiate or Resume Operation
             SM sname I

         This operation initiates or resumes operation on a symbiont device. The system
         initiates an inactive onsite device; resumes operation of an onsite device after a
         previous S, L, T, R (card reader), or Q operation; or simulates an attention interrupt
         (that is, the device status changes from stop to run).

         L—Lock Out
             SM sname L

         This operation locks out a symbiont device. If the device is inactive, it is locked out
         immediately. Otherwise, the device is locked out after the input file (card reader) or
         the output file (card punch or printer) finishes processing. Use the L operation only for
         onsite devices. You can resume device operation by using the SM keyin with the I
         operation specified.

         Q—Requeue
             SM sname Q

         This operation requeues files and lock out of a symbiont device. This operation has no
         meaning for card readers. Use this operation when abnormal printing or punching
         occurs due to a device malfunction. The complete file is reentered into the symbiont
         queue and rescheduled for output. When the system is printing a tape, the Q
         operation releases the print tape and reassigns it when a printer is available. The Q
         operation is used only for onsite devices. You can resume device operation by using
         the SM keyin and specifying the I operation.

         R—Reprint or Repunch on Input Device
             SM sname R

         Specifies reprinting or repunching on an input symbiont device. The R operation
         without a number or ALL can be used only for input devices. The R response removes
         the runstream that the input device is currently reading. The remainder of the batch
         input (card deck) is bypassed until a new @RUN card or @RUN statement is
         encountered. For more information on the @RUN control statement, see the
         Executive Control Language (ECL) and FURPUR Reference Manual (7830 7949).




7831 0281–034                                                                                11–181
Keyin Reference




         Raaa—Reprint or Repunch Specified Pages
             SM sname Raaa

         Specifies a number of pages to reprint or cards to repunch on a symbiont device,
         where aaa is a decimal number equal to or less than 504 for symbiont handlers. The
         output can be incorrect if the file contains multiple changes of forms, margins, print
         bands, and so on, since the whole file is not scanned from the beginning. The keyin
         Raaa cannot be used for full-duplex remote (FDR) devices.

         R+aaa—Advance for Reprinting or Repunching
             SM sname R+aaa

         Advances a specific number of pages for printing or cards for punching, where aaa is a
         decimal number equal to or less than 504 for symbiont handlers. The output can be
         incorrect if the file contains multiple changes of forms, margins, print bands, and so
         on, since the whole file is not scanned from the beginning. Do not use the keyin R+aaa
         for FDR devices.

         RALL—Reprint or Repunch an Entire File
             SM sname RALL

         Reprints or repunches an entire file. The symbiont device reprints or repunches the
         file as follows:

             Printers immediately start printing from the beginning of the file without aligning
              the paper, unless you have changed the paper to a different physical length.
             The system displays the following message, allowing you to empty the punch
              hopper:

             sname - READY PUNCH - A

         Tape files that the symbiont device is printing or punching are rewound to the
         previous EOF mark, or to the start-of-reel; printing or punching begins from that point.
         If the tape file is a multireel file and a previous EOF mark is on the previous reel, the
         operator begins at the beginning of the current reel.

         S—Suspend Operation
             SM sname S

         This operation suspends the operation of a symbiont device. When you specify the
         suspend device (S) operation, the device is locked out immediately. Use this option for
         quick-stop situations only, such as aligning print or reloading cards into a punch unit.
         The S operation is used only with onsite devices. You can resume device operation by
         executing an SM keyin and specifying the I operation.




11–182                                                                              7831 0281–034
                                                                                      Keyin Reference




         T—Terminate a Device
           SM sname T

         This operation terminates the device, losing the remainder of the file; the device is
         locked out. If the device is a card reader, the run that the card reader is reading is
         removed from the system. When the device is either a remote device or a demand
         terminal, both the site and the run are terminated. If a run is suspended and you enter
         an SM site-id T from another terminal, the run will complete (FIN) first. Demand
         terminal users must sign on again. If the device is a printer, only the file that is printing
         is terminated, unless you also indicate a run-ID (sname). You can restore the onsite
         symbiont device by executing an SM keyin with the I operation.

11.70.3. System Response
         When you enter an SM sname keyin, the system responds with one of the following
         status messages:

           sname state1,state2,PAGES PRINTED = numpag

         where:

         sname
             is the symbiont device name.

         state1
             is one of the following states:

             ACTIVE               Specifies in use.
             INACTIVE             Specifies available, but not in use.
             LOCKED               Specifies unavailable, because the device is locked. Use the SM
                                  sname I keyin to unlock the device.
             SUSPENDED            Specifies available for I/O operations only.
             WAITING              Specifies waiting for input from the print queue.

         state2
             is one of the following states:

             IO ERROR             Specifies recovering from an I/O error.
             PRINTING             Specifies printing a file.
             REPRINTING           Specifies repositioning a file because an SM sname Rn or SM sname
                                  RALL (reprint file) keyin is in effect.
             SKIPPING             Specifies repositioning a file because an SM sname R+n (advance file)
                                  keyin is in effect.
             STOPPED              Specifies inactive or locked.




7831 0281–034                                                                                   11–183
Keyin Reference




         numpag
            is the number of pages in the current file that the symbiont device printed.

11.70.4. Examples
         Example 1
           SM PRC B

            Displays the cartridge-ID of the print band on printer PRC. The system displays this
            message:
              PRC CARTRIDGE-ID, 01

         Example 2
           SM PRC

            Displays the status of printer PRC. The system displays this message:
              PRC ACTIVE, PRINTING, PAGES PRINTED=3

         Example 3
           SM PRA B,02

            Changes the default cartridge-ID for printer PRA. The system displays this
            message:
              PRA CARTRIDGE-ID, 02

         Example 4
           SM PRA C

            Displays the current page format of printer PRA, an 0776 imprint printer. The
            system displays this message:
              PRA PSIZ=66, TOP=6, BOT=3, LPI=6

         Example 5
           SM PRC C,66,2,1,8

            Changes the current page format values for printer PRC, an 0776 impact printer.
            The system displays this message:
              PRC PSIZ=66, TOP=2, BOT=1, LPI=8

         Example 6
           SM PRC C,DEF

            Restores all values to system standard for the printer (0776 impact printers). The
            system displays this message:
              PRC PSIZ=88, TOP=3, BOT=3, LPI=8



11–184                                                                           7831 0281–034
                                                                          Keyin Reference




         Example 7
           SM PRC C,*,2,*,8

            Restores the system-standard values only for lines per inch and for the bottom
            margin (0776 impact printers). The system displays this message:
                PRC PSIZ=88, TOP=2, BOT=3, LPI=6

         Example 8
           SM PRC E

            Terminates an active print file on printer PRC.

         Example 9
           SM PRA I

            Reactivates printer PRA. The system displays this message:
                PRA UNLOCKED

         Example 10
           SM PRA J ON

            Initiates jogging of output on printer PRA.

         Example 11
           SM PRC L

            Locks out printer PRC. The system displays this message:
                PRC LOCKED
                PRC LOCKED , STOPPED

         Example 12
           SM PRA Q

            Requeues print files and lock out printer PRA. The system displays this message:
                PRA REQUEUED AND LOCKED

         Example 13
           SM PRA R12

            Reprints 12 pages on printer PRA. The system displays this message:
                PRA NOW REPRINTING 12 PAGES




7831 0281–034                                                                          11–185
Keyin Reference




         Example 14
           SM PRC R+2

            Skips two pages on printer PRC. The system displays this message:
              PRC COMPLETED SKIPPING 2 PAGES

            If the keyin advances the file to the end-of-file mark, the system displays this
            message:
              sname SKIPPED n PAGES - EOF! REPRINT? PAGES OR N

         Example 15
           SM PRC R+17

            Skips 17 pages on printer PRC. There are only 14 pages left in the file, so the
            system displays this message:
              0-PRC SKIPPED 14 PAGES - EOF! REPRINT? PAGES OR N

            To have three pages reprinted, respond with
              0 3

            The system displays this message:
              PRC NOW REPRINTING 3 PAGES

         Example 16
           SM PRC RALL

            Reprints the entire file on printer PRC. Only 12 pages of the file have been printed,
            so the system displays this message:
              0-PRC ONLY 12 PAGES PRINTED - REPRINT ALL? - YN

            To have the 12 pages reprinted, respond with
              0 Y

            The system displays this message:
              PRC NOW REPRINTING 12 PAGES

         Example 17
           SM PRC S

            Completely suspends the operation of printer PRC. The system displays this
            message:
              PRC SUSPENDED




11–186                                                                            7831 0281–034
                                                                                    Keyin Reference




         Example 18
             SM PRC C,88,,,

              Changes only the size field on a Model 0770 printer.
              The system-standard values are taken from the system-standard configuration,
              not from the standards for the particular device involved. The page size, top
              margin, and bottom margin system-standard values are controlled by system
              software configuration parameters. However, no software configuration
              parameter defines the system-standard value for lines per inch; the system value
              is always 8.


11.71. SP—Suspend a Run
         The SP keyin lets you suspend active runs.

11.71.1. Format
             SP[,{B|D}U] ID1[,ID2,...,IDn]

         where:

         B

              specifies to suspend batch runs only.

         D

              specifies to suspend demand runs only. Both B and D cannot be specified.

         U
              indicates that the ID arguments are user IDs.

         One or more IDs can be specified, separated by commas and with or without masking.
         If the U option is not specified, the IDs refer to run IDs; if the U option is specified, the
         IDs refer to user IDs.

         Examples

              SP,BU JUDGE         Suspends all batch runs executing under user ID JUDGE
              SP,D R2D2*          Suspends all demand runs that start with R2D2
              SP R1*,R2?          Suspends all runs that start with R1and all runs that start with R2 and
                                  that are up to 3 characters




7831 0281–034                                                                                     11–187
Keyin Reference




11.71.2. System Response
         When the keyin is accepted, the system responds as follows:
             run-id SUSPENDED yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm:ss zone

         If the activity you are suspending is running with the Exec key set, it is performing an
         extended mode Exec Service. Extended mode services cannot be suspended until the
         service has completed. The standard system method of waiting for extended mode
         Exec services completion usually suspends the job.

         The following are instances of extended mode Exec services for which the standard
         method of waiting for completion does not apply:
             ILES$MANAGE
             CARTTAPELIB$
             TIP$FILE$FUN
             SERVER$GET

         If an activity of the job you intend to suspend is performing any of these extended
         mode Exec services, the job is not suspended and the system displays the following
         message:
             run-id CANNOT BE SUSPENDED NOW - TRY AGAIN LATER

         If the keyin is not accepted for any other reason, the system displays a message that
         indicates why it could not be suspended.

11.71.3. Restrictions
         The E and X keyins cannot be used to terminate suspended runs. You must resume
         them before they can be terminated. If a run is suspended, it will complete (FIN) only if
         a $$CLOSE or @@TERM is issued from the run’s demand terminal or an SM siteid T is
         issued from another terminal.

         Only demand console users with a console mode capability of FULL, DISPLAY, or
         RESPONSE can enter the SP keyin.




11–188                                                                             7831 0281–034
                                                                               Keyin Reference




11.72. SQ—Display Print File Status
         The SQ keyin displays the status of the print files in the print queue.

11.72.1. Formats
         All Devices and Symbiont Groups

           SQ

             Displays one line of information for each device and symbiont group. The system
             output is as follows:

                  sname: nn FILES nnn [PAGES|CARDS] nn TAPES

         Specified Symbiont Queue

           SQ sname

             Displays one line of information for the specified symbiont queue. The system
             output is as follows:

                  sname: nn FILES nnn PAGES nn TAPE

         Total Number of Files Queued to User-IDs

           SQ USR*ID

             Displays the total number of files queued to all user-IDs. The system output is as
             follows:

                  USR*ID HAS NO PRINT/PUNCH FILES QUEUED

             or
                  USR*ID: nnnn FILES nnnn PRINT nnnn PUNCH nnnn TAPE

         Each File Queued by User-ID
           SQ USR*ID *

             Displays file names, number of pages or cards, and priority for all files queued to
             all user-ids. The system output is as follows:

                  user-id: run-id filename nn EPGS priority

             where priority is one of the following:

             0        ROLOUT/ROLBAK runs
             1        Critical deadline runs
             A        Run priorities A, B, and C



7831 0281–034                                                                              11–189
Keyin Reference




            D        Run priorities D, E, and F
            G        Run priorities G, H, and I
            J        Run priorities J, K, and L
            M        Run priorities M, N, and O
            P        Run priorities P, Q, and R
            S        Run priorities S, T, and U
            V        Run priorities V, W, and X
            Y        Run priorities Y and Z

         Files Queued to Symbiont Queue
           SQ sname *

            Displays the file names, number of pages or cards, and priority for a specific
            device or group. The system output could be one or several of the following lines:

                sname: run-id file nnn EPGS priority active mmm PPGS
                sname: run-id file nnn EPGS priority active
                sname: run-id file nnn ECDS priority active mmm PCDS
                sname: run-id file TAPE priority active

         Each File Queued by Run-ID
           SQ run-id * R

            Displays file names, number of pages or cards, and rank for the given run-id(s).
            The system output is as follows:

                run-id: file nnnnn EPGS priority rank/sname
                run-id: file nnnnn CDS priority rank/sname
                run-id: file TAPE priority rank/sname

            For files restored with an SR keyin, the run-id is the original run-ID for primary print
            or punch files, or EXEC8 for user-created print or punch files.

           SQ,R run-id1[,...run-idn] [filename]

            Displays run-id, file name, number of pages or cards, priority and rank for the given
            set of run-ids.




11–190                                                                              7831 0281–034
                                                                                 Keyin Reference




         User-ID
           SQ user-id * U

            Displays file names, number of pages or cards, and priority for the user-id(s). The
            system output is as follows:

                user-id: run-id file nnn EPGS priority
                user-id: run-id file nnn CDS priority
                user-id: run-id file TAPE priority

           SQ,U[A] user-id1[,...user-idn] [filename]

            If A option is not specified, displays user-id, run-id, file name, number of pages or
            cards, priority and device for the given set of user-ids.
            If A option is specified, displays user-id, run-id, file name, priority and rank for the
            given set of user-ids.

         Priority
           SQ run-id priority

            Changes the priority order for all files of the run-id.

           SQ run-id filename priority

            Changes the priority order for a specified file.

         Redirection (QTO)
           SQ sname QTO sname

            Redirects all files queued to sname to queue sname.

           SQ sname1 QTO sname2

            Redirects present queues for sname1 to sname2.

           SQ run-id file QTO sname

            Redirects a single file to a different queue.

           SQ run-id file QTO sname

                  Redirects a specific file to sname.

           SQ run-id file QTO user-id/U

                  Redirects a specific file to user-id




7831 0281–034                                                                                  11–191
Keyin Reference




         Redirection (TO)
           SQ sname1 TO sname2

            Redirects future files for sname1 to sname2, where sname1 is a specific on-site
            device that is locked out and idle and sname2 is a specific device that receives the
            future files for sname1. The system displays a confirming message:

                   SQ - FUTURE FILES FOR sname1 WILL BE DIRECTED TO sname2

                 Do not use this keyin for remote batch runs.

         Printerless Systems
           SQ PR AQ

            Allows files to be queued to the default print queue (printerless systems only).

           SQ PR PQ

            Prohibits queuing on the default print queue (printerless systems).

11.72.2. Examples
           SQ

            Displays information for each device and symbiont group, as follows:
                RG2621: 21 FILES 1022 PAGES
                B112X: 7 FILES 1793 PAGES
                PR: 1 FILE 1153 PAGES
                NOT777: 2 FILES 10 PAGES

           SQ PR

            Displays information for printer PR, as follows:
                PR: 1 FILE 1153 PAGES

           SQ PR *

            Displays information for each file in queue PR, as follows:
                PR: JJP JJP*A 1022 EPGS D
                PR: DPE PRRRIN*0129240 30 EPGS J
                PR: SLATTE IOUE*PRINT 1040 EPGS M




11–192                                                                            7831 0281–034
                                                                                  Keyin Reference




11.72.3. Operator Interfaces
         The following rules apply for the SQ keyins:

         The run-id and user-id can contain wildcard characters (‘*’, matching zero or any
         number of characters) and masking characters (‘?’, matching any single character). The
         run-id can also be specified as ‘EXEC 8’ and ‘TIP$’.

         1.    PR or PR/ or PR/nnn where nnn is a decimal number in the range 0 through 511,
               inclusive. This form is used for primary print files.
         2.    PU or PU/ or PU/nnn where nnn is a decimal number in the range 0 through 511,
               inclusive. This form is used for primary punch files.
         3.    qualifier*filename(cycle) having the standard syntax of file names. Qualifier and
               asterisk may be missing, which is interpreted as any qualifier.
         4. qualifier'*' filename(cycle). In addition to standard characters, qualifier and filename
            may also contain wildcard (*) and masking (?) characters. Both qualifier and
            filename must be provided.
               Note: The ‘*’ following the qualifier must be enclosed in quotes as an indicator
               that:
               1. Either wildcard or masking characters or both are present
               2. This particular * is not interpreted as a wildcard character.
         5.    qualifier\*filename is alternate syntax to step 4). The backslash character indicates
               that either wildcard or masking characters or both are present.

11.73.SR—Restore Saved Symbiont Files
         The SR keyin restores symbiont files that were previously saved to mass storage so
         you can queue them for printing (or punching).

         If your site has Security Level 2 or 3 or a Security Level 3 trusted environment, you
         can transport labeled print files from system to system by using the SR and SV keyins,
         in addition to saving the current print queue for a single host. If you save and restore a
         nonstandard file (using the SV and SR keyins), the banner from the @SYM control
         statement is lost. The run-IDis used as the banner.

         As with the SV keyin, use a labeled tape if your system was configured to require a
         prelabeled tape.

11.73.1. Formats
              SR ,unit/reeln A/F

               Restores and queues all symbiont files for the specified reel, reeln.
              SR ,unit/reeln A/F sname

               Restores all files for the specified symbiont group or device.




7831 0281–034                                                                                11–193
Keyin Reference




           SR ,unit/reeln run-id,...,run-id

             Restores all files for specific runs (up to five run-IDs).
           SR ,unit/reeln run-id file

             Restores specified files.

         Note: To restore Exec files, use the SEC, RESTORE keyin.

11.73.2. System Response
         If there is more than one reel of tape to restore, the system prompts you with the
         following message:
           REEL NUMBER FOR SRKYINfilename nnnnnn or N

         You can add a new tape number, or you can answer N to terminate the SR keyin.

         As each file is restored to mass storage, the system displays this message:
           SRKYINfilename: run-id file/sname

         In this case, the run-IDs are internally named EXEC 8 to avoid duplication of run-IDs in
         the system or in the output queue. The exceptions are primary PRINT$ or PUNCH$
         files, where the displayed run-ID is the original run-ID.

         Note: When the Exec restores a primary print file and displays the file name, it
         substitutes a dollar sign ( $ ) for the ampersand ( @ ) in the name.

         When all files have been restored, the system displays a message giving you the total
         number of restored files.

         Contact the site administrator if you see the following message while restoring files:
           SRKEYINfilename BLOCK COUNT ERROR - GO

         The preceding message indicates the data restored into storage is incorrect because
         the block count calculated during the restore operation does not match the block
         count stored on the tape. Responding GO allows SR processing to continue in spite of
         the data being corrupt. The corrupt data remains in storage and should not be used.
         Files restored to the print queue will be printed even though the block count error
         indicates they are in error.

11.73.3. Restrictions
         Files restored by an SR keyin cannot be deleted by the SX run-id or the SX run-id
         filename keyins. They can be deleted only by the SX A/F or SX A/F sname keyins.




11–194                                                                             7831 0281–034
                                                                                  Keyin Reference




11.73.4. Operator Interfaces
         The following rules apply for the SR keyins:

         The run-id and user-id can contain wildcard characters (‘*’, matching zero or any
         number of characters) and masking characters (‘?’, matching any single character). The
         run-id can also be specified as ‘EXEC 8’ and ‘TIP$’.

         1.    PR or PR/ or PR/nnn where nnn is a decimal number in the range 0 through 511,
               inclusive. This form is used for primary print files.
         2.    PU or PU/ or PU/nnn where nnn is a decimal number in the range 0 through 511,
               inclusive. This form is used for primary punch files.
         3.    qualifier*filename(cycle) having the standard syntax of file names. Qualifier and
               asterisk may be missing, which is interpreted as any qualifier.
         4. qualifier'*' filename(cycle). In addition to standard characters, qualifier and filename
            may also contain wildcard (*) and masking (?) characters. Both qualifier and
            filename must be provided.
               Note: The ‘*’ following the qualifier must be enclosed in quotes as an indicator
               that:
               1. Either wildcard or masking characters or both are present
               2. This particular * is not interpreted as a wildcard character.
         5.    qualifier\*filename is an alternate syntax to Step 4). The backslash character
               indicates that either wildcard or masking characters or both are present.

11.74. SS—Display System Status
         The SS keyin displays information describing the system status. The SS keyin
         response is seven lines long. The first three lines are the fixed portion of the SS keyin
         response; the last four lines are the same as those shown continuously at the top of
         the system console display.

         The four lines of the continuous console display are actually displayed as two lines in a
         rotating sequence. Each sequence is updated twice (6 seconds apart) before the other
         sequence is displayed. The continuous console display lines are sent to the console
         printer or a remote console only as a result of the SS keyin.

         The instruction processor percentages in the keyin response are based on instruction
         processor usage. Instruction processor usage is determined by the amount of
         instruction processor time a given program type used compared to the total amount
         of instruction processor time. These values cannot total 100 percent because the Exec
         always uses some percentage of instruction processor time.

         Asterisks appear in display fields when the field’s value exceeds its size.

11.74.1. Format
              SS




7831 0281–034                                                                                   11–195
Keyin Reference




11.74.2. System Response
         The response to the SS keyin is as follows:

           STATUS: OMSG nnnn, STRT nnn, DDLN nnn, yyyy-dd-mm hh:mm:ss zone
           HELD: OPER nnnn, TAPE nnnn, MASS nnnn, SOPT nnn
           ACTV: OPEN nnnn, ITLD nnnn SITE: PART-A HOST: A
           IP: BAT xx% DEM xx% R/T xx% TIP xx% IDLE xx%
           OPEN: BAT nnnn DEM nnnn UNOPND: nnnn HOLDS holds
           PAGE IO/M: nnnn MASS: nnnnnnnn KWMEM: nnnnnnnn yyyy-dd-mm hh:mm:ss zone
           EXP: xx%-nnnnnn E11:nnn ID: level TIP: fnnnnnn/r Q: nnnn A: nnnn

         Table 11-2 lists the parameters displayed in response to the SS keyin.


                       Table 11–2. Parameters Displayed by the SS Keyin


           Parameter                                          Description

         yyyy-dd-mm         date and time of the keyin in standard display format—that is year,
         hh:mm:ss zone      month, day, hours, minutes, seconds, and configured time zone
                            mnemonic.
         OMSG nnnn          Number of outstanding read-and-reply messages sent to all consoles,
                            regardless of whether they are actually displayed yet.
         STRT nnn           Unopened runs waiting for start time.
         DDLN nnn           Unopened deadline runs.
         OPER nnn           Runs held by the CS H run-ID keyin.
         TAPE nnnn          Runs held for tape or other peripheral facilities.
         MASS nnnn          Runs held for mass storage facilities or cataloged files.
         SOPT nnnn          Runs waiting for other runs to finish due to an @RUN statement S
                            option.
         OPEN nnn           Runs in main storage.
         ITLD nnnn          Runs waiting for initial load of a task.
         IP: BAT xx%        Percentage of IP SUPs used by user programs using batch and deadline
                            batch processing.
         DEM xx%            Percentage of IP SUPs used by user programs using demand
                            processing.
                            If demand processing is not configured, this entire field is left blank.
         R/T xx%            Percentage of IP SUPs used by user programs using real-time
                            processing.
         TIP xx%            Percentage of IP SUPs used by TIP transactions.
                            If TIP is not configured, this entire field is left blank.
         IDLE xx%           This parameter is present only when the aggregation of IP SUPs used
                            by user programs is negligible. Note that this does not include IP
                            utilization by the Exec on behalf of user programs.




11–196                                                                                    7831 0281–034
                                                                                     Keyin Reference




                       Table 11–2. Parameters Displayed by the SS Keyin


           Parameter                                       Description

         UNOPD nnn         Number of backlogged runs, including runs that are waiting to be
                           opened and runs that are being held.
         HOLDS holds       Indicates the reasons why runs are being held. The value holds can
                           contain any combination of the following letters. If none applies, the
                           entire field is blank.
                           A     All batch runs are held. This results from a CS H keyin or
                                 scheduling being suspended because the mass storage limit has
                                 been reached.
                           D     Demand runs are being held due to a CS HD keyin.
                           E     Insufficient EXPOOL space is available to open additional runs.
                                 This occurs when EXPOOL space availability has reached the
                                 critical threshold.
                           I     At least one run is suspended on the system.
                           L     Demand terminals are being held due to a CS HT keyin.
                           M     Mass storage facilities or cataloged files are not available.
                           O     Indicates at least one outstanding read-and-reply message for an
                                 onsite or remote console.
                           R     Operator hold by means of CS H run-ID keyin.
                           S     The S option appeared on some @RUN statements, but the
                                 previous run from the same peripheral device has not terminated.
                           T     Tape or other peripheral facilities not available. Includes tape units,
                                 removable disk units, reel-IDs, ACS queries, and facilities other

                           U    Low availability of one or more resource areas used internally by
                                Exec to support run execution.
         PAGE IO/M         For systems that support paging. Indicates the number of page
         nnnn              input/outputs per minute; if the number of paging operations per minute
         or                exceeds 999, it is reported as operations per second.
         PAGE IO/S
         nnnn
         PAGE PG/M         For systems that do not support paging. Displays the rate at which
         nnnn              4096-word areas (“pages”) of memory have been written to, or read

                           interval.
         PAGE PG/S
         nnnn

         nnnnnnn           measured in kilowords (Kwords).
         MEM               For systems that do not support paging. Indicates two percentages:
         Pnnn%             P is the percentage of Physical space currently in use.
         or                S is the percentage of Swapfile space currently in use.
         Snnn%             If either value is a negative number, it indicates the value is decreasing
                           or remaining constant. If either value is a positive number, it indicates
                           the value is increasing.
