11.75.ST—Start a Stored Runstream
         The ST keyin submits a runstream stored in a file or element for execution. The
         runstream will be scheduled and executed as an independent run.

         Also see the System Console Messages Reference Manual (7833 3275) for
         information about the console messages related to the ST keyin.

11.75.1. Format
           ST,priority/run-options/pdp/name,setc,run-id,acct-id/user-id,
           project-id,est-run-time/deadln,pages/cards,start-time,sequence-id

         where:

         priority
               If specified, replaces the scheduling priority given on the @RUN statement for the
               stored run.

               Note: When the Exec is holding a run in backlog because resources are not
               available (such as a file), other lower priority runs are not allowed to start. To
               avoid holding up the start of runs, start as many runs as possible at the same
               priority.

         run-options
               If specified, replace those on the @RUN statement for the stored runstream. If the
               S option is used on the @RUN statement, it is ignored. The remaining options (Z, U,
               and V) are described in 0.

         pdp
               If specified, replaces the processor dispatching priority on the @RUN statement
               for the stored runs.

         name
               Identifies the file or element that contains the stored runstream. The name must
               include the name of a file. If the name is a file name only, the file must be a data
               file.

         setc
               Specifies a positive octal number (not more than 4 digits) to be placed in T2 of the
               condition word of the run being scheduled.



11–200                                                                                7831 0281–034
                                                                                  Keyin Reference




         run-id
             If specified, this run-ID replaces the one on the @RUN statement for the stored
             runstream.
         acct-id
             If specified, this account number replaces the one on the @RUN statement for the
             stored runstream.
         user-id
             If specified, this user-ID replaces the one on the @RUN statement for the stored
             runstream.
             Note: Remote ST keyins that specify a user-ID other than the issuer’s user-ID
             must have the SSSTRTZOPT privilege to successfully start the run. For
             example, if @@CONS ST qual*file.elt,,,acct/B is entered by user-ID A, then user-
             ID A must have the SSSTRTZOPT privilege to start the run.
         project-id
             If specified, this project-ID replaces the one on the @RUN statement for the
             stored runstream.
         est-run-time
             If specified, this estimated run time replaces the one on the @RUN statement for
             the stored runstream.
         deadln
             If specified, this deadline replaces the one on the @RUN statement for the stored
             runstream.
         pages
             If specified, this estimate of output replaces the one on the @RUN statement for
             the stored runstream.
         cards
             If specified, this estimate of output replaces the one on the @RUN statement for
             the stored runstream.
         start-time
             If specified, this start time replaces the one on the @RUN statement for the stored
             runstream.
         sequence-id
             If specified, this identifier is displayed as [id] at the end of an ST keyin error
             message if the keyin fails. It is displayed in the format
                  ST KEYIN ERROR - text [id]

             The sequence-id can be 1 or 2 alphanumeric characters. This identifier can be
             helpful in correlating the error message with the failing keyin.




7831 0281–034                                                                                     11–201
Keyin Reference




11.75.2. Options
         U Option
         The U option on the ST keyin (ST,/U) allows a run to be started and executed under the
         user-ID of the @RUN statement in the stored runstream, instead of under the user-ID
         specified on the ST keyin.

         Rules for the U option are as follows. Note that the rules concerning execute access
         and file ownership can be bypassed by means of a security privilege (see 4 and 5
         below). The bypass allows runstreams with different user-IDs to be stored in the
         same file.
         1.   The U option is valid only when user security is configured (SENTRY_CONTROL is
              TRUE).
         2.   Both the user-ID and account number must be present on the stored @RUN
              statement.
         3.   The user-ID on the ST keyin must have execute access to the user-ID on the
              stored @RUN statement.
         4. The user-ID on the ST keyin must have execute access to the start file unless the
            following two conditions hold:
                 The ST keyin is entered from the physical console.
                 The user-ID on the ST keyin has the SSSTUBYPASSO privilege.
         5.   The user-ID on the stored @RUN statement must own the file containing the
              stored runstream, unless the user-ID on the ST keyin has either the
              SSSTUBYPASSO or the SSBYPASSOWNR privilege.
         6. The user-ID and account number of the run to be started must be specified on the
            ST keyin.

         V Option
         The V option on the ST keyin allows a run to be started with the minimum clearance
         level and default compartment set of the user-id on the @RUN statement. Without the
         V option, the started run assumes the default compartment set of the user-ID on the
         ST keyin, and the operator is solicited for the minimum clearance level.

         When the V option is combined with the U option (ST,/UV), the clearance level and
         compartment set of the start file must equal the minimum clearance level and the
         default compartment set of the user-ID on the ST keyin. There are two exceptions to
         this rule:
             If the user-ID on the ST keyin is sufficiently privileged to change the start copy file
              to the minimum clearance level and default compartment set of the user-ID on the
              @RUN image, then the run is started at that minimum level with the default
              compartment set.
             If the start file is system low (clearance level 0 and NULL compartment set), then
              the run is started at system low.




11–202                                                                               7831 0281–034
                                                                             Keyin Reference




         The purpose of the V option is to let a trusted user-ID—that on the ST keyin—start a
         more privileged run. It is useful for starting runstreams that can run productively
         below their top security level.

         Z Option
         The Z option on the ST keyin (ST,/Z) lets you initiate runs that execute at system high,
         provided that you specify a user-ID that can execute at system high, and that you have
         full console capability. If you specify the U option, the run that was started must
         possess the Z-option privilege.

         When SENTRY_CONTROL is TRUE, the following read-and-reply message is
         suppressed for ST keyins that specify the Z option:
             ENTER C.L. FOR: filename

         Starting Runstreams for Other Software Products
         The ST keyin also starts runstreams for other software products. Use the following ST
         keyin formats:
             ST DPREP

              Starts the runstream that executes DPREP software. For information on preparing
              disks, refer to the Exec System Software Installation and Configuration Guide
              (7830 7915).
             ST INSTALL

              Starts a batch run to execute the INSTALL element for COMUS.

11.75.3. System Configuration Parameters and the ST Keyin
         The format for the ST keyin depends on the values for the system configuration
         parameters SENTRY_CONTROL and QUOTA_ENFORCEMENT_BY_USERID.

         The following configurations are possible:


                          SENTRY_CONTROL                    QUOTA_ENFORCEMENT_BY_USERID

         1                      FALSE                                     FALSE
         2                      FALSE                                      TRUE
         3                      TRUE                                      FALSE
         4                      TRUE                                       TRUE




7831 0281–034                                                                             11–203
Keyin Reference




         You can use the ST keyin with different configuration parameters, as described in the
         following:

         1. Both Parameters FALSE
         When SENTRY_CONTROL and QUOTA_ENFORCEMENT_BY_USERID are both FALSE,
         use the following keyin formats:

           ST element

         The system searches for the element in these files:

           SYS$*RUN$.
           SYS$LIB$*RUN$.

         If the element is found, the account number and user-ID are taken from the @RUN
         statement, and the run is started. For more information on the @RUN control
         statement, refer to the Executive Control Language (ECL) and FURPUR Programming
         Reference Manual (7830 7949).

           ST element,,,account

             or
           ST element,,,account/user-id

         The system ignores the user-ID on the keyin. The user-ID comes from the @RUN
         statement, and the account number comes from the keyin. If there is no user-ID on
         the @RUN statement, then a MISSING USERID error is displayed, and the keyin
         terminates.

           ST qualifier*filename.element,,,account

         The run is started with the user-ID from the @RUN statement, and account is taken
         from the keyin.ST qualifier*filename.element,,,account/user-id

         Both account and user-id come from the keyin. If the user-ID is the security officer’s
         user-ID, then the system displays the following read-and-reply message:

           ENTER PASSWD/C.L. FOR: qualifier*filename.element

         Using a space in the reply (in other words, no password) indicates a blank password. If
         TSS_CONTROL is FALSE, the keyin is allowed and the started run executes with the
         user-ID specified, but using the minimum security attributes. If TSS_CONTROL is
         TRUE, then a blank password is accepted, but only if the TSS configuration parameter
         ALLID is set.




11–204                                                                            7831 0281–034
                                                                              Keyin Reference




         2. SENTRY_CONTROL=FALSE and
            QUOTA_ENFORCEMENT_BY_USERID=TRUE
         When SENTRY_CONTROL is FALSE and QUOTA_ENFORCEMENT_BY_USERID is TRUE,
         the ST keyin is the same as when QUOTA_ENFORCEMENT_BY_USERID is FALSE,
         except that the following keyin is not allowed without the user-ID:

           ST qualifier*filename.element,,,account

         3. SENTRY_CONTROL=TRUE and
            QUOTA_ENFORCEMENT_BY_USERID=FALSE
         When SENTRY_CONTROL is TRUE and QUOTA_ENFORCEMENT_BY_USERID is FALSE,
         you must specify a valid account-ID and user-ID on the keyin. No password solicitation
         occurs. The system displays this read-and-reply message:

           ENTER C. L. FOR: qualifier*filename.element

         A blank or null response is assigned the minimum clearance level. The only allowed
         keyins are as follows:

           ST element,,,account/user-id

         The account and user-ID come from the keyin.

           ST qualifier*filename.element,,,account/user-id

         The account and user-ID come from the keyin.

         4. Both Parameters TRUE
         When both SENTRY_CONTROL and QUOTA_ENFORCEMENT_BY_USERID are TRUE,
         keyins have the same effect as when SENTRY_CONTROL is TRUE and
         QUOTA_ENFORCEMENT_BY_USERID is FALSE (see the immediately preceding topic).


11.76. SU—Suspend a Component
         The SU keyin suspends devices from being allocated. Use the keyin if you are getting
         frequent I/O errors on a component. Suspend permits access to files already on the
         component, but prevents any more from being placed on it. The SU keyin is a facility
         keyin.

11.76.1. Formats
           SU devnam

            Inhibits further allocation on the component while still permitting I/O operations.
           SU,ALL cu

            Suspends further allocation on all devices attached to the specified control unit
            while still permitting I/O to the devices.




7831 0281–034                                                                             11–205
Keyin Reference




11.77. SV—Save Queued Output Files to Tape
         The SV keyin saves queued print and punch output files to magnetic tape. Files
         queued to USR*ID cannot be unloaded with the SV keyin.

         Note: You must use the SEC,SAVE keyin to save EXEC-8 files.

11.77.1. SV Keyin Format
             SV [,[*[unit]][/reeln][,[options][,CTL-pool][,expiration][/MMspec][,data-
             comp][/buf-write]] file-spec.

         where:
                comma as the first character of the keyin text indicates that details and options of
                the tape assignment follow the comma. If the comma is not the first character
                then the keyin text contains file-spec only. In this case a blank tape from the
                standard system equipment type is assigned for the save operation.
         *
                is optional and a specific tape unit.
         unit
                is optional and a specific tape unit if preceded by ‘*’. Otherwise, unit is tape assign
                mnemonic. If not specified, the configured standard system equipment type is
                used.
         reeln
                specifies the tape reel number. If no number is specified, the system requests a
                blank tape.
         option
                options can be one of the following:

                N       Keeps the file in the queue and saves the contents on tape. If you save a file once
                        with this option, you cannot save it again. Subsequent SV keyins bypass the file.
                R       Allows removable disk files also to be saved on tape. Note that using the SR keyin
                        to restore these removable disk files will restore them to fixed mass storage.
                S       Writes the tape in 224-word blocks instead of the default 1,792-word blocks.
                        These tapes can be directly queued for printing or punching from a run with an
                        @SYM statement.
                U       file-spec contains user-ids, not run-ids. If this option is specified, A/F and A/F
                        sname are invalid in file-spec field.

         CTL-pool

                is optional and specifies the CTL pool used for blank tape allocation.




11–206                                                                                         7831 0281–034
                                                                                     Keyin Reference




         expiration

               is optional and specifies the number of days that the tape is retained.

         MMspec

               is the optional Media Manager specification, when the Media Manager is used.

         data-comp

               is the optional data compression specification; for example, CMPON

         buf-write

               is the optiona buffered write specification; for example, BUFTAP, BUFFIL

         file-spec

               specifies the files that are saved to tape. It can be one of the following:

               A/F                           Saves all files on all queues to tape. Files queued to user-
                                             IDs are not saved.
               A/F sname                     Saves all files queued to symbiont group or device name.
               run-id1[,runid...,run-idn]    Saves files from the specified runs. This can be many files if
               [filename]                    any run-id and/or filename containes masking or wildcard
                                             characters.
               user-id1[,…,user-idn]         Saves files from the specified user-IDs. This can be
               [filename]                    many files if any user-id and/or filename contains
                                             masking/wildcard characters. The U option must be
                                             specified.

11.77.2. System Response
         For each SV keyin, the system requests a tape SVKYINnnnnnn to load; each tape has a
         unique number.

         As each file is read to tape, the system displays this message:

              SVKYINtapename: run-id file/sname

         If the file to be saved is a removable disk file and the R option was not specified, the
         file is not saved and a message with the reel number or disk pack is displayed in one
         of the following formats:

              SVKYINtapename: run-id file TAPE-reeln

         or
              SVKYINtapename: run-id file PACK-pack-id

         where SVKYINtapename identifies the file to the Exec.



7831 0281–034                                                                                      11–207
Keyin Reference




         When all files have been read, the system displays the total number of files saved.

         The following error cases can occur when saving removable disk files (with the R
         option specified):

            If the main item address within the smoque entry is not the correct main item
             address, then the smoque entry is not processed and the following E$ORMSG
             read-only message (message number 0350) is displayed.
             SVKYINtapename: The symbiont queue entry for file filename from run run-id is
             not valid for SV processing; therefore, it has been bypassed.

             This can occur when the RP keyin has removed the file from the master file
             directory (MFD). When the file has been removed, the main item in the smoque
             entry is no longer valid. A subsequent FA keyin can reestablish the file, but the file
             will receive a new main item address.
             This error case can also occur because of a small timing window after the SV
             keyin has verified that the smoque entry is valid during which the RP keyin can be
             performed before the file is actually assigned. This can also generate an I/O error
             021 with the file in a "not cataloged" state.
            If there is an I/O error 021 while reading the removable disk file during SV
             processing and the files status is "not cataloged,"the following E$ORMSG
             read-only message (message number 0150) is displayed.
             SVKYINtapename: The queued file filename from run run-id is on a removable
             pack but could not be assigned for SV processing.

             This case can occur if a file spans multiple packs and one of the packs is down.
             This case can also occur even when the file is cataloged—when the file is
             exclusively assigned by a user at the time of the SV keyin.
            When shared removable files are queued and file sharing is down, SV processing
             skips saving such files and displays an appropriate message at the conclusion of
             SV processing.
             SVKYINtapename: The queued file filename from run run-id is on a removable
             pack and received I/O error errornum during SV processing.

             SVKYINtapename: Remove queue entry (may delete the file) for filename Y/N?


11.77.3. Restrictions
         You cannot mount an unlabeled tape for an SV keyin if the parameter
         PRELABELED_TAPES_REQUIRED was turned on when your system was configured.

         If you save and restore (using the SV and SR keyins) a nonstandard file, the banner
         from the @SYM statement is lost. The run-ID is used as the banner.

         You must use the SEC,SAVE and SEC,RESTORE keyins to save and restore Exec files.




11–208                                                                              7831 0281–034
                                                                                  Keyin Reference




11.77.4. Examples
              SV ,HICL/PRO231 A/F

               This keyin saves all queued print and punch output files to reel PRO231 on any
               cartridge tape unit.

              SV ,HICL/PRO234,R GARTH REPORT*PRINT9

               This keyin saves the removable disk file REPORT*PRINT9, which was queued by
               the run GARTH.

11.77.5. Operator Interfaces
         The following rules apply for the SV keyins:

         The run-id and user-id can contain wildcard characters (‘*’, matching zero or any
         number of characters) and masking characters (‘?’, matching any single character). The
         run-id can also be specified as ‘EXEC 8’ and ‘TIP$’.

         1.    PR or PR/ or PR/nnn where nnn is a decimal number in the range 0 through 511,
               inclusive. This form is used for primary print files.

         2.    PU or PU/ or PU/nnn where nnn is a decimal number in the range 0 through 511,
               inclusive. This form is used for primary punch files.

         3.    qualifier*filename(cycle) having the standard syntax of file names. Qualifier and
               asterisk may be missing, which is interpreted as any qualifier.

         4. qualifier'*' filename(cycle). In addition to standard characters, qualifier and filename
            may also contain wildcard (*) and masking (?) characters. Both qualifier and
            filename must be provided.

               Note: The ‘*’ following the qualifier must be enclosed in quotes as an indicator
               that:
               1. Either wildcard or masking characters or both are present

               2. This particular * is not interpreted as a wildcard character.

         5.    qualifier\*filename is alternate syntax to step 4). The backslash character indicates
               that either wildcard or masking characters or both are present.




7831 0281–034                                                                                11–209
Keyin Reference




11.78. SX—Delete Queued Files
         The SX keyin deletes queued print and punch files from the system.

11.78.1. Formats
         All Queued Files

           SX A/F

            Deletes all print/punch files except those queued to user-IDs and generates the
            following message:

               REALLY WANT TO DESTROY ALL PRINT/PUNCH FILES? Y OR N-

            Answer Y to continue the keyin. Answer N to cause the keyin to be ignored.

         Files for a Symbiont Group or Specified Device

           SX A/F sname

            Deletes all print/punch files queued to the specific symbiont name.

         Files for a User-ID

           SX A/F USR*ID

            Deletes all print/punch files queued to all user-IDs.

           SX A/F user-id *U

            Deletes all print/punch files queued to a specific user-ID.

           SX,U user-id1[,...,user-idn] [filename]

            Delete print/punch files for the specified user-IDs. If the optional file name is
            specified only that file is deleted (or matching files if wildcard/masking is used).

         Files for a Run-ID

           SX run-id,...,run-idn

            Deletes all files for up to five specified run-IDs.

           SX run-id[1,...,run-idn] [filename]

            Deletes print/punch files for the specific run-id. If the optional file name is
            specified only that the file is deleted (or matching files, if wildcard/masking is
            used).




11–210                                                                              7831 0281–034
                                                                               Keyin Reference




11.78.2. System Response
         When the SX keyin processing is finished, the system displays the following message
         when one file is deleted:

           1 FILE DELETED VIA SX KEYIN

         The system displays the following message when multiple files are deleted:

           number FILES DELETED VIA SX KEYIN

         When the symbiont queues are large, it can take several minutes for the Exec to
         complete an SX keyin. The SX A/F and SX A/F sname keyins are especially slow. Any
         symbiont control keyins (SR, SV, SX, SQ) entered while an SX keyin is in progress
         cause further delays, and in some cases could cause an EXPOOL hold.

11.78.3. Restrictions
         You cannot use the SX keyin on a print file that is exclusively assigned to a run.

11.78.4. Examples
         Example 1
           SX A/F

             Deletes all files queued.

         Example 2
           SX A/F PR1

             Deletes all files queued to printer PR1.

         Example 3
           SX RUN1

             Deletes all queued files belonging to run-ID RUN.

         Example 4
           SX RUN1,ABC,DICK

             Deletes all queued files for run-IDs RUN1, ABC, and DICK.

         Example 5
           SX JIP YUR$KO*DDRB$PR$NT

             Deletes the user file YUR$KO*DDRB$PR$NT for run-ID JIP.




7831 0281–034                                                                                 11–211
Keyin Reference




         Example 6
           SX ABC QUAL*FILE

               Deletes the user file QUAL*FILE for run-ID ABC.

         Example 7
           SX A/F ABC *U

               Deletes all queued files for user-ID ABC.
               If any shared symbiont files are queued for printing (@SYM) and then deleted from
               the queue while file sharing is down, the following message is displayed:
                 xx FILES BYPASSED, FILE SHARING DOWN

               where xx is the number of files that could not be deleted.


11.79. T_Display Demand and Batch Run
       Information
         The T keyin displays information about active demand runs, active batch runs, held
         runs, and suspended runs.

         Use the B option with the T keyin if you want only the run-IDs of active batch runs;
         use the D option if you want only the run-IDs and site-IDs of active demand runs. If
         you do not specify the B or D option, you get information on both batch and demand
         runs. If you want more detailed information, specify the D parameter in addition to
         either of those options.

11.79.1. Format
           T[,{B|D}UN] [D|H|S] [ID1,ID2,...,IDn]

         where:

           B and D

               is batch or demand. Both B and D cannot be specified.

           U

               indicates that the ID arguments are user IDs.

           N

               indicates to negate any mask that is displays all runs except those specified.

           D

               specifies run details.



11–212                                                                              7831 0281–034
                                                                                      Keyin Reference




             H

                 specifies held runs.

             S

                 specifies suspended runs.

         One or more IDs can be specified, separated by commas and with or without masking.
         If the U option is not specified, the IDs refer to run IDs; if the U option is specified, the
         IDs refer to user IDs.

         Examples:

                 TD                 Detailed output of all runs in the system
                 T D,RUN?           Information for run D and all runs that start with RUN and maximum of
                                    4 characters in length
                 T A*               All run IDs that start with A
                 T *A               All run IDs that end with A
                 T,U D U1           Details of all runs executing under user ID U1
                 T,UND U1           All demand runs not executing under user ID U1.
                 T ??????           Same as 'T' or 'T *' keyins

         Note: If the first field (D|H|S) is not specified, but a single character ID is specified,
         and that character is D, H, or S, then the ID field is ignored and the single ID
         character is taken to be the D|H|S field. You can use the character and the mask
         character to achieve the desired result. For example, “T D?” to display the run “D”
         instead of just “T D” which can be unclear.

11.79.2. System Response
         T
         The response to a T keyin contains one or more lines with several run-IDs and (for
         demand runs) site-IDs in the following format:

             ACTIVE: run-id[+] [/site-id] run-id[+] [/site-id] ....

         A plus (+) after run-ID indicates that the run is in main storage; /site-id indicates that it
         is a demand run initiated from device site-id.

         T,B
         The response to a T,B keyin contains one or more lines with several run-IDs of batch
         runs in the following format:

             BATCH: run-id[+] run-id[+] run-id[+] ....




7831 0281–034                                                                                      11–213
Keyin Reference




         T,D
         The response to a T,D keyin also has one or more lines with several run-IDs and site-
         IDs of demand runs in the following format:

           DEMAND: run-id[+]/site-id run-id[+]/site-id run-id[+]/site-id ....

         D Parameter with T, T,B, or T,D
         If you use the D parameter with a T, T,B, or T,D keyin, the run-IDs are displayed in
         three columns, and detailed storage information is displayed for the runs. The run-IDs
         and (for demand runs) site-IDs are displayed in this format:
           run-id/[site-id]run-state storage-size

         run-ID1/[site-id] state size run-ID2/[site-id] state size run-id3/[site-id] state size

         where:
         run-state
             is one of the following:

             +       in main storage
             -       swapped out
             *       in facility wait

         storage-size
             is decimal storage size in thousands of words. It is the total size of all banks
             contained in the program and activity level BDTs.

         For a T D keyin, the first line of the display includes information in this format:

           keyword: BATCH/MAXOPN=active-batch/max-open DEMAND=active-demand
                    yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm:ss zone

         where:

         keyword

             is ACTIVE for the T D keyin, BATCH for the T,B D keyin, and DEMAND for the T,D D
             keyin.

         active-batch
             is the number of active batch runs.

         max-open
             is the maximum number of open batch runs permitted.

         active-demand
             is the number of active demand runs.



11–214                                                                                   7831 0281–034
                                                                                Keyin Reference




         yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm:ss zone
              is the date and time the keyin was submitted in standard display format—that is,
              year, month, day, hours, minutes, seconds, and configured time zone mnemonic.

         TH
         The response to a T H keyin contains one or more lines with several run-IDs of held
         runs in the following format:

           HELD:    run-id run-id run-id run-id

         TS
         The response to a T S keyin contains one or more lines with several run-IDs of
         suspended runs in the following format:

           SUSPENDED: run-id run-id run-id run-id


11.79.3. Examples
         Example 1
         This example of a T display shows nine active batch runs and four active demand runs:

           ACTIVE: MSCP+ DCR+ RUNNER+ MAP8TM+ AP8TIM+
           ACTIVE: SDTTIM SILAS+ CPCOMM+ WLSCON+/RE4323 AA085B+/RE4201
           ACTIVE: JTNDAT+/RY0726 MCB1+ JTNDAU+/RY0725

         Example 2
         Here is a partial response to a T D display, showing information on 8 of the 29 active
         batch runs and 4 of the 89 active demand runs. These are the same run-IDs that are
         shown in the example of the T display:

           ACTIVE: BATCH/MAXOPN=29/30 DEMAND=89 2013-05-22 17:24:35 CDT
           CPCOMM/+46024        SILAS/+3141         ARC/+76
           CSC/+3575            MSCPRT/+35          CIPHER/+470
           WEBDMD/G00002+447    RDO/RDO002+33       GOE/RGOE23+33
           RDOA/RDO003+33       JHND/RJMH23+33

         If you enter a T,B D keyin, the format of the first display line is the same as for the T D
         keyin, except that it begins with BATCH instead of ACTIVE. Similarly, the first display
         line of the T,D D keyin begins with DEMAND.

         Example 3
         If you enter a T D keyin when no runs are active, the system displays the message:

           ACTIVE: - NONE




7831 0281–034                                                                                11–215
Keyin Reference




         Example 4
         If you enter a T,B D keyin when no batch runs are active, the system displays:

           BATCH: - NONE

         Example 5
         If you enter a T,D D keyin when no demand runs are active, the system displays the
         message:

           DEMAND: - NONE

         Example 6
         To see the run-IDs of held runs, use the T H keyin. Here is an example of the
         response:

           HELD: JAKEY CR JJIP PLPPL
           HELD: ALOG BKC03 MARKKE TT
           HELD: JOKU WEBQ BEWNE

         Example 7
         To see the run-IDs of suspended runs, use the T S keyin. Here is an example of the
         response:

           SUSPENDED:    TQAGGA    XB0001    7PCCHY   XB0000
           SUSPENDED:    XE0000    XS0000    TQAGG    XM0001
           SUSPENDED:    XM0000    XS0001    XE0001


11.80. TB—Send a Message to Demand Terminals
         The TB (terminal broadcast) keyin sends a message to all active demand terminals.

11.80.1. Formats
           TB text

            Displays the specified text at all active demand terminals.

           TB /I text

            Used for emergency messages; it overrides any holds at the terminal.

11.80.2. Example
           TB 7-015 TAPE UNIT WILL HAVE MAINTENANCE AT 18:00

            The system sends this message to all active demand terminals:
           *TB* 7-015 TAPE UNIT WILL HAVE MAINTENANCE AT 18:00




11–216                                                                           7831 0281–034
                                                                                  Keyin Reference




11.81. TF—Initialize TIP File Control
         The TF keyin recovers or initializes TIP File Control. Use the keyin after the SYS FIN
         message.

11.81.1. Formats
           TF ALL D

              Initializes TIP File Control and deletes and recatalogs Exec files that contained TIP
              system files.

           TF ALL I

              Initializes TIP File Control and catalogs Exec files used for TIP system files.

           TF ALL R

              Recovers TIP File Control.

11.81.2. System Response
         Requests for initialization are rejected if the component is not in the deferred
         initialization state. TIP can be in a deferred state as a result of two actions: (1) During a
         boot, a message is displayed (if jump key 7 is set), asking you whether you want to
         start TIP or defer the start. If you respond D, the start is deferred. (2) An Exec error
         will put TIP in a deferred state so that you can fix the error and restart TIP without
         booting the system.


11.82. TIP—Display or Change TIP Automatic
       Recovery Status
         The TIP keyin displays or changes the status of TIP automatic recovery, which lets you
         automatically restore the TIP system environment after a system failure. Control
         information saved from a previous session lets the system restore TIP to its previous
         state.

         For more information on the TIP keyin, refer to the Transaction Processing
         Administration and Operations Reference Manual (7830 7881).

11.82.1. Format
           TIP action

         where action is one of the following:

         DF
              changes state of TIP automatic recovery from DN (down) to DF (deferred).




7831 0281–034                                                                                   11–217
Keyin Reference




         DN
               disables TIP automatic recovery.

         FS
               displays the status of TIP automatic recovery. The status is one of the following:

               DF          TIP automatic recovery is in a deferred state.
               DIP         TIP automatic recovery is in the process of being disabled.
               DN          ARF has been freed from the Exec, and TIP application group state changes are
                           not being tracked.
               EIP         TIP automatic recovery is in the process of being enabled.
               MSG         A read-and-reply message relating to restarting TIP has not been answered.
               RS          The TIP application group is in the process of being restarted.
               UIP         The Exec is in the process of updating the ARF.
               UP          Currently tracking TIP application group state changes in the ARF.

         RS
               restarts the TIP application group; reads the ARF and reestablishes the TIP
               environment.

         UP
               enables TIP automatic recovery and saves the control information for a future
               recovery.


11.83. TIPSS—Display TIP Session Control
       Statistics
         The TIPSS keyin displays TIP Session Control statistics.

11.83.1. Format
              TIPSS application-group [function]
              TIPSS OPEN

         Displays the number of open sessions, the number of memory pages in use, the
         number of input and output messages processed per minute, and the paging success
         rate.

         where:
         application-group
               is either the application group number or ALL. ALL causes the information to be
               displayed for each application group in sequence.




11–218                                                                                       7831 0281–034
                                                                                        Keyin Reference




         function
              indicates the type of TIP Session Control data you want displayed:

      SESSIONS          Displays one line of statistics for open TIP sessions.
      PAGES             Displays one line of paging statistics for TIP Session Control.
      MESSAGES          Displays one line describing the number of messages processed in a minute.
      STATUS            Displays all the TIP Session Control statistics (all three of the lines).

         OPEN
              displays the number of active local clients, active remote clients, and
              close-pending clients in OPEN TIP sessions. These statistics are also displayed by
              the TIPSS ALL keyin.

11.83.2. System Response
         The system displays the information described in the following for the different keyin
         function options.

         SESSIONS
             APP n SESSIONS:s TERMINALS:t CAPACITY:x%

         where:
         s
              is the number of active TIP sessions, including all open terminal sessions, the
              number of terminal sessions marked closed but retained until outstanding
              messages are processed, and the number of batch and demand runs connected to
              TIP under this application group.

         t
              is the number of terminals assigned to active TIP sessions, including sessions
              where the user is in the process of signing on.

         x%
              is the fraction of the configured maximum number of TIP sessions that are
              currently in use.

         PAGES
             APP n PAGES:p CAPACITY:y% SUCCESS RATE:r%

         where:

         p
              is the number of memory pages that are currently allocated. The size of the main
              storage paging area is dynamically adjusted by the system to respond to system
              load.




7831 0281–034                                                                                       11–219
Keyin Reference




         y%
              is the fraction of the configured maximum number of memory pages that are
              currently allocated.

         r%
              is the probability that the system can obtain space from the main storage paging
              area without displacing an existing page. The amount of mass storage
              input/output necessary to support the application group is inversely related to the
              paging success rate. The success rate, in turn, is affected by the number of active
              sessions and the configured size of the main storage paging area.

         MESSAGES
             APP n MESSAGES PER MINUTE- INPUT:i OUTPUT:o

         where:

         i
              is the number of input messages received from the communications network and
              pass-off messages generated by application group programs.

         o
              is the number of output messages generated by the application group in 1 minute.
              This statistic refers only to the output message creation rate, not to the message
              delivery rate, which depends on the state of the communications network
              hardware and software.

         STATUS
              displays each of the preceding one-line messages, in the order shown. If you
              specify ALL instead of an application group number, you see the complete set of
              messages for each application group in numerical order; you might see the
              message APP n IS NOT CONFIGURED or APP n IS NOT UP instead of the set of
              messages for a particular application group.

         OPEN
             Sessions: xxxxxx (Active Remote: yyyyyy; Active Local: zzzzzz)

         where:

         xxxxxx
              is the total number of all OPEN TIP sessions: active remote sessions, active local
              sessions, and close-pending sessions. If the sum of the active remote and active
              local does not equal xxxxxx, then the difference is the number of close-pending
              sessions.

         yyyyyy
              is the number of all remote sessions not in a close-pending state; remote sessions
              originate from the network.



11–220                                                                             7831 0281–034
                                                                                Keyin Reference




         zzzzzz
              is the number of all local sessions not in a close-pending state; local sessions are
              created by OS 2200 program requests to an Open CMS handler, such as OLTP.

         Errors
         If the system detects an error in the TIPSS keyin processing, it might display one of
         the following messages:

             TIPSS KEY ERROR: APPLICATION NUMBER IS NOT NUMERIC

             TIPSS KEY ERROR: FUNCTION IS INVALID
             TIPSS KEYIN NOT REGISTERED*

         Open session control is not initialized.


11.84. TM—Send a Message to a Site-ID, Run-ID, or
       User-ID
         The TM keyin sends a message to a site-ID, run-ID, or user-ID.

11.84.1. Formats
             TM site-id text

              Sends the message text to the specified site-ID.

             TM run-id/R text

              Sends the message text to the specified run-ID.

             TM user-id/U text

              Sends the message text to the specified user-ID.

11.84.2. System Response
         The system displays an error message if any of the following conditions occur:

             The site-ID, run-ID, or user-ID is not active.
             The site-ID is not a demand terminal.
             The addressee’s message queue is full.

11.84.3. Example
             TM JIP/R I CAN'T FIND YOUR TAPE.

              The following message is sent to run-ID JIP:
                  *TM* I CAN'T FIND YOUR TAPE.



7831 0281–034                                                                                11–221
Keyin Reference




11.85. TP—Control Transaction Processing
           The TP keyin controls Transaction Processing. The TP keyin has several formats to

              Get the status of the transaction code
              Clear indicators
              Load and unload copies of RTPS programs
              Display the status of RTPS programs
              Control maximum concurrency
              Display the length of queues
              List run condition information
              List the status of transaction programs
              Start and stop the collection of continuous statistics.

           Table 11-3 lists the TP format variations.


                               Table 11–3. TP Format Variations


                 Format                                            Description
