11.85.1. TP A Keyin
             TP A [transaction-code] or [#application-number]

         Lists the status of the currently active transaction program specified by transaction
         code. If you do not specify a transaction code or application number, the keyin lists
         the transaction code and status of all transaction programs that are currently active in
         the system.

         The system displays the information in the following format:

             ACT=transaction-code          AG=appl-num   Q-q   *runid [, *runid]

         where :

         appl-num
              is the application group number.
              The application group displayed by this keyin is derived from the AUDIT field in the
              VALTAB for the transaction. This number can differ from the application group
              displayed by the TP RC keyin. TP RC displays the application group in which the
              transaction is executing.

         q
              is the number of scheduling requests (messages) queued within the Exec TIP
              complex for that transaction code. This count does not include scheduling
              requests for transactions which have not been dequeued from the Step Control
              input tree.

         runid
              is the TIP-generated run-ID.

         s
              is the status of the run:

              =      Copy not in storage
              +      Copy in execution
              -      Copy stuck
              *      Copy terminating
              !      RTPS copy deactivated

         If no TIP transaction programs are active, the system displays the following message:

             *TIP* TP keyin found no active transaction programs




7831 0281–034                                                                              11–225
Keyin Reference




11.85.2. TP DTQ Keyin
           TP DTQ "selection criteria" <A>

             The TP DTQ keyin will delete TIP scheduling requests (TIP transaction input
             messages) queued on the system. This includes TIP scheduling requests queued
             in the TIP scheduling complex and optionally (if the "A" parameter is specified)
             queued on step control application input/scheduling tree(s).

             The "selection criteria" parameter must be specified as part of the TP DTQ keyin in
             one of the following formats:

               TP DTQ txncod

                  Delete TIP scheduling requests queued for the specified TIP transaction code
                  (in this case, transaction "txncod").

               TP DTQ #1

                  Delete queued TIP scheduling requests associated with the specified
                  application group number (in this case, application number 1).

               TP DTQ #ALL

                  Delete all queued TIP scheduling requests.

         If the optional "A" parameter is specified after the required "selection criteria"
         parameter, step control application input/scheduling tree(s) are first searched. If any
         TIP input messages (queue items) are found which match the "selection criteria" they
         are deleted. Examples of specifying the "A" parameter on the TP DTQ keyins
         previously described:

           TP DTQ txncod A
           TP DTQ #1 A
           TP DTQ #ALL A

         In all cases the TIP scheduling complex is then searched. If any TIP input messages
         (scheduling packets) are found which match the "selection criteria" they are deleted. If
         any TIP scheduling requests were deleted the following console message is displayed
         ("xxx" is the total number deleted):

           *TIP* TP DTQ keyin deleted xxx queued TIP input messages.

         One of the following console messages will be displayed if no queued TIP scheduling
         requests are found which satisfy the selection criteria or the submitted selection
         criteria is missing or invalid:

           *TIP* TP DTQ did not find/delete any TIP input messages.
           *TIP* TP DTQ selection criteria required but not specified.
           *TIP* TP DTQ application number specified is invalid.
           *TIP* TP DTQ keyin selection criteria specified is invalid.




11–226                                                                            7831 0281–034
                                                                              Keyin Reference




           *TIP* TP DTQ keyin syntax error - TP DTQ keyin rejected.

         The total number of TIP scheduling requests currently queued on the system is
         reported as part of the continuous console display ("Q:" field) or as output from the
         "SS" system console keyin.

         The "TP TQ" system console keyin will display additional information about TIP
         scheduling requests queued in the TIP scheduling complex as well as the total number
         of TIP scheduling requests queued to step control application input/scheduling trees.

         Notes:
                 When a TIP transaction code is specified as the "selection criteria" and the
                  "A" option is also specified, information from the TIP transaction's current
                  VALTAB/VINDEX entry (AUD, QPR, and REC fields) is used to search the
                  appropriate step control application input/scheduling tree.
                 To be located on the step control input tree by transaction code, MCB
                  transactions must have the audit field (AUD,x) and the "J" status (STA,J)
                  specified on their VALTAB entries.
                 As part of deleting queued TIP scheduling requests, any associated input
                  messages (MCB or COMPOOL) are released.

11.85.3. TP MAX Keyin
           TP MAX

             Displays a console message indicating the current state of TP MAX enforcement.

           TP MAX number

             Sets a new concurrency maximum, which overrides the default limit set by TIPTPS
             and any previous TP MAX limit. The system displays a message confirming the
             enforcement of this new value.
             You can set TPMAX to zero even if there are RTPS programs registered on the
             system, but you cannot set TPMAX to a value other than zero that is less than the
             total number of dedicated RTPS transactions registered on the system. Such an
             attempt results in an appropriate error message.

           TP MAX OFF

             Ends enforcement of the current TP MAX limit and reinstates enforcement of the
             system default limit (the TIPTPS value). The system displays a message confirming
             this action, or a TP MAX KEY ERROR message if TP MAX enforcement is already
             off.




7831 0281–034                                                                              11–227
Keyin Reference




             TP MAX ON

              Reinstates the enforcement of the most recent TP MAX limit after it has been
              disabled by a TP MAX OFF keyin. This limit overrides the system default limit (the
              TIPTPS value). The system displays a message confirming the enforcement of the
              TP MAX limit, or a TP MAX KEY ERROR message if TP MAX enforcement is
              already on.

         Use the TP MAX keyin to change the maximum number of transaction programs that
         can be concurrently active in the system. You can also use this keyin to find out the
         current limit and the number of currently active programs. After a disk recovery of the
         system, TIP scheduling enforces the TP MAX concurrency limit set by the last TP MAX
         keyin or, if no value is currently in effect (TP MAX OFF), the system default limit (the
         TIPTPS configuration parameter value).

         After a tape recovery of the system, if TIP Automatic Recovery has been enabled from
         the previous session, TIP scheduling enforces the TP MAX concurrently limit set by
         the last TP MAX keyin. If TIP Automatic recovery is not enabled, the system default
         limit (the TIPTPS configuration parameter value) is enforced by TIP scheduling.

         In a multihost environment, concurrency is supported on a local basis to each host.
         The TP MAX keyin controls concurrency only on the local host and displays values only
         for that host.

         The system displays a message confirming enforcement of a TP MAX limit if you enter
         one of the following keyins:

             A TP MAX number keyin
             A TP MAX ON keyin
             A TP MAX keyin without any parameters while a TP MAX limit is in effect

         A console message confirming TP MAX enforcement has this format:

             TPMAX mmmmm, USE uuuuu, ALL aaaaa, RQC cccccccccccc, STK ssss,
             RTPS-D ddddd, RTPS-N nnnnn, DACT-D ddact, DACT-N ndact
             Last TPMAX change at YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS zone

         where:

         mmmmm
              is the maximum number of concurrently active programs allowed in the system.

         uuuuu
              is the number of currently active programs.

         aaaaa
              is the total number of transactions programs, active or not, currently existing on
              the system, plus any programs being loaded or flushed.




11–228                                                                             7831 0281–034
                                                                              Keyin Reference




         cccccccccccc
              is the number of times the scheduling of transaction programs was deferred
              because of TPMAX limit enforcement. This requeue count is reset to zero after
              each TP MAX number keyin and after each system reboot.

         yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm:ss zone
              is the date the current TPMAX limit was established, in year, month, day, hours,
              minutes and seconds. The zone field contains the configured time zone
              mnemonic.

         ssss
              is the total number of stuck transactions currently on the system.

         ddddd
              is the total number of dedicated RTPS transactions, active or not, currently on the
              system.

         nnnnn
              is the total number of nondedicated RTPS transactions, active or not, currently on
              the system.

         ddact
              is the total number of dedicated RTPS transactions in the RTPS-deactivated state
              that are currently on the system.

         ndact
              is the total number of nondedicated RTPS transactions in the RTPS-deactivated
              state that are currently on the system.

         The system displays a message indicating that no TP MAX limit is in effect if you enter
         one of the following keyins:

             A TP MAX OFF keyin
             A TP MAX keyin without any parameters sometime after a TP MAX OFF keyin.

         A message indicating no TP MAX enforcement has the following format:

             TP MAX number *OFF* yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm:ss zone

         On the date and time (yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm:ss zone) indicated in the message, a TP
         MAX OFF keyin ended enforcement of that TP MAX limit. TIP is now enforcing the
         system default limit (the TIPTPS value).




7831 0281–034                                                                              11–229
Keyin Reference




         To ensure that a TP MAX limit is not exceeded, TIP calculates the number of currently
         active programs (the USE value):

          total number of transaction programs (the ALL value),

          minus the number of programs stuck in main storage (the STK value),

          minus the number of RTPS-deactivated nondedicated RTPS transactions (the DACT-N
          value)

         The system displays a warning message the first time when the enforced TP MAX
         limit is exceeded:

           *TIP* TPMAX number *initially exceeded*

         If you use a TP MAX number keyin to lower the concurrency maximum during online
         operation, the number of active programs can temporarily exceed the new limit.
         However, TIP will not force any programs to abort in order to satisfy the new limit. If
         you enter a TP MAX 0 keyin, you effectively suspend TIP scheduling.

         If RTPS programs are being used, you must not specify a nonzero value for the
         concurrency maximum below the total number of dedicated RTPS copies requested
         on the TP I keyin. If you attempt to do so, the system displays the following error
         message:

           *TIP* MAX keyin error - TPMAX value is greater than zero and less than
           the total number of dedicated RTPS copies requested.


11.85.4. TP QUE Keyin
         This format tells the system to display the length of current queues and the status of
         the system counters. There can be more than one Communications Management
         System (CMS) program registered, and the CMS queue and counter values reflect total
         counts.

         In a multihost environment, the TP QUE keyin displays only values that are local to the
         host.

         When you enter a TP QUE keyin, the system displays a TIP STATUS message:

           TIP STATUS: <EXEC level> <site name> hh:mm:ss mm:dd:yy zone

         The rest of the display consists of labels and a 2-digit value for each. The labels
         represent the following:

         ALT           SUB-Qs built from ALTERNATE library cycle.
         AVL           Current available count.
         CNEC          Batch-connected programs.
         CORE          COMPOOL storage.




11–230                                                                              7831 0281–034
                                                                             Keyin Reference




         D432   SUB-Qs built from ONLINED0432A file.
         D782   SUB-Qs built from ONLINED1782A file.
         DAQ    Outstanding dynamic allocation (DA) main storage requests.
         DF2    SUB-Qs built from ONLINEFAST2A file.
         INQ    SUB-Qs not yet built.
         LODQ   SUB-Qs on initial load chain.
         LOW    Lowest available count.
         MAIN   SUB-Qs built from MAIN library cycle.
         MSQ    RTRANO/RTRANU schedule packets waiting for mass storage COMPOOL read.
         OPEN   Open runs (batch and demand).
         OUTQ   Messages on CMS output queues.
         OVQ    Entries on CMS overflow queues.
         PRIM   Primary mass storage COMPOOL.
         REQ    Total request count.
         SEC    Secondary mass storage COMPOOL.
         SEXQ   Scheduling packets queued for SEXEM.
         STK    Stuck program copies.
         SUBS   SUB-Q count.
         TACT   Online transaction programs.
         TEST   SUB-Qs built from TEST library cycle.
         TOT    Total count specified at system generation.
         TRWT   Waiting transaction programs.
         UNOP   Runs in backlog (system wide).




7831 0281–034                                                                         11–231
Keyin Reference




11.85.5. TP RA, TP RS, and TP RH Keyins
         These formats help identify bottlenecks and other problems in high-volume
         transaction environments by displaying detailed information about specific
         transactions.

         The TP RA keyin displays information about active transactions. When no transaction
         code or application number is specified, the keyin displays information about all active
         transactions. When a transaction code is specified, information is displayed only
         about active copies of the specified transaction code. When an application number is
         specified, information is displayed only about transactions (active copies) currently
         associated with the specified application group.

         The TP RS keyin displays information about stuck or resident transaction program
         system (RTPS) deactivated transactions. When no transaction code or application
         number is specified, the keyin displays information about all stuck or RTPS deactivated
         transactions. When a transaction code is specified, information is displayed only
         about stuck or RTPS deactivated transaction program copies of the specified
         transaction code. When an application number is specified, information is displayed
         only about stuck or RTPS deactivated transaction copies associated with the specified
         application group.

         The TP RH keyin displays information about all active transactions that have remained
         active for the specified number of seconds. If no number-of-seconds parameter is
         specified, a default of 5 seconds is used. The maximum value that can be specified is
         999999 seconds.

         Information for each transaction is displayed in the following format:

              run-id ag t-code t-type program lv state ex-time sups i/os pid

         where:

         run-id
                is a unique system-generated transaction run identifier.

         ag
                is the application group number or 0 if the run is not associated with an
                application group.

         t-code
                is the transaction code (same as ACTION keyword from the associated VALTAB
                entry).




11–232                                                                              7831 0281–034
                                                                                      Keyin Reference




         t-type
                 is the transaction program type, which can be any of the following:

                BTCH           Online batch program
                HVTP           HVTIP program
                RENT           Reentrant TIP program
                REN*           Reentrant TIP program requested but TIP loaded program as SINT
                SDES           Self-destructive TIP program
                SINT           Self-initializing TIP program

         program
              is the program name for non-HVTIP transactions. For HVTIP transactions, this is
              the current library/bank number.

         lv
              If state is ILOAD, the initial switching level (same as LEVEL keyword from
              associated VALTAB entry).

              If state is not ILOAD, the current switching level. This can be less than LEVEL in
              the VALTAB entry.

         state
                 is the current program execution state, which can be any of the following:

                ACTIVE         Active TIP program.
                ERROR          TIP program in error termination.
                ILOAD          TIP program with initial load status. Values for ex-time, sups, i/os, and pid
                               are not available for transactions in this state.
                RTPSD          Active dedicated RTPS program.
                RTPSN          Active nondedicated RTPS program.
                DACTD          RTPS-deactivated dedicated RTPS program.
                DACTN          RTPS-deactivated nondedicated RTPS program.
                STUCK          Stuck TIP program. When a transaction is in this state, the pid, i/os, sups,
                               lv, and ex-time fields contain values for the last execution of the
                               transaction.
                TERM           Terminating TIP program.

         ex-time
                 is the execution time (current time minus run start time) in hh:mm:ss format. If
                 this field overflows, the value is displayed as ******. If the state of the
                 transaction is ILOAD, this value is unavailable (N/A).




7831 0281–034                                                                                       11–233
Keyin Reference




         sups
                is the number of standard units of processing (SUP) used by this program. The
                value is either total accumulated SUPs or IP SUPs, depending on the VALTAB S
                indicator setting and the MAX_TIME_IP_ONLY configuration parameter. If the
                value is the number of IP SUPs, an asterisk is appended to the value.
                If this field overflows, the value is displayed as ******. If the state of the
                transaction is ILOAD, L-I-P, or SWAP, this value is unavailable (N/A).

         i/os
                is the total number of I/O requests. If this field overflows, the value is displayed
                as ******. If the state of the transaction is ILOAD, this value is unavailable (N/A).

         pid
               is the position id of the input terminal. If the state of the transaction is ILOAD, this
               value is unavailable (N/A).

11.85.6. TP RC Keyin
         This format lists run condition information about a specific execution of a transaction
         program, which you identify by the TIP generated run-ID.

         The system then displays a two-line to five-line status summary for that program.
         Lines 1 and 2 are always reported. Line 3 is reported only for HVTIP programs. Lines 4
         and 5 are reported only if the Program Control Table (PCT) for the transaction program
         is currently in main storage.

         Line 1:       runid trans-code state=state program-type Prog=pppppp [RTPS=rrrr]


         Line 2:       NAME=name [FILE=name [program-library-cycle]] Level=# [LOCAL]


         Line 3:       [ICPLib=# ICPBank=# Lib=# Bank=# LLib=# LBank=#]


         Line 4:       [Pid=PID Size=# SUPS=# [Pages=#] [mode]]


         Line 5:       [Start=time Activities=# I/O=# AG=appl-num] | [Size=#] [AG=appl-num]



         For a TIP program in a SUPUR program file, the second line has this format:

            Name=name File=SUPUR-file# program-library-cycle

         If the program is in fast-load format, a period appears after the file number:

            FILE=SUPUR-file#.

         The program-library-cycle is MAIN, ALTERNATE, or TEST.




11–234                                                                                  7831 0281–034
                                                                                Keyin Reference




         These fields appear, or might appear, in a four-line response to a TP RC keyin:

         runid
                 is the TIP-generated run-ID.

         trans-code
                 is the transaction (action) code by which the program is scheduled.

         state
                 is the current program state:

                 XQTING          Executing
                 INTLOD          Initial load
                 TERM            In termination
                 STUCK           TIP stuck copy
                 RTPDAC          RTPS deactivated copy
                 ERROR           In error termination

         prog-type
                 is the program type:

                 RE-ENT          Reentrant
                 S-INIT          Self-initializing
                 S-DEST          Self-destructive
                 ONBTCH          TIP online batch
                 HVTIP           High-Volume TIP

         PROG=#
                 is the TIP program number from the VALTAB entry for the transaction code.

         RTPS=#
                 is the RTPS number of the program.

         NAME=name
                 is the name of the absolute element in the load file.

         MAIN
                 is the MAIN HVTIP library file cycle if the program is an HVTIP program.




7831 0281–034                                                                               11–235
Keyin Reference




         FILE=name
              is the name of the standard online program file or the number of the SUPUR
              program file from which the absolute element was loaded. If the SUPUR program
              is in fast-load format, a period follows the file number. For standard online file
              loading, the file name is one of the following:

              D0432A             ONLINED0432A
              D1782A             ONLINED1782A
              FAST2A             ONLINEFAST2A

         program-library-cycle

              is the cycle of the SUPUR program library file if the program is an absolute. The
              value is MAIN, ALTERNATE, or TEST.

         LEVEL=#
              is the execution level of the transaction program as specified in the VALTAB
              entry for the transaction code.

         LOCAL
              is an indicator that the transaction’s VALTAB entry has been registered as local in
              an XTC environment by means of the HOST VALTAB parameter. The LOCAL field
              is reported for all transaction program types.

         ICPLIB=#
              is the HVTIP library number of the initial control program I-bank.

         ICPBANK=#
              is the HVTIP bank number of the initial control program I-bank.

         LIB=#
              is the HVTIP library number of the current I-bank.

         BANK=#
              is the HVTIP bank number of the current I-bank.

         LLIB=#
              is the logged library number provided by the TPUR COPY,L option.

         LBANK=#
              is the logged bank number provided by the TPUR COPY,L option.

         PID=#
              is the position-ID of the requesting workstation.




11–236                                                                             7831 0281–034
                                                                                Keyin Reference




         SIZE=#
                is the program size in decimal 64-word blocks.

         SUPS=#
                is the number of standard units of processing (SUP) used by this program. The
                value is either total accumulated SUPs or IP SUPs, depending on the VALTAB S
                indicator setting and the MAX_TIME_IP_ONLY configuration parameter. If the
                value is the number of IP SUPs, an asterisk is appended to the value.

         PAGES=#
                is the total actual pages.

         mode
                is the mode in which the transaction program is executed, if other than normal
                mode; TESTMODE, TRAINING mode, or TEST/TRAINING mode.

         START=time
                is the start date and time in yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm:ss zone ( that is year, month,
                day, hours, minutes, seconds, and configured time zone mnemonic).

         ACTIVITIES=#
                is the number of activities currently in execution for this program.

         I/O=#
                is the number of I/O operations performed by this program.

         AG=appl-num
                The application number displayed by this keyin identifies the application group in
                which the transaction is executing. This number can differ from the application
                group displayed by the TP A or TP T keyins. These keyins display the application
                group specified by the transaction’s VALTAB entry.

                Example
                 TP RC *BBBBB

                   The system displays the following console messages:

                     *BBBBB XICPC State=XQTING HVTIP Prog=608
                     Name=XICPC MAIN Level=14
                     ICPLib=13 ICPBank=1 Lib=13 Bank=1 LLib=13 LBank=1
                     PID=0 Size=359 SUPS=0
                     Start=2013-04-25 16:33:13 CDT Activities=1 I/O=0 AG=0

                If the transaction program copy identified by the run-ID is not active, the system
                displays the following message:
                     *TIP* run-id not found




7831 0281–034                                                                               11–237
Keyin Reference




11.85.7. TP RL Keyin
         This format displays all FCSS locks that have runs or transactions queued to them on
         the system. The run-ID of the lock’s owner and the run-ids of all queued
         runs/transactions are displayed. The TIP file number and locked record range are also
         displayed.

         On XTC systems, the run-IDs of local runs or transactions queued to shared (XPC-L)
         FCSS locks are displayed. If the owner (run or transaction) of a shared lock exists on
         the host its run-ID is also displayed. If the owner of a shared lock cannot be found on
         the host its owner is displayed as -SHRD-.

         Example 1
           TP RL

             The system displays the message
              OWNER      FILE#     START-REC     END-REC     QUEUED-TRANSACTION/RUN
              *BBBBG      103            151         151     *BBBBJ *BBBBL *BBBBN *BBBBQ
                                                             *BBBBS *BBBBV *BBBBX RDOB
              RDOA        106            124          124    *BBBBH *BBBBK *BBBBM
              -SHRD-      109           5211         5211    *BBBBG

             If there are no runs/transactions queued to FCSS locks on the system, the output
             of the TP RL keyin is as follows:
               *TIP* No transactions/runs found for TP RL keyin.

         Example 2
           TP RL file#, START-REC# <,END-REC>

             This form of the keyin displays the owner of an FCSS lock regardless of whether
             there are runs or transactions queued to the lock. If the end record number is not
             specified, it is assumed to be the same as the start record number.The output of
             this keyin is as follows:
           OWNER FILE# START-REC               END-REC          QUEUED-TRANSACTION/RUN
           *BBBBF 254    300                      310           -NONE-

             If an invalid TIP file is specified, the following console message are displayed:
               *TIP* Invalid TIP file number specified for TP RL keyin.




11–238                                                                              7831 0281–034
                                                                               Keyin Reference




11.85.8. TP XL Keyin
         This format displays all runs and transactions that are queued to shared (XPC-L) TIP
         FCSS locks on a host in an XTC environment. With the exception of the "H/Q" field, the
         information displayed is the same as the "TP RL" keyin (see 11.85.7 for description). The
         "H/Q" field displays the following information:

             H - The HOSTID where the lock owner ("Owner" field RUNID) resides.
             Q - Indicates whether the lock owner ("Owner" field RUNID) is also queued to a
              shared (XPC-L) TIP FCSS lock (Y = Yes, N = No).
         Example
             TP XL

              The system displays the following console messages:

             Owner    H/Q     FILE#   START-REC    END-REC      QUEUED-TRANSACTION/RUN-HOST-A
             *BBBBG   A/Y       103         151        151      *BBBBJ *BBBBL *BBBBN *BBBBQ
             *BBBBL   A/Y       106         124        124      *BBBBH *BBBBK *BBBBM
             *BBBBX   B/Y       109        5211       5211      *BBBBK
             *BBBBY   C/N       109        5300       5300      *BBBBN *BBBBL *BBBBG
             *BBBBZ   D/Y       110         200        200      *BBBBP

             If there are no runs or transactions queued to the shared (XPC-L) TIP FCSS locks on
             the system, the output of the TP XL keyin is as follows:

             *TIP* No transactions/runs found for TP XL keyin.

             Only one TP XL keyin is allowed to be active on a system at any given time. If a TP
             XL keyin is already active at the time another one is issued, the output of the TP XL
             keyin is as follows:

             *TIP* TP XL keyin already in progress - try again later.




7831 0281–034                                                                               11–239
Keyin Reference




11.85.9. TP S Keyin
         This format displays the status of one or all RTPS programs, which you load with the
         TP I keyin and delete with the TP D keyin. If you do not specify an RTPS program
         number in the keyin, you see the status of all loaded resident online programs.

         In a multihost environment, RTPS programs are a local resource. The TP S keyin
         displays only the status of RTPS programs that are local to the host.

         The system displays a response message in the following format:

             RTPS#-rrrr Prg#-pppppp Name-name Actual=aaa Desired=ddd Type=t

         where:

         rrrr
                is the RTPS program number.

         pppppp
                is the TIP program number.

         name
                is the TIP program name.

         aaa
                is the number of RTPS copies actually loaded.

         ddd
                is the desired number of RTPS copies.

         t
                is the RTPS program type indicator:

                D     for dedicated RTPS programs
                N     for nondedicated RTPS programs




11–240                                                                          7831 0281–034
                                                                               Keyin Reference




11.85.10.TP T Keyin
                    TP T [program-name] or [#application-number]

         Lists the status of the currently active transaction program specified by program
         name. If you do not specify a program name or application number, the keyin lists the
         program name and status of all programs on the system.

         The system displays the information in the following format:

             ACT=program-name AG=appl-num Q-q *runid [, *runid]

         where:

         appl-num
                is the application group number.

                The application group displayed by this keyin is derived from the AUDIT VALTAB
                for the transaction. This number can differ from the application displayed by the
                TP RC keyin. TP RC displays the application group in transaction it is executing.

         q
                is the number of scheduling requests (messages) queued within the complex for
                that transaction code. This count does not include scheduling for transactions
                that have not been dequeued from the Step Control, runid is the TIP generated
                run-ID.

         s
                is the status of run:

                =          Copy not in storage

                +          Copy in execution

                -          Copy stuck

                *          Copy terminating

                !          RTPS copy deactivated

         If no TIP transaction programs are active, the system displays the following message:

             *TIP* TP keyin found no active transaction programs




7831 0281–034                                                                              11–241
Keyin Reference




11.85.11. TP TA, TP TS, TP TQ, and TP TR Keyins
             TP TA [transaction-code] or [#application-number]

             TP TS [transaction-code] or [#application-number]

             TP TQ [transaction-code] or [#application-number]

             TP TR [transaction-code] or [#application-number]

         These keyins display information based on transaction codes, as follows:

         TP TA       Displays information about transaction codes that have at least one currently active
                     transaction.
         TP TS       Displays information about transaction codes that have at least one currently stuck
                     or RTPS deactivated transaction.
         TP TQ       Displays information about transaction codes that have at least one currently
                     queued request.
         TP TR       Displays information about transaction codes that had an available nonzero
                     completion rate over the last complete DAPA time interval. (A DAPA interval is
                     approximately 6.5 seconds.)

         If a transaction code is specified, only information about the specified transaction is
         displayed. If an application number is specified, only information about transactions
         that have the AUDIT field in their VALTAB entries set to the specified application group
         are displayed. The transactions must also meet the selection criteria of the specific
         keyin. If no transactions are found that meet the selection criteria, the following
         console message is displayed for the TP TA, TP TS, and TP TR keyins:

             No transactions found for the TP Tx keyin.

         The last line displayed for a TP TQ keyin will be a summary line as follows (if no
         transactions meet the selection criteria this is the only line displayed):

         Total queued: x TIP queue: y Step Control queue(s): z

         where x,y,z are defined as follows:

         x

              number of TIP scheduling requests (schedule packets) queued in the TIP
              scheduling complex and input messages (queue items) queued to nodes of Step
              Control input tree(s).

         y

              number of TIP scheduling requests (schedule packets) queued in the TIP
              scheduling complex.




11–242                                                                                  7831 0281–034
                                                                                    Keyin Reference




         z

              number of input messages (queue items) queued to nodes of Step Control input
              tree(s).

         If a syntax error is detected on a TP TA, TP TS, TP TQ, or TP TR keyin, a console
         message is displayed:

             TP keyin syntax error - TP keyin rejected.

         All these keyins display output in the same format, as in the following example:

         T-CODE     TIPQ     ACTIVE     MAXACT         STUCK        DEACT   TYPE   LEVEL     STAT     RATE

         TIPTX1     33       102        102            0            0       HVTP   2         0        37/S

         TIPTX2     5        15         15             0                    SINT   7         0        11/S

         *TIPTX3    0        1          1              0                    SDES   14        0        9/M



         Definitions of the output fields are as follows:

     T-CODE                Transaction code.
                           An asterisk ( * ) preceding the transaction code indicates a TEST mode
                           transaction.
     TIPQ                  Number of scheduling requests for the transaction code currently queued in
                           the TIP scheduling complex. This does not include input messages queued to
                           nodes of Step Control input trees.
     ACTIVE                Number of copies of the transaction currently active.
     MAXACT                Maximum number of copies of the transaction allowed to be concurrently
                           active.
     STUCK                 Number of transaction copies currently stuck.
     DEACT                 Number of copies of the transaction currently RTPS deactivated.
     TYPE                  Transaction type, as follows:
                           BTCH               Online batch
                           HVTP               HVTIP
                           RENT               Reentrant
                           SDES               Self-destructive
                           SINT               Self-initializing
     LEVEL                 Transaction’s initial switching level.
     STAT                  Current transaction scheduling status. If this value is nonzero, the system
                           copy of the transaction’s VALTAB entry is in the process of being rebuilt
                           (scheduling for this transaction code is currently suspended). This value
                           corresponds to the TIP sticking status contained in the TIP termination (type
                           103) system log entry.




7831 0281–034                                                                                     11–243
Keyin Reference




     RATE                   Approximate transaction rate computed or extrapolated from the number of
                            completions over the last complete DAPA time interval. (A DAPA time
                            interval is approximately 6.5 seconds.) This value is displayed in transactions
                            per second (/S) or per minute (/M). If a complete DAPA time interval of data
                            has not yet been collected, N/A is displayed.

11.85.12. TP TIP Keyin
         This format tells the system to display the status of transaction programs.

         In a multihost environment, the TP TIP keyin displays only values that are local to the
         host. When you enter a TP TIP keyin, you see a TIP STATUS message.

         The rest of the display contains labels and a 11-digit value for each. The labels
         represent the following:

         BXFR             Block transfer loads

         CGY              Contingencies generated/presented
         ERR              TIP scheduling errors

         ILOD             Initial loads

         INIT             Multiple INITAL call iterations

         MOUT             Messages placed on all CMS output queues

         OPFAIL           Transaction scheduling failures

         RACT             Stuck programs reactivated

         RSTR             Transaction program restarts

         SUBB             SUB-Qs built

         SXMA             Times SEXEM activated for TP error

         TRAN             Transaction programs executed


         Example 1
            TP D 1,40,TTA1

                The system displays the message:
                  RTPS- 1,TIP PROG- 40,NAME-TTA1 UNLOADED

         Example 2
            TP I 1,40,TTA1

                The system displays the message:
                  RTPS- 1,TIP PROG- 40,NAME-TTA1 LOADED




11–244                                                                                     7831 0281–034
                                                                              Keyin Reference




         Example 3
           TP MAX OFF

               The system displays the message:
                 *TIP* TPMAX mm *OFF* YYYY:MM:DD HH:MM:SS Zone

         Example 4
           TP RC *TPC456

               The system displays the message:
                 TPC456 T12345 State=XQTING Rentrant Prog = 40 RTPS=1
                 Name=TTA1 FIle=1000 Level=1
                 Size=16 SUPS=0 Pages=555

         Example 5
           TP T

               The system displays the message:
                 Name= SECSVR AG=9 Q=0 : *BBBD9+
                 Name= XICPC AG=0 Q=2 : *BBBHD- *BBBHF- *BBBHG-
                 Name= XICPC : *BBBHH-
                 Name= XICPC : *BBBHI- *BBBHJ- *BBBHK- * BBBHL- *BBBHM-


11.86. TS—List Disabled Terminals and Enable
       Terminals
         The TS keyin checks which terminals are disabled and then restores any or all
         terminals you specify.

11.86.1. Format
           TS[,ALL] command [,id] [,id] . . .

         where:
         ALL
               in conjunction with the LIST command, displays the status of every terminal either
               disabled or waiting for entry of a user-ID/password.

               In conjunction with the ENABLE command, ALL indicates that every waiting or
               disabled terminal should be enabled.




7831 0281–034                                                                              11–245
Keyin Reference




         command
              is either LIST or ENABLE.
              LIST displays the status of specified terminals on the system console. You can
              abbreviate LIST to L, LI, or LIS. In conjunction with ALL, LIST causes the security
              console to display the terminal-IDs that are either waiting for a sign-on solicitation
              or are disabled.

              ENABLE restores disabled terminals or terminals waiting for a sign-on solicitation
              to a normal sign-on sequence. You can abbreviate ENABLE to E, EN, ENB, ENAB, or
              ENABL. In conjunction with ALL, ENABLE enables every waiting or disabled
              terminal.

         id[,id] ...
              specifies the terminal-IDs to list or enable. The valid characters for this field are A
              through Z and 0 through 9. If you have specified ALL, this field is ignored.

11.86.2. Examples
         Example 1
            TS,ALL L[IST]

              Lists all site-IDs or Position Identifiers (PID) that are disabled or are waiting to
              solicit the message
                 Enter your user-id/password and clearance level:

         Example 2
            TS L[IST] [id], [id],...[id]

              Lists the status of the terminals specified by terminal-ID.

         Example 3
            TS,ALL E[NABLE]

              Returns every terminal to the normal sign-on sequence.

            TS E[NABLE] [id], [id],...[id]

              Returns the specified terminals to the normal sign-on sequence.

11.86.3. Restrictions
         The configuration parameter DELAYED_SIGN_ON_SOLICITATION must be TRUE in
         order to execute the TS keyin.




11–246                                                                                  7831 0281–034
                                                                                Keyin Reference




11.87. TU—Release a Tape Unit
         The TU keyin unconditionally releases a tape unit from exclusive use. This keyin can
         force another system to lose exclusive use of the device. The keyin should be used
         only when the host that has exclusive use of the tape devices is down or has not been
         able to perform channel initialization for some reason. Do not use this keyin if the host
         is still up and functioning. If channel initialization has been performed, other systems in
         the tape subsystem environment can acquire the devices exclusively without the TU
         keyin.

         After the keyin is complete, any system can then acquire the unit by bringing up the
         tape unit.

         Use this keyin with caution, because it could stop another system from booting by
         removing exclusive use of the tape unit from the system.

11.87.1. Format
           TU tape-unit-name [,tape unit name,...]

         where tape-unit-name is the name of the tape unit.

11.87.2. System Response
         If an error occurs during the processing of the TU keyin and the unit is not released,
         the following message is displayed:

           TU OF tape-unit-name FAILED: UNABLE TO RELEASE THE UNIT

         The keyin aborts, and no further processing takes place.


11.88. UD—Establish Unit Duplexing
         The UD keyin establishes a unit-duplexed environment in a running system by joining a
         fixed or removable disk pack to an identical drive that is unassigned and reserved. It
         does this by copying the source unit to the destination unit.

         You can use Unit Duplexing only with identical equipment types (identical in the Exec
         configuration). Unit Duplexing of dissimilar equipment types is not permitted.

         For more information on Unit Duplexing, see the Unit Duplexing Planning, Installation,
         and Operations Overview (7830 7592).




7831 0281–034                                                                                11–247
Keyin Reference




11.88.1. Format
             UD devnam1,devnam2

         where:

         devnam1
              identifies a disk unit that is up (UP) or suspended (SU), in use, and not currently in
              Unit Duplexing mode.

         devnam2
              identifies an unassigned unit that is reserved (RV).

         Note: Use the ABORT keyin to stop processing of the UD keyin.


11.89. UL—Rewind Tape Units (with Interlock)
         The UL keyin rewinds a tape unit or all tape units connected to a control unit, and
         clears the premount status of the tape units. The UL keyin is used only on systems
         with TAVR. The keyin is allowed if the unit is up (UP) or reserved (RV).

         The UL keyin, which is applicable only to tape units, does not cause a change in the
         status of a unit. Rather, it makes a unit containing a premounted reel available to you
         for a subsequent reel mount.

         Use this keyin instead of manually rewinding and removing a premounted reel at the
         tape unit. It ensures that the system is aware that the reel is no longer premounted.

         Also use the UL keyin:

             When you need to unload premounted reels that you expect cannot be assigned
              in the near future
             When a run terminates in error before all anticipated reels have been used
             When the run does not start as you expected

11.89.1. Formats
             UL devnam/cu

              Rewinds a specified tape unit connected to a control unit and clears the premount
              status.

             UL,ALL cu

              Rewinds all tape units connected to a control unit and clears the premount status.




11–248                                                                                7831 0281–034
                                                                          Keyin Reference




11.90. UP—Bring Up Components and Interfaces
         The UP keyin brings up a component that is down, suspended, or reserved. The keyin
         works for any component with a status of down (DN) or reserved (RV), or any disk
         drive with a status of suspended (SU). You cannot use the component, however,
         unless a path to the component is available.

11.90.1. Formats
         There are many formats to the UP keyin. You can specify a particular component,
         specify a path, or use an option with or without a particular component.

         Bringing Up Components
         The following keyins specify a component:

           UP ip

            Adds the specified instruction processor to the partition.

           UP iop

            Adds the specified IOP to the partition.

           UP xiipname

            Adds the specified XIIP to the partition.

           UP xpcname

            Adds the specified XPC to the partition.

           UP mmod

            Adds the specified memory module to the partition.

           UP cmod

            Adds the specified channel module to the partition.

           UP cu

            Adds the specified control unit to the partition.

           UP devnam

            Adds the specified device to the partition.
           UP,TIME iopname

            Makes the time sources associated with iopname available to Exec.




7831 0281–034                                                                         11–249
Keyin Reference




         If entering console keyins related to CSB maintenance on a system that does not
         support a clock calendar or an external time source, the following message is
         displayed:
           UP,TIME keyins are not supported on this system

         Bringing Up an Interface
         The following keyin specifies an interface:
           UP cmod/[intf#]/cu

             Makes the interface from the specified channel module to the control unit
             available. If you do not specify an interface number, the Exec supplies a value.
             Also use this keyin for bringing up an interface to the record lock processor (RLP).

         Bringing Up Devices
         The following keyins specify an option:
           UP,ALL cu

             Adds all devices attached to the specified control unit to the partition.
           UP,PACK pack-id[/[dir-id]// [pack-id]...]

             Places the specified packs in the UP state.

         Note: To use the UP,PACK keyin in a local environment, the NEWCON configuration
         parameter REQNBRPST0 must be greater than zero. To use the UP,PACK keyin in a
         shared environment, the NEWCON configuration parameter REQNBRPST1 must be
         greater than zero. See the Exec System Software Installation and Configuration Guide
         (7830 7915) for more information on these configuration parameters.

         Enabling XPC Caching
           UP,XCACHE xpcname
           UP,XCACHE xpcname/LOCAL
           UP,XCACHE xpcname/SHARED

             Enables caching with the specified XPC (xpcname).
             Use the UP,XCACHE xpcname keyin to enable local and shared caching for each
             host using the XPC.
             Use the UP,XCACHE xpcname/LOCAL keyin to enable a host’s local caching using
             the XPC. If local caching was disabled using the DN,XCACHE xpcname/LOCAL
             keyin, local caching can only be enabled using an UP,XCACHE xpcname/LOCAL
             keyin.
             Use the UP,XCACHE xpcname/SHARED keyin to enable a host’s shared caching
             using the XPC. Once specified, shared caching can be disabled using a DN,XCACHE
             xpcname/SHARED keyin or a DN,XCACHE xpcname keyin. The UP,XCACHE
             xpcname/SHARED keyin can be issued when the XPC is in MHM mode.




11–250                                                                              7831 0281–034
                                                                                 Keyin Reference




           UP,MHM xpcname

             Initializes the XPC for use in a multihost environment.

11.90.2. System Response
         Some messages warn of undesirable consequences of your keyin and ask for a yes/no
         response.

11.90.3. Restrictions
         If your site has Security Level 2 or 3 or a Security Level 3 trusted environment, and the
         printout labeling feature is configured, do not use the UP keyin to bring up a printer. If
         you do, print files do not have security labels, are treated as system high, and the
         printout labeling feature is overridden. Use the RV keyin instead.

         Remember, when you bring up a fixed disk, you initialize the disk and that initialization
         erases all disk files.

         Before the system can bring up a control unit, it must enable all usable interfaces to
         the control unit. If the system cannot enable all the interfaces, it displays this
         message:

           ENABLE OF channel/module/cu INTERFACE FAILED

         Bring down the failing interface and bring up the control unit again.

         Bringing up a disk that is prepped as removable causes the files on that disk to be
         registered with the system.

         If your system uses the Tape Automatic Volume Recognition (TAVR) feature, do not
         perform the UP keyin on a tape unit with a mounted reel when the MODIFY CONFIG
         message appears. Wait until the SYS START message is complete. If you try to
         premount a tape before this point, the system displays this message:

           device [ipnam]/iopnam/cmname/cuname NOTRDY REWI run-id A,B,M

         Answer B to this message.

         Bring the reel to load point before you attempt input to or output from the tape unit. If
         you attempt input or output to the tape first, the system displays this message:

           tape-unit IS SELECTED

         The FS keyin shows the identifier of the reel on the mounted tape, but it does not
         show the status as mounted.




7831 0281–034                                                                               11–251
Keyin Reference




11.90.4. Examples
         Example 1
           UP BMCA

            Brings up component BMCA and displays this message:
              BMCA UP

         Example 2
           UP Z0T0.Z0T1

            Brings up tapes Z0T0 and Z0T1 and displays this message:
              Z0T0 UP , Z0T1 UP

         Example 3
           UP,ALL CUTAPD

            Brings up all the devices (TAPED3 and TAPED4) attached to control unit CUTAPD
            and displays this message:
              CUTAPD UPTAPED3 UP , TAPED4 UP

         Example 4
           UP XPCA

            Brings up XPC on host A and displays the mode of the XPC:
              XPCA UP LOCAL - XPC LOCAL CACHING ENABLED FOR THIS HOST

         Example 5
           UP,TIME IOP18

            Both time sources are installed in IOP18. However, the primary time source is
            shown as having been selected by Exec as its time reference.
              IOP18 (TIME)            PRIMARY        UP        EXEC
                 SECONDARY      UP

            When only the primary time source is installed, the following message is
            displayed:
              UP,TIME IOP18
              IOP18 (TIME)      PRIMARY         UP
                                SECONDARY       DN        NP

            where NP indicates that the secondary time source hardware is not present.




11–252                                                                         7831 0281–034
                                                                             Keyin Reference




             If entering console keyins related to CSB maintenance on a system that does not
             support a clock calendar or an external time source, the following message is
             displayed:
                UP,TIME keyins are not supported on this system

             When the remaining external source has lost access to its time reference, the
             following message is displayed:
                UP,TIME IOP18
                IOP18 (TIME)          PRIMARY         DN
                                      SECONDARY       UP   DEGR     EXEC

             When the request fails, the following message is displayed:
                UP,TIME IOP18
                x Primary time source on IOP18 failed (NZCC 006). Answer GO
                IOP18 (TIME)    PRIMARY      DN
                                SECONDARY    UP


11.91. X—Terminate a Run
         The X keyin aborts the specified batch run. It terminates the current task on a demand
         run, but allows the run to continue with the next control statement.
         Use the X keyin only when the E keyin is ineffective. The E keyin allows shared
         software subsystems and common banks (for example Checkpoint/Restart) to remove
         the run from internal tables. The X keyin does not notify shared software subsystems
         nor common banks, and as in the example, a new checkpoints or restarts cannot be
         processed because they are queued.
         You can abort a user run at the user’s request; however, do not abort system runs
         that use UDS unless directed to do so by your site administrator.
         Multiple X keyins can be required to terminate a run because the first terminates the
         run, but can start a termination task. If the operating system’s dynamic configuration
         parameter run_termination_task is not blank, then a program will run after the first X
         keyin.

11.91.1. Format
           X run-id

         where run-id is the name of the active run to terminate.

11.91.2. System Response
         If you try to terminate a run that is suspended, the system responds as follows:
           run-id IS SUSPENDED - X KEYIN NOT ALLOWED

         You must resume the run-ID before you can terminate it.




7831 0281–034                                                                               11–253
Keyin Reference




11.91.3. Example
             X BOB

              The system responds with
                BOB ABORT
                BOB ABORT FIN


11.92. XPCDUMP—Dump XPC Storage
         The XPCDUMP keyin dumps selected nonvolatile storage (NVS) segments from the
         XPC to tape or mass storage.

11.92.1. Format
             XPCDUMP[,MS|X] [xpc-name]

         where:

         MS
              indicates the dump is to mass storage. If MS is not specified, the dump is to tape.

         X
              aborts a previously initiated XPCDUMP.

         xpc-name
              is the XPC name. This field is optional, but if it is entered, the name is checked for
              validity. If no XPC is identified, the currently configured XPC is selected.

              Note: Use the ABORT keyin to stop processing of the XPCDUMP keyin.

11.92.2. Restrictions
         If an XPCDUMP is initiated at MODIFY CONFIG time, the MODIFY CONFIG should not
         be answered "done" until the XPCDUMP is complete. If it is answered "done" before
         the XPCDUMP completes, the XPCDUMP can fail and the dump path can be downed.

         The XPCDUMP keyin is not supported on XPC FO environment and will be rejected
         with a new console message. The message is as follows:
             XPCDUMP keyin is not supported on this model of XPC

         Other keyins relative to XPC caching terminate without any action and display existing
         console messages that indicate that XPC caching is not supported.




11–254                                                                               7831 0281–034
                                                                                Keyin Reference




11.92.3. Example
           XPCDUMP

           The system responds with

                XPC dump: Enter tape device name <EXIT$>

            Reply with the name of the tape device to be used for the XPC dump.

            The system responds with

                XPC dump: Enter tape reel ID <EXIT$>

            Premount the tape on the unit selected and reply with the reel ID of the tape to be
            used for the XPC dump.

            The system responds with

                XPC dump: Enter dump script <default>, directory indexes, EXIT, or ?

            Reply with one of the following dump scripts or a string of Directory Table indexes
            or index ranges:

            DIRECTORY         Dumps only the header blocks out to the dump file. The Directory
                              Table is included in the header blocks.
            DEFAULT           Dumps the NVS areas defined by Directory Table indexes 1 through
                              300. This dump script is automatically selected if you respond to the
                              read and reply message with no output.
            ALL               Dumps the NVS areas defined by all entries in the Directory Table.

            EXIT              Terminates the XPCDUMP process.

            ?                 Displays a list of the valid dump scripts (DIRECTORY, DEFAULT, and
                              ALL).
            string of         Dumps the storage areas specified in the Directory Table indexes. For
            Directory         example, enter 2–20, 27,30, to dump the storage areas defined by
            Table indexes     Directory Table indexes 2 through 20, 27, and 30.
            or index
            ranges




7831 0281–034                                                                                 11–255
Keyin Reference




         At the completion of the dump, the system responds with the following:

           XPC dump: Completed writing block-cnt blocks
           XPC dump: File name is SYS$*XPC$DUMP$.
           XPC dump: Tape reel ID is reel-id
           XPC dump: Do you want to dump more on same tape? Y/<N>

         where:

         block-cnt
             Indicates the number of blocks written to the file.

         reel-id
             Indicates the reel number of the tape file.

         If you reply with an N, the XPCDUMP process is terminated.




11–256                                                                        7831 0281–034
Appendix A
Restrictions and Operating
Considerations

         This appendix describes restrictions and operating considerations that do not appear
         elsewhere in this manual.


A.1. Restrictions
         If the compartment security feature is installed on your system, and you execute the
         CK keyin and then the RS keyin, the run is not necessarily restarted with the
         compartment set in execution at the time of the checkpoint. If you perform the RS
         keyin, the default compartment set for the user-id entered is given to the restarted
         run.

         If the common bank protection feature is installed on your system, you cannot
         execute the CK or RS keyins. The CKRS product is not compatible with the common
         bank protection feature. If you attempt to do the CK or RS keyins, the system displays
         an error message.

         See the ClearPath OS 2200 Software Products Installation Guide (7831 0612) for
         additional restrictions.


A.2. Operating Considerations
         This subsection describes permanent limitations on the operation of a system,
         product, or feature.

A.2.1. Dumps
         While booting the system, you cannot access user files on mass storage and copy the
         contents of those files into a dump file. You also cannot obtain a dump listing of those
         files.

A.2.2. Symbiont Control
         You cannot use the SX keyin on a print file that is exclusively assigned to a run.




7831 0281–034                                                                                 A–1
Restrictions and Operating Considerations




A.2.3. System Logging
        For systems with log configured to tape, if the system log tapes are not mounted
        quickly, an XER 070 system stop results.

A.2.4. TIP Session Control
        TIP Session Control is valid only if the highest application group number configured on
        the system is nine or less.
